#+title: Interfaces register
#+author: jefffarr
#+startup: overview

* About this register

This file is the authoritative catalogue of the contracts that flow across
module boundaries in the isolated emacs config. It is the artifact the
opsx-orchestrator's Architect role audits against in three triggers:

- *On-touch* (in execute) — when an Implementor diff modifies code cited
  in a =load_bearing: true= entry, the Architect runs against that entry
  only.
- *End-of-cycle* (in integrate) — full signal-class run across the cycle's
  diffs and the register.
- *Between-cycle* (=/architect-audit=, on demand) — register vs. whole repo;
  consolidation-round-as-a-button.

Each entry is a YAML-fenced block under one of the four tier-headings below,
following the templates at:

- =~/.claude/skills/opsx-orchestrate/templates/register-entry-shape.md=
- =~/.claude/skills/opsx-orchestrate/templates/register-entry-vocabulary.md=
- =~/.claude/skills/opsx-orchestrate/templates/register-entry-boundary.md=
- =~/.claude/skills/opsx-orchestrate/templates/register-entry-invariant.md=

Per-entry =status= is one of =speculated | confirmed | divergent |
reconciled=. =load_bearing: true= signals the entry should receive on-touch
Architect attention whenever cited code changes. New entries land as
=speculated= during plan-phase forward-mode and are reconciled at integrate
based on Implementor =## Discoveries=.

Lifecycle reference: =~/.claude/skills/opsx-orchestrate/SKILL.md=,
=scaffolding.md=, =flows/{plan,execute,integrate}.md=.

* Conventions

- *One entry per concept.* If two entries describe the same shape with
  different field sets, that's drift — reconcile, don't duplicate.
- *Tier discipline.* Before creating an invariant entry, check whether the
  rule fits =shape=, =vocabulary=, or =boundary= — those tiers carry their
  own enforcement; the invariant tier is the safety net for rules that
  don't fit structurally.
- *Cite specific files and functions.* "the validator" is not a citation;
  =config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org=, function
  =jf/gptel-scope--load-config= is.
- *Past incidents anchor entries.* The =why_it_matters= field of an
  invariant is most credible when it names an incident this project
  has actually paid for.

* Shape

  Entries describe plists, alists, structs, records that cross module
  boundaries. Producers and consumers are listed; the canonical
  constructor (if any) is the single allowed producer.

** scope-config-plist
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-scope-config-plist
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/scope-config-plist
   tier: shape
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): :paths sub-plist now carries six
     list-valued keys (:read :read-metadata :write :modify :execute :deny)
     per ask 10A; :security removed everywhere. Reader (loader) and writer
     (mapper) both honor the new shape. Fixture-locus pending in cycle-3
     migrate-* tasks. See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1777470320/
     reconciliations/shape-scope-config-plist.md.
     cycle-4 (2026-04-29) Ask 2 disposition: :cloud.allowed-providers
     sub-shape narrowed from "list of provider names" to "list of
     keywords"; the loader at --load-from-buffer is the normalisation
     boundary (string drawer values → keywords at parse time). The
     cloud-auth ops detector and the validator both work in keywords;
     the loader closes the type-mismatch.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Loaded scope configuration plist (post-:security-removal)
   purpose: |
     The plist returned by the scope-validation loader and consumed by every
     stage of the validation pipeline (paths matcher, glob matcher, cloud-auth
     gate). Previously the loader produced a three-key plist (:paths :cloud
     :security); this change collapses to two keys because :security collapses
     into module-level constants. Every consumer that read (plist-get config
     :security ...) becomes a constant read; every consumer of :paths and
     :cloud is unchanged in shape.

   required_keys:
     - name: ":paths"
       type: plist
       description: |
         Sub-plist with six list-valued keys: :read :read-metadata :write
         :modify :execute :deny. Each is a list of glob patterns; missing
         keys default to nil (empty list, i.e. "no patterns of this kind").
         :read-metadata was added in cycle-2 (ask 10A) to support the
         GPTEL_SCOPE_READ_METADATA bucket; metadata-only paths in this list
         permit stat-style operations but not content reads.
     - name: ":cloud"
       type: plist
       description: |
         Sub-plist with two keys: :auth-detection (string, one of "allow" /
         "warn" / "deny"; defaults to "warn") and :allowed-providers
         (list of keywords, e.g. `:aws`, `:gcp`; defaults to nil).
         The drawer carries provider names as bare strings
         (`:GPTEL_SCOPE_CLOUD_PROVIDERS: aws gcp`); the loader at
         `jf/gptel-scope--load-from-buffer` normalizes those strings
         to keywords at parse time so this plist key holds keywords
         throughout the validation pipeline. The validator's
         `(member provider allowed-providers)` check at
         `jf/gptel-scope--validate-cloud-auth` therefore compares
         keywords against keywords (the cloud-auth ops detector emits
         keyword providers like `:aws`, `:aws-cli`).

   forbidden_keys:
     - name: ":security"
       reason: |
         Removed in this change. The two former values
         (:enforce-parse-complete, :max-coverage-threshold) become module-
         level defconsts in scope-validation.el. Any (plist-get config
         :security ...) read in implementation code is a divergence signal.

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--load-from-buffer
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--load-from-file
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--load-config

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope-authorize-tool-call
       reads_keys: [":paths", ":cloud"]
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
       function: validate-bash (Stages 2-4)
       reads_keys: [":paths"]

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-scope-config-plist (val)
       "Returns nil on success; an error symbol otherwise."
       (cond ((not (plistp val)) 'not-a-plist)
             ((plist-member val :security) 'forbidden-security-key)
             ((not (plistp (plist-get val :paths))) 'paths-not-plist)
             ((not (plistp (plist-get val :cloud))) 'cloud-not-plist)
             ;; :cloud.allowed-providers, when present, must be a
             ;; list of keywords (cycle-4 Ask 2; loader-side normalised).
             ((let ((ap (plist-get (plist-get val :cloud)
                                    :allowed-providers)))
                (and ap (not (cl-every #'keywordp ap))))
              'allowed-providers-not-keywords)
             (t nil)))

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** drawer-text-block
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-drawer-text-block
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/drawer-text-block
   tier: shape
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): both consumers (sessions/commands.org
     and tools/persistent-agent.org) consume the renderer's drawer-text
     string via (concat drawer-text body) and write in a single
     with-temp-file. Structural anchors hold. See .orchestrator/cycles/
     cycle-1777470320/reconciliations/shape-drawer-text-block.md.
     cycle-5 integrate (2026-05-01): the shape extension landed.
     jf/gptel-scope-profile--render-drawer-text now accepts an optional
     PRESET-SPEC and emits :GPTEL_MODEL:, :GPTEL_BACKEND:, :GPTEL_TOOLS:,
     :GPTEL_TEMPERATURE:, :GPTEL_MAX_TOKENS:, :GPTEL_NUM_MESSAGES_TO_SEND:
     between :GPTEL_PRESET:/:GPTEL_PARENT_SESSION_ID: and the scope keys
     when PRESET-SPEC is non-nil. :GPTEL_SYSTEM: is never emitted by any
     producer (verified by spec absence assertions). The chat-mode save
     path (gptel-chat--save-state, via the new
     gptel-chat--write-config-drawer helper) is a peer producer that
     writes the same key set from buffer-local state on every save
     (full snapshot, no delta-from-preset deletion). Round-trip
     idempotency between the renderer's output and the save-path's
     output is exercised by snapshot-rendering-spec.el and the new
     end-to-end scenarios in preset-application-spec.el. Cited by
     tasks: extend-render-drawer-text-with-preset-snapshot (43d045f),
     replace-chat-save-with-full-snapshot-writer (5f9d762),
     wire-snapshot-into-session-creation (9f4b5fd),
     update-preset-application-spec-for-snapshot (3a8abaf).
     cycle-1781448273 integrate (2026-06-14, change
     gptel-content-addressed-session-activation): CONFIRMED under
     extension. emit-identity-keys-in-writers (merge b286997) splices the
     identity keys (:GPTEL_SESSION_ID:, :GPTEL_BRANCH:) into this block at
     all three writers via the existing jf/gptel--append-drawer-property
     (sessions) and a buffer-based set helper (branching). The shape is
     UNCHANGED — identity keys are additional key-lines already permitted by
     the structural invariants (one :PROPERTIES:/:END:, one key-line each,
     trailing newline); the reviewer confirmed all four validator checks
     stay clean across the three writers. A speculative second REPLACE
     helper (jf/gptel--replace-drawer-property) was added then removed as
     dead code (review F1, inline-fixed f891d34) so exactly one append
     primitive and one branch buffer-setter remain.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Drawer-text string returned by render-drawer-text
   purpose: |
     A string of the form ":PROPERTIES:\n<key-lines>\n:END:\n" that callers
     prepend to the chat-mode initial content when writing a fresh
     session.org. Producer is jf/gptel-scope-profile--render-drawer-text;
     consumers are session-creation (sessions/commands.org) and persistent-
     agent creation (tools/persistent-agent.org). The shape is "an org
     :PROPERTIES: block as a whole string"; mis-shaping it (missing :END:,
     stray characters before :PROPERTIES:, doubled drawer) corrupts every
     subsequent reader on the file.

   required_substring_anchors:
     - anchor: ":PROPERTIES:"
       must_appear: at byte 0 (or after a leading whitespace-only prefix)
     - anchor: ":END:"
       must_appear: exactly once, after :PROPERTIES: and after all key lines
     - anchor: ":GPTEL_PRESET:"
       must_appear: exactly once between :PROPERTIES: and :END:

   structural_invariants:
     - the block contains zero ":PROPERTIES:" tokens after the first one
     - every line between :PROPERTIES: and :END: matches the regex
       "^:[A-Z_+]+:[ \\t].*$"
     - the trailing newline after :END: is included (so callers can simply
       concat with the chat-mode initial content)

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope-profiles.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope-profile--render-drawer-text

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/commands.org
       function: jf/gptel-persistent-session
       usage: prepend to chat-mode initial content
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/activities-integration.org
       function: <session-creation entrypoint>
       usage: same
     - file: config/gptel/tools/persistent-agent.org
       function: <agent-creation function around persistent-agent.el:111>
       usage: same

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-drawer-text-block (val)
       (cond ((not (stringp val)) 'not-a-string)
             ((not (string-match-p "\\`:PROPERTIES:\n" val))
              'missing-properties-header)
             ((not (string-match-p "\n:END:\n\\'" val))
              'missing-end-or-trailing-newline)
             ((/= 1 (cl-count-if (lambda (line)
                                   (string= line ":PROPERTIES:"))
                                 (split-string val "\n")))
              'duplicated-properties-header)
             (t nil)))

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** violation-info
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-violation-info
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/violation-info
   tier: shape
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): no shape change; cycle-1 reconciliation
     carries forward. The expansion writer reads :operation and :resource
     only.
   load_bearing: true
   title: violation-info plist returned by validator and consumed by expansion writer
   purpose: |
     The plist a validator-pipeline failure builds and the expansion UI
     consumes. Listed here because this change rewires the writer that
     consumes it (jf/gptel-scope--add-to-scope and friends switch from a
     YAML mutator to a drawer mutator), which means the writer's reads
     of :operation, :resource, :validation-type, and :tool become the
     authoritative consumer-set for the new flow. Pre-existing fragmentation
     of the shape across producer sites (validation, bash-parser branches)
     is in scope for the on-touch architect.

   required_keys:
     - name: ":tool"
       type: string
       description: name of the scoped tool (e.g. "read_file_in_scope")
     - name: ":resource"
       type: string
       description: the path / pattern the tool tried to access
     - name: ":operation"
       type: keyword
       description: |
         One of (:read :read-directory :read-metadata :match-pattern :write
         :create :create-or-modify :append :delete :modify :execute). The
         writer collapses these to one of five drawer keys via
         jf/gptel-scope--map-operation-to-drawer-key.
     - name: ":validation-type"
       type: keyword
       description: one of (:path :bash); selects the writer dispatch arm

   optional_keys:
     - name: ":reason"
       type: string
       description: human-readable reason; surfaced in the expansion menu
     - name: ":error"
       type: string
       description: machine-readable error code (e.g. "no_scope_config")

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
       function: build-violation-info (and the bash-pipeline variants)

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-to-scope
       reads_keys: [":validation-type", ":operation", ":resource", ":tool"]
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-path-to-scope
       reads_keys: [":operation", ":resource"]
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-bash-to-scope
       reads_keys: [":operation", ":resource"]

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-violation-info (val)
       (cond ((not (plistp val)) 'not-a-plist)
             ((not (stringp (plist-get val :tool))) 'missing-tool)
             ((not (stringp (plist-get val :resource))) 'missing-resource)
             ((not (memq (plist-get val :validation-type) '(:path :bash)))
              'bad-validation-type)
             (t nil)))

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** session-document-layout
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-session-document-layout
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/session-document-layout
   tier: shape
   status: superseded
   superseded_by: register/shape/session-sibling-system-prompt-file
   superseded_in_cycle: cycle-1779568860
   supersession_note: |
     replace-system-prompt-heading-with-sibling-file (cycles
     1779565028 + 1779568860) wholesale removed the `* System Prompt`
     and `* Chat` headings this shape pinned. The canonical session.org
     layout collapses to: configuration :PROPERTIES: drawer at
     point-min (now carrying an OPTIONAL :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE:
     key pointing at a sibling system-prompt.<ext> file), followed
     directly by zero or more #+begin_user / #+begin_assistant turn
     blocks. The four structural_invariants below — singleton
     `* System Prompt' heading, singleton `* Chat' heading, turn
     blocks under `* Chat', and the embedded validator's two
     "*-not-singleton" symbols — no longer hold for any session
     emitted by the post-cycle producers. The entry is retained for
     historical record; the replacement contract is at
     register/shape/session-sibling-system-prompt-file.
   status_note: |
     cycle-7 plan (2026-05-22) — speculated by forward-mode against
     design.md §Addendum (Finding B, Finding C, Decisions B and C).
     cycle-7 integrate (2026-05-22): reconciled. The shape held for
     the session-creation renderer (commands.org / commands.el via the
     new jf/gptel--session-headings-block helper) and for the chat-mode
     save path materialise-on-first-save fallback. Producers prose
     tightened: the commands.org helper emits only the two heading
     blocks (config drawer is prepended separately by the renderer),
     and persistent-agent.org is added as a third producer that
     currently bypasses the layout (see follow-up task route-agent-
     session-creation-through-canonical-layout). See cycle-1779477564/
     reconciliations/shape-session-document-layout.md.
     cycle-8 integrate (2026-05-23): narrative update — the
     persistent-agent.org CYCLE-7 GAP closed by task route-agent-
     session-creation-through-canonical-layout (merge_commit f052eb9):
     jf/gptel-persistent-agent--initial-body now delegates to the
     shared jf/gptel--session-headings-block helper to emit the folded
     `* System Prompt` (body seeded from the agent preset's :system
     text when present) followed by `* Chat`, with the file-level
     config drawer prepended by the agent caller. Layout validator
     pins the four invariants for agent-created session.org files via
     new buttercup specs in tools/test/persistent-agent/creation-spec.el.
     The pre-Addendum exemplar helpers (`jf/gptel--initial-session-content`,
     `jf/gptel-persistent-agent--initial-content`) and their tests
     were deleted by task remove-dead-initial-session-content-helpers
     (merge_commit 4488b71); the canonical layout is now the only
     producer shape any reader encounters. cycle-7 helper-extraction
     in chat/menu.org and cycle-8 harden-system-prompt-save (against
     missing `* Chat`) preserve the four structural invariants. See
     cycle-1779522837/reconciliations/shape-session-document-layout.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Structural layout of a freshly-created session.org document
   purpose: |
     The whole-document shape of a session.org file as emitted by the
     session-creation renderer (and as materialised on first save for
     pre-Addendum sessions). Where register/shape/drawer-text-block is
     the shape of the :PROPERTIES: block in isolation, this entry is
     the shape of the *document that contains it*: the drawer plus the
     two headings the Addendum introduces. Mis-shaping the document
     (drawer not at point-min, two `* System Prompt` headings, turn
     blocks above `* Chat`) breaks the restore path and the chat
     parser. Drawer/document-shape corruption has cost real time on
     this change — hence load_bearing.

   document_regions:
     - region: file-level config drawer
       location: point-min
       shape: |
         A file-level org :PROPERTIES: ... :END: block at point-min,
         exactly the string produced by register/shape/drawer-text-block.
         Location is unchanged from pre-Addendum sessions — chosen over
         making it the `* System Prompt` heading's own drawer because
         org-entry-put / gptel-org--entry-properties at point-min are
         unchanged (lowest code risk, Decision B).
       emitted: first, before any heading
     - region: System Prompt heading
       location: first heading after the config drawer
       shape: |
         A `* System Prompt` heading carrying its own
         :PROPERTIES:/:VISIBILITY: folded/:END: drawer, followed by a
         body. The body is seeded at creation from the active preset's
         :system text; when the preset has no :system the body is empty
         (heading present, no body text). The heading body — not a
         drawer property — is the authoritative system prompt (see
         register/invariant/system-prompt-heading-authoritative).
       emitted: after the config drawer
     - region: Chat heading
       location: last heading
       shape: |
         A `* Chat` heading under which #+begin_user / #+begin_assistant
         turn blocks live. Turn blocks never appear above `* Chat`.
       emitted: after the System Prompt heading

   structural_invariants:
     - the file-level config drawer is still at point-min (no heading,
       no blank line, no content precedes ":PROPERTIES:")
     - exactly one "* System Prompt" heading exists in the document
     - exactly one "* Chat" heading exists in the document
     - every #+begin_user / #+begin_assistant turn block appears under
       the "* Chat" heading, never under "* System Prompt" and never
       before any heading
     - the chat parser stays heading-indifferent: it locates turn
       blocks by #+begin_user / #+begin_assistant markers, not by
       headings, so gptel-chat-new scratch buffers (bare #+begin_user
       blocks, no headings, no drawer) remain valid input

   known_limitation: |
     Because the chat parser is heading-indifferent (Decision 12 /
     design.md §Finding B), a `* System Prompt` body containing a
     literal column-0 "#+begin_user" line would be mis-parsed as a
     turn block. Preset system prompts in this repo do not contain
     such a line; flagged for the implementor to accept or guard.

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/commands.org
       function: |
         jf/gptel--session-headings-block (heading helper) +
         session-creation renderer
       usage: |
         The heading helper emits only the two heading blocks
         (`* System Prompt` seeded from the active preset's :system,
         then `* Chat`). The file-level config :PROPERTIES: drawer is
         prepended by the session renderer (delegated to register/
         boundary/scope-profile-applicator stage 2a) before the
         heading helper's output.
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: chat-mode save path (gptel-chat--save-state and
         helpers)
       usage: |
         materialises the `* System Prompt` heading for pre-Addendum
         sessions on first save under the new code (graceful-degrade
         path, cf. Decision 6 / Decision B), and on every save
         serialises gptel--system-message back into the heading body
         (cf. register/invariant/system-prompt-heading-authoritative).
     - file: config/gptel/tools/persistent-agent.org
       function: |
         jf/gptel-persistent-agent--initial-body (agent-session
         creator)
       usage: |
         Conforming producer (cycle-8 resolved arch-cycle-1779477564-1):
         delegates to jf/gptel--session-headings-block to emit the
         folded `* System Prompt` (body seeded from the agent preset's
         :system text when present) followed by `* Chat`. The
         file-level config drawer is prepended by the agent caller
         (jf/gptel-persistent-agent--task) via the scope-profile
         applicator boundary. Agent-created session.org files satisfy
         the validator's four structural invariants — pinned by
         buttercup specs in config/gptel/tools/test/persistent-agent/
         creation-spec.el ("PersistentAgent session.org matches the
         canonical document layout"). Tracking task
         route-agent-session-creation-through-canonical-layout
         closed cycle-8 (merge_commit f052eb9).

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: |
         gptel-chat--apply-system-prompt-heading (invoked as the final
         step of gptel-chat--apply-declared-preset)
       usage: reads the `* System Prompt` heading body via
         gptel-chat--system-prompt-heading-body and installs the
         result as buffer-local gptel--system-message (per register/
         invariant/system-prompt-heading-authoritative)
     - file: config/gptel/chat/mode.org
       function: gptel-chat--fold-config-drawer (attached to gptel-chat-
         mode-hook)
       usage: folds the file-level config :PROPERTIES: drawer on
         gptel-chat-mode activation (per register/boundary/chat-mode-
         session-display override C)
     - file: config/gptel/chat/parser.org
       function: chat turn parser
       usage: locates turn blocks under `* Chat` (heading-indifferent)

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-session-document-layout (buf)
       "Return nil when BUF matches the speculated session-document
     layout, or a symbol naming the first violated invariant."
       (with-current-buffer buf
         (save-excursion
           (goto-char (point-min))
           (cond
            ((not (looking-at-p "[ \t\n]*:PROPERTIES:"))
             'config-drawer-not-at-point-min)
            ((/= 1 (count-matches "^\\* System Prompt[ \t]*$"
                                  (point-min) (point-max)))
             'system-prompt-heading-not-singleton)
            ((/= 1 (count-matches "^\\* Chat[ \t]*$"
                                  (point-min) (point-max)))
             'chat-heading-not-singleton)
            (t
             (let ((chat-pos (progn (goto-char (point-min))
                                    (re-search-forward
                                     "^\\* Chat[ \t]*$" nil t)))
                   (turn-pos (progn (goto-char (point-min))
                                    (re-search-forward
                                     "^#\\+begin_\\(user\\|assistant\\)"
                                     nil t))))
               (if (and turn-pos chat-pos (< turn-pos chat-pos))
                   'turn-block-before-chat-heading
                 nil)))))))

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
     - register/invariant/system-prompt-heading-authoritative
     - register/boundary/chat-mode-session-display
     - register/shape/session-sibling-system-prompt-file        # replacement

   discovered_from: gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** session-sibling-system-prompt-file
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-session-sibling-system-prompt-file
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/session-sibling-system-prompt-file
   tier: shape
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1779568860 (2026-05-23) — created by task
     mark-superseded-interfaces-register-entries as part of
     `replace-system-prompt-heading-with-sibling-file`. Pinned by
     producers (jf/gptel--create-session-core,
     jf/gptel-persistent-agent--task) writing the sibling file at
     creation, by chat-mode consumers
     (gptel-chat--apply-system-prompt-file at activation,
     gptel-chat--refresh-system-prompt-from-file before every
     gptel-request), and by buttercup specs covering both producer
     and consumer paths (session-org-creation-spec.el,
     persistent-agent/creation-spec.el, system-prompt-file-spec.el,
     pre-send-refresh-spec.el). Supersedes
     register/shape/session-document-layout.
     cycle-1779572373 (2026-05-24) — task replace-system-prompt-
     infix-with-file-opener added gptel-chat--edit-system-prompt-
     file as a fourth consumer of gptel-chat--system-prompt-file-
     path. The new affordance reads the drawer property via the
     shared resolver (pinning the basename-resolution rule), opens
     the resolved file via find-file-other-window (pinning the
     "file is canonical, buffer is cache" mental model from the
     paired invariant entry), and when the property is unset
     writes :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: + creates the sibling file
     at the resolved path (exercising the
     drawer-property-inside-PROPERTIES-block and sibling-in-same-
     dir-as-session.org invariants during creation). Four new
     buttercup specs in system-prompt-file-spec.el cover the
     affordance; the reviewer-driven follow-up (commit 5cd26b0)
     additionally pins the save-buffer side-effect via spy + on-
     disk re-read and the read-string DEFAULT-VALUE argument via
     spy-calls-args-for.
     cycle-1781857401 (2026-06-19) — task pre-send-compose-and-wire
     WIDENED the gptel-chat--refresh-system-prompt-from-file consumer
     from a sibling-file cache refresh into the per-send COMPOSER
     (role + "\n\n" + environment-block; see the updated consumers
     entry and register/shape/environment-block + register/invariant/
     composed-system-message-write-only). The on-disk file SHAPE and the
     :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: drawer key are UNCHANGED — only the
     consumer's job widened — so this shape entry STAYS confirmed.
     On-touch Architect arch-cycle-1781857401-on1 (advisory interface-
     drift) flagged exactly this consumer-prose lag; reconciled here.
   load_bearing: true
   title: |
     Shape of the on-disk sibling system-prompt file and its
     drawer-property reference
   purpose: |
     The system prompt for a session.org buffer is materialised on
     disk as a sibling file (system-prompt.<ext>) next to session.org,
     and the session's configuration drawer carries a
     :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: property pointing at its basename (or
     an absolute path). This is the load-bearing shape for the
     restore precedence pinned by
     register/invariant/system-prompt-file-authoritative — without
     a well-defined shape for the on-disk file and its drawer
     reference, the invariant cannot enforce.

     Supersedes register/shape/session-document-layout (now status:
     superseded), which carried the system prompt as a `* System
     Prompt` heading body inside session.org. The heading traded one
     set of org-mode interactions for another (markdown content
     inside an org heading body broke fontification, folding, and
     the chat parser); the sibling-file shape lets the prompt render
     as the format the preset authored it in.

   document_regions:
     - region: file-level config drawer entry
       location: |
         Inside session.org's file-level :PROPERTIES: ... :END: block
         at point-min, alongside :GPTEL_PRESET: and the other
         drawer-resident keys.
       shape: |
         A single line of the form
         ":GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: <value>", where <value> is
         either an absolute path or a basename. Basenames resolve
         relative to (file-name-directory buffer-file-name) at
         consumer time. The key is OPTIONAL: a session whose preset
         declares no :system carries no sibling file and no
         property.
       emitted: |
         Spliced into the rendered drawer text by
         jf/gptel--append-drawer-property at session creation,
         before the drawer's :END: line — preserving the drawer +
         body adjacency invariant (no separator between :END: and
         the first turn block).
     - region: sibling file on disk
       location: |
         (file-name-as-directory <session-dir>) + "system-prompt." +
         <ext>, where <ext> is the preset's source-file extension
         (today: "md"; jf/gptel--preset-source-file-extension
         defensively defaults to "md" when the preset's source file
         cannot be resolved).
       shape: |
         A text file whose contents are the preset's :system body
         VERBATIM — no frontmatter, no escaping, no transformation.
         The on-disk file is the canonical source; the buffer-local
         gptel--system-message is a per-request cache, refreshed
         before every gptel-request from a chat-mode buffer.
       emitted: |
         Written by jf/gptel--write-system-prompt-sibling-file via
         write-region (no buffer mutation). Skipped when the
         preset's :system is nil, empty, or whitespace-only.

   structural_invariants:
     - the :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: drawer key (when present) is
       inside the drawer's :PROPERTIES: / :END: block — never
       between :END: and the first turn block
     - the sibling file (when present) lives in the same directory
       as session.org, resolved via expand-file-name against the
       buffer's file-name directory
     - the drawer property and the sibling file are emitted
       atomically by the same writer call: either both or neither
       (no orphan property pointing at a missing file at creation
       time; a user-driven delete-after-creation is handled
       gracefully by the consumer's file-readable-p guard)
     - the document shape is otherwise unchanged from the
       pre-Addendum layout: configuration :PROPERTIES: drawer at
       point-min, followed directly by zero or more turn blocks; no
       headings emitted

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/commands.org
       function: jf/gptel--create-session-core
       usage: |
         Calls jf/gptel--write-system-prompt-sibling-file with the
         resolved (main-branch-dir, preset-name, preset-spec), then
         splices :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: into the rendered drawer
         text via jf/gptel--append-drawer-property. Skipped when the
         caller supplies INITIAL-CONTENT (the override contract owns
         the whole shape).
     - file: config/gptel/tools/persistent-agent.org
       function: jf/gptel-persistent-agent--task
       usage: |
         Mirrors the interactive producer's pattern against the
         agent's flat session-dir (no branches/ subdirectory). The
         basename is threaded into the agent's drawer text via the
         same helper.

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: gptel-chat--system-prompt-file-path
       usage: |
         Resolves :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: from the drawer at
         point-min via org-entry-get. Returns nil when unset;
         resolves a basename against
         (file-name-directory buffer-file-name); returns an absolute
         path verbatim. Callers' file-readable-p check is the gate.
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: gptel-chat--apply-system-prompt-file
       usage: |
         Activation-time installer (runs LAST in
         gptel-chat--apply-declared-preset). Reads the file via
         insert-file-contents in a with-temp-buffer; installs the
         contents as buffer-local gptel--system-message when
         non-blank. No-op on nil path, unreadable file, or blank
         body.
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: gptel-chat--refresh-system-prompt-from-file
       usage: |
         The per-send COMPOSER installed as :before advice on
         gptel-request (widened from a cache refresh in cycle-
         1781857401 by task pre-send-compose-and-wire). For every send
         from a gptel-chat-mode buffer it recomposes the buffer-local
         gptel--system-message WHOLESALE as
         role + "\n\n" + (gptel-chat--build-environment-block), with
         the environment block as the tail (register/shape/
         environment-block). ROLE is resolved from STABLE sources only:
         the sibling file body re-read each send when
         :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: is set/readable/non-blank, else the
         buffer-local role base (register/invariant/composed-system-
         message-write-only, gptel-chat--system-prompt-base), else "".
         An unreadable/blank sibling logs a warning and falls back to
         the base (the original defensive guard, preserved) rather than
         dropping the prompt. The composer NEVER reads the prior
         composed gptel--system-message as input (idempotency). This
         widens the CONSUMER's job only — the on-disk sibling-file SHAPE
         and the :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: drawer key are UNCHANGED, so
         this entry stays confirmed.
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: gptel-chat--edit-system-prompt-file
       usage: |
         Chat-mode menu's "Edit system prompt" affordance
         (replaces upstream gptel-system-prompt in the chat-mode
         prefix only). Resolves the path via the shared resolver;
         when the property is set and the file exists, opens it
         via find-file-other-window; when the property is set but
         the file is absent, re-creates the empty file at the
         resolved path (no re-prompt) and opens it; when the
         property is unset, prompts for a basename (default
         system-prompt.md), writes :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: into
         the drawer via org-entry-put at point-min, persists with
         save-buffer, creates the empty file via write-region, and
         opens it. Edits target the file, not the buffer, so the
         next pre-send refresh has nothing to overwrite.

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-session-sibling-system-prompt-file (buf)
       "Return nil when BUF + its sibling file satisfy the shape
     contract, or a symbol naming the first violated invariant."
       (with-current-buffer buf
         (save-excursion
           (goto-char (point-min))
           (cond
            ((not (looking-at-p "[ \t\n]*:PROPERTIES:"))
             'config-drawer-not-at-point-min)
            ((let ((raw (and (fboundp 'org-entry-get)
                             (org-entry-get
                              (point-min)
                              "GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE"
                              'selective))))
               (and raw (not (stringp raw))))
             'drawer-property-not-a-string)
            ;; Adjacency check: when the property is set, the key
            ;; line precedes :END:.
            ((let* ((content (buffer-string))
                    (file-pos (string-match
                               ":GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE:"
                               content))
                    (end-pos (string-match "\n:END:\n" content)))
               (and file-pos end-pos (>= file-pos end-pos)))
             'drawer-property-after-end-marker)
            (t nil)))))

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
     - register/invariant/system-prompt-file-authoritative
     - register/invariant/drawer-system-key-write-exclusion
     - register/shape/session-document-layout                  # superseded predecessor

   supersedes:
     - register/shape/session-document-layout

   discovered_from: replace-system-prompt-heading-with-sibling-file
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** workspace-buffer-struct
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-workspace-buffer-struct
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/workspace-buffer-struct
   tier: shape
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260613-161149 integrate (2026-06-13, task serialize-window-
     params-readable): the =:bookmark= slot is now SCRUBBED at capture by
     =workspace--serialize-buffer= → =workspace--scrub-bookmark-record=,
     which nulls any record-prop VALUE satisfying
     =workspace--unreadable-object-p= (buffer/marker/overlay/frame/window/
     process/non-symbol-fn) while preserving keys. Slot type unchanged
     (bookmark-record | nil); this only guarantees the record is read-able
     (closes the bug#56643 class: a mode embedding an unreadable object in
     its bookmark record). The file/name fallback in
     register/vocabulary/buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain still covers
     a nulled-bookmark buffer. why_tests_missed: no prior test serialized
     a record with an embedded live object; new serialization-robustness-
     spec.el scenario (f) now does. See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-
     20260613-161149/reconciliations/shape-workspace-buffer-struct.md.

     cycle-20260524-200631 integrate (2026-05-24): producer/consumer
     function names updated to match shipped code
     (workspace--serialize-buffer / workspace--deserialize-buffer /
     workspace--bookmark-buffer / workspace--error-buffer /
     workspace--window-state-{serialize,deserialize}); required_keys /
     optional_keys / validator unchanged. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260524-200631/reconciliations/shape-workspace-buffer-struct.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: workspace-buffer cl-defstruct embedded in each window-state leaf's parameters
   purpose: |
     The cl-defstruct carried in the =parameters= slot of every leaf in a
     saved window-state. The capture-side walker (workspace--capture-frameset)
     constructs it from the live buffer; the restore-side walker
     (workspace--restore-frameset) consumes it and reincarnates the buffer
     via the four-step fallback chain (see register/vocabulary/buffer-
     reincarnation-fallback-chain and register/boundary/buffer-reincarnation-
     pipeline). The struct is the unit of "what was in this window slot at
     save time" — it replaces the v1 implicit "buffer-name in window-state"
     contract.

   required_keys:
     - name: bookmark
       type: bookmark-record | nil
       description: |
         Result of (bookmark-make-record) on the live buffer at save time.
         Nil when the buffer's major mode does not register a
         bookmark-make-record-function (rare; most modes inherit a default).
         When non-nil, it is the primary reincarnation source — it preserves
         point, narrowing, and major-mode-specific state, and works for
         non-file buffers (magit-status, eshell, *Messages*).
     - name: filename
       type: string | nil
       description: |
         The file path for file-backed buffers; nil otherwise. The
         second fallback when the bookmark restore errors or returns no
         buffer (e.g. help-mode bug#56643).
     - name: name
       type: string
       description: |
         The buffer-name string at save time. The third fallback, only
         hit when neither bookmark nor filename yields a buffer but a
         live buffer of the same name happens to exist in the session.
     - name: narrowed-p
       type: boolean
       description: |
         Recorded for diagnostic / future-use. Narrowing itself is
         carried by the bookmark record; this flag lets the restore path
         emit an error-buffer note when narrowing was expected but the
         bookmark restore did not restore it.
     - name: indirect-p
       type: boolean
       description: |
         Whether the saved buffer was an indirect (base-buffer) buffer.
         Indirect buffers cannot be re-created from a bookmark; the flag
         exists so the restorer can short-circuit to the error-buffer
         step when it would otherwise restore the base buffer.

   optional_keys:
     - name: local-variables
       type: alist | nil
       description: |
         Reserved for the buffer-local-variables capture extension
         (D7 non-goal). Always nil in v2; consumers must tolerate nil.
     - name: etc
       type: alist | nil
       description: |
         Forward-compat alist for per-leaf metadata that does not
         warrant a struct field. Matches activities-activity-state-etc.

   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
       function: workspace--capture-frameset (replaces v1 frameset-save path)
     - file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
       function: workspace--capture-leaf (new; one workspace-buffer per leaf)

   consumers:
     - file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
       function: workspace--restore-frameset
       reads_keys: [bookmark, filename, name, narrowed-p, indirect-p]
     - file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
       function: workspace--restore-leaf
       reads_keys: [bookmark, filename, name]

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-workspace-buffer (val)
       "Returns nil on success; error symbol otherwise."
       (cond ((not (workspace-buffer-p val)) 'not-a-workspace-buffer)
             ((not (or (null (workspace-buffer-bookmark val))
                       (consp (workspace-buffer-bookmark val))))
              'bookmark-not-record-shaped)
             ((not (or (null (workspace-buffer-filename val))
                       (stringp (workspace-buffer-filename val))))
              'filename-not-string-or-nil)
             ((not (stringp (workspace-buffer-name val))) 'name-not-string)
             (t nil)))

   test_corpus:
     valid:
       - { bookmark: '(("foo.el" (filename . "/p/foo.el") (position . 234)))',
           filename: "/p/foo.el", name: "foo.el",
           narrowed-p: false, indirect-p: false }
       - { bookmark: nil, filename: nil, name: "*scratch*",
           narrowed-p: false, indirect-p: false }
     invalid:
       - { bookmark: nil, filename: nil, name: nil }   # name required

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** layout-v2-plist
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-layout-v2-plist
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/layout-v2-plist
   tier: shape
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260613-161149 integrate (2026-06-13, task serialize-window-
     params-readable): the window-state stored in =:saved-state= /
     =:working-state= is now READABLE BY CONSTRUCTION. Buffer-bearing
     window parameters are run through
     =workspace-window-parameter-translators= in
     =workspace--window-state-serialize= (and reversed in
     =-deserialize=, with per-param =condition-case-unless-debug=
     drop-on-fail). Seeded with =window-preserved-size=: its
     =(BUFFER DIR SIZE)= value is serialized with the buffer's NAME
     (readable) and rehydrated via =get-buffer=. This fixes the root
     cause of the =#<killed buffer>= corruption (window-state-get's
     =writable= flag emits writable-param VALUES verbatim and does NOT
     sanitize an embedded buffer object). No required/forbidden key
     change — the slot values are merely guaranteed read-able.
     why_tests_missed: no prior test captured a killed buffer referenced
     by window-preserved-size; serialization-robustness-spec.el scenario
     (a) now does (teeth-confirmed). See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-
     20260613-161149/reconciliations/shape-layout-v2-plist.md.

     cycle-20260524-200631 integrate (2026-05-24): required_keys /
     forbidden_keys exact-match shipped plist; added
     producer_fragmentation_note for the latent :etc-divergence between
     workspace-save (copy-and-mutate) and workspace-save-layout /
     workspace--capture-home-layout (fresh layout-make + upsert). Latent
     today (:etc universally nil per design.md §D7); follow-up task at
     integrate. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260524-200631/reconciliations/shape-layout-v2-plist.md.

     cycle-20260525-082618 integrate (2026-05-25): producer fragmentation
     RESOLVED. Cycle-2 unify-layout-construction-paths (commit 4d1afee →
     merged in df89ce2) funnels workspace-save-layout and workspace--
     capture-home-layout through workspace--autosave-current-layout
     :saved-state. workspace--layout-make is now called only from within
     the canonical helper; the three explicit-save variants share one
     construction path and :etc preservation is uniform. Two follow-up
     items captured in .tasks/: a re-save :working-state regression
     test and a workspace--explicit-save-into-group helper extraction.
     See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-082618/reconciliations/shape-layout-v2-plist.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Two-state layout plist (schema v2)
   purpose: |
     The layout shape stored under each workspace's layout-group. Replaces
     the v1 single-frameset plist (=(:timestamp T :frameset W :git-state G)=)
     with a two-slot shape that separates explicit-save state from
     autosave drift, plus a forward-compat =:etc= slot. Restore precedence
     is encoded in =workspace--layout-effective-state=: =:working-state=
     wins when non-nil, else =:saved-state=. The shape's invariants
     (autosave never writes :saved-state; explicit save clears
     :working-state) live as separate invariant entries.

   required_keys:
     - name: ":timestamp"
       type: time-list
       description: Wall-clock time of the most recent write to this layout.
     - name: ":saved-state"
       type: window-state | nil
       description: |
         The window-state produced by =window-state-get= on the live
         frame at workspace-save time (or by workspace-save-layout,
         workspace-switch-layout, or workspace-new's home stamp). The
         authoritative across-restart state. Each leaf carries a
         workspace-buffer in its parameters slot (see register/shape/
         workspace-buffer-struct).
     - name: ":working-state"
       type: window-state | nil
       description: |
         The window-state produced by an autosave path (tab-switch
         advice, idle timer, kill-emacs-hook). May be nil. When
         non-nil, it is preferred by =workspace--layout-effective-state=
         on restore. Cleared to nil by every explicit =workspace-save=.
     - name: ":etc"
       type: alist | nil
       description: |
         Forward-compat alist; replaces the v1 single-purpose
         =:git-state= placeholder. Consumers must tolerate nil and
         must tolerate unknown keys.

   forbidden_keys:
     - name: ":frameset"
       reason: |
         v1 key. Removed in v2; reader rejects non-v2 files with an
         *Messages* notice. Any (plist-get layout :frameset) read in
         implementation code is a divergence signal.
     - name: ":git-state"
       reason: |
         v1 placeholder. Replaced by :etc. Any read is a divergence
         signal.

   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--layout-make (constructs the plist)
       note: |
         Post-cycle-2 unify-layout-construction-paths: called ONLY from
         workspace--autosave-current-layout's cond `t` arm. Not called
         from any user-facing save command directly.
     - file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
       function: workspace--autosave-current-layout
       note: |
         The canonical construction point. Three call sites flow into
         it as :saved-state producers (workspace-save, workspace-save-
         layout, workspace--capture-home-layout); four as :working-
         state producers (the four stage-1 autosave entry points of
         register/boundary/autosave-guard-pipeline).
   canonical_producer: workspace--autosave-current-layout

   consumers:
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--layout-saved-state (accessor)
       reads_keys: [":saved-state"]
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--layout-working-state (accessor)
       reads_keys: [":working-state"]
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--layout-effective-state (precedence: working over saved)
       reads_keys: [":saved-state", ":working-state"]
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--apply-saved-layout (calls effective-state)
       reads_keys: [":saved-state", ":working-state"]
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--write-state (serializes the full plist)

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-layout-v2-plist (val)
       "Returns nil on success; error symbol otherwise."
       (cond ((not (plistp val)) 'not-a-plist)
             ((plist-member val :frameset) 'forbidden-v1-frameset)
             ((plist-member val :git-state) 'forbidden-v1-git-state)
             ((not (plist-member val :saved-state)) 'missing-saved-state)
             ((not (plist-member val :working-state)) 'missing-working-state)
             (t nil)))

   test_corpus:
     valid:
       - { :timestamp: "...", :saved-state: "<window-state>",
           :working-state: nil, :etc: nil }
       - { :timestamp: "...", :saved-state: "<S>",
           :working-state: "<W>", :etc: nil }
     invalid:
       - { :timestamp: "...", :frameset: "<W>" }   # v1 shape; rejected

   related_entries:
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot
     - register/invariant/restore-precedence-working-over-saved
     - register/invariant/explicit-save-clears-working-state
     - register/invariant/autosave-never-writes-saved-state

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** workspace-plist-v3
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-workspace-plist-v3
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/workspace-plist-v3
   tier: shape
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260613-150904 integrate (2026-06-13, task registry-hydrate-
     only-startup): the =:restore-pending= optional runtime-tag was
     REMOVED from this shape. prior_form: optional_keys = {:broken,
     :restore-pending}; new_form: optional_keys = {:broken} only. The
     lazy-frameset-restoration path that set/read it (startup
     =workspace--restore-tabs= + the =:after= tab-switch advice
     =workspace--persistence-after-tab-switch= → =workspace--activate-
     pending-workspace=) was removed in full (design.md §D1/§D2); startup
     now hydrates the registry without creating tabs. The persisted-slot
     whitelist (:name :home :recent-layout-group :buffer-files
     :layout-groups) and the =:broken= runtime tag are UNCHANGED.
     why_tests_missed: N/A — planned vocabulary contraction, not a test
     gap; the persisted slots were always correct and stay covered, and
     the deserialize/serialize round-trip + no-startup-tabs specs are
     green (config/workspaces, 334/0). See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260613-150904/reconciliations/shape-workspace-plist-v3.md.

     2026-06-06 manual review (reconcile-home-canonical-form-deserialiser-
     promise): resolved the converged advisory finding (on-touch architect
     arch-cycle-20260528-180230-on-touch-canonicalize + reviewer F2) via
     Option A. The :home canonical_form sub-field, the deserialiser
     producer note, and the validator-block comment previously PROMISED
     that a non-canonical persisted :home would "surface in *Messages*",
     but the deserialiser (config/workspaces/persistence.el:94-114) only
     notices missing/non-string and non-absolute :home — a non-canonical-
     but-absolute hand-edited path loads verbatim and silently. Prose now
     describes the actual behaviour: canonical_form is an IN-SESSION
     guarantee (workspace--make / workspace--set-home); the persistence
     loader deliberately preserves :home verbatim and the home-not-
     canonical validator clause is documentation-only, not a load-time
     check. No code change (verbatim-preservation is intentional and
     Option B would collide with broken-tag-runtime-only). The same
     canonical_form-silence shape recurs at spec.md, externalised to
     .tasks/refresh-workspaces-spec-md-v3-schema-and-canonical-form.

     cycle-5 task canonicalize-workspace-home-path-form (cycle-20260528-
     180230): proposes reconciled-recommended. Closes cycle-4 reviewer
     Finding 1 on workspace-new-anchor-existing (representational
     asymmetry on :home between the three known producers) and absorbs
     architect arch-cycle-20260526-191802-02 (multi-slot canonical_form
     extended frame).

     The shape entry is extended with a canonical_form sub-field for
     every string-typed slot (:name, :home, :buffer-files); the
     :home pin is enforced structurally at workspace--make (and at
     workspace--set-home, the post-construction sibling reached by
     workspace-re-anchor). The validator block now asserts the :home
     canonical form. The :name canonical form (exact basename of :home,
     case-sensitive) is the existing de-facto invariant pinned by
     register/invariant/registry-name-equals-basename. The :buffer-files
     element form (each absolute, expand-file-name applied) is pinned
     as documentation of the de-facto behavior; no consumer cross-
     workspace-equality-compares :buffer-files today so this is a
     latent-class pin awaiting either a buffer-membership-equality
     consumer or a constructor-side enforcement pass.

     Integrate-phase architect: please review the new canonical_form
     fields, particularly whether the :buffer-files pin should be
     structurally enforced (e.g. via workspace--add-buffer-file) in a
     follow-up cycle or stay documentation-only.

     Prior status_note (cycle-20260526-171719 integrate, 2026-05-26):
     reconciled. Cycle-3 added the missing setter-helper sibling:
     =workspace--set-name= joins =workspace--set-home= in data-model.el
     (inline-fix c6c1b22 after reviewer F1 on broken-home-tolerance
     flagged the asymmetry as the root cause of the BLOCKING
     registry-name-equals-basename violation). Accessor-pair completeness
     is now structural: every mutable slot has a setter helper. The
     lockstep constraint with register/invariant/registry-name-equals-
     basename is enforced at workspace-re-anchor's rename branch via the
     workspace--clear-broken ∘ workspace--set-name ∘ workspace--set-home
     chain. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260526-171719/reconciliations/shape-workspace-plist-v3.md.

     Prior status_note (cycle-20260525-200459 integrate, 2026-05-25):
     speculation matched implementation. Cycle-1 add-home-slot-to-data-model
     task introduced workspace--make(name home), accessors workspace--home /
     workspace--set-home, and the :broken runtime tag with three helpers
     (workspace--broken-p / workspace--mark-broken / workspace--clear-broken).
     The wire-home-into-callsites mid-cycle follow-up closed the 76-failure
     cascade so the contract is bearable across the full test suite.
     Validator's name-not-basename-of-home check holds at the canonical
     caller (synthesizes name from basename(home)). Cycle-3 sharp-edge
     captured: workspace--set-home alone is insufficient for re-anchor's
     rekey dance; absorbed into cycle-3 broken-home-tolerance refinement
     (and the inline-fix above).
   load_bearing: true
   title: Top-level workspace plist (post-:home extension)
   purpose: |
     The plist held in =workspace--registry= under each workspace's
     registry-name key. v2 (pre-change) carries =:name=,
     =:recent-layout-group=, =:buffer-files=, =:layout-groups=. v3
     adds a required =:home= (absolute filesystem path) and a
     runtime-only =:broken= tag (set by the deserializer when
     =:home= no longer exists on disk; NEVER serialized back to
     disk). The =:home= addition is structural — it carries the new
     filesystem-anchoring identity that every downstream module
     (scaffold, persistence, tabs, gptel-sessions consult, delete/
     purge, broken-state handling) reads. The basename of =:home=
     is the workspace's registry-name; pinning this in the validator
     prevents the registry from drifting out of sync with the
     filesystem anchor.

   required_keys:
     - name: ":name"
       type: string
       description: |
         The workspace's registry-name. MUST equal
         =(file-name-nondirectory (directory-file-name :home))=.
         Used as the =workspace--registry= hash key, the
         =tab-bar-tab-name= for the workspace's tab, and the key in
         the persistence file. Display name (mode-line, prompts) MAY
         differ — see =workspace-home-org-title= override and the
         =home-org-user-authored-after-creation= invariant.
       canonical_form: |
         Exact =basename(:home)= (i.e. =(file-name-nondirectory
         (directory-file-name :home))=); case-sensitive; no
         whitespace canonicalisation. The de-facto contract since
         cycle-1; pinned by =register/invariant/registry-name-
         equals-basename=. Constructor-side enforcement is at the
         canonical call site (synthesises NAME from
         basename(HOME)); the data layer does not re-derive on
         construction.
     - name: ":home"
       type: string (absolute filesystem path)
       description: |
         The workspace's home directory. Absolute path. Required —
         a workspace without =:home= cannot be constructed
         (=workspace--make= signature requires it) and is rejected
         by the deserializer with a =*Messages*= notice. The
         directory's contents are the cross-machine portable
         carrier for the workspace (=home.org=, =sessions/=, =.git=);
         see =register/shape/home-directory-layout= (cycle-2
         candidate). NOT optional: pre-alpha floating-workspace
         support is removed in this change.
       canonical_form: |
         Absolute path; trailing slash present; =expand-file-name=
         applied (tilde and relative prefixes resolved against
         =default-directory=); no double-slashes (=expand-file-name=
         collapses internal =//=); symlinks NOT resolved (preserve
         the user's resolution choice). Pinned at =workspace--make=
         and =workspace--set-home= via
         =(file-name-as-directory (expand-file-name HOME))=
         (cycle-5 task =canonicalize-workspace-home-path-form=).
         All three in-session producers reach :home through one of
         these two attachment points: =workspace--new-default-path=
         and =workspace--new-anchor-existing= via =workspace--make=,
         =workspace-re-anchor= via =workspace--set-home=. This
         canonical_form is therefore guaranteed only for plists
         constructed in-session (or read from a file an in-session
         producer wrote).

         The persistence-loader path is the deliberate exception:
         it bypasses both attachment points and preserves =:home=
         VERBATIM on read. It does NOT re-canonicalise, and it does
         NOT reject or emit a notice for a non-canonical-but-absolute
         persisted path (e.g. a hand-edited =:home= with no trailing
         slash, or an un-expanded =~=). Such a value loads as-is.
         Only =:home= that is missing/non-string or non-absolute is
         rejected with a =*Messages*= notice (see the deserialiser
         producer note below). Verbatim-preservation is intentional:
         silently rewriting a user's persistence file would mask the
         drift rather than surface it, and the value the constructor
         next touches (re-anchor, set-home) re-canonicalises anyway.
         The =home-not-canonical= validator clause below therefore
         describes the IN-SESSION shape contract, not a load-time
         check.
     - name: ":recent-layout-group"
       type: string | nil
       description: |
         Unchanged from v2. The name of the layout-group most
         recently activated for this workspace; nil if none.
       canonical_form: |
         Non-path; no canonicalisation. The value is a layout-group
         name (matched by =equal= against
         =(workspace--group-name g)=); whitespace and case are
         preserved as user-supplied.
     - name: ":buffer-files"
       type: list of strings | nil
       description: |
         Unchanged from v2. The workspace's buffer membership
         (filenames). Reincarnation handled separately per
         =register/shape/workspace-buffer-struct=.
       canonical_form: |
         Each element is an absolute path with =expand-file-name=
         applied; symlinks NOT resolved. De-facto form across all
         existing producers (=workspace--add-buffer-file= forwards
         the caller's path unchanged; callers in
         =buffer-membership.org= compute via =expand-file-name= or
         =buffer-file-name=). Documentation-only pin as of cycle-5;
         no consumer cross-workspace-equality-compares
         =:buffer-files= today, so the form is latent-class. A
         future cross-workspace membership query would surface
         drift here; the architect-recommended structural pin is
         to canonicalise inside =workspace--add-buffer-file= when
         that consumer lands (cycle-5
         architect-extended-frame deferred follow-up).
     - name: ":layout-groups"
       type: alist (name . layout-group) | nil
       description: |
         Unchanged from v2. The workspace's layout-groups, each
         carrying v2 two-state layouts per
         =register/shape/layout-v2-plist=.

   optional_keys:
     - name: ":broken"
       type: t | nil
       description: |
         Runtime-only tag set by =persistence.org=
         (=workspace--persistence-deserialize-workspace=) when the
         workspace's =:home= is not a directory at load time.
         Activation paths (=workspace-switch=, =workspace-restore=)
         consult =workspace--broken-p= and signal =user-error= when
         set. NEVER written to disk — the serializer filters this
         key. Mutations route through =workspace--mark-broken= and
         =workspace--clear-broken= per cycle-1's
         =arch-cycle-20260525-200459-2= finding (no inline
         =plist-put=). The =broken-home-no-auto-recreate= invariant
         pins the "log and tag, never recreate" semantics.

   forbidden_keys: []

   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--make
       note: |
         The canonical constructor. Signature changes from
         =(workspace--make name)= to =(workspace--make name home)=
         in task =add-home-slot-to-data-model=. As of cycle-5 task
         =canonicalize-workspace-home-path-form=, =workspace--make=
         is the canonical :home canonicalisation choke-point:
         =HOME= is wrapped via
         =(file-name-as-directory (expand-file-name HOME))= so
         the registered plist always carries the canonical form
         pinned in the =:home= =canonical_form= sub-field above.
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--set-home
       note: |
         Post-construction :home producer. Reached by
         =workspace-re-anchor= (cycle-3 third call-site enumerated
         by architect =arch-cycle-20260526-191802-02=).
         Canonicalises identically to =workspace--make= so the
         shape invariant holds through re-anchor.
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--persistence-deserialize-workspace
       note: |
         Reconstructs from disk. Rejects (notice + continue) entries
         whose =:home= is missing/non-string or non-absolute. Tags
         entries whose =:home= directory does not exist on disk with
         =:broken t= (also with a notice). Otherwise preserves
         =:home= VERBATIM from the on-disk form — does NOT
         re-canonicalise and does NOT notice/reject a non-canonical-
         but-absolute path (e.g. missing trailing slash, un-expanded
         =~=); such a value is loaded as-is. This is the deliberate
         persistence-boundary exception to the =:home=
         =canonical_form= pin: the loader never rewrites a user's
         file. A file written by the cycle-5 constructor-side
         canonicalisation is already canonical on read, so the
         exception only matters for hand-edited files.
   canonical_producer: workspace--make

   consumers:
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--name (accessor)
       reads_keys: [":name"]
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--set-name (setter — added cycle-3 via inline-fix c6c1b22)
       writes_keys: [":name"]
       note: |
         Sibling to workspace--set-home. Lockstep partner at
         workspace-re-anchor's rename branch per
         register/invariant/registry-name-equals-basename.
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--home (NEW accessor — this change)
       reads_keys: [":home"]
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--broken-p (NEW predicate — this change)
       reads_keys: [":broken"]
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--recent-group (accessor)
       reads_keys: [":recent-layout-group"]
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--buffer-files (accessor)
       reads_keys: [":buffer-files"]
     - file: config/workspaces/data-model.org
       function: workspace--layout-groups (accessor)
       reads_keys: [":layout-groups"]
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--persistence-serialize-workspace
       note: |
         Writes the plist to disk MINUS the =:broken= tag. See the
         =broken-home-no-auto-recreate= invariant for the
         filter-on-write contract.
     - file: config/workspaces/tabs.org
       function: workspace-new (default scaffold branch + anchor branch)
       note: |
         Wraps =workspace--make= with the scaffolded =HOME=. Cycle-2
         tasks =workspace-new-default-path= and
         =workspace-new-anchor-existing=.
     - file: config/workspaces/scaffold.org (NEW module — cycle-2)
       function: workspace-scaffold
       note: |
         Produces the HOME directory; calls =workspace--make= to
         register the resulting workspace. Cycle-2 task
         =scaffold-module=.
     - file: config/workspaces/workspaces.org
       function: workspace-sessions-dir (NEW — cycle-2)
       reads_keys: [":home"]
       note: |
         Reads =:home= to compute =<HOME>/sessions/= for the gptel
         consult. Cycle-2 task =gptel-sessions-workspace-consult=.

   validator: |
     ;; Shape-tier opted out of scaffolding per overlay
     ;; (scaffolding.tiers defaults to [invariant, vocabulary,
     ;; boundary]); the validator lives here as YAML rather than as
     ;; a scaffolded file. Promote to a scaffold if a second
     ;; producer turns up during execute.
     ;;
     ;; The :home canonical-form check (home-not-canonical) is the
     ;; cycle-5 addition closing reviewer Finding 1 on cycle-4's
     ;; workspace-new-anchor-existing. Constructor-side
     ;; canonicalisation in workspace--make / workspace--set-home
     ;; means an in-session workspace that fails this check has
     ;; bypassed both attachment points — a structural-drift signal.
     ;;
     ;; SCOPE: this validator describes the IN-SESSION shape contract.
     ;; It is documentation-only (no production caller) and is NOT run
     ;; by the persistence deserialiser, which deliberately preserves
     ;; :home verbatim (see the :home canonical_form note and the
     ;; deserialiser producer note above). A hand-edited persistence
     ;; file carrying a non-canonical-but-absolute :home loads as-is
     ;; with no notice; home-not-canonical would flag it only if some
     ;; future caller chose to validate a deserialised plist against
     ;; this shape — the deserialiser intentionally does not.
     (defun shape/validate-workspace-plist-v3 (ws)
       "Returns nil on success; error symbol otherwise."
       (cond ((not (plistp ws)) 'not-a-plist)
             ((not (stringp (plist-get ws :name))) 'missing-name)
             ((not (stringp (plist-get ws :home))) 'missing-home)
             ((not (file-name-absolute-p (plist-get ws :home)))
              'home-not-absolute)
             ;; :home canonical-form check (cycle-5
             ;; canonicalize-workspace-home-path-form): the
             ;; constructor pins
             ;; (file-name-as-directory (expand-file-name HOME)).
             ;; A :home that differs from that form has bypassed
             ;; workspace--make and workspace--set-home — surface as
             ;; a distinct error symbol so audits can tell apart
             ;; "missing :home" from "non-canonical :home".
             ((not (equal (plist-get ws :home)
                          (file-name-as-directory
                           (expand-file-name (plist-get ws :home)))))
              'home-not-canonical)
             ((not (equal (plist-get ws :name)
                          (file-name-nondirectory
                           (directory-file-name (plist-get ws :home)))))
              'name-not-basename-of-home)
             (t nil)))

   test_corpus:
     valid:
       - { :name: "myproj", :home: "/Users/jefffarr/emacs-workspaces/myproj/",
           :recent-layout-group: nil, :buffer-files: nil, :layout-groups: nil }
       - { :name: "myproj", :home: "/Users/jefffarr/emacs-workspaces/myproj/",
           :broken: t, :recent-layout-group: nil, :buffer-files: nil,
           :layout-groups: nil }
     invalid:
       - { :name: "myproj" }                        # missing :home (floating)
       - { :name: "myproj", :home: "relative/path" } # :home not absolute
       - { :name: "alpha", :home: "/Users/jefffarr/emacs-workspaces/beta/" }
                                                    # name ≠ basename(:home)
       - { :name: "myproj", :home: "/Users/jefffarr/emacs-workspaces/myproj" }
                                                    # :home not canonical (no trailing slash)
       - { :name: "myproj", :home: "~/emacs-workspaces/myproj/" }
                                                    # :home not canonical (tilde not expanded)

   related_entries:
     - register/invariant/home-required-no-floating-workspaces
     - register/invariant/registry-name-equals-basename
     - register/invariant/broken-home-no-auto-recreate  # cycle-2+ entry
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-broken-disposition
     - register/shape/layout-v2-plist

   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** workspace-integration-anchor-payload
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-workspace-integration-anchor-payload
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/workspace-integration-anchor-payload
   tier: shape
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260612-143558 (workspace-integration-menu) integrate:
     created CONFIRMED from the merged shape in
     =config/workspaces/integrations.el= (=workspace--integration-payload=).
     Realised verbatim by every consumer; clean reviews.

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): RECONCILED (was confirmed) — an EXTRA touched
     at integrate by the user decisions routed here (ask P2 / the
     missing-repo-choice spec-signals: "anchor-payload gains optional
     :source-repo; contexts purge / bookmark-jump"). The four required keys
     and the ONE constructor are unchanged. (1) A second PRODUCER exists:
     portable-bookmarks' =workspace-bookmark--add-worktree-payload=
     (portable-bookmarks.el:806-817) APPENDS an optional =:source-repo= to
     =(workspace--integration-payload NAME HOME 'bookmark-jump)=,
     fboundp-guarded; only when core's constructor is unbound does it spell
     the plist itself (without =:sessions-dir=). Its first cut built the
     plist by hand without =:sessions-dir= (arch-on-touch-1790453714-33,
     fixed in merge 4dec801c; pinned by missing-repo-spec "is core's anchor
     payload for the workspace, with context bookmark-jump, plus
     :source-repo"). (2) =:source-repo= is an OPTIONAL key read by
     git-worktrees' =jf/workspace--add-worktree=. (3) =:context= carries
     six members (=register/vocabulary/workspace-creation-context=), not
     four. (4) consumed_by was missing git-worktrees' two surfaces. Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/shape-workspace-integration-anchor-payload.md

     cycle-1790604160 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 3 against c65efb86; status unchanged. Constructor
     unchanged (integrations.el:53-67); the second producer's fallback is
     still spelled at portable-bookmarks.el:813-817 and still pinned by
     missing-repo-spec.el:789-795. # owner: outcome-action-mapping
     (cycle-1790604160) — eoc-1790453714-04: the fallback list is
     UNREACHABLE (the offer gate at :605-616 requires
     =workspace--integration-menu-command-p=, defined in the same file as
     the constructor); the task calls the constructor unconditionally and
     deletes the fallback and its spec, after which second_producer reads
     "appends :source-repo to the constructor's output; builds nothing of
     its own" with no unbound-constructor clause — re-stated at integrate. #
     owner: doc-sync-cycle2 (cycle-1790604160) — the new
     =specs/workspace-integrations/spec.md= carries the NARROWED payload
     clause (ask-cycle-1790453714-5): workspaces itself adds no domain keys;
     a caller invoking a :menu command directly MAY add keys that command
     documents (=:source-repo=). load_bearing stays false: one constructor,
     one optional key, consumers read only what they need.
   load_bearing: false
   title: integration anchor payload (the plist pushed to every handler)
   purpose: |
     The flat, extensible plist workspaces PUSHES to each integration
     handler (both the creation-dispatch site and the transient's menu
     commands). One private constructor =workspace--integration-payload
     NAME HOME CONTEXT= is the single source of the shape, so push sites
     never diverge. A plist (not a cl-defstruct) keeps it open for the
     deferred β creation-flow keys (e.g. =:integration-choices=) without
     a struct redefinition.
   keys:
     required:
       - key: ":name"
         type: string
         note: the workspace name (registry basename).
       - key: ":home"
         type: string (absolute path)
         note: the workspace home dir; handlers use it as project-root for scoping.
       - key: ":sessions-dir"
         type: string (absolute path)
         note: =(workspace--sessions-dir home)= — derived via the canonical
               helper, never duplicated. Where a session integration files output.
       - key: ":context"
         type: symbol
         note: one of =register/vocabulary/workspace-creation-context= members
               (=fresh= / =anchored-scaffolded= / =anchored-existing= at birth;
               =menu-invoke= from the transient; =purge= at teardown;
               =bookmark-jump= from portable-bookmarks' add-worktree answer).
               Handlers branch on it (gptel skips =anchored-existing=).
     optional:
       - key: ":source-repo"
         type: string (absolute local directory)
         note: |
           (cycle-1790453714) The source repository a caller has ALREADY
           decided. git-worktrees' =:menu= command then makes the worktree
           from it with no source prompt and no candidate discovery, and
           answers =(failed . REASON)= without prompting when it is not an
           existing local directory. Produced only by portable-bookmarks'
           missing-repo chooser (the resolver's admitted canonical
           =:checkout=); absent from every registry-dispatched payload.
     optional_future:
       - key: ":integration-choices"
         note: reserved for the deferred β interactive-creation flow; not built.
   constructed_by:
     file: config/workspaces/integrations.org
     function: workspace--integration-payload   # integrations.el:53
     second_producer: |
       (cycle-1790453714) config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org § Add
       worktree here — =workspace-bookmark--add-worktree-payload= APPENDS
       =:source-repo= to the constructor's output for context
       =bookmark-jump=; it builds nothing of its own while the constructor
       is loaded. The payload is FUNCALLED into =jf/workspace--add-worktree=,
       never dispatched through the registry.
   consumed_by:
     - config/gptel/sessions/workspace-integration.org (jf/gptel--workspace-on-create / -add-session)
