     - config/workspaces/git-worktrees.org (jf/workspace--add-worktree — reads :name, :home, :source-repo; jf/workspace--worktree-on-purge — reads :home)
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/workspace-integration-registry
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-creation-context
     - register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
     - register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer
   cited_by_tasks:
     - integration-registry-core
     - gptel-session-integration
     - missing-repo-choice                           # cycle-1790453714: second producer, :source-repo
     - discovery-memo-dead-helpers-and-picker-scope  # cycle-1790453714: routed through the constructor
     - outcome-action-mapping   # cycle-1790604160: unreachable fallback removed
     - doc-sync-cycle2          # cycle-1790604160: narrowed payload clause in the delta spec
   discovered_from: workspace-integration-menu
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** environment-block
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-environment-block
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/environment-block
   tier: shape
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781857401 integrate (2026-06-19): speculated → CONFIRMED.
     env-block-builder (merge 8f05e41e) implemented
     gptel-chat--build-environment-block rendering the D3 block VERBATIM
     ("# Environment" + framing/live-note + Working directory + verbatim
     Readable/Writable/Denied globs) and the D5 degradation line ("- File
     access: no scope restrictions (this buffer is not a scoped session)").
     pre-send-compose-and-wire (merge 4d8cad0e) confirmed tail placement
     (role + "\n\n" + block, block last) and wholesale rebuild via the
     two-send single-block idempotency spec. Builder + composition specs
     green (148 builder specs; chat 559/0). Author-blind reviews of both
     tasks CLEAN. See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781857401/reconciliations/
     shape-environment-block.md.
     ---
     cycle-1781857401 plan (2026-06-19): forward-mode speculation for
     change gptel-dynamic-environment-preamble. The markdown block appended
     to the TAIL of gptel--system-message on every send from a
     gptel-chat-mode buffer (interactive chat AND persistent agents — both
     are chat-mode). Speculated shape per design D3 (content) + D5
     (degradation). Pressure-tested by env-block-builder (the producer) and
     pre-send-compose-and-wire (the composer that appends it).
   load_bearing: false
   title: |
     The dynamic environment block appended to a chat-mode system message
   purpose: |
     A short, always-current markdown block that tells the model its
     environment — its working directory and file-access scope — so a chat
     session or agent does not spend tool calls discovering its cwd and
     boundary. Composed per-send from intrinsic buffer inputs only (the work
     root = default-directory, established from GPTEL_WORK_ROOT; the
     GPTEL_SCOPE_* drawer keys) and appended after the session's role
     content. It is the structural payload the proposal
     gptel-dynamic-environment-preamble delivers; its shape is what the
     idempotency invariant (register/invariant/composed-system-message-
     write-only) and the input-neutrality boundary
     (register/boundary/environment-block-input-neutrality) constrain.
   document_regions:
     - region: block header + prose framing
       location: |
         First lines of the block, after a "# Environment" heading.
       shape: |
         A "# Environment" markdown heading followed by one short prose
         sentence framing what the information is and stating it is current
         as of this message (the live-note). Speculated text (design D3):
         "You are working in the directory below. The file-access scope
         lists what you may read and write; this information is current as
         of this message."
     - region: working-directory line
       location: |
         First bullet of the block.
       shape: |
         "- Working directory: <default-directory>" — the buffer's
         default-directory verbatim (already a directory path).
     - region: scope lines (scoped session)
       location: |
         Following bullets, present iff the buffer carries GPTEL_SCOPE_*
         keys.
       shape: |
         Three bullets rendering the read / write / deny patterns VERBATIM
         (the exact globs the validator enforces, comma-joined; not
         summarised):
           "- Readable: <GPTEL_SCOPE_READ globs>"
           "- Writable: <GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE globs>"
           "- Denied:   <GPTEL_SCOPE_DENY globs>"
     - region: degradation line (drawerless chat)
       location: |
         Replaces the three scope lines when NO GPTEL_SCOPE_* keys are
         present (design D5).
       shape: |
         A single bullet, speculated text: "- File access: no scope
         restrictions (this buffer is not a scoped session)". Never an
         error, never an empty block.
   structural_invariants:
     - the block is the LAST section of the composed gptel--system-message
       (tail placement, design D4); the role content precedes it, joined by
       a blank line ("\n\n")
     - the block is rebuilt wholesale each send and never read back from the
       prior composed value (the idempotency contract lives in
       register/invariant/composed-system-message-write-only; this shape is
       the thing that would otherwise accumulate)
     - scope patterns are rendered verbatim, not summarised — the model sees
       the real boundary the validator enforces
     - the block references no workspaces symbol — built from drawer +
       default-directory only (boundary/environment-block-input-neutrality)
   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: gptel-chat--build-environment-block
       usage: |
         SPECULATED. No-arg builder operating on the current buffer. Reads
         the scope drawer keys via jf/gptel--scan-session-drawer-keys (the
         same scanner the binder uses; reuse the existing +-collapse
         accessor, do not re-implement it), the work root from
         default-directory, and returns the block string. Degrades per D5
         when no GPTEL_SCOPE_* keys are present. Added by task
         env-block-builder.
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: gptel-chat--refresh-system-prompt-from-file (widened to composer)
       usage: |
         SPECULATED. The existing pre-send :before advice on gptel-request,
         widened to compose gptel--system-message = role + "\n\n" + block
         wholesale, behind the (derived-mode-p 'gptel-chat-mode) guard.
         Appends the block unconditionally (every chat-mode buffer, even
         with no sibling system-prompt file). Added by task
         pre-send-compose-and-wire. Touches register/shape/session-sibling-
         system-prompt-file (that entry describes this consumer as a
         "per-request cache refresh"; widening it to a composer is an
         interface-drift candidate to reconcile at integrate).
   related_entries:
     - register/invariant/composed-system-message-write-only
     - register/boundary/environment-block-input-neutrality
     - register/shape/session-sibling-system-prompt-file
   cited_by_tasks:
     - env-block-builder
     - pre-send-compose-and-wire
   discovered_from: gptel-dynamic-environment-preamble
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** fragment
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-fragment
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/fragment
   tier: shape
   status: reconciled
   status_note: "cycle-1781883616 integrate RECONCILED. prior_shape: representation unpinned (struct|cons|plist open). new_shape: plist (:kind SYMBOL :sections LIST), :kind a symbol in {static,dynamic}, :sections an ordered list of (name . body). why_tests_missed: representation was a free degree of freedom in the speculated entry; only the parse/render specs exercise the value, none asserted its concrete container until the implementation pinned plist."
   load_bearing: false
   title: Parsed fragment value (kind + ordered sections)
   purpose: |
     The in-memory value a fragment source parses into, flowing from the
     parser to both the renderer and the composer. A fragment is one .org
     source file's worth of content: a kind (static | dynamic) plus an
     ordered list of section values (see register/shape/section). Producer
     is jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source (config/gptel/presets/fragments.org);
     consumers are jf/gptel-fragment-render (the renderer) and the composer.
     Section order is significant — it is the order in which the renderer
     emits blocks — so the shape MUST be an ordered list, not a hash/alist
     keyed by name where order is incidental.

   required_keys:
     - name: ":kind"
       type: symbol
       description: |
         One of the closed set register/vocabulary/fragment-kind
         ({static, dynamic}). A source that declares no kind parses to
         static (the default). The composer dispatches on this: static
         fragments consume pre-rendered text; dynamic fragments are
         evaluated at compose time.
     - name: ":sections"
       type: list
       description: |
         An ordered list of section values (register/shape/section), one
         per top-level Org heading in the source, in source order. A valid
         fragment has at least one section.

   optional_keys: []
   # cycle-1781885402 amendment (end-of-cycle Architect, arch-cycle-1781885402-2):
   # the speculative optional :fn key is REMOVED. jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source
   # never emits :fn. The compose-time function lives on the fragment-REFERENCE
   # (register/shape/fragment-reference, :kind dynamic :fn FN), not on the parsed
   # fragment plist. The composer consumes references, not fragment plists.

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: jf/gptel-fragment-render
       reads_keys: [":kind", ":sections"]
     # NOTE: the composer does NOT consume this shape directly — it consumes
     # register/shape/fragment-reference. A dynamic reference's :fn produces text
     # that may itself be (or wrap) a rendered fragment, but the parsed fragment
     # plist has no :fn.

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-fragment (val)
       "Returns nil on success, an error symbol otherwise."
       (cond ((not (plistp val)) 'not-a-plist)
             ((not (memq (plist-get val :kind) '(static dynamic)))
              'bad-kind)
             ((not (listp (plist-get val :sections))) 'sections-not-list)
             ((null (plist-get val :sections)) 'no-sections)
             (t nil)))

   test_corpus:
     valid:
       - { kind: static, sections: [ "(\"Role\" . \"You are X.\")" ] }
     invalid:
       - { kind: static, sections: [] }   # a fragment has >= 1 section

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** section
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-section
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/section
   tier: shape
   status: confirmed
   status_note: "cycle-1781883616 integrate: speculation matched. section is a cons (name . body); name is the verbatim trimmed heading text (string); body is a whitespace-trimmed string."
   load_bearing: false
   title: Section cons (name . body)
   purpose: |
     The atomic unit of a fragment: one top-level Org heading and its body.
     A section is a cons (name . body) where name is the heading text (a
     string, e.g. "Role", "Output Format") and body is the trimmed content
     beneath that heading (a string). Produced by
     jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source as the elements of a fragment's
     :sections list; consumed by jf/gptel-fragment-render, which maps each
     section to a backend-delimited block (name → tag via
     register/vocabulary/section-name-to-tag; body → block content).

   required_keys:
     - name: "car (name)"
       type: string
       description: |
         The top-level Org heading text, verbatim (case preserved). The
         renderer derives the backend tag from it
         (register/vocabulary/section-name-to-tag); the name itself is NOT
         lowercased/snake_cased in the shape — only in the derived tag.
     - name: "cdr (body)"
       type: string
       description: |
         The content beneath the heading, trimmed of leading/trailing
         whitespace. May be multi-line. Emitted verbatim as the block body
         by the renderer (no Markdown re-formatting).

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: jf/gptel-fragment-render
       reads_keys: ["car (name)", "cdr (body)"]

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-section (val)
       (cond ((not (consp val)) 'not-a-cons)
             ((not (stringp (car val))) 'name-not-string)
             ((not (stringp (cdr val))) 'body-not-string)
             (t nil)))

   test_corpus:
     valid:
       - "(\"Role\" . \"You are X.\")"
       - "(\"Output Format\" . \"Reply in prose.\")"
     invalid:
       - "(\"Role\" . nil)"   # body must be a string, even if empty

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** composition
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-composition
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/composition
   tier: shape
   status: reconciled
   load_bearing: false
   title: Composition (ordered fragment-reference list)
   reconciliation:
     prior_shape: |
       Speculated as a plist (:fragments LIST :config PLIST) — the ordered
       fragment list AND the preset config bundled together.
     new_shape: |
       Implemented as a BARE ordered list of fragment references (see
       register/shape/fragment-reference); config is NOT part of a composition.
     why_tests_missed: |
       composer-spec asserts the list shape directly ([prelude, role, env]) and
       never references the speculated plist; specs encode the implementation, not
       the register, so the divergence from the speculated container was invisible.
     rationale: |
       Sound separation of concerns: config travels with the preset registration
       (register/shape/preset-config-plist, owned by registration-rewrite); folding
       it into a composition would couple the composer to registration. A
       composition is purely the ordered reference list the composer renders+joins.
     note_path: .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781885402/reconciliations/shape-composition.md
     discovered_by: architect (end-of-cycle, arch-cycle-1781885402-3)
   purpose: |
     What a preset effectively is at compose time: an ordered list of
     fragments (register/shape/fragment) together with the config a preset
     carries (register/shape/preset-config-plist). The composer consumes the
     ordered fragment list, renders each fragment in order, and joins the
     results into the effective system message. The default fragment list is
     derived from send context (register/invariant/context-default-composition)
     — chat vs agent — and a preset author normally supplies only config plus
     a single role fragment that slots into the role position. The list is
     ordered: order is the send order of blocks, so the static prefix stays
     stable for prompt caching (register/invariant/static-prerender-dynamic-compose).

   required_keys:
     - name: "fragments"
       type: list
       description: |
         An ordered list of fragment values. The default is the
         context-derived list with the preset's role fragment in the role
         position; a composition MAY override (add/remove/reorder) via the
         composer seam, but the common case supplies only a role fragment.
     - name: "config"
       type: plist
       description: |
         The preset config plist (register/shape/preset-config-plist) —
         backend/model/tools/etc. The :backend value selects the renderer
         backend; the rest passes through to gptel-make-preset.

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/registration.org
       function: <preset registration> (registration-rewrite.md task)
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: <context-default composition builder> (composer.md task)
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: <composer> (composer.md task)
       reads_keys: ["fragments", "config"]

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-composition (val)
       (cond ((not (plistp val)) 'not-a-plist)
             ((not (listp (plist-get val :fragments))) 'fragments-not-list)
             ((null (plist-get val :fragments)) 'no-fragments)
             (t nil)))

   test_corpus:
     valid:
       - { fragments: ["emacs-prelude", "role", "environment"], config: "(:backend claude)" }
     invalid:
       - { fragments: [], config: "(:backend claude)" }

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** fragment-reference
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-fragment-reference
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/fragment-reference
   tier: shape
   status: confirmed
   load_bearing: false
   title: Fragment reference (a composition element the composer realizes)
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781885402 integrate: created + confirmed in the same cycle. Surfaced by
     the end-of-cycle Architect audit (arch-cycle-1781885402-2): the composer does
     NOT consume fragment plists — it consumes fragment REFERENCES. A reference is
     the runtime projection of a fragment's nature into a composition slot. Pinned
     up front by the composer Implementor (value-pinning), so zero reconcile cost.
   purpose: |
     The element type of a composition (register/shape/composition). The composer
     (jf/gptel-fragment-compose) realizes each reference in list order. Its :kind
     intentionally mirrors the fragment plist's :kind (register/vocabulary/fragment-kind)
     so the two shapes read symmetrically, but a reference is a DISTINCT shape: it
     carries already-resolved realization data, not parsed sections.
   variants:
     - kind: static
       shape: "(:kind static :text STRING)"
       description: |
         Pre-rendered text consumed verbatim at compose time (no call into
         jf/gptel-fragment-render). Upholds register/invariant/static-prerender-dynamic-compose.
     - kind: dynamic
       shape: "(:kind dynamic :fn FUNCTION)"
       description: |
         :fn is funcalled at compose time to produce the text. NOTE: :fn lives on
         the REFERENCE, not on the fragment plist — register/shape/fragment must
         drop its speculative optional :fn key (the parser never emits :fn).
   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: jf/gptel-fragment--ref-static / --ref-dynamic / --default-composition
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: jf/gptel-fragment--realize-ref (dispatches on reference :kind)
   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** preset-config-plist
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-preset-config-plist
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/preset-config-plist
   tier: shape
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781900938 integrate: confirmed → RECONCILED. The :backend key was
     wrong: cycle-1781885402 confirmed it as a "renderer-backend symbol" (claude),
     but :backend is forwarded UNCHANGED to gptel-make-preset, whose apply path
     (gptel--apply-preset, gptel.el:2207) resolves a backend by cl-etypecase
     (gptel-backend | NAME STRING). The symbol `claude' fails it — a reproducible
     full-suite regression (drawer-fontification spec applies :GPTEL_PRESET:
     system-explorer via gptel-chat-mode). Reconciled: :backend is the gptel
     backend NAME STRING ("Claude", as registered by gptel-make-anthropic). The
     RENDERER backend symbol (claude) is a SEPARATE concern, supplied directly at
     the jf/gptel-fragment-render call inside preset.el — NOT carried in this
     config key. Resolved in code by orchestrator inline fix ec6b951a (user-chosen
     Option B: presets declare :backend "Claude"). See reconciliation note +
     finding arch-cycle-1781900938-2.
     prior cycle-1781885402 integrate: speculated → confirmed (the :backend type
     was mis-confirmed as symbol; only manifested when a preset was APPLIED, which
     no preset spec exercised against the real gptel apply path).
   load_bearing: false
   title: Native-Elisp preset config block (replaces YAML frontmatter)
   purpose: |
     The Elisp config block authored in a preset .org source, which tangles
     into a gptel-make-preset registration call. This REPLACES the old
     YAML-frontmatter shape: because the block is native Elisp, there is no
     parse / snake_case→kebab-case normalize / YAML-type coercion step
     (that pipeline is deleted — design.md Decision 6). Registration
     (config/gptel/presets/registration.org) reads native keys directly,
     extracts the scope/mode keys into jf/gptel-preset--scope-defaults (an
     alist of preset-name symbol → scope plist), and passes the remaining
     keys to gptel-make-preset with :system set to the rendered role text.

   required_keys:
     - name: ":description"
       type: string
       description: |
         Preset annotation shown in the transient menu and inline mentions.
     - name: ":backend"
       type: string
       description: |
         The gptel backend NAME STRING ("Claude" — the name gptel-make-anthropic
         registered). Forwarded UNCHANGED to gptel-make-preset; gptel's apply
         path (gptel--apply-preset) resolves the backend by name via
         cl-etypecase (gptel-backend | string). A SYMBOL here breaks that
         etypecase — do not author :backend 'claude. NOTE: the fragment RENDERER
         backend (the symbol `claude', register/vocabulary/render-backend) is a
         DISTINCT concept, passed directly to jf/gptel-fragment-render inside the
         preset .org at render time; it is NOT this config key. Reconciled
         cycle-1781900938 (was mis-typed as a renderer symbol).
     - name: ":model"
       type: symbol
       description: |
         A model symbol (NOT a YAML string needing interning — authored as a
         symbol directly).

   optional_keys:
     - name: ":tools"
       type: list of strings | null
       description: Tool names enabled for the preset.
     - name: ":temperature"
       type: number | null
       description: Passed through to gptel-make-preset.
     - name: ":paths"
       type: plist | null
       description: |
         Scope key — extracted into jf/gptel-preset--scope-defaults and
         REMOVED from the plist passed to gptel-make-preset.
     - name: ":shell-commands"
       type: list | null
       description: Scope key — extracted (not passed to gptel-make-preset).
     - name: ":bash-tools"
       type: list | null
       description: Scope key — extracted (not passed to gptel-make-preset).
     - name: ":scope-profile"
       type: string | null
       description: |
         Scope key — extracted into the scope defaults entry; e.g.
         "coding". The legacy :org-roam-patterns key is NOT a scope key and
         is not extracted.

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/workspace-assistant/  (preset .org)
       function: <config block>
     - file: config/gptel/presets/system-explorer/  (preset .org)
       function: <config block>
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/registration.org
       function: jf/gptel-preset--scope-defaults (scope-key extraction)
       reads_keys: [":paths", ":shell-commands", ":bash-tools", ":scope-profile"]
     - file: config/gptel/presets/registration.org
       function: gptel-make-preset (post-extraction, with :system = rendered role)
       reads_keys: [":description", ":backend", ":model", ":tools", ":temperature"]

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-preset-config-plist (val)
       (cond ((not (plistp val)) 'not-a-plist)
             ((not (stringp (plist-get val :description))) 'missing-description)
             ((not (symbolp (plist-get val :backend))) 'backend-not-symbol)
             ((and (plist-member val :model)
                   (not (symbolp (plist-get val :model)))) 'model-not-symbol)
             (t nil)))

   test_corpus:
     valid:
       - "(:description \"d\" :backend claude :model claude-x :tools (\"read_file\"))"
     invalid:
       - "(:description \"d\" :backend claude :model \"claude-x\")"   # :model must be a symbol, not a YAML-style string

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** portable-bookmark-coordinates
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-portable-bookmark-coordinates
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/portable-bookmark-coordinates
   tier: shape
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): RECONCILED.
     Both speculated axes were settled by =stamp-seam= — THREE flat
     properties, literal symbols =workspace-bookmark-repo= /
     =workspace-bookmark-relpath= / =workspace-bookmark-format=, no nesting
     (portable-bookmarks.el:13-45). Reconciled with DD-7 (identity memo),
     DD-8 (relpath from git, not string arithmetic), DD-9 (defconst names,
     format version 1, all three or none, untracked left unstamped). Read
     side confirmed by =resolver-outcomes= (ac50b041): confinement is
     LEXICAL (DD-16). The stamp-seam on-touch's proposed pin 5 (staleness
     check against filename) was WITHDRAWN by the user (ask 5): stamps are
     trusted; see known_limitations. Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1789935943/reconciliations/shape-portable-bookmark-coordinates.md

     cycle-1789935943 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     populated from proposal "Stamp portable coordinates at bookmark
     creation" + design D3/D4 + tasks =module-scaffold-and-fixtures= (step 3,
     property-name defvars), =repo-identity=, =stamp-seam= (steps 1-2).
     SPECULATED on two axes the implementation must settle: (a) the literal
     property SYMBOLS written into the bookmark record, and (b) whether the
     pair lives as two flat properties or one nested plist.

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): STAYS RECONCILED. The on-disk shape did not
     change (three flat properties, defconst names, format 1, all three or
     none, lexical read-side confinement — all re-verified; no stamp-side
     code changed this cycle beyond the identity predicate's home and the
     memo stamp). Three statements had become FALSE against the code and are
     corrected: (1) validator 2 and the canonical_constructor note said the
     module NEVER calls =bookmark-prop-set=; since jump-seam it calls it
     EXACTLY ONCE — on ='filename= of the fresh =copy-tree= in
     =workspace-bookmark--substituted-record= (portable-bookmarks.el:416), a
     jump-time copy, never a stored record and never a stamped property;
     the stamp path still builds records without it; (2) the consumer "the
     bookmark-handle-bookmark advice" is no longer pass-through: the seam
     reads the three properties only through the canonical reader
     (=--stamped-coordinates=, for the off-workspace hand-off) and the
     resolver; (3) the relpath re-anchor check now uses CORE
     =workspace--same-file-p= (the module copy was deleted), and the
     identity memo's stamp is the PAIR (shared config, config.worktree).
     Anchors re-based. Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/shape-portable-bookmark-coordinates.md
   load_bearing: true
   title: Portable bookmark coordinates — the three properties stamped onto a bookmark record
   purpose: |
     The on-disk contract of this whole capability. A bookmark record is an
     open bag of properties =(NAME . ALIST)=; this shape pins the three the
     capability ADDS, and pins what it must NOT touch.

     This is a PERSISTED shape, not an in-memory one, and it is persisted in
     TWO PLACES: =bookmark-default-file= (written by =bookmark-write-file=,
     read back by =bookmark-load=, potentially by a stock Emacs that has
     never loaded this module) AND the workspaces state file, via the layout
     records =layouts.el= captures through =bookmark-make-record= (ask 4).
     Renaming any property after records exist in the wild silently
     downgrades every stamped bookmark to =non-portable= with no error and
     no warning — the shape's field names are therefore as load-bearing as
     its field meanings.

     Five pins:

     1. =ADDITIVE ONLY=: =filename= and every existing property are kept, in
        order, with the same values; the stamped record is a NEW record
        (=workspace-bookmark--with-coordinates=, portable-bookmarks.el:366)
        and the input record is never mutated. Unstampable records come back
        =eq=. Verified against a stock-Emacs round trip.
     2. =NAMED IN ONE PLACE, AS CONSTANTS=:
        =workspace-bookmark-repo-property=, =-relpath-property=,
        =-format-property= and =workspace-bookmark-format-version= are
        =defconst=s (portable-bookmarks.el:14-46); never a =defcustom= (DD-9:
        the names are an on-disk format, not a preference). Every read and
        write goes through them; portable-bookmarks-spec.el pins the literal
        values and =(custom-variable-p VAR)= = nil for all four.
     3. =ALL THREE OR NONE=: by construction on the write side
        (portable-bookmarks.el:395-402 — a fresh record is built only after
        every fallible step has succeeded) and by rule on the read side:
        =workspace-bookmark--stamped-coordinates= (portable-bookmarks.el:1642)
        accepts only a non-empty string identity, a version in
        =workspace-bookmark--known-format-versions=, and a relpath passing
        =workspace-bookmark--confined-relpath-p= (:1627); anything else is
        =non-portable= before any git runs.
     4. =VERSIONED MEANING=: =workspace-bookmark-format= = 1. Bump it when
        the MEANING of identity or relpath changes (a normalizer change that
        could merge two previously distinct identities; a change to how the
        relpath is derived). The resolver treats any other version as
        =non-portable=.
     5. =READ-SIDE CONFINEMENT IS LEXICAL= (DD-16): a relpath that is
        absolute (=file-name-absolute-p=), has any component exactly =..=,
        or holds NUL is refused (=non-portable=). Nothing else is refused: a
        relpath that leaves the checkout only through a symbolic link
        resolves by its spelling.

   fields:
     - name: workspace-bookmark-repo   # literal symbol; held in workspace-bookmark-repo-property
       type: string
       required: true
       description: |
         The repository's normalized identity, e.g. =github.com/moogah/emacs=:
         =workspace-bookmark--normalize-identity= of =git remote get-url
         origin= (else the sole remote). Never empty, and NEVER carries
         userinfo — the value is persisted, so a credential-bearing remote
         (=https://TOKEN@host/...=) is stripped before it can reach disk
         (disc-repo-identity-4). No usable remote => the record is left
         entirely unstamped. The normalization contract lives in
         =register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery=.
     - name: workspace-bookmark-relpath   # literal symbol; held in workspace-bookmark-relpath-property
       type: string
       required: true
       description: |
         VERBATIM git spelling, decoded as UTF-8: for a file, the single path
         =git --literal-pathspecs ls-files -z --full-name -- BASENAME=
         reports (run in the file's directory; conflict stages collapsed);
         for a directory, =rev-parse --show-prefix= ("sub/"), and only when
         the directory is in HEAD (=rev-parse HEAD:./=,
         portable-bookmarks.el:295-307); for the work-tree root, "./".
         Directory relpaths end in "/", files never do — the slash is the
         record's KIND. Leading/trailing whitespace is part of the value and
         is persisted verbatim. Accepted at stamp time only if it re-anchors
         to the location by file identity (core =workspace--same-file-p=,
         portable-bookmarks.el:344). Re-anchor in
         any checkout with =(expand-file-name RELPATH CHECKOUT)=. No =..=
         guard exists on the write side or is needed: git reports nothing
         outside the work tree (DD-8); the read-side guard is pin 5.
     - name: workspace-bookmark-format   # literal symbol; held in workspace-bookmark-format-property
       type: integer
       required: true
       description: |
         Stamp-format version at stamp time:
         =workspace-bookmark-format-version= (1). See pin 4.
     - name: filename
       type: string
       required: true
       description: |
         PRE-EXISTING. Emacs's absolute path. NOT written by this capability.
         Listed here because "left unchanged" is part of the shape's contract:
         the =non-portable= outcome and stock-Emacs readability both depend
         on it surviving untouched through stamp AND through jump.

   canonical_constructor:
     file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
     function: workspace-bookmark--coordinates   # portable-bookmarks.el:347
     note: |
       =(location) -> (identity . relpath) | nil=. The SOLE producer of the
       pair. =workspace-bookmark--with-coordinates=
       (portable-bookmarks.el:366) builds the fresh stamped record from it;
       the stamp path never calls =bookmark-prop-set= (reconciled: the plan
       text said the advice "writes it with bookmark-prop-set" — it does
       not; disc-stamp-seam-4). The module's ONE =bookmark-prop-set= (:416)
       is the jump seam's, on ='filename= of a fresh copy
       (=register/invariant/stored-record-never-mutated-by-jump=); it never
       writes a stamped property.

   canonical_reader:
     file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
     function: workspace-bookmark--stamped-coordinates   # portable-bookmarks.el:1642
     note: |
       =(record) -> (identity . relpath) | nil=. The ONLY reader of the
       three properties; the resolver never touches them directly.

   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
       function: workspace-bookmark--stamp-record   # portable-bookmarks.el:382
       via: ":filter-return advice on bookmark-make-record (see register/boundary/bookmark-advice-seams)"

   consumers:
     - file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
       function: workspace-bookmark--resolve   # portable-bookmarks.el:1763, via the canonical reader
       note: any subset of the three, an unknown version or a lexical escape -> non-portable
     - file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
       function: workspace-bookmark--jump-plan   # portable-bookmarks.el:499, the seam's decision
       note: |
         (cycle-1790453714) Live. Reads the triple ONLY through the
         canonical reader, to decide the off-workspace hand-off (a STAMPED
         record with no current workspace, :524-526); everything else goes
         through the resolver. =--off-workspace-plan= (:1044) takes the
         identity from the same reader to order the workspace candidates.
     - external: stock Emacs bookmark.el
       note: |
         Reads the record and IGNORES all three properties. The round-trip
         (=bookmark-write-file= then =bookmark-load=) preserving them
         unmodified is the property that makes this design additive; it is
         asserted behaviorally in portable-bookmarks-stamp-spec.el:210.

   tier_1_boundary: |
     Gate =workspace-bookmark--record-location= (portable-bookmarks.el:263,
     no subprocess): =filename= is a string, absolute (=~= counts), local,
     exists, and the record has no non-string buffer/window property.
     Concrete negative corpus on this machine: four =tab-bookmark= records
     whose =filename= is the LABEL ="3 buffers"=, carrying nested bookmark
     records, window trees, and =#N=/#N#= shared structure — those come back
     nil from the gate. Past the gate only git-TRACKED locations are stamped
     (DD-9): untracked, ignored, =.git/= and paths in no work tree come back
     =eq=. Nested/window records are Tier 2/3 and explicitly out of scope
     for this slice.

   known_limitations: |
     STALE STAMPS (user decision, ask 5): =bookmark-relocate= /
     =bookmark-set-filename= / a hand edit rewrite =filename= and leave the
     triple; the resolver TRUSTS the triple. Detection on read is
     .tasks/portable-bookmarks-stale-stamp-detection.md. A directory
     bookmarked through a tracked directory LINK is stamped with the
     target's relpath (git runs in the physical directory); harmless.
     Identity memo: a nil config stamp is never a memo hit
     (disc-wave3-review-fixes-3).

   validator: |
     Structural (no scaffolding in this project; per .claude/orchestrator/config.yaml):
     1. grep -n 'workspace-bookmark-\(repo\|relpath\|format\)' \
          config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el
        -> defconsts, docstrings and constant references only; ZERO
        quoted-literal property names at call sites.
     2. The STAMP path never calls bookmark-prop-set or bookmark-set-filename;
        the module's only =bookmark-prop-set= is the jump seam's, on
        ='filename= of a fresh copy (grep at f860103c: one hit, :416; zero
        =bookmark-set-filename=).
     3. Round-trip spec: bookmark-write-file + bookmark-load; the record is
        `equal` (portable-bookmarks-stamp-spec.el:210, "survives
        bookmark-write-file and bookmark-load unchanged").
     4. Resolver spec: any strict subset of the three, an unknown version, a
        non-string, or a "..", absolute or NUL relpath resolves non-portable
        (portable-bookmarks-resolver-spec.el § "confinement of a relpath to
        its checkout" :374-405, § "non-portable" :617-685).

   test_corpus:
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-stamp-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-resolver-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/bookmark-advice-seams
     - register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery
     - register/vocabulary/bookmark-resolution-outcome
     - register/invariant/stored-record-never-mutated-by-jump

   cited_by_tasks:
     - module-scaffold-and-fixtures
     - repo-identity
     - stamp-seam
     - wave3-review-fixes
     - resolver-outcomes
     - disposition-spec-design-amendments   # cycle-1790453714: spec/design text to the three-property stamp
   discovered_from: workspace-portable-bookmarks
   discovered_by: architect
   reconciled_in: cycle-1789935943
   #+end_src

* Vocabulary

  Entries describe enumerations / symbol sets / value sets that span a
  boundary, plus the canonical mapping (=pcase= / dispatch table) between
  layers. The motivating failure is bash-parser semantic-op set ↔
  scope section keys: a =pcase= inlined at multiple sites, missing values
  at one of them, silently mis-routing operations.

** identity-drawer-keys
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-identity-drawer-keys
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/identity-drawer-keys
   tier: vocabulary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781448273 integrate (2026-06-14): speculated → confirmed.
     Writer side (emit-identity-keys-in-writers, merge b286997): all three
     keys emitted — create-session-core writes GPTEL_SESSION_ID +
     GPTEL_BRANCH:main; persistent-agent--task writes the agent's OWN id +
     GPTEL_BRANCH:main + GPTEL_PARENT_SESSION_ID; branch creation shares the
     parent id and sets its own GPTEL_BRANCH. Reader side
     (drawer-signature-and-head-read, merge 15f76fb): head-read returns the
     full OPEN set keyed by bare key string. Both implementors confirmed the
     vocabulary; reviewers confirmed values match the drawer-first resolver's
     expectations. The type-inference function jf/gptel--session-type
     (parent-id present ⇒ agent) is consumed by the not-yet-built resolver
     task (drawer-identity-resolver).
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781448273/reconciliations/vocabulary-identity-drawer-keys.md.
   load_bearing: true
   enforcement_loci: [reader, writer]
   unmapped_policy: ignore
   unmapped_policy_rationale: |
     Unlike the scope-key set (closed, error-on-unknown), the identity
     key set is OPEN: a session.org drawer may legitimately carry other
     :GPTEL_* keys (preset, model snapshot, scope keys). The signature
     predicate (register/boundary/session-content-signature) keys on the
     PRESENCE of any :GPTEL_ key, not on a closed identity set; the
     resolvers read only the three identity keys and ignore the rest.
   title: The session-identity drawer keys and the type-inference mapping
   purpose: |
     The set of :PROPERTIES:-drawer keys that carry a session's IDENTITY
     (as distinct from its scope configuration, register/vocabulary/
     drawer-key-set). This change makes these authoritative: writers emit
     them (emit-identity-keys-in-writers), the resolvers read them
     (register/boundary/drawer-first-identity-resolution), and discovery
     keys the registry on them (discovery-reads-drawers). The canonical
     mapping is drawer-key -> identity-field plus the type inference
     (parent-id present => agent; absent => branch). Mis-reading branch
     identity out of the directory path instead of this key set is the
     path-archaeology coupling this change removes.
   members:
     - value: ":GPTEL_SESSION_ID:"
       cardinality: scalar
       description: |
         The session's authoritative id. Drawer value wins; absent =>
         fall back to jf/gptel--session-id-from-directory (basename).
         Agents carry their OWN id (register/invariant/
         branch-drawer-shares-id-not-branch governs branches).
       identity_field: session-id
       speculated_since: cycle-1 plan
     - value: ":GPTEL_BRANCH:"
       cardinality: scalar
       description: |
         The branch name. Drawer value wins; absent => fall back to the
         enclosing branches/<branch>/ path segment. New (non-agent)
         sessions write ":GPTEL_BRANCH: main".
       identity_field: branch-name
       speculated_since: cycle-1 plan
     - value: ":GPTEL_PARENT_SESSION_ID:"
       cardinality: scalar
       description: |
         Present on agent sessions only; links the agent to its parent
         session. Already written today by persistent-agent--task. Its
         PRESENCE is the sole discriminator of session type — no path
         layout (branch / nested-agent / flat-agent) is consulted.
       identity_field: parent-session-id
       type_discriminator: true
     - value: ":GPTEL_PRESET:"
       cardinality: scalar
       description: |
         Pre-existing key (NOT new in this change). Listed here because
         it is the :GPTEL_ key that legacy sessions already carry, so the
         signature predicate recognizes pre-change sessions and the
         basename-fallback grace path applies to them.
       identity_field: none (legacy signature-bearer)
   canonical_mapping_function: |
     ;; Type inference — the only allowed discriminator of session type.
     ;; If a branch/nested-agent/flat-agent path-regex cond reappears
     ;; anywhere in activation/identity/discovery code, that is the
     ;; register/invariant/activation-and-identity-are-content-not-path
     ;; violation, not a second mapping.
     (defun jf/gptel--session-type (drawer-alist)
       "Return `agent' when the drawer carries GPTEL_PARENT_SESSION_ID,
     else `branch'."
       (if (alist-get "GPTEL_PARENT_SESSION_ID" drawer-alist nil nil #'equal)
           'agent
         'branch))
   closed_set: false
   related_entries:
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
     - register/boundary/drawer-first-identity-resolution
     - register/boundary/session-content-signature
     - register/invariant/branch-drawer-shares-id-not-branch
   discovered_from: gptel-content-addressed-session-activation
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** drawer-key-set
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-drawer-key-set
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/drawer-key-set
   tier: vocabulary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): writer-locus (rewire-expansion-writer
     mapping function emits READ_METADATA) and reader-locus (rewire-
     validator-config-load loader accepts the eight-key set) both honor
     the entry. Fixture-locus pending in cycle-3 migrate-* tasks. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1777470320/reconciliations/
     vocabulary-drawer-key-set.md.
   load_bearing: true
   enforcement_loci: [reader, writer, fixture]
   unmapped_policy: error
   unmapped_policy_rationale: |
     Loader is strict on unknown drawer keys: a typo like
     :GPTEL_SCOPE_READ_META: silently widens nothing today, but as
     cycle-1 reconciliation noted, an "unknown key" path is exactly
     where the legacy YAML loader's silent-widening incidents lived.
     The buffer-first config loader emits a warning on unknown
     :GPTEL_SCOPE_* keys (or errors, behind a defcustom — see
     register/boundary/scope-config-loader stage 2 reconciliation).
   title: The eight :GPTEL_SCOPE_* drawer keys and their plist field mapping
   purpose: |
     The closed set of property keys this change introduces in the file-
     level :PROPERTIES: drawer of session.org, plus the canonical mapping
     between each drawer key (and its + suffix variant for list values)
     and the corresponding plist path under (:paths ...) or (:cloud ...).
     Mis-routing a drawer key into the wrong plist field is the same class
     of bug as bash-parser's :read-metadata-into-:paths.write incident:
     it fails silent because validation still runs, just under the wrong
     section.

   members:
     - value: ":GPTEL_SCOPE_READ:" (with "+:" suffix variant)
       cardinality: list
       description: read-allowed glob patterns (content reads)
       plist_path: (:paths :read)
     - value: ":GPTEL_SCOPE_READ_METADATA:" (with "+:" suffix variant)
       cardinality: list
       description: |
         metadata-only read patterns. Permits stat-style operations
         (test -f, which, dirname, ls -l) but not content reads.
         Introduced cycle-2 (ask 10A) to keep `[ -f /etc/passwd ]`
         from grant-by-side-effect to `cat /etc/passwd`. The validator
         accepts a content-read against a path *iff* the path matches
         GPTEL_SCOPE_READ; metadata-only paths do not satisfy the
         content-read predicate.
       plist_path: (:paths :read-metadata)
       speculated_since: cycle-2 plan
     - value: ":GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE:" (with "+:" suffix variant)
       cardinality: list
       description: write-allowed glob patterns (covers :create, :create-or-modify, :append, :delete)
       plist_path: (:paths :write)
     - value: ":GPTEL_SCOPE_MODIFY:" (with "+:" suffix variant)
       cardinality: list
       description: modify-allowed glob patterns
       plist_path: (:paths :modify)
     - value: ":GPTEL_SCOPE_EXECUTE:" (with "+:" suffix variant)
       cardinality: list
       description: execute-allowed glob patterns
       plist_path: (:paths :execute)
     - value: ":GPTEL_SCOPE_DENY:" (with "+:" suffix variant)
       cardinality: list
       description: deny-list glob patterns; deny wins over allow
       plist_path: (:paths :deny)
     - value: ":GPTEL_SCOPE_CLOUD_AUTH:"
       cardinality: scalar
       description: cloud authentication detection mode
       plist_path: (:cloud :auth-detection)
       allowed_scalar_values: ["allow", "warn", "deny"]
     - value: ":GPTEL_SCOPE_CLOUD_PROVIDERS:" (with "+:" suffix variant)
       cardinality: list
       description: explicitly-allowed cloud-auth provider names
       plist_path: (:cloud :allowed-providers)

   canonical_mapping_function: |
     ;; Lives in scope-validation.org as the only translation between
     ;; drawer keys and plist paths. If you find a similar pcase / cond
     ;; inlined elsewhere translating drawer keys, that is a duplication
     ;; bug.
     (defun jf/gptel-scope--drawer-key-to-plist-path (key)
       "Map a drawer KEY string (without the trailing colon or +) to a
     two-element plist-path list."
       (pcase key
         ("GPTEL_SCOPE_READ"            '(:paths :read))
         ("GPTEL_SCOPE_READ_METADATA"   '(:paths :read-metadata))
         ("GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE"           '(:paths :write))
         ("GPTEL_SCOPE_MODIFY"          '(:paths :modify))
         ("GPTEL_SCOPE_EXECUTE"         '(:paths :execute))
         ("GPTEL_SCOPE_DENY"            '(:paths :deny))
         ("GPTEL_SCOPE_CLOUD_AUTH"      '(:cloud :auth-detection))
         ("GPTEL_SCOPE_CLOUD_PROVIDERS" '(:cloud :allowed-providers))
         (_ (error "Unknown scope drawer key: %s" key))))

   validator: |
     (defun vocabulary/validate-drawer-key (key)
       (memq key '("GPTEL_SCOPE_READ"
                   "GPTEL_SCOPE_READ_METADATA"
                   "GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE"
                   "GPTEL_SCOPE_MODIFY"
                   "GPTEL_SCOPE_EXECUTE"
                   "GPTEL_SCOPE_DENY"
                   "GPTEL_SCOPE_CLOUD_AUTH"
                   "GPTEL_SCOPE_CLOUD_PROVIDERS")))

   closed_set: true

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** operation-to-drawer-key
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-operation-to-drawer-key
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/operation-to-drawer-key
   tier: vocabulary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1 reconciled the closed-set discipline, the strict-error
     unmapped_policy, and the absence of :deny / nil-operation arms.
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): asks 10A/B/C all encoded with
     strict-error fallbacks; the new buttercup spec at
     config/gptel/scope/test/expansion/operation-to-drawer-key-spec.el
     pins all three dispositions plus the cycle-1 strict-error contract.
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1777470320/reconciliations/
     vocabulary-operation-to-drawer-key.md.
     cycle-4 (2026-04-29) Ask 1 disposition: the :match-pattern member's
     redirect_target is dropped; the action handler refuses with
     directing user-error rather than redirecting. The reify-the-cluster
     route is externalized to
     .tasks/fix-match-pattern-parser-validator-boundary.md (the
     `--validate-file-operations` first-denial-wins constraint at
     scope-validation.el:412 means the action handler never sees a
     full violation cluster, so the cycle-2 ask-10B redirect is not
     implementable without parser/validator work).
   load_bearing: true
   enforcement_loci: [writer]
   title: Granular operation → drawer-key collapse
   purpose: |
     Maps the granular :operation values produced by the validator pipeline
     and the bash-parser semantic plugins onto the five list-shaped drawer
     keys (READ / READ_METADATA / WRITE / MODIFY / EXECUTE). This is the
     structural analogue of bash-parser's 11-op-to-3-section mapping that
     drove the prior incident. The mapping lives in exactly one place —
     jf/gptel-scope--map-operation-to-drawer-key — and any pcase / cond
     performing the same translation at another site is a duplication bug.

     GPTEL_SCOPE_DENY is *not* a collapse target: deny-listing is
     expressed at the action layer (an explicit "add to deny scope"
     handler), not as an :operation input. A :deny arm in this mapping
     would be dead today (no producer emits :operation :deny) and would
     conflate violation-outcome (denied) with operation-kind (read/write/
     etc) which are different axes.

     :match-pattern is *also* not in the writer's collapse domain. The
     bash-parser emits :match-pattern with the literal pattern as
     :resource (e.g. "*.txt" from `find -name`); routing the pattern
     into a path-allowlist drawer key would grant reads on every
     filesystem match. Cycle-4 (Ask 1) resolved this as Option B:
     the action handler refuses with a directing user-error
     ("use c for custom pattern, e for manual edit") rather than
     redirecting. The cycle-2 ask-10B "smart redirect" is not
     implementable in cycle-3-as-shipped because
     `--validate-file-operations` (scope-validation.el:412) throws on
     the first denial via `(catch 'error-found ...)`, so the action
     handler never receives a full violation cluster from which to
     pick a sibling :read-directory resource. The writer's strict-error
     fallback therefore signals "match-pattern reached the writer" as
     a defect, not a vocabulary gap.

   members:
     - value: :read
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_READ
       producer: validator pipeline (path), bash-parser read-ops plugin
     - value: :read-directory
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_READ
       producer: filesystem-tools read-directory dispatch
     - value: :read-metadata
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_READ_METADATA
       producer: bash-parser stat / metadata plugin
       decision_note: |
         ask 10A resolved (cycle-2, 2026-04-29) to option (1): introduce
         GPTEL_SCOPE_READ_METADATA as a 6th drawer key. Metadata stats
         (e.g. `[ -f file ]`, `which python`, `dirname /path`) grant only
         metadata access; they do not grant content-read on the file.
         Implementation: rewire-expansion-writer task in cycle-2 batch.
     - value: :match-pattern
       collapses_to: NONE  # action handler refuses with directing user-error
       producer: bash-parser globbing plugin (find/ls)
       decision_note: |
         Cycle-2 ask-10B resolved as "smart redirect at the action
         handler"; cycle-3 implementation discovered that
         `--validate-file-operations` (scope-validation.el:412) throws
         on first denial via `(catch 'error-found ...)`, so the action
         handler never receives a violation cluster. Cycle-4 plan
         resolved Ask 1 as Option B: the action handler refuses with
         a directing user-error ("use c for custom pattern, e for
         manual edit") rather than redirecting. The reify-the-cluster
         route is externalized to
         .tasks/fix-match-pattern-parser-validator-boundary.md.
         The writer never sees :match-pattern; if it does, that is a
         defect (the action handler did not refuse). Implementation:
         harden-add-to-scope-action-handler task in cycle-3 batch
         (former: refuse-add-to-scope-on-nil-operation, broadened);
         refusal-with-guidance contract finalised in cycle-4.
     - value: :write
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE
       producer: validator pipeline (path), bash-parser write-ops plugin
     - value: :create
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE
       producer: bash-parser create-ops plugin (touch, mkdir)
     - value: :create-or-modify
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE
       producer: bash-parser redirect plugin
     - value: :append
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE
       producer: bash-parser redirect plugin (>> form)
     - value: :delete
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE
       producer: bash-parser delete-ops plugin
       decision_note: |
         ask 10C resolved (cycle-2, 2026-04-29) to option (2): keep
         current WRITE collapse. Granting WRITE on a directory implies
         delete-on-the-directory's-files. Tradeoff documented; not
         introducing a separate GPTEL_SCOPE_DELETE bucket. The
         recovery-asymmetry between overwrite (VCS-recoverable) and
         delete (often not) is accepted as a known limitation; users
         who want deletion-locked-out can add the file pattern to
         GPTEL_SCOPE_DENY explicitly.
     - value: :modify
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_MODIFY
       producer: filesystem-tools edit_file_in_scope dispatch
     - value: :execute
       collapses_to: GPTEL_SCOPE_EXECUTE
       producer: validator pipeline (bash command-execution check)

   unmapped_policy: error
   unmapped_policy_rationale: |
     The strict-error fallback forces the typo / new-producer-value /
     nil-from-cloud-auth case to surface at the user-visible expansion UI
     the moment it happens, rather than silently bloating GPTEL_SCOPE_READ
     (an allowlist — adding to it grants read access). This matches the
     "closed-set vocabulary fails loudly" discipline that the rest of the
     scope subsystem honours (cloud-auth scalar set; the seven drawer-key
     set's loader-side enforcement). For violations that legitimately
     arrive with :operation nil (cloud-auth, parse-incomplete), the
     correct fix is upstream at the --add-to-scope action handler, not at
     the writer. The action handler should refuse to run when the
     violation is bash and :operation is nil, and prompt the user with a
     different action (allow-once for metadata; "add provider to allow-
     list" for cloud-auth; "request review" for parse-incomplete).

   canonical_mapping_function: |
     (defun jf/gptel-scope--map-operation-to-drawer-key (operation)
       "Map a denied OPERATION keyword to the matching drawer key string.
     The writer's input domain is ten :operation values (eleven minus
     :match-pattern, which the action handler redirects upstream).
     Signals an error on anything outside the closed set, including nil
     and :match-pattern."
       (cond
        ((memq operation '(:read :read-directory))
         "GPTEL_SCOPE_READ")
        ((eq operation :read-metadata)
         "GPTEL_SCOPE_READ_METADATA")
        ((memq operation '(:write :create :create-or-modify :append :delete))
         "GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE")
        ((eq operation :modify)  "GPTEL_SCOPE_MODIFY")
        ((eq operation :execute) "GPTEL_SCOPE_EXECUTE")
        ((eq operation :match-pattern)
         (error "scope-expansion: :match-pattern reached the writer — action handler should have redirected to :read-directory"))
        ((null operation)
         (error "scope-expansion: cannot map nil :operation — handle at the action layer"))
        (t
         (error "scope-expansion: unmapped :operation %S — extend the mapping or use the deny action"
                operation))))

   validator: |
     (defun vocabulary/validate-op-collapse (op)
       (memq op '(:read :read-directory :read-metadata :match-pattern
                  :write :create :create-or-modify :append :delete
                  :modify :execute)))

   closed_set: true
   closed_set_note: |
     Eleven members in the producer-vocabulary domain (validator
     produces these); ten in the writer's collapse domain
     (:match-pattern is action-layer-only). Any new bash-parser
     semantic op type must be added here, in the canonical mapping
     function (writer arm) or in the action handler (redirect arm),
     and to the filesystem-tools / validator-pipeline producer in the
     same change; the architect's vocabulary-mismatch scan flags any
     pcase/cond that mentions an op not in this entry. The
     unmapped_policy is `error` so a producer adding a value without
     updating this entry surfaces at the writer immediately.

   reconciled_from:
     - speculated → divergent (cycle 1, found by reviewer of
       implement-drawer-writer)
     - divergent → reconciled (cycle 1, integrate, after user-authorised
       audit produced findings 8/9/10 and inline code edits brought the
       implementation in line with this entry's strict-error contract)
     - reconciled → speculated (cycle 2 plan, after user dispositions
       10A/B/C added READ_METADATA as a separate collapse target and
       removed :match-pattern from the writer's domain; pending
       rewire-expansion-writer + harden-add-to-scope-action-handler
       implementation in cycle-2 / cycle-3)

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** workspace-state-slot
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-workspace-state-slot
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot
   tier: vocabulary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260524-200631 integrate (2026-05-24):
     canonical_mapping_function workspace--slot-for-trigger NOT adopted;
     routing is caller-side keyword (literal :saved-state / :working-state
     at each of 5 call sites). Closed-set enforcement landed via
     workspace--state-slot-p predicate at
     workspace--autosave-current-layout entry (inline-fix commit b70a5b4).
     Scaffolding archived. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260524-200631/reconciliations/vocabulary-workspace-state-slot.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Workspace layout state-slot vocabulary and trigger → slot routing
   purpose: |
     The closed set of slots that hold a window-state on a v2 layout,
     plus the canonical mapping from "what triggered this write" to
     "which slot gets written". The motivating failure (v1 MVP gap D8):
     re-introducing tab-switch advice in v1 wrote to the single
     :frameset slot and clobbered explicit saves. With two slots and a
     fixed trigger → slot map, the clobber is structurally impossible.
     Mis-mapping a trigger (e.g. writing an autosave to :saved-state)
     is the load-bearing failure mode.

   members:
     - value: :saved-state
       description: |
         The explicit-save slot. Written only by =workspace-save= and
         its variants (=workspace-save-layout=, =workspace-switch-
         layout=, =workspace-new='s home stamp). Authoritative across
         restarts when no =:working-state= is present.
     - value: :working-state
       description: |
         The autosave slot. Written by tab-switch advice, the
         workspaces-mode idle timer, and kill-emacs-hook. Cleared to
         nil by every explicit =workspace-save=. May be nil at any
         time.

   consumer_mapping:
     - consumer: workspace-save (explicit)
       trigger: user gesture
       slot: :saved-state
       side_effect: clears :working-state to nil
     - consumer: workspace-save-layout (explicit; named layout)
       trigger: user gesture (variant)
       slot: :saved-state
       side_effect: clears :working-state to nil on the named layout
     - consumer: workspace-switch-layout
       trigger: intra-workspace layout switch
       slot: :working-state (of outgoing layout)
       side_effect: none
     - consumer: workspace-new home stamp
       trigger: workspace creation
       slot: :saved-state (of new home group)
       side_effect: none
     - consumer: tab-bar-select-tab / tab-bar-switch-to-tab advice
       trigger: workspace context switch
       slot: :working-state (of outgoing workspace's recent layout)
       side_effect: none
     - consumer: workspaces-mode idle timer
       trigger: idle past workspaces-mode-idle-frequency
       slot: :working-state (of current workspace's recent layout)
       side_effect: none
     - consumer: workspace--kill-emacs-flush
       trigger: kill-emacs-hook
       slot: :working-state (of current workspace's recent layout)
       side_effect: synchronous flush after capture

   canonical_mapping_function: |
     ;; The single source of truth for "which slot does this trigger
     ;; write?". If a pcase / cond translating triggers → slots appears
     ;; anywhere else, that's a duplication bug. Implementor task
     ;; two-state-layout owns this function.
     (defun workspace--slot-for-trigger (trigger)
       (pcase trigger
         ((or 'explicit-save 'save-layout 'new-home-stamp) :saved-state)
         ((or 'switch-layout 'tab-switch 'idle 'kill-emacs) :working-state)))

   validator: |
     (defun vocabulary/validate-workspace-state-slot (slot)
       (memq slot '(:saved-state :working-state)))

   closed_set: true

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/layout-v2-plist
     - register/invariant/autosave-never-writes-saved-state
     - register/invariant/explicit-save-clears-working-state

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/vocabularies/workspace-state-slot.el
   #+end_src

** buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain
   tier: vocabulary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260524-200631 integrate (2026-05-24): closed ordered chain
     (bookmark → filename → name → error-buffer) shipped via the `or'
     inside workspace--deserialize-buffer. Per-step producer functions for
     filename/name INLINED (not extracted); step 1
     (workspace--bookmark-buffer) and step 4 (workspace--error-buffer)
     remain standalone helpers. closed_set: true is documentary; no runtime
     pcase predicate ships. Scaffolding archived. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260524-200631/reconciliations/vocabulary-buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain.md.

     cycle-20260525-082618 integrate (2026-05-25): closed-set discipline
     is now code-level. workspace--valid-reincarnation-steps defconst +
     workspace--reincarnation-step-p predicate ship in layouts.org (cycle-
     2 task add-reincarnation-step-predicate, commit a374f87 → merged in
     abffa09). The validator below is no longer documentary; it points
     at the new predicate. closed_set_enforcement is now symmetric with
     register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-082618/reconciliations/vocabulary-buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain.md.

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): STAYS RECONCILED. Entry NOT cited at plan;
     touched by off-workspace-restore (user asks cycle-1790453714-1 = GATE,
     -2 = a reason buffer instead of the stored path) and its fix pass
     (merge 30a8bba4). The closed set is UNCHANGED (four steps, same order,
     =workspace--valid-reincarnation-steps= untouched). What changed is the
     SHAPE of step 1's answer and the dispatch over it: step 1
     (=workspace--bookmark-buffer=) now answers exactly one of a live
     BUFFER (win), nil (FAILED: any non-=user-error= error, or no landing ->
     steps 2-4 as before) or =(declined . MESSAGE)= (the handler signalled
     =user-error= -> a GATE, not a fifth step: steps 2 and 3 are SKIPPED and
     step 4 carries the reason); =quit= propagates. The dispatch in
     =workspace--deserialize-buffer= is a =cond= with an arm per answer and
     a SIGNALLING default (arch-on-touch-1790453714-42), so a future fourth
     shape can never reach the filename step. Declines of one restore are
     collected and reported ONCE by =workspace--restore-frameset=. The leaf
     jump runs with the neutral core variable
     =workspace--restoring-layout-leaf= bound — NOT =inhibit-interaction=:
     the first cut bound =inhibit-interaction= and treated
     =inhibited-interaction= as a decline, and Emacs 30.2's
     =read-multiple-choice= then looped forever on a stock handler's
     large-file prompt (arch-on-touch-1790453714-37); the fix removed both,
     and a stock handler's question is now asked exactly once, as on any
     visit. The draft paragraph in findings/on-touch-off-workspace-restore.md
     described the removed mechanism and was REDRAFTED, not applied. The
     member producer names were stale since cycle-20260525 (functions that
     never shipped) and are corrected to the code. Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/vocabulary-buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain.md

     cycle-1790604160 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 3 against c65efb86; status unchanged. Code
     unchanged at c65efb86 (layouts.el:213-258 =workspace--bookmark-buffer=,
     :260-286 collector + report, :288-329 =workspace--deserialize-buffer=;
     the =user-error= arm at :248-250). # owner: doc-sync-cycle2
     (cycle-1790604160) — the DECLINE GATE has no delta spec
     (eoc-1790453714-02): the new =specs/workspaces/spec.md= MODIFIED
     "Buffer reincarnation across restart" requirement records "a handler's
     =user-error= is a DECLINE: steps 2-3 skipped, reason buffer, one
     aggregated report per restore"; entry text is the source, the delta
     spec must not contradict it. # owner: outcome-action-mapping
     (cycle-1790604160) — CONSEQUENCE, not a change here: once the seam
     declines (instead of falling through) on an internal error for a
     STAMPED record, such a leaf reaches this gate as =(declined . MESSAGE)=
     and lands on the reason buffer; previously it reached step 1 as a
     buffer of the STORED path. The four-step set and the three-answer shape
     are unchanged.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Buffer-reincarnation fallback steps (closed, ordered)
   purpose: |
     The closed, ordered set of fallback steps that
     =workspace--deserialize-buffer= (layouts.el:288; called per leaf by
     =workspace--window-state-deserialize=, :356) attempts when
     reincarnating a window's buffer at restore time. Order is load-bearing: bookmark restore
     comes first because it preserves point, narrowing, and non-file-
     buffer state; filename is the cheap fast-path when bookmark
     restore fails (e.g. help-mode bug#56643); name is the
     session-local last-resort; error-buffer guarantees the window
     slot is never left empty.

     The vocabulary is closed at four steps; a fifth fallback is a
     design change, not a code-level addition.

     THE DECLINE GATE (cycle-1790453714). Step 1 has exactly three answers,
     and the dispatch has an arm for each plus a signalling default:
       buffer               -> step 1 wins;
       nil (FAILED)         -> steps 2, 3, 4 in order (unchanged);
       (declined . MESSAGE) -> the handler signalled =user-error=: steps 2
                               and 3 are SKIPPED — the stored filename is the
                               path the buffer had when the layout was
                               captured, and a handler that has just refused
                               its bookmark's location must not be
                               second-guessed by opening that path anyway —
                               and step 4 carries MESSAGE; the decline is
                               noted for the restore's single report.
     It applies to EVERY bookmark handler, stock or third-party (see
     =register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer= core_protocol).

   members:
     - value: bookmark
       description: |
         =bookmark-jump= on the workspace-buffer's =bookmark= field, in a
         temp-buffer probe under =save-window-excursion= (the host swallows
         "file no longer exists" for a record, so only a buffer change
         proves a landing). Restores point, narrowing, and major-mode
         state. The only step that works for non-file buffers
         (magit-status, eshell, *Messages*). Runs with
         =workspace--restoring-layout-leaf= bound non-nil (layouts.el:244)
         and nothing else changed — a handler that asks the user is asked
         as on any other visit. Answers a buffer, nil or
         =(declined . MESSAGE)=: a =user-error= is a DECLINE, any other
         =error= (a read-time failure such as Emacs bug#56643, a missing
         handler) is a FAILURE that falls through to step 2 instead of
         aborting the whole restore; =quit= propagates (and, being outside
         every barrier, aborts the restore — e.g. C-g at a stock handler's
         large-file prompt).
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
         function: workspace--bookmark-buffer   # layouts.el:213-258
     - value: filename
       description: |
         Call =find-file-noselect= on the workspace-buffer's
         =filename= field. Cheap fast path for plain file buffers
         where the bookmark restore errored or returned nil.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
         function: "workspace--deserialize-buffer (inlined, =ignore-errors= around find-file-noselect; layouts.el:319-322) — reached ONLY when step 1 FAILED"
     - value: name
       description: |
         Call =get-buffer= on the workspace-buffer's =name= field.
         Catches the case where a live buffer of the same name happens
         to exist in the session despite the file no longer existing
         and no bookmark.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
         function: "workspace--deserialize-buffer (inlined get-buffer; layouts.el:324-325) — reached ONLY when step 1 FAILED"
     - value: error-buffer
       description: |
         Construct and return a named, visible buffer describing the
         failure. The window slot is never left empty. This step
         cannot fail; it is the terminating fallback. With the optional
         DECLINED message (cycle-1790453714) it says the handler declined
         and that the saved filename was deliberately NOT opened, instead
         of listing four failed steps.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
         function: "workspace--error-buffer (ws-buffer &optional declined)   # layouts.el:159"

   consumer_mapping:
     - consumer: workspace--deserialize-buffer   # layouts.el:288-329 (was named workspace--restore-leaf)
       order: [bookmark, filename, name, error-buffer]
       short_circuit_policy: deliberate
       short_circuit_on: |
         step 1: a buffer wins; a DECLINE goes straight to step 4 with the
         reason; nil (failed) continues. Steps 2-3: the first non-nil
         buffer wins; step 4 always answers.
       notes: |
         The chain MUST be tried in this order; reordering changes the
         semantics (e.g. trying filename first loses narrowing). The order
         and the gate are asserted by buffer-reincarnation-spec.el; the
         original scaffold (scaffolding/vocabularies/
         buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain.el) is archived.
         Collector: =workspace--reincarnation-declines= (layouts.el:260), bound
         by =workspace--restore-frameset= around the leaf walk and reported
         in ONE message (=workspace--report-reincarnation-declines=, :274)
         after the walk and before the deferred paint.

   canonical_mapping_function: |
     ;; The only call site that decides which step to run next
     ;; (layouts.el:288-329, abridged). Step 1's answer is dispatched by a
     ;; `cond' with an arm per answer and a signalling default; the `or'
     ;; of steps 2-4 lives only in the FAILED arm. Any other call site
     ;; iterating these steps is a duplication bug.
     (defun workspace--deserialize-buffer (ws-buffer)
       (let ((jumped (and (workspace-buffer-bookmark ws-buffer)
                          (condition-case-unless-debug _err
                              (workspace--bookmark-buffer ws-buffer)
                            (error nil)))))
         (cond
          ((bufferp jumped) jumped)                               ; 1. win
          ((eq (car-safe jumped) 'declined)                       ; the gate
           (workspace--note-reincarnation-decline ws-buffer (cdr jumped))
           (workspace--error-buffer ws-buffer (cdr jumped)))
          ((null jumped)                                          ; 1. failed
           (or (and (workspace-buffer-filename ws-buffer)         ; 2.
                    (ignore-errors (find-file-noselect
                                    (workspace-buffer-filename ws-buffer))))
               (and (workspace-buffer-name ws-buffer)             ; 3.
                    (get-buffer (workspace-buffer-name ws-buffer)))
               (workspace--error-buffer ws-buffer)))              ; 4.
          (t (error "Not a reincarnation step-1 result: %S" jumped)))))

   validator: |
     ;; cycle-20260525-082618: code-level predicate now ships in
     ;; config/workspaces/layouts.org. The defconst is the closed set;
     ;; the predicate is the membership check. Mirrors workspace--state-
     ;; slot-p / workspace--valid-state-slots from cycle 1.
     (defconst workspace--valid-reincarnation-steps
       '(bookmark filename name error-buffer))
     (defun workspace--reincarnation-step-p (step)
       (memq step workspace--valid-reincarnation-steps))

   closed_set: true
   closed_set_enforcement: code-level predicate (workspace--reincarnation-step-p); step 1's three-answer result set closed by the dispatch's signalling default (cycle-1790453714)

   test_corpus:
     - config/workspaces/test/buffer-reincarnation-spec.el   # § "the gate: a bookmark handler that declines" (:273): skip + reason; the stock large-file prompt asked exactly once through the REAL read-multiple-choice; the leaf flag bound only around the jump; the default arm signals; a mere FAILURE still falls to the filename; one report per restore
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-off-workspace-spec.el   # § "layout reincarnation chain: a leaf whose bookmark declines" (:762): not-on-branch and missing-repo leaves restore to a reason buffer, one report, no prompt, stored path never visited

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/workspace-buffer-struct
     - register/boundary/buffer-reincarnation-pipeline
     - register/invariant/no-silent-wrong-file-open           # prohibition 4's layout clause
     - register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer          # core_protocol
     - register/vocabulary/bookmark-resolution-outcome

   cited_by_tasks:
     - doc-sync-cycle2              # cycle-1790604160: delta spec for the decline gate
     - outcome-action-mapping       # cycle-1790604160: consequence — stamped-leaf seam errors now decline
   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/vocabularies/buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain.el
   #+end_src

** workspace-broken-disposition
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-workspace-broken-disposition
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/workspace-broken-disposition
   tier: vocabulary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260525-200459 integrate (2026-05-25): closed-set vocabulary
     (switch/restore/re-anchor/purge/delete × :refused/:allowed) holds
     as speculated. Cycle 1 implemented the runtime predicate
     workspace--broken-p (the structural anchor at the data layer);
     the user-facing commands and their inline guards land in cycle 3
     via broken-home-tolerance and workspace-delete-and-purge. The
     canonical pcase mapping is reference material for the end-of-cycle
     architect audit (signal-class 2: vocabulary-mismatch), not runtime
     authority — each command's inline guard is the runtime enforcement.
     Implementor noted (disc-add-home-slot-3): vocabulary covers
     user-facing dispositions only, NOT internal helpers
     (workspace--mark-broken / clear-broken); the scaffolding documents
