     the boundary clearly. Scaffold archived; remains as audit reference.
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-200459/reconciliations/vocabulary-workspace-broken-disposition.md.

     cycle-1790604160 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 3 against c65efb86; status unchanged. (First cited
     by a workspace-portable-bookmarks task.) The five members and their
     guards are unchanged; =workspace--broken-p= data-model.el:238; guards
     at tabs.el:173 / :376, persistence.el:462, workspaces.el:285. THE
     MOTIVATING FAILURE HAPPENED, in a CONSUMER (eoc-1790453714-01,
     BLOCKING): portable-bookmarks' "add worktree here" offer
     (=workspace-bookmark--missing-repo-withheld=,
     portable-bookmarks.el:618-643) is never withheld for a broken current
     workspace, and =jf/workspace--add-worktree= then recreates the missing
     home as a bare directory (probe P1/P2). The offer is not one of the
     five commands, so it is recorded as a SIXTH consumer_mapping row below,
     =:refused=, SPECULATED. # owner: add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate
     (cycle-1790604160). The rule that the offer and core's Integrations
     menu share ONE health notion is the new
     =register/invariant/add-worktree-offer-shares-workspace-health-gate=;
     this entry keeps only the disposition. NOTE for core (not this batch):
     the menu's own gate =workspace--menu-healthy-p=
     (workspaces-transient.el:11-14) is =broken-p= only, so a home deleted
     MID-SESSION (untagged — the broken tag is load-time,
     =register/invariant/broken-tag-runtime-only=) still reaches the same
     directory-creating command through =workspace--menu-invoke-
     integration= (:25-38) — finding arch-cycle-1790604160-plan-01, routed
     to .tasks/workspaces-registration-hardening-leftovers.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Per-command disposition for broken-state workspaces
   purpose: |
     The closed set of user-facing workspace operations, each tagged
     with how it MUST behave when invoked on a workspace whose =:home=
     no longer exists on disk (=workspace--broken-p= returns t). The
     motivating failure mode this vocabulary closes: a new command
     added later that forgets the broken-state guard silently
     activates a broken workspace and triggers downstream errors
     (find-file on a missing path, git invocation in a non-existent
     dir, etc.). With the vocabulary pinned and a canonical
     dispatcher, every command that touches a workspace by name is
     audit-able against this table.

     Two dispositions:
     - =:refused= — command signals =user-error= naming the missing
       =:home= and directs the user to =workspace-re-anchor= or
       =workspace-purge=. NO side effects.
     - =:allowed= — command operates; for purge/delete this means
       registry-only cleanup (no filesystem deletion to perform).
       For re-anchor this means the broken tag is cleared by
       operation success.

   members:
     - value: switch
       description: |
         =workspace-switch NAME= activates a workspace's tab. On
         broken: refused. Activation MUST NOT happen on a workspace
         whose :home is missing — downstream code (=workspace-home-
         builder= default = =find-file <home>/home.org=) would fail
         on the missing path.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/tabs.org
         function: workspace-switch
     - value: restore
       description: |
         =workspace-restore NAME= reactivates a persisted workspace
         after restart. On broken: refused. Same reasoning as switch;
         additionally, layout reincarnation would attempt to open
         buffer files relative to the missing :home.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
         function: workspace-restore
     - value: re-anchor
       description: |
         =workspace-re-anchor NAME PATH= points a broken workspace at
         a new :home. On broken: allowed (this is the recovery
         command). Success clears the broken tag and updates :home;
         if =(file-name-nondirectory (directory-file-name PATH))=
         differs from NAME, the registry entry is renamed per the
         =registry-name-equals-basename= invariant.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/workspaces.org
         function: workspace-re-anchor
     - value: purge
       description: |
         =workspace-purge NAME= removes a workspace destructively. On
         broken: allowed (registry-only cleanup — no filesystem
         deletion to perform; the path is already absent). The
         default-parent safeguard still applies (refuses to delete
         dirs outside =workspaces-default-parent-directory= without
         a prefix-arg opt-in) but is moot when the path is missing.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/workspaces.org
         function: workspace-purge
     - value: delete
       description: |
         =workspace-delete NAME= removes a workspace non-destructively
         (registry-only). On broken: allowed. No filesystem impact in
         either broken or healthy state — see =workspace-delete-is-
         non-destructive= invariant (cycle-2+ entry).
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/workspaces.org
         function: workspace-delete

   consumer_mapping:
     - consumer: workspace-switch
       broken_disposition: :refused
       guard_location: top of function body, after registry-lookup
     - consumer: workspace-restore
       broken_disposition: :refused
       guard_location: top of function body, after registry-lookup
     - consumer: workspace-re-anchor
       broken_disposition: :allowed
       guard_location: N/A (operates ON broken-state by design)
     - consumer: workspace-purge
       broken_disposition: :allowed
       guard_location: skip filesystem-deletion step if broken
     - consumer: workspace-delete
       broken_disposition: :allowed
       guard_location: N/A (no filesystem-touching code path)

     - consumer: workspace-bookmark--missing-repo-withheld   # portable-bookmarks.el:618; the add-worktree OFFER, not a command (cycle-1790604160, SPECULATED, owner add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate)
       broken_disposition: :refused
       guard_location: |
         an arm of the withheld alist: add-worktree is withheld (with its
         reason in the prompt) when the current workspace is broken OR its
         :home is not an existing local directory (file-remote-p first, then
         file-directory-p — the mid-session case the load-time tag misses);
         decided by the ONE module health predicate, shared with the
         off-workspace candidate filter (--offerable-workspaces :903-915).
         Two offer-matrix specs: tagged-broken; home-vanished.

   canonical_mapping_function: |
     ;; The single source of truth for "is this command allowed on a
     ;; broken workspace?". Used by audit (architect signal-class:
     ;; vocabulary-mismatch); not necessarily invoked at runtime
     ;; (each command's guard is inline). If runtime guards drift,
     ;; this table catches the divergence.
     (defun vocabulary/workspace-broken-disposition (op)
       (pcase op
         ((or 'switch 'restore) :refused)
         ((or 're-anchor 'purge 'delete) :allowed)))

   validator: |
     (defun vocabulary/validate-workspace-broken-op (op)
       (memq op '(switch restore re-anchor purge delete)))

   closed_set: true
   closed_set_enforcement: |
     Audit-only at first. Cycle-1 scaffolding generates the
     pcase-with-error-arms shell; any command added in a later cycle
     without a corresponding pcase arm will fall through to the
     generated =(error "vocabulary: unhandled broken-op %s" op)=
     fallback. If the project later adds enough workspace commands
     that runtime dispatch is worth the indirection, the canonical
     mapping function above becomes the runtime authority.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/workspace-plist-v3
     - register/invariant/broken-home-no-auto-recreate  # cycle-2+

   cited_by_tasks:
     - add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate   # cycle-1790604160: sixth consumer row (the offer), :refused
   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/add-workspace-home-directory/scaffolding/vocabularies/workspace-broken-disposition.el
   #+end_src

** workspace-integration-outcome
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-workspace-integration-outcome
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/workspace-integration-outcome
   tier: vocabulary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260612-143558 (workspace-integration-menu) integrate:
     created CONFIRMED from =workspace--run-one-integration='s pcase in
     =config/workspaces/integrations.el=. Realised verbatim; clean reviews.
   load_bearing: false
   title: integration handler result protocol
   purpose: |
     The closed set of outcomes an integration handler reports, by RETURN
     VALUE, and how the dispatcher normalises them. Two ergonomic failure
     paths (return =failed=, or just signal) collapse to one.
   members:
     - value: "ok"
       meaning: handler performed its action.
     - value: "skipped"
       meaning: handler deliberately no-op'd (e.g. gptel on =anchored-existing=,
                or nil preset).
     - value: "(failed . REASON)"
       meaning: handler tried and failed; REASON is a string. message'd by
                dispatch, never fatal.
   normalisation:
     pcase_site: "config/workspaces/integrations.el — workspace--run-one-integration"
     rules: |
       'ok -> (id . ok); 'skipped -> (id . skipped);
       (failed . r) -> (id . (failed . r));
       a SIGNALLED error -> (id . (failed . (error-message-string err)));
       any UNKNOWN return -> (id . ok)  [lenient: benign handler drift not punished].
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/workspace-integration-registry
   cited_by_tasks:
     - integration-registry-core
   discovered_from: workspace-integration-menu
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** workspace-creation-context
   :PROPERTIES:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/workspace-creation-context
   tier: vocabulary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260612-143558 (workspace-integration-menu) integrate:
     created CONFIRMED. The =:context= label set carried in the anchor
     payload and dispatched at the three =workspace--new-*= birth sites
     (=wire-create-dispatch=) plus the transient's invoke site
     (=workspaces-transient-menu=).

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): RECONCILED (was confirmed). The set is SIX
     members, not four: (1) =bookmark-jump= is NEW this cycle —
     portable-bookmarks' missing-repo chooser builds the anchor payload for
     its "add worktree here" answer with it (missing-repo-choice, then
     routed through core's constructor in discovery-memo-dead-helpers-and-
     picker-scope; arch-on-touch-1790453714-33); (2) =purge= was ALREADY
     produced at b56f385a (=workspace-purge= -> 
     =workspace--dispatch-purge-integrations name home 'purge=) and was
     simply never registered here (arch-on-touch-1790453714-59). Both are
     recorded; the set stays closed. No handler branches on either value
     today (gptel self-gates only on =anchored-existing=; git-worktrees reads
     no =:context=). The constructor's docstring now enumerates all six and
     cites this entry (integrations.el:53-66). Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/vocabulary-workspace-creation-context.md

     cycle-1790604160 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 3 against c65efb86; status unchanged. Six members,
     unchanged (integrations.el:53-63 docstring enumerates them; =purge=
     dispatched at workspaces.el =workspace--dispatch-purge-integrations=;
     =bookmark-jump= built at portable-bookmarks.el:815). # owner:
     doc-sync-cycle2 (cycle-1790604160) — the new
     =specs/workspace-integrations/spec.md= MODIFIED "Creation-time
     dispatch" requirement lists the six members (eoc-1790453714-02); the
     entry is the source of the list. No code task touches the set.
   load_bearing: false
   title: workspace creation/invocation context labels
   purpose: |
     The closed symbol set that tells an integration handler WHICH birth
     (or on-demand) context it is firing in, so it can self-gate. Carried
     as the payload =:context= key.
   members:
     - value: "fresh"
       meaning: brand-new workspace (default-path; anchor non-repo). Full scaffold ran.
     - value: "anchored-scaffolded"
       meaning: anchored existing repo that had no home.org; scaffolded, no commit.
     - value: "anchored-existing"
       meaning: anchored existing repo WITH home.org (register-only, no scaffold).
                gptel's :on-create SKIPS this — "don't inject artifacts into an
                adopted workspace."
     - value: "menu-invoke"
       meaning: NOT a birth context — built by the transient when a user invokes
                an integration's :menu command on the current workspace.
     - value: "purge"
       meaning: NOT a birth context — the TEARDOWN payload =workspace-purge= hands
                every =:on-purge= handler through
                =workspace--dispatch-purge-integrations= (workspaces.el:266). Present
                since before cycle-1790453714; registered here then. Never built
                for a remote home (purge of one unregisters only).
     - value: "bookmark-jump"
       meaning: NOT a birth context and NOT a transient invocation — built by
                portable-bookmarks' missing-repo chooser
                (=workspace-bookmark--add-worktree-payload=, portable-bookmarks.el:806-817,
                via core's =workspace--integration-payload=) for a DIRECT call of
                git-worktrees' =:menu= command when the user answers "add worktree
                here" at a bookmark jump. The payload also carries =:source-repo=.
                It is funcalled, never dispatched through the registry, so no other
                handler (gptel sessions) ever receives it. (cycle-1790453714)
   closed: true
   closed_note: |
     Six members at f860103c. A seventh value is a register change here AND in
     the constructor's docstring (integrations.org); handlers that self-gate on
     =:context= must say what they do with an unknown value (gptel skips only
     =anchored-existing=).
   dispatched_at:
     - config/workspaces/tabs.org (workspace--new-default-path -> fresh)
     - config/workspaces/tabs.org (workspace--new-anchor-existing -> the three anchor cases)
     - config/workspaces/workspaces-transient.org (menu-invoke)
     - config/workspaces/workspaces.org (workspace-purge -> purge, via workspace--dispatch-purge-integrations)
     - config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org (§ Add worktree here -> bookmark-jump; a direct funcall, not a registry dispatch)
   related_entries:
     - register/shape/workspace-integration-anchor-payload
     - register/boundary/workspace-integration-registry
     - register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer
     - register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
   cited_by_tasks:

     - workspaces-transient-menu
     - missing-repo-choice                           # cycle-1790453714: produces bookmark-jump
     - discovery-memo-dead-helpers-and-picker-scope  # cycle-1790453714: payload via the one constructor
     - doc-sync-cycle2   # cycle-1790604160: six-member vocabulary into the delta spec

** agent-path-params
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-agent-path-params
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/agent-path-params
   tier: vocabulary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     CONFIRMED cycle-1781718724 integrate.  TOOL-SURFACE LOCUS now landed:
     agent-workroot-and-paths (merge 614b95a7) renamed the single allowed_paths
     arg to the closed symmetric set — the tool :args advertises EXACTLY
     {preset, description, prompt, work_root, read_paths, write_paths} and
     EXCLUDES allowed_paths/denied_paths (verified by work-root-spec.el's
     args-schema assertion AND the author-blind reviewer reading the diff cold).
     work_root -> :GPTEL_WORK_ROOT: (omitted -> parent default-directory frozen
     via expand-file-name, a parent-supplied default NOT runtime inheritance);
     read_paths -> build-scope-plist :read -> :GPTEL_SCOPE_READ:; write_paths ->
     :write with /tmp/** appended LAST as scratch -> :GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE(+):.
     The helper locus was confirmed cycle-1781716077; this cycle confirms the
     tool surface, completing the confirm gate (exact six-param set + suite
     green).  The 16 quarantined wrong-number-of-arguments failures the cycle-1
     split left are CLEARED (--task now passes (read-paths write-paths)); the
     gptel suite returned 37 -> 21 (pre-existing floor), zero new signatures.
     Reconciliation note: .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781718724/reconciliations/
     vocabulary-agent-path-params.md.  CARRIED (separate, non-blocking) SPEC-
     SIGNAL on the D7 guardrail: <root>/** glob does NOT match the no-trailing-
     slash root expand-file-name yields, so work_root=/p + read_paths=[/p/**]
     warns every spawn — routed to user (does not affect this vocabulary entry).
     RECONCILED cycle-1781723720 integrate (confirmed -> reconciled): the
     work_root member's maps_to WIDENED -- it now maps to BOTH the
     :GPTEL_WORK_ROOT: drawer key AND a (directory-file-name work_root)/**
     pattern prepended (deduped via member) into :GPTEL_SCOPE_READ:, so the
     work root is readable by construction (relative read under it ALLOWED
     with empty read_paths; work-root-spec.el:200-251). The closed six-param
     SET is UNCHANGED. The CARRIED D7 guardrail spec-signal is RESOLVED by
     removal (user Option D): the <root>/** glob false-alarm is moot because
     the guardrail + its sole-purpose (require 'jf-gptel-scope-validation) are
     gone (agent-work-root-auto-read, merge 9e0d60c3). RESIDUAL
     docs-allowed-paths-rename CLOSED this cycle (verified no-op; 614b95a7 had
     already cleared the prose). Reconciliation note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781723720/reconciliations/
     vocabulary-agent-path-params.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: The PersistentAgent tool path/work-root parameter vocabulary (post allowed_paths split)
   purpose: |
     The closed set of path/work-root parameters the PersistentAgent tool
     surface advertises after this change. The single allowed_paths
     parameter (whose write behavior was accidentally "/tmp only") is
     replaced by a symmetric, parent-controlled set. /tmp/** is no longer
     the write default -- it is the LAST element auto-appended to :write as
     guaranteed scratch. Advertising a removed param (allowed_paths /
     denied_paths) or mis-routing write_paths so /tmp becomes the sole
     write target is a vocabulary-mismatch bug of the same class as the
     drawer-key mis-routing tracked by register/vocabulary/drawer-key-set.

   members:
     - value: "work_root"
       cardinality: scalar
       description: |
         absolute dir the agent operates in; written to the agent drawer as
         :GPTEL_WORK_ROOT:. Omitted -> default to the parent buffer's work
         root (default-directory at spawn), frozen into the drawer (a
         parent-supplied default, NOT runtime inheritance). Auto-included in
         the agent read scope (cycle-1781723720): (directory-file-name
         work_root)/** is PREPENDED (deduped via member) into :read so the
         work root is readable by construction -- a relative file under it
         validates ALLOWED even with empty read_paths.
       maps_to: |
         ":GPTEL_WORK_ROOT: drawer key" AND prepended into
         "build-scope-plist :read -> :GPTEL_SCOPE_READ:" as
         (directory-file-name work_root)/** (deduped via member)
     - value: "read_paths"
       cardinality: list
       description: agent read scope; replaces the read role of allowed_paths.
       maps_to: "build-scope-plist :read -> :GPTEL_SCOPE_READ:"
     - value: "write_paths"
       cardinality: list
       description: |
         agent write scope (parent-controlled). build-scope-plist appends
         /tmp/** as the last :write element -> serializes via the existing
         +-multivalue convention (:GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE+: /tmp/**).
       maps_to: "build-scope-plist :write -> :GPTEL_SCOPE_WRITE:(+)"

   forbidden_values:
     - value: "allowed_paths"
       reason: |
         Removed from the tool surface (BREAKING). Its read role moves to
         read_paths; its accidental "/tmp-only write" behavior is deleted.
         No persisted-data migration (model re-reads the schema each
         session).
     - value: "denied_paths"
       reason: |
         Dropped from the surface; deny stays the standard
         jf/gptel-persistent-agent--standard-deny-paths set.

   closed_set: true
   unmapped_policy: error
   unmapped_policy_rationale: |
     The tool :args schema is the closed surface the model reads. A param
     outside {preset, description, prompt, work_root, read_paths,
     write_paths} -- especially a resurrected allowed_paths -- must not
     appear; the agent-workroot-and-paths spec asserts the exact arg set.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/scope-config-plist
     - register/vocabulary/drawer-key-set
     - register/invariant/cwd-scope-agreement
   cited_by_tasks:
     - agent-build-scope-plist-split
     - agent-workroot-and-paths
     - agent-work-root-auto-read
     - docs-allowed-paths-rename
     - verify-change
     - docs-allowed-paths-rename
     - verify-change
   discovered_from: gptel-work-root-default-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** fragment-kind
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-fragment-kind
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/fragment-kind
   tier: vocabulary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: "cycle-1781883616 integrate: speculation matched. Closed set {static,dynamic} (jf/gptel-fragment-kinds), default static (jf/gptel-fragment-default-kind). NEW DETAIL: source declares kind via a #+fragment_kind: Org keyword (case-insensitive); unrecognized value falls back to static."
   load_bearing: false
   title: Fragment kind ({static, dynamic})
   purpose: |
     The closed set of fragment kinds a fragment source may declare. A
     fragment that declares no kind defaults to static. The value is read
     off the parsed fragment (register/shape/fragment, :kind) and the
     composer dispatches on it: static → consume pre-rendered text (no
     compose-time work); dynamic → evaluate the fragment function at compose
     time (register/invariant/static-prerender-dynamic-compose). Chosen by
     this change (design.md Decision 1).

   members:
     - value: static
       description: |
         Rendered text is fixed at tangle time (committed artifact). The
         composer consumes the pre-rendered text and performs no rendering
         per send. This is the default when no kind is declared.
       producer:
         file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
         function: jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source
     - value: dynamic
       description: |
         Text is produced by evaluating a function at compose time. Default
         position is the composition tail (prompt-cache friendliness).
       producer:
         file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
         function: jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source

   consumer_mapping:
     - consumer: <composer> (composer.md task)
       consumer_field: per-fragment dispatch
       accepts: [static, dynamic]
       routing_function: <composer>

   validator: |
     (defun vocabulary/validate-fragment-kind (k)
       (memq k '(static dynamic)))

   closed_set: true

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** section-name-to-tag
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-section-name-to-tag
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/section-name-to-tag
   tier: vocabulary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: "cycle-1781883616 integrate RECONCILED. prior_form: tag = (replace-regexp-in-string " " "_" (downcase name)) -- naive, would not trim or collapse runs. new_form: jf/gptel-fragment--section-name-to-tag trims leading/trailing whitespace then collapses runs of whitespace to a single "_" ("Output   Format" -> output_format; no leading/trailing underscore). why_tests_missed: the speculated entry stated the naive regex and the only multi-word golden fixture used a single space, so run-collapsing/trimming edge cases were unpinned until the implementation added them."
   load_bearing: false
   title: Section-name → Claude XML tag derivation (snake_case)
   purpose: |
     The single derivation from a section's Org-heading name to its Claude
     XML tag. The casing convention is closed: snake_case. A section named N
     yields tag = (replace-regexp-in-string " " "_" (downcase N)) — e.g.
     "Output Format" → output_format, "Role" → role, "Constraints" →
     constraints. The renderer (jf/gptel-fragment-render, claude branch)
     wraps the body as "<tag>\nbody\n</tag>". This is a derivation, not an
     enum of fixed values, but the casing rule is the closed contract: any
     other casing (camelCase, kebab-case, preserved spaces) is a divergence.
     Golden-snapshot specs pin the exact tag text per section, so a casing
     drift shows up as a golden diff.

   members:
     - value: "downcase"
       description: heading text is lowercased.
       producer:
         file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
         function: jf/gptel-fragment-render
     - value: "space → underscore"
       description: |
         each ASCII space in the (downcased) name becomes an underscore.
       producer:
         file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
         function: jf/gptel-fragment-render

   canonical_mapping_function: |
     ;; The single source of truth for section-name → tag. If you find a
     ;; second downcase/replace of heading text anywhere else, that's a
     ;; duplication bug.
     (defun vocabulary/section-name-to-tag (name)
       (replace-regexp-in-string " " "_" (downcase name)))

   validator: |
     (defun vocabulary/validate-section-tag (tag)
       ;; snake_case: lowercase letters, digits, underscores only.
       (string-match-p "\\`[a-z0-9_]+\\'" tag))

   closed_set: true   # the casing convention is closed (snake_case)

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** render-backend
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-render-backend
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/render-backend
   tier: vocabulary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: "cycle-1781883616 integrate: speculation matched. claude is the only implemented backend; others are reported (log+signal), never silently rendered."
   load_bearing: false
   title: Renderer backend symbol set
   purpose: |
     The set of backend symbols jf/gptel-fragment-render dispatches on. The
     set is OPEN for extension (the renderer takes backend as a seam so new
     backends are a pure addition — design.md Decision 2), but unlike a
     normal open enum, an unimplemented member MUST be reported (signal or
     jf/gptel--log 'warn + signal), never silently rendered as Claude or
     Markdown (register/invariant/renderer-never-markdown). claude is the
     only implemented member this change.

   members:
     - value: claude
       description: |
         The only implemented rendering. Maps each section to an XML block
         "<tag>\nbody\n</tag>" with tag per
         register/vocabulary/section-name-to-tag.
       producer:
         file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
         function: jf/gptel-fragment-render

   consumer_mapping:
     - consumer: jf/gptel-fragment-render
       consumer_field: backend dispatch
       accepts: [claude]
       routing_function: jf/gptel-fragment-render

   validator: |
     (defun vocabulary/validate-render-backend (b)
       ;; only claude is implemented; any other symbol must be reported,
       ;; not silently rendered.
       (eq b 'claude))

   closed_set: false   # open for extension; unimplemented members reported, not rendered

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** bookmark-resolution-outcome
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-bookmark-resolution-outcome
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/bookmark-resolution-outcome
   tier: vocabulary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1790453714 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     entry stays =reconciled=; the NESTED "missing-repo action" set stays
     =speculated= and its OWNER is =missing-repo-choice= (wave 3; on-touch
     fires on its first merge). The speculation execute settles is the
     ask-16 default as drafted: (a) missing-repo may carry a nil :path with
     :checkout set — the resolver already produces it
     (=workspace-bookmark--resolve-anchored=, portable-bookmarks.el:938-959,
     'absent arm) when the canonical checkout lacks the relpath; (b)
     view-canonical-read-only is offered ONLY when :path is non-nil, so the
     offered set is {add-worktree, cancel} when nil; (c) add-worktree
     targets :checkout and never re-runs discovery; (d) whether this prompt
     and off-workspace-restore's empty-registry fallback are ONE chooser —
     if they merge, the nested set gains workspace candidates and is
     re-stated at integrate. If the user answers ask 16 otherwise, the
     missing-repo member text and the nested set are rewritten BEFORE wave
     3 starts, not after it. TAG, binding on EVERY consumer (jump-seam,
     missing-repo-choice, off-workspace-restore, and docs-readme's prose):
     :path and :checkout are compared by FILE IDENTITY (device+inode,
     =register/invariant/directory-identity-by-device-inode=), never by
     string — a workspace checkout carries the registry's spelling
     (/var/...), the canonical checkout git's truename (/private/var/...)
     (end-of-cycle finding 7; resolver-spec.el:541). The absent
     missing-repo-spec.el in test_corpus is owned by missing-repo-choice.
     =discovery-cleanup-singulars-and-docstrings= (wave 2) rewrites the two
     anchoring docstrings (portable-bookmarks.el:872-873, :922-924); the
     member descriptions here already carry the corrected wording
     ("presence and kind are asked of the filesystem"), so no register
     change follows from that task.

     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): RECONCILED.

   load_bearing: true
   title: Bookmark resolution outcomes (closed, ORDERED) and the missing-repo action set
   purpose: |
     The safety vocabulary of this capability. Resolution answers exactly one
     of SIX outcomes (DD-14), and the ordering is part of the contract
     because the predicates overlap: a workspace can hold a checkout AND a
     canonical checkout can be in view, and only first-match-wins keeps the
     answer deterministic. The order table is =portable-bookmarks.org=
     § "The decision".

     The motivating failure mode this vocabulary closes is a SEVENTH outcome
     arriving by accident — a new branch in the resolver that returns a stray
     symbol, or a caller that treats an unknown symbol as "just open the
     stored path". That is precisely the silent wrong-file open the whole
     capability exists to remove. The defconst plus a closed-set predicate
     makes a new outcome a deliberate two-place change; =ambiguous= was
     added exactly that way (DD-14).

     Mirrors the existing closed-set discipline in
     =workspace--valid-reincarnation-steps= (=layouts.el=) and
     =register/vocabulary/buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain=.

     PRODUCER DISCIPLINE: =workspace-bookmark--resolve=
     (portable-bookmarks.el:1763) is a DECISION FUNCTION. It may run git
     queries; it MUST NOT open a buffer, prompt, or message. Interactive
     policy belongs to the jump seam and the prompt tasks. This is what keeps
     the awkward outcomes unit-testable.

   ordered: true
   result_shape: |
     =(:outcome O :path P :identity I :relpath R :candidates C :checkout K)=
     — ALWAYS six keys, built only by =workspace-bookmark--outcome=
     (portable-bookmarks.el:1610), which signals on a symbol outside
     =workspace-bookmark--valid-outcomes= (:1617-1618). =:candidates= is non-nil
     only for =ambiguous=; =:checkout= is non-nil only for =resolved=,
     =not-on-branch= and =missing-repo=. Consumers compare =:path= and
     =:checkout= by FILE IDENTITY, never by string: a workspace checkout
     carries the registry's spelling, the canonical checkout git's truename
     (end-of-cycle finding 7; portable-bookmarks-resolver-spec.el:541
     already does).
   members:
     - value: non-portable
       order: 1
       description: |
         Not a record; or the stamp is not all three of repo / relpath /
         format 1 (=register/shape/portable-bookmark-coordinates= "ALL THREE
         OR NONE"); or the relpath escapes LEXICALLY (absolute, a =..=
         component, NUL). =:path= is the record's stored absolute
         =filename= — the ONLY outcome permitted to use it; =:identity= and
         =:relpath= nil. Decided before any git runs. A VALID stamp never
         resolves non-portable: an on-disk (link) escape is NOT non-portable
         (DD-16).
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--resolve
     - value: resolved
       order: 2
       description: |
         WORKSPACE-NAME holds exactly ONE checkout of the identity and
         something of the relpath's KIND (trailing "/" = directory) exists
         at =<checkout>/<relpath>= by spelling — presence and kind are asked
         of the filesystem, which follows links
         (=workspace-bookmark--anchor=, portable-bookmarks.el:1674-1700). =:path=
         is that spelling;
         =:checkout= the checkout. The only outcome that opens anything
         without asking.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--resolve
     - value: not-on-branch
       order: 2
       description: |
         That one checkout lacks the relpath, or the kind there differs, or
         the link there dangles (renamed, deleted, or absent on this
         branch). =:path= nil; =:checkout= the checkout. MUST be reported
         and MUST NOT be visited. The subtle member. (Corrected
         cycle-1790453714, arch-on-touch-1790453714-3: Emacs 30.2's
         =bookmark-default-handler= does NOT create an empty buffer for a
         nonexistent path — it signals =bookmark-error-no-filename=; the
         stock trap is the host's RELOCATE prompt for a bookmark jumped by
         NAME, which on "yes" rewrites the stored record to wherever the
         user points and persists it. The seam declines before the host
         runs, so neither can happen.)
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--resolve
     - value: ambiguous
       order: 3 and 5
       description: |
         The workspace holds 2+ checkouts of the identity (order 3), or holds
         none and 2+ DISTINCT canonical checkouts are in view (order 5; ask
         11). =:candidates= lists them in discovery order; =:path= and
         =:checkout= nil. Reported; nothing opened; nothing chosen (DD-14:
         "report and open nothing").
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--resolve
     - value: missing-repo
       order: 4
       description: |
         The workspace holds none; exactly one canonical checkout is in view.
         =:checkout= = that canonical checkout (the target of
         =add-worktree=). =:path= = =<checkout>/<relpath>= when something of
         the relpath's kind is present there, ELSE NIL — the canonical
         checkout may lack the relpath (a file that exists only on the
         workspace branch where it was bookmarked). A CANDIDATE to offer,
         never acted on; with =:path= nil only =add-worktree= (and =cancel=)
         are offerable (ask-cycle-1789935943-16, accepted 2026-09-26).
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--resolve
       consumer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--offer-missing-repo   # portable-bookmarks.el:690; also reached from --no-workspace-plan (:1009)
     - value: no-clone
       order: 6
       description: |
         Nothing in view, or a discovery error once the identity is known
         (see error_mapping). =:path= and =:checkout= nil. Reported; nothing
         opened.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--resolve

   consumers: |
     (cycle-1790453714) Every dispatch over the set is closed:
     - =workspace-bookmark--jump-plan= (portable-bookmarks.el:529-540), the
       jump seam: non-portable -> call through with the ORIGINAL argument;
       resolved -> =--plan-to-open= (pre-flight, then the one copy site);
       missing-repo -> =--offer-missing-repo=; not-on-branch / ambiguous /
       no-clone -> decline with =--decline-message=; default SIGNALS.
     - =workspace-bookmark--decline-message= (:471-485): the four
       non-opening members; reads only :identity / :relpath / :checkout /
       :candidates; default SIGNALS.
     - =workspace-bookmark--no-workspace-plan= (:1018-1026), the
       off-workspace fallback: missing-repo -> the chooser; ambiguous /
       no-clone -> decline; ANY other outcome -> decline (never opens).
     - =workspace-bookmark--after-add-message= (:819-826), after an
       add-worktree re-resolve: anything but resolved declines.
     Consumers never re-check confinement, never follow links and never
     compare :path / :checkout by string.

   nested_vocabulary:
     name: missing-repo action
     closed: true
     status: reconciled
     cycle_2_owner: missing-repo-choice   # cycle-1790453714; settles the ask-16 default
     speculated_note: |
       STILL SPECULATED at cycle-1789935943 integrate: =missing-repo-choice=
       is deferred to cycle 2 and ask-cycle-1789935943-16 is OPEN. Two rules
       are carried from the resolver-outcomes on-touch and the end-of-cycle
       audit, PENDING USER CONFIRMATION: (1) =view-canonical-read-only= is
       offered ONLY when =:path= is non-nil; (2) =add-worktree= targets
       =:checkout= and never re-runs discovery. The plan-time question —
       whether this prompt and the off-workspace workspace-picker are one
       "where should this open?" question — stays open until
       missing-repo-choice / off-workspace-restore land; if they merge, this
       nested set gains workspace candidates and is re-stated then.
     reconciled_note: |
       cycle-1790453714: IMPLEMENTED AND CLOSED as
       =workspace-bookmark--missing-repo-actions= (portable-bookmarks.el:589-601),
       three =(ACTION KEY NAME DESCRIPTION)= entries in prompt order, offered
       through =read-multiple-choice= (nothing free-form can be typed) and
       dispatched by a =pcase= with a signalling default (:714-721); a
       fourth member is a two-place change (table + dispatch). The chooser
       =workspace-bookmark--offer-missing-repo (record resolution)= (:690)
       returns a PLAN and never opens: =(view COPY . CHECKOUT)=,
       =(call-through . COPY)= after a successful add and ONE fresh resolve,
       =(decline . MESSAGE)=, or =(cancel)=; every COPY comes from
       =--plan-to-open= -> =--substituted-record=. Any error inside the
       choice DECLINES (its own barrier, :704-726), so the seam's
       stored-path fall-through is unreachable from a missing-repo record.
       When no one can answer (=workspace-bookmark--can-prompt-p= nil: a
       non-interactive Emacs, =inhibit-interaction= bound, or core restoring
       a layout leaf) it declines with the seam's missing-repo report instead
       of prompting. The prompt names the bookmark, the workspace, the
       identity and the canonical checkout, and states each withheld
       option's reason. The two plan-time questions are settled: the
       off-workspace fallback reuses this chooser; the workspace picker is a
       separate prompt, so no workspace candidates joined the set.
     members:
       - add-worktree: |
           Add a worktree of the repository at =:checkout= to the current
           workspace via git-worktrees' =jf/workspace--add-worktree=, handing
           =:checkout= as the payload's =:source-repo= (the payload is
           core's =workspace--integration-payload= output for context
           =bookmark-jump= plus =:source-repo=); git-worktrees makes the
           worktree from it with no source prompt and no discovery. Then
           resolve again, ONCE, in a fresh discovery memo: only =resolved=
           opens (pre-flight included); anything else declines with its
           report, never a second prompt; =(failed . REASON)= / =skipped=
           decline naming the reason. The only member that mutates the
           filesystem. Offerable whether or not =:path= is nil. WITHHELD
           (with the reason stated in the prompt) when no workspace is
           current, when the command is not runnable (not =fboundp=, or not
           registered as an integration's =:menu= command —
           =workspace-bookmark--add-worktree-runnable-p=, asked of core
           through =workspace--integration-menu-command-p=), or when the
           workspace has no home.
       - view-canonical-read-only: |
           Open =:path= under the canonical checkout in =view-mode= with a
           VISIBLE marker that this is the canonical checkout, not a
           workspace checkout. Read-only-ness is a hard requirement, not a
           courtesy: a lookup must not become an unnoticed edit of main.
           Offered ONLY when =:path= is non-nil (ask 16, accepted). The
           marker is a buffer-local mode-line suffix, applied only in the
           buffer the host LANDED in; it outlives =C-x C-q= and a
           mode-resetting revert (see
           =register/invariant/no-silent-wrong-file-open= residual (e)).
       - cancel: |
           Open nothing and say nothing; end the jump with =quit=
           (cycle-1790453714 — was "signal nothing"): a silent nil return
           would let =bookmark--jump-via= run its display tail on the
           originating buffer, and a =user-error= would be caught as a
           decline by every =(error ...)= barrier. C-g at the prompt is the
           same answer. Always offered.

   error_mapping: |
     No error raised by a discovery helper may propagate into the bookmark
     machinery. Mapping is by what is known at the point of failure:
       identity known    -> no-clone
       identity unknown  -> non-portable
     A jump must not break because git misbehaved; this code runs inside a
     core Emacs interaction on behalf of a peripheral feature. Verified by
     probe and by portable-bookmarks-resolver-spec.el § "Resolution with
     discovery mocked" (:550-685); =quit= propagates by design (C-g is not
     an error).

   canonical_mapping_function: |
     ;; SPECULATED cycle-1790604160, owner outcome-action-mapping (eoc-1790453714-05 §1).
     ;; The ONE site that maps an outcome to the seam's plan. Any other
     ;; pcase / cond over the six members outside --decline-message and
     ;; --after-add-message (which read the constant) is a duplication bug.
     ;; Matrix (rows = outcome, columns = context):
     ;;   in-workspace           none (no workspace)     after-add
     ;; non-portable   (call-through . ORIGINAL)  decline            decline (after-add report)
     ;; resolved       --plan-to-open call-through  decline          --plan-to-open call-through
     ;; missing-repo   --offer-missing-repo     --offer-missing-repo   decline (after-add report)
     ;; not-on-branch  decline (--decline-message)  decline          decline (after-add report)
     ;; ambiguous      decline                  decline                decline
     ;; no-clone       decline                  decline                decline
     ;; anything else  SIGNAL                   SIGNAL                 SIGNAL
     ;; (call-through . ORIGINAL) is produced ONLY by (non-portable, in-workspace).
     (defconst workspace-bookmark--declining-outcomes
       '(not-on-branch ambiguous missing-repo no-clone))
     (defun workspace-bookmark--plan-for-outcome (record resolution context original)
       (pcase (list (plist-get resolution :outcome) context)
         ;; ... one arm per cell above; the default arm SIGNALS ...
         (_ (error "Not a bookmark-resolution outcome: %S" resolution))))

   validator: |
     Structural (no scaffolding in this project):
     1. A defconst =workspace-bookmark--valid-outcomes= exists listing
        exactly the SIX members (portable-bookmarks.el:1597-1598);
        portable-bookmarks-resolver-spec.el § "Every outcome the resolver
        returned in this file" (:794-815) asserts every plist returned
        across the file is in the set, has six keys, and that every member
        is covered.
     2. grep -n 'find-file\|switch-to-buffer\|completing-read\|message\|display-buffer\|read-'
        over portable-bookmarks.el:1597-1813 (the resolver section) -> zero
        hits (verified at f860103c); resolver-spec.el:69-139 traps the
        interaction functions (producer discipline).
     3. At least one spec per member; the ordering asserted where two
        predicates hold at once (resolver-spec "wins over a canonical
        checkout in view (first match wins)", :202); ambiguous asserted from
        BOTH the workspace and the canonical side (:286, :298, :745, :759).
     4. =workspace-bookmark--resolve-anchored='s default arm signals
        (portable-bookmarks.el:1723); no arm returns a location it did not
        anchor.
     5. (cycle-1790453714) Every consumer dispatch listed under =consumers=
        has either a SIGNALLING default (=--jump-plan=, =--decline-message=)
        or a non-opening DECLINE default (=--no-workspace-plan=); none has a
        default that calls through. The nested set's dispatch (:714-721)
        signals inside the chooser barrier, i.e. declines.
     6. (cycle-1790604160, SPECULATED; owner outcome-action-mapping) Exactly ONE
        function dispatches an outcome to a plan: grep -n "pcase (plist-get"
        config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el over the seam / missing-repo /
        off-workspace sections -> the mapping function only (plus
        =--decline-message= and =--after-add-message=, which read
        =workspace-bookmark--declining-outcomes= instead of re-listing the
        members); and a structural spec iterates =workspace-bookmark--valid-
        outcomes= x {in-workspace, none, after-add} and asserts a known plan
        variant per cell, with (call-through . ORIGINAL) only for
        (non-portable, in-workspace).

   test_corpus:
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-resolver-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-missing-repo-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-jump-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-off-workspace-spec.el

   related_entries:
     - register/vocabulary/checkout-classification
     - register/invariant/no-silent-wrong-file-open
     - register/shape/portable-bookmark-coordinates
     - register/vocabulary/buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-integration-outcome

   cited_by_tasks:
     - resolver-outcomes
     - missing-repo-choice
     - docs-readme
     - jump-seam                                   # cycle-1790453714: dispatches on the six members
     - off-workspace-restore                       # cycle-1790453714: resolves after restore
     - discovery-cleanup-singulars-and-docstrings  # cycle-1790453714: anchoring docstrings
     - disposition-spec-design-amendments          # cycle-1790453714: spec text to six members
     - add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate         # cycle-1790604160: the add-worktree member's withheld reasons
     - outcome-action-mapping                     # cycle-1790604160: canonical_mapping_function + validator 6
   discovered_from: workspace-portable-bookmarks
   discovered_by: architect
   reconciled_in: cycle-1789935943
   #+end_src

** checkout-classification
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-vocabulary-checkout-classification
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/vocabulary/checkout-classification
   tier: vocabulary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): RECONCILED.
     The speculated question is answered: the two canonical signals DO
     disagree in practice — for bare, bare-in-.git, --separate-git-dir
     (non-.git name) and submodules (each -> nil); they agree and are BOTH
     WRONG for core.worktree (caught by check 3) and for the two .git-named
     husks (caught by check 4). Ownership reconciled with DD-11 (symlinks
     confer nothing), ask 10 (a contained home owns), DD-13 (except a
     same-repository worktree home; nested worktree homes confer nothing),
     and "existing homes only" (165d44b6). The git-worktrees
     --classify-alone route is CLOSED: one admission predicate
     (canonical-predicate-consolidation, e433c5c9). Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1789935943/reconciliations/vocabulary-checkout-classification.md

     cycle-1789935943 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     populated from specs/portable-bookmarks/spec.md § "Checkout
     classification and canonical-checkout safety" + design D6 + task
     =checkout-discovery= (step 3). SPECULATED on whether the two canonical
     signals (=--git-common-dir= and =.git=-is-a-directory) can actually
     disagree in practice, and on what the classifier does when they do.

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): STAYS RECONCILED. The three members, the
     precedence, the four canonical checks and the ONE admission predicate
     are unchanged in behaviour (no classification or resolution outcome
     changed this cycle; the discovery specs' expected classifications are
     unchanged — their edits were fixture routing, identity assertions
     spelled with the core predicate, and helper specs re-homed). What
     moved: (1) the identity and containment predicates
     the workspace-owned test uses are now CORE — =workspace--same-file-p= /
     =workspace--path-within-p= (data-model.el:264, :288) — and the module
     copies are deleted (workspaces-file-equal-p-timestamp-race,
     cycle2-review-cleanups); (2) the canonical test is answered from a
     per-call discovery memo (position, listing and tracked-file check shared
     within a call; the top-level check answered from DIR's own position
     when it is the candidate) with the same answers; (3) the git-worktrees
     source picker is no longer the only non-resolver consumer of an
     admitted checkout: the missing-repo chooser's "add worktree here" hands
     the resolver's already-admitted =:checkout= to git-worktrees as
     =:source-repo=, and git-worktrees makes the worktree from it with NO
     free-form source and no second admission test — so the free-form floor
     (=jf/workspace--worktree-read-repo=) is unreachable from a bookmark
     prompt (arch-on-touch-1790453714-31 CLOSED). Anchors re-based. Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/vocabulary-checkout-classification.md
   load_bearing: true
   title: Checkout classification (canonical / workspace-owned / other) and the canonical-safety rule
   purpose: |
     Every checkout this capability discovers is classified into exactly one
     of three kinds, and ONE of them may be offered as "the canonical
     checkout". The rule is negative and it is the point of the entry:

       A workspace-owned worktree is NEVER canonical.
       A linked worktree owned by no workspace is NEVER canonical.

     Without this rule the =missing-repo= "view read-only" action becomes a
     lottery: it could land in ANOTHER workspace's checkout — opening one
     workspace's files while the user is in a different one, which is
     verbatim the defect this whole capability exists to remove. It could
     equally land in a stale feature branch and show content from neither.

     Concrete stakes on this machine: the =emacs= repository has SEVEN live
     checkouts — =~/emacs= (main), =~/.emacs.d= (locked production, a LINKED
     worktree whose =.git= is a 54-byte file), three legacy =~/emacs-*=
     feature worktrees, and two workspace-owned worktrees under
     =~/emacs-workspaces/=. Six of the seven must be refused as canonical.

   members:
     - value: canonical
       description: |
         STRUCTURAL ONLY: =.git= is a directory and no workspace owns it
         (=workspace-bookmark--classify=, portable-bookmarks.el:1392,
         :1404-1413). Necessary, NOT sufficient, for an offer (see
         canonical_safety_rule). The main-worktree query is
         =workspace-bookmark--canonical-checkout= (portable-bookmarks.el:1300),
         FOUR checks all required:
           (1) =rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir= is an
               absolute directory literally named =.git=; the candidate is
               its parent;
           (2) the FIRST =worktree list --porcelain= entry is non-bare and
               =--same-file-p= to it;
           (3) =rev-parse --show-toplevel= run IN the candidate names it
               (core.worktree);
           (4) at least one =ls-files -z= path exists under it, skipping
               OS/sync-client artifacts (=workspace-bookmark--os-artifact-names=,
               case-insensitive; =._*=).
         Disagreement between the signals yields nil, deliberately:
         submodule and --separate-git-dir checkouts are never canonical.
         Residuals: a husk holding a stale copy of the tree, or a tracked
         file under an unlisted artifact name written by another program.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--classify   # structural test; the query is workspace-bookmark--canonical-checkout
     - value: workspace-owned
       description: |
         A registered, EXISTING home IS the checkout, contains it, or lies
         inside it — by FILE IDENTITY (core =workspace--path-within-p=,
         data-model.el:288, walks truenamed ancestors comparing device+inode
         through core =workspace--same-file-p=, :264; moved from the module
         cycle-1790453714) — EXCEPT that a contained home that is itself a
         worktree of the checkout's own repository
         (=workspace-bookmark--same-repository-worktree-p=,
         portable-bookmarks.el:1352, decided by
         git: 1-2 subprocesses per contained home) confers nothing (DD-13).
         Symbolic links confer nothing in either direction (DD-11). Tested
         FIRST (precedence). Never =string-prefix-p=, =file-in-directory-p=
         or =file-equal-p= (the last compares timestamps and fails open
         under a concurrent writer; validator 2).
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--classify   # via workspace-bookmark--owning-workspace, portable-bookmarks.el:1374
     - value: other
       description: |
         Everything else — a linked worktree no workspace owns (the legacy
         =~/emacs-*= checkouts), a remote or non-string input, any error.
         Usable as a discovery source; never canonical.
       producer:
         file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
         function: workspace-bookmark--classify

   precedence: |
     Ownership before structure; required by the negative rule, not chosen.
     A full clone inside a registered home is a main worktree by git's
     account and MUST classify workspace-owned, never canonical
     (disc-checkout-discovery-1).

   canonical_safety_rule: |
     =workspace-bookmark--offerable-canonical-p= (portable-bookmarks.el:1485)
     is the ONLY admission test: DIR classifies =canonical= AND is
     =workspace--same-file-p= to =--canonical-checkout= of DIR (:1494-1497).
     Each half is necessary, not sufficient (classify alone admits husks /
     core.worktree / bare-in-.git; =--canonical-checkout= alone admits a
     workspace's full clone). Every offer path goes through
     =workspace-bookmark--canonical-checkouts-among= (:1506): the plural
     =--canonical-checkouts-for-identity= (:1525), the public
     =workspace-bookmark-canonical-checkouts-in-view= (:1550), and through it
     both git-worktrees sources. A checkout admitted there may be HANDED ON
     without a second test: the resolver's =missing-repo= =:checkout= goes to
     git-worktrees as =:source-repo= (cycle-1790453714), which then asks no
     source and runs no discovery. If no main worktree passes, the correct
     answer is NO canonical checkout (which the resolver maps to
     =no-clone=) — never "the closest thing in view".

   ambiguity_rule: |
     Two or more DISTINCT canonical checkouts of one identity are ambiguous
     (ask 11): =--canonical-checkouts-for-identity= returns all of them,
     the current workspace's first then registry order; the resolver maps
     that to =ambiguous=. Two worktrees of ONE repository are not ambiguous
     (they share a main worktree).

   known_limitations: |
     - A clone linked into a home, or holding a link to a home, is not owned
       (DD-11); a main clone symlinked into a home may therefore be offered
       to other workspaces as canonical. Add a worktree; do not link a clone in.
     - A repository at $HOME with a real ~/.git contains every home under
       ~/emacs-workspaces, so it is always owned (by the first registered
       workspace) and never offered; it owns nothing else.
     - A home under an untracked/ignored subdirectory of a clone owns that
       clone (the decision as worded, ask 10).
     - Ownership by tracked content, and anchored repos in the picker:
       deferred (.tasks/portable-bookmarks-ownership-refinements-after-use.md).

   validator: |
     Structural (no scaffolding in this project):
     1. discovery-spec builds owned + stale worktrees; neither is returned
        (portable-bookmarks-discovery-spec.el "never lists the
        workspace-owned or the stale worktree", :243; "never returns a
        checkout in view, owned or linked", :1181).
     2. grep 'string-prefix-p\|file-in-directory-p\|file-equal-p' over
        =workspace--path-within-p= (data-model.el) and
        =--owning-workspace= / =--classify= (portable-bookmarks.el) -> zero
        code hits (data-model.el's two rejecting docstrings excepted);
        discovery-spec spies =file-in-directory-p= and asserts it is never
        called (:1027-1032).
     3. .git-is-a-file classifies non-canonical (the ~/.emacs.d shape;
        discovery-spec.el:188).
     4. Specs pin nil for the --separate-git-dir husk, the symlinked-.git
        husk, a husk with an untracked file, and a husk with a TRACKED
        .DS_Store (discovery-spec.el:370-478).
     5. Case-flipped home spelling still owns (:508); anchored-at-main never
        offered (:548).
     6. grep "workspace-bookmark--canonical-checkout\b\|(workspace-bookmark--classify"
        over config/workspaces/*.el finds calls only inside
        --offerable-canonical-p (verified at f860103c:
        portable-bookmarks.el:1495, :1497).
     7. A nested same-repo worktree home leaves main canonical (:1296); a
        home that no longer exists owns nothing (:1367).

   test_corpus:
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-discovery-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/git-worktrees-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-missing-repo-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/file-identity-spec.el

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery
     - register/vocabulary/bookmark-resolution-outcome
     - register/invariant/no-silent-wrong-file-open
     - register/invariant/registry-name-equals-basename
     - register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer

   cited_by_tasks:
     - checkout-discovery
     - missing-repo-choice
     - wave3-review-fixes
     - discovery-refinements
     - worktree-candidates-git-first
     - canonical-predicate-consolidation
     - ownership-and-picker-polish
   discovered_from: workspace-portable-bookmarks
   discovered_by: architect
   reconciled_in: cycle-1789935943
   #+end_src

* Boundary

  Entries describe canonical mapping functions between subsystems. The
  motivating failure is a reshape (module A's output → module B's input)
  inlined at >1 site instead of going through a single canonical function;
  the inline copies drift over time.

** session-content-signature
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-session-content-signature
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/session-content-signature
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781448273 integrate (2026-06-14): speculated → reconciled.
     Task drawer-signature-and-head-read (merge 15f76fb) implemented the
     two functions, both delegating to a private engine
     jf/gptel--scan-session-drawer-keys (a third symbol, not in the
     speculation) that GUARANTEES predicate == head-read recognition.
     why_tests_missed: the speculation left the head-read return shape
     unspecified; implementation pinned it. CONCRETE SHAPE (now
     authoritative): jf/gptel--read-session-drawer-head returns an ALIST
     keyed by the bare key string (e.g. ("GPTEL_SESSION_ID" . "<id>")),
     string values (trimmed), nil when no point-min drawer or the file is
     unreadable; the predicate is (and (jf/gptel--scan-session-drawer-keys) t).
     The scanner binds case-fold-search nil (review F1) so recognition is
     case-sensitive on the uppercase tokens. Reviewer confirmed the
     menu.org parsing-shape reuse (org-independent; safe at magic-mode-alist
     time). Byte-cap structured but not applied (uncapped read correct
     today; drawer is always at file head).
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781448273/reconciliations/boundary-session-content-signature.md.
   load_bearing: true
   canonical_engine: jf/gptel--scan-session-drawer-keys (config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org)
   title: Content recognition of a gptel session by its point-min drawer
   purpose: |
     The single recognition primitive shared by content-addressed
     ACTIVATION (magic-mode-alist, register/boundary/chat-mode-session-
     display) and offline DISCOVERY (init-registry). It answers one
     question — "is this buffer/file a gptel session?" — by content, not
     by path. Two functions form the boundary: a buffer predicate
     (jf/gptel--session-signature-p, used by magic-mode-alist and the
     mode-hook binder guard) and an on-disk head-read
     (jf/gptel--read-session-drawer-head, used by discovery) that runs
     the same drawer scan over a temp buffer. Reuses the native,
     org-independent drawer-parsing shape proven in
     gptel-chat--declared-preset (config/gptel/chat/menu.org:152-173) so
     it is safe to run at magic-mode-alist time before org is loaded.
   boundary_contract: |
     RECOGNIZES a buffer/file as a gptel session iff, scanning only the
     head: the first non-blank content is a ":PROPERTIES:" line, and
     before the drawer's ":END:" there is at least one line whose key
     matches "^[ \t]*:GPTEL_[A-Z0-9_]+:". Returns nil (never signals) on
     a non-org or plain-text buffer. The head-read variant additionally
     returns an alist of the :GPTEL_* keys found (at least
     GPTEL_SESSION_ID, GPTEL_BRANCH, GPTEL_PARENT_SESSION_ID,
     GPTEL_PRESET) for the resolvers to consume.
   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
       function: jf/gptel--session-signature-p
       note: buffer predicate (current buffer)
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
       function: jf/gptel--read-session-drawer-head
       note: on-disk head-read -> drawer alist
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/mode.org
       function: magic-mode-alist registration
       usage: select gptel-chat-mode by content
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/commands.org
       function: jf/gptel--bind-session-buffer
       usage: guard (no-op without a signature)
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/registry.org
       function: jf/gptel--init-registry
       usage: identity head-read per located session.org
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
       function: discovery callers (:209, :291)
       usage: identity head-read instead of basename
   anchored_by: register/invariant/signature-anchored-to-point-min-drawer
   related_entries:
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
     - register/vocabulary/identity-drawer-keys
     - register/boundary/chat-mode-session-display
   discovered_from: gptel-content-addressed-session-activation
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** drawer-first-identity-resolution
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-drawer-first-identity-resolution
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/drawer-first-identity-resolution
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781468009 integrate (2026-06-14): CONFIRMED (unchanged) + MINT-TIME
     framing note. consumer-migration (merged 4043ec7) adopted the resolver at
     two new call sites: branching.org reads the parent branch's drawer and calls
     jf/gptel--resolve-session-id drawer-first (move-safe), and persistent-agent.org
     calls (jf/gptel--resolve-session-id nil session-dir) at AGENT-CREATION MINT
     time — before the agent's session.org (and thus its drawer) exists. The nil
     DRAWER-ALIST is contract-correct: the resolver's documented "(cdr (assoc KEY
     nil)) => nil => basename fallback" handles it, so the agent's directory
     basename is the canonical id at creation. AMENDED CONTRACT FRAMING: this
     boundary covers not only READ-of-existing-session resolution but also
     MINT-TIME identity sourcing (called with a nil drawer when no session.org
     exists yet); resolve-session-id is the SINGLE sanctioned identity seam in both
     cases, so no caller derives identity from the path outside it. (on-touch
     architect arch-cycle-1781468009-ontouch-2 + implementor disc-consumer-migration-3;
     advisory interface-drift = register-wording hygiene, not a code defect.)
     ----
     cycle-1781451784 integrate (2026-06-14): speculated -> CONFIRMED. The
     drawer-identity-resolver task (merged 1ec479f) built the three resolvers
     (jf/gptel--resolve-session-id / --resolve-branch-name / --session-type)
     and the boundary_contract held 1:1 — independently confirmed by the
     implementor push-back, the author-blind reviewer (clean), and the
     on-touch architect (clean, no scan-engine duplication). Resolver shape
     the code wanted: per-resolver path args (session-id takes session-dir;
     branch takes any branches/<branch>/-containing path; type takes the
     drawer-alist alone). Drawer value wins; the single targeted
     "/branches/\\([^/]+\\)/" regex (NOT the retired 3-layout matcher) and
     basename are consulted only on the fallback branch; legacy no-key drawers
     fall back correctly (beta/no-migration grace, asserted). NOTE: the three
     CONSUMERS (bind-session-buffer, init-registry, discovery callers :209/:291)
     are NOT yet rewired onto the resolvers — that is downstream integration
     (discovery-reads-drawers, mode-hook-binder), classified by the architect
     as planned scope, not drift. The mapping-function contract is confirmed;
     consumer adoption is tracked by those open tasks.
     ----
     cycle-1781448273 integrate (2026-06-14): UNCHANGED (still speculated) —
     producer side only; resolvers not yet built.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Drawer-first, basename-fallback resolution of session identity
   purpose: |
     The canonical mapping from "a session's drawer (+ its file/dir path
     for fallback)" to its resolved identity: session-id, branch-name,
     and type. Drawer value wins; absent => fall back to the legacy
     path-derived value. This is the seam that lets new (self-describing)
     sessions be move-safe while legacy sessions keep working with zero
     rewrite — the no-migration grace path the beta constraint requires.
     jf/gptel--session-id-from-directory is retained but DEMOTED to the
     fallback branch only.
   boundary_contract: |
     resolve-session-id: drawer GPTEL_SESSION_ID, else
       (jf/gptel--session-id-from-directory <session-dir>).
     resolve-branch-name: drawer GPTEL_BRANCH, else the enclosing
       branches/<branch>/ path segment (single targeted regex, NOT the
       retired 3-layout matcher). CALLER OBLIGATION (cycle-3): the fallback
       regex "/branches/\\([^/]+\\)/" requires a TRAILING separator after the
       branch segment, so callers must pass a path that includes the trailing
       branches/<branch>/ segment — e.g. the session.org FILE path
       (.../branches/<branch>/session.org), NOT a bare branch-dir
       (.../branches/<branch>, no trailing slash) which would yield nil on the
       fallback branch. All current consumers (init-registry, discovery
       callers) honor this by passing the session-file path.
     session-type: per register/vocabulary/identity-drawer-keys
       (GPTEL_PARENT_SESSION_ID present => agent; absent => branch).
     The directory basename/segment is consulted ONLY on the fallback
     branch; when the drawer carries the key, the path is never read.
   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
       function: jf/gptel--resolve-session-id
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
       function: jf/gptel--resolve-branch-name
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
       function: jf/gptel--session-type
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/commands.org
       function: jf/gptel--bind-session-buffer
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/registry.org
       function: jf/gptel--init-registry
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
       function: discovery callers (:209, :291)
   fallback_function:
     file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
     function: jf/gptel--session-id-from-directory
     status: retained-as-fallback-only
   related_entries:
     - register/vocabulary/identity-drawer-keys
     - register/boundary/session-content-signature
     - register/invariant/activation-and-identity-are-content-not-path
   discovered_from: gptel-content-addressed-session-activation
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** session-dir-marker-walk
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-session-dir-marker-walk
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/session-dir-marker-walk
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781453946 integrate (2026-06-14): speculated -> CONFIRMED. The
     session-dir-ancestor-walk task (merged df3dcf2) built
     jf/gptel--session-dir-from-branch-dir and the boundary_contract held 1:1
     — independently confirmed by the implementor push-back ("held 1:1"), the
     author-blind reviewer (1 test-robustness advisory only, inline-fixed
     3efea01), the on-touch path (n/a, load_bearing:false) and the end-of-cycle
     architect (clean; contract matched merged code exactly). Implemented via
     locate-dominating-file with a predicate combining file-directory-p AND
     string-prefix-p (the enclosure check is load-bearing: rejects a stray
     branches/ ancestor that does not enclose the buffer); fully
     depth-independent (proven by a deeper/relocated-layout spec). PRODUCER
     SIGNATURE PINNED: (branch-dir type) where `type` is the symbol returned by
     jf/gptel--session-type (`branch'|`agent') — the function takes the
     resolved TYPE symbol, not a drawer-alist, keeping it a pure structural
     walk decoupled from drawer parsing. CONSUMER (bind-session-buffer) is the
     downstream mode-hook-binder task — planned adoption, not drift. Return
     value is NOT truename-normalized (locate-dominating-file derives from the
     expanded branch-dir); consumers must compare paths semantically
     (file-equal-p), as the spec does — recorded as disc-session-dir-ancestor-walk-2.
   load_bearing: false
   title: session-dir derived by ancestor-marker walk, not fixed ../.. count
   purpose: |
     After the `current` symlink retires (register entry not needed; see
     change D6), session-dir's only remaining job is "the directory under
     which branches/ lives". This boundary computes that as its literal
     definition via an ancestor-marker walk, replacing the retired fixed
     ../.. / ../../.. layout walks. Move-safe; stores nothing. Finding a
     CONTAINER by structural marker is legitimate navigation — distinct
     from the rejected practice of parsing IDENTITY out of path segments.
   boundary_contract: |
     jf/gptel--session-dir-from-branch-dir, given branch-dir (the file's
     own directory) and session type:
       branch: nearest ancestor D of branch-dir such that
         (file-directory-p (expand-file-name "branches" D)) and branch-dir
         is under D/branches/ — via locate-dominating-file or an explicit
         parent loop; depth-independent (no fixed ../.. count). Return D.
       agent: return branch-dir itself (agents do not branch).
     Robust when no branches/ ancestor exists (corrupt/standalone):
     return branch-dir and log at debug.
   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
       function: jf/gptel--session-dir-from-branch-dir
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/commands.org
       function: jf/gptel--bind-session-buffer
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/drawer-first-identity-resolution
     - register/invariant/activation-and-identity-are-content-not-path
   discovered_from: gptel-content-addressed-session-activation
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** persistence-robustness-io
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-persistence-robustness-io
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/persistence-robustness-io
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260613-161149 integrate (2026-06-13, task corruption-safe-
     persistence-io): created CONFIRMED from the merged, clean-reviewed
     shape (designed-change mid-stream adoption; entry created at
     integrate). Captures the corruption-safety contract for the
     workspaces persistence file.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Corruption-safe persistence I/O (atomic write, never-clobber-unreadable, absent-vs-unreadable, autosave gate)
   purpose: |
     The boundary between the in-memory registry and the on-disk
     =workspaces.eld=. Reads and writes MUST NOT lose data: an
     interrupted write must not truncate the live file, and a file that
     cannot be parsed must never be silently overwritten with empty/
     partial state.
   contract: |
     Four coupled rules, all enforced in config/workspaces/persistence.el:

     1. ATOMIC WRITE — =workspace--write-state= serializes to a temp file
        in the state directory and =rename-file='s it over the target.
        A crash mid-write cannot truncate the existing file.

     2. WRITE-TIME READABLE ASSERT — before committing, the form is
        round-tripped through =read= (=workspace--state-readable-p=,
        via =prin1-to-string= → =read=; =readablep= not used because the
        repo pins no minimum Emacs version). On failure the write is
        REFUSED (prior good file left intact) with a loud warning. This
        is the backstop for any unreadable value that slips past the
        serializer translators (register/invariant/persisted-form-
        readable-by-construction).

     3. ABSENT vs PRESENT-BUT-UNREADABLE — =workspace--read-state=
        returns nil for a MISSING file (fresh start, writes permitted)
        and the sentinel symbol =workspace--unreadable= for an existing
        file that fails =read=. On the latter it renames the file to
        =workspaces.eld.corrupt-<ts>=, warns naming both paths, and sets
        the session flag below. =workspace--restore= never deserializes
        the sentinel. (Version-mismatch still → nil: a recognized
        ignore-and-start-fresh, NOT corruption — not backed up, not
        blocked.)

     4. ONE-WAY AUTOSAVE GATE — =workspace--persistence-blocked= (session
        flag, set by rule 3) suppresses EVERY writer for the session:
        =workspace--write-state= (choke point), with explicit
        early-returns in =workspace--kill-emacs-flush= and
        =workspace-save-state=. It does not auto-clear within a session
        (the in-memory registry after a failed load is untrustworthy).
   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--write-state
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--read-state
   consumers:
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--restore
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--kill-emacs-flush
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace-save-state
   enforcement_mechanism: test
   test_corpus: config/workspaces/test/serialization-robustness-spec.el (Layer B specs + e2e clobber-cascade regression)
   discovered_from: corruption-safe-persistence-io
   discovered_by: implementor
   relates_to:
     - register/boundary/autosave-guard-pipeline (the autosave gate is an additive precondition on stage-4 flush)
     - register/invariant/persisted-form-readable-by-construction (rule 2 is the write-side backstop for that invariant)
   #+end_src

** scope-config-loader
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-scope-config-loader
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/scope-config-loader
   tier: boundary
   status: divergent
   status_note: |
     cycle-3 disposition-empty-drawer-collapse (2026-04-29): Option B
     applied at the code layer. Stage 3 of --load-config now composes
     deny-all defaults (empty :paths sub-lists, "deny" cloud auth) via
     jf/gptel-scope--deny-all-defaults rather than collapsing to nil.
     The dispatcher proceeds straight to validation; per-violation
     deny replaces the historical no_scope_config short-circuit (which
     has been removed from authorize-tool-call and final-deny-response).
     Status remains `divergent` until cycle-3 integrate flips it to
     `reconciled` with prior_shape (Stage 3 collapse) / new_shape
     (Stage 3 deny-all composition) documented. Tests pinned to the
     old semantics will be migrated by the parallel
     `migrate-validation-tests` task.

     Prior cycle-2 integrate note: implementation honored the cycle-1
     reconciliation note's "stage-3 collapse to nil" semantic; cycle-1's
     user-resolved ask-arch-cycle-1777460733-2 said the opposite ("empty
     drawer = deny-all defaults"). Both authorities cannot stand. User
