     disposition via cycle-3 task `disposition-empty-drawer-collapse`.
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1777470320/reconciliations/
     boundary-scope-config-loader.md for the divergence escalation.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Buffer-first / file-fallback drawer reader pipeline
   purpose: |
     The two-stage resolver that produces the scope-config plist for the
     authorize-tool-call dispatcher. Stage 1 (buffer-first) reads the
     :PROPERTIES: drawer of the live chat buffer when one is associated
     with the session. Stage 2 (file-fallback) opens session.org head-
     lessly when no buffer is loaded. Stage 1 is the source-of-truth
     stage; stage 2 exists only for programmatic / out-of-buffer call
     paths (e.g. request_scope_expansion). Stage 3 composes deny-all
     defaults when neither stage produces a plist with :GPTEL_SCOPE_*
     keys, so the dispatcher always sees a usable plist.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: buffer-first read
       input: chat-buffer object | nil (registry's :buffer field for jf/gptel--branch-dir)
       output: register/shape/scope-config-plist | nil
       can_fail_with: [no-buffer-found, drawer-empty]
       short_circuit_policy: optional
       notes: |
         When the registry has a buffer for the current branch-dir,
         stage 1 returns the parsed plist and stages 2 must not run.
         When there is no buffer (registry miss), stage 1 returns nil
         and stage 2 takes over.
     - n: 2
       name: file-fallback read
       input: session.org file path
       output: register/shape/scope-config-plist | nil
       can_fail_with: [file-not-found, drawer-empty, malformed-drawer]
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         Opens the file via with-temp-buffer + insert-file-contents,
         enables org-mode for the temp buffer, runs the same drawer
         reader as stage 1. Discards the temp buffer. The plist shape
         is identical to stage 1's output.
     - n: 3
       name: empty-plist → deny-all defaults composition
       input: register/shape/scope-config-plist | nil (output of stages 1/2)
       output: register/shape/scope-config-plist (always non-nil)
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         When stages 1+2 yield nil OR a plist with no :GPTEL_SCOPE_*
         keys, --load-config returns the deny-all defaults plist
         composed by jf/gptel-scope--deny-all-defaults: empty :paths
         sub-lists (:read, :read-metadata, :write, :modify, :execute,
         :deny) and :cloud (:auth-detection "deny" :allowed-providers
         nil). The authorization dispatcher proceeds to validation;
         per-violation deny is the authorisation outcome. Replaces
         the historical Stage-3 collapse-to-nil → no_scope_config
         semantic (Option B; cycle-3).

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/scope-no-security-key-in-plist
     - The plist shape produced by stage 1 must equal the plist shape
       produced by stage 2 byte-for-byte for the same drawer text. Tested
       by a round-trip spec: write a drawer string to a temp file, read
       it via stage 2; insert it into a temp chat buffer, read it via
       stage 1; assert plist equality.

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--load-config
       enters_at_stage: 1

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope-authorize-tool-call
       reads_output_of_stage: 2

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** scope-profile-applicator
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-scope-profile-applicator
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/scope-profile-applicator
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): Mode 2a (--render-drawer-text) is the
     sole route from a profile-resolved scope-plist to drawer state in both
     consumer paths (sessions/commands.org and tools/persistent-agent.org).
     Branching uses byte-copy from parent (structurally equivalent to Mode
     2a re-render given cross-mode idempotency). See .orchestrator/cycles/
     cycle-1777470320/reconciliations/boundary-scope-profile-applicator.md.
     cycle-5 integrate (2026-05-01): stage 2a's signature gained an
     optional PRESET-SPEC arg; stage 2b (--apply-to-drawer) gained the
     symmetric arg. Both stages emit the chat-mode snapshot keys when
     PRESET-SPEC is non-nil. The chat-mode save path
     (gptel-chat--save-state → gptel-chat--write-config-drawer) is a
     *peer producer* of an equivalent drawer key set from buffer-local
     state — no delta-from-preset deletion. The new cross-stage
     invariant (rendered-at-creation == saved-after-no-edit) is
     exercised by the round-trip idempotency scenario in
     snapshot-rendering-spec.el and the end-to-end scenarios in
     preset-application-spec.el. The two consumer call sites were
     updated to thread the resolved preset spec via gptel-get-preset:
     - jf/gptel--create-session-core (sessions/commands.org)
     - persistent-agent.org's --task flow (direct
       --render-drawer-text call)
     Cited by tasks: extend-render-drawer-text-with-preset-snapshot
     (43d045f), wire-snapshot-into-session-creation (9f4b5fd),
     replace-chat-save-with-full-snapshot-writer (5f9d762).
     cycle-6 integrate (2026-05-03): dedupe extracted a shared
     internal enumeration jf/gptel-scope-profile--snapshot-entries
     (used by both --snapshot-lines and --apply-to-drawer) so the
     two PRESET-SPEC-fed producers can no longer drift on key set
     or ordering. The chat-mode save path's gptel-chat--write-config-
     drawer remains a third (peer) producer of the same six-key set
     from buffer-local state — open follow-up task harden-snapshot-
     emission-cross-stage-parity is queued to either route the chat
     writer through the same enumeration or, at minimum, narrow the
     internal helper's docstring claim. Multivalued-formatter parity
     between mode 2a (string) and mode 2b (buffer) is also covered
     by that follow-up — currently 2a uses bare mapconcat while 2b
     escapes via org-entry-protect-space; the asymmetry is documented
     but not enforced. Cited by task fix-snapshot-tools-test-mock-
     and-dedupe-applicator (c7a1f1c).
   load_bearing: true
   title: Profile resolution → drawer text or buffer-applied properties
   purpose: |
     The two-mode terminal step of the scope-profile pipeline. Mode A
     (string mode, --render-drawer-text) produces a register/shape/
     drawer-text-block string for embedding in a fresh session.org. Mode B
     (buffer mode, --apply-to-drawer) calls org-entry-put against an
     already-open buffer's drawer. Both modes must be the only routes
     from a profile-resolved scope-plist to drawer state; any open-coded
     ":PROPERTIES:" string-building or org-entry-put loop elsewhere is a
     duplication bug.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: profile resolution
       input: preset-name | profile-template-path
       output: register/shape/scope-config-plist
       can_fail_with: [profile-not-found, variable-expansion-failed]
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         Loads the profile YAML template, expands ${project_root} and
         related variables, deep-merges with defaults. Unchanged by this
         change.
     - n: 2a
       name: render-drawer-text (string mode)
       input: preset-name + parent-session-id + scope-plist
       output: register/shape/drawer-text-block
       can_fail_with: [malformed-plist]
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         Returns a string. Caller is responsible for prepending the result
         to chat-mode initial content and calling write-region. No buffer
         is opened during this mode.
     - n: 2b
       name: apply-to-drawer (buffer mode)
       input: chat-buffer + preset-name + parent-session-id + scope-plist
       output: side-effect (drawer mutated; non-scope keys preserved)
       can_fail_with: [buffer-not-org-mode, properties-drawer-corrupted]
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         Calls org-entry-put for scalars and the multi-value helper for
         lists. Existing :GPTEL_PRESET: and :GPTEL_PARENT_SESSION_ID: keys
         are preserved; only :GPTEL_SCOPE_* keys are written.

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/scope-drawer-no-duplication
     - mode 2a's output, if written verbatim to a fresh session.org file,
       produces a drawer that mode 2b's apply pass would leave unchanged
       (idempotency between the two modes for the same scope-plist).

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope-profiles.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope-profile--render-drawer-text
       enters_at_stage: 2a
     - file: config/gptel/scope-profiles.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope-profile--apply-to-drawer
       enters_at_stage: 2b
     - file: config/gptel/scope-profiles.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope-profile--create-for-session
       enters_at_stage: 1
       notes: dispatches to 2a or 2b based on caller context

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/commands.org
       function: jf/gptel-persistent-session
       reads_output_of_stage: 2a
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/activities-integration.org
       function: <activities flow session creation>
       reads_output_of_stage: 2a
     - file: config/gptel/tools/persistent-agent.org
       function: <agent-creation function>
       reads_output_of_stage: 2a

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** scope-expansion-action-handler
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-scope-expansion-action-handler
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/scope-expansion-action-handler
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     New entry, cycle-2 plan (2026-04-29). Surfaced by the cycle-1
     audit's finding-9 (writer's strict-error on nil :operation is
     correct but user-hostile) and finding-10B (smart-redirect for
     :match-pattern). The cycle-1 disposition tasks (refuse-add-to-
     scope-on-nil-operation; disposition-match-pattern-handling)
     point at this layer. Implemented in cycle-3 task
     `harden-add-to-scope-action-handler`.

     cycle-4 (2026-04-29) Ask 1 disposition: Stage 2 was renamed
     to "match-pattern refuse-with-guidance" (its prior cycle-2 name
     held the redirect verb that no longer applies).
     The cycle-2 ask-10B "smart redirect" was discovered to be
     unimplementable in cycle-3-as-shipped because
     `jf/gptel-scope--validate-file-operations` (scope-validation.el:412)
     throws on first denial via `(catch 'error-found ...)`. The action
     handler therefore never receives a full violation cluster from
     which to pick a sibling :read-directory resource. Option B was
     chosen: the handler refuses with a directing user-error ("use c
     for custom pattern, e for manual edit") and points the user at
     the manual-edit affordances. The reify-the-cluster route is
     externalized to
     .tasks/fix-match-pattern-parser-validator-boundary.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Action-layer dispatch for the expansion UI's "add to scope" key
   purpose: |
     The function the expansion UI calls when the user presses `a` on a
     denied violation. It must decide what *kind* of add-to-scope action
     applies before delegating to the writer; the writer's job is the
     drawer mutation, not the user-intent disambiguation. Mis-placing
     intent logic in the writer is the same class of bug as putting
     bash-parser semantic-op routing inside the YAML mutator: it forces
     the writer to know about the action layer's vocabulary, and it
     erases the writer's "single closed mapping" property.

     Three pre-conditions block the writer:
       1. nil :operation (cloud-auth, parse-incomplete violations) →
          refuse the add-to-scope action; surface the right alternative
          (allow-once for metadata; "add provider to allow-list" for
          cloud-auth; "request review" for parse-incomplete).
       2. :match-pattern :operation → redirect to the sibling
          :read-directory operation in the same violation cluster
          (find /home -name '*.txt' emits both :read-directory /home
          and :match-pattern '*.txt'; the user's intent on `a` is to
          scope /home, not '*.txt').
       3. all other valid (:operation . :resource) pairs → delegate
          to register/boundary/scope-pattern-writer.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: nil-operation refuse
       input: violation-info plist
       output: refuse | continue
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: terminating
       notes: |
         When :operation is nil, the action handler displays a
         non-error message naming the right action ("metadata stat —
         use allow-once instead"; "cloud-auth — add provider to allow-
         list"; "parse-incomplete — review needed"). It does not call
         the writer. The writer's strict-error on nil :operation
         remains; this stage prevents that error from surfacing in
         normal use.
     - n: 2
       name: match-pattern refuse-with-guidance
       input: violation-info plist
       output: refuse | continue
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: terminating
       notes: |
         When :operation is :match-pattern, the action handler refuses
         the add-to-scope action with a directing user-error pointing
         the user at the manual-edit affordances ("use c for custom
         pattern, e for manual edit"). It does not call the writer
         and does not attempt a sibling-:read-directory redirect.

         Constraint: `jf/gptel-scope--validate-file-operations`
         (scope-validation.el:412) throws on the first denial via
         `(catch 'error-found ...)`, so by the time the action handler
         is invoked the violation cluster has been narrowed to a
         single violation — the sibling :read-directory operation that
         a `find /home -name '*.txt'` invocation would also produce is
         not present alongside the :match-pattern violation. The
         cycle-2 ask-10B "smart redirect" cannot be honoured without
         parser/validator-side work; that work is externalized to
         .tasks/fix-match-pattern-parser-validator-boundary.md and
         this stage's "refuse with guidance" disposition is the
         cycle-3-as-shipped contract (Ask 1, cycle-4 plan).
     - n: 3
       name: writer delegation
       input: violation-info plist (passed-through or redirected)
       output: side-effect (writer mutates drawer)
       can_fail_with: [unmapped-operation]
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         Calls register/boundary/scope-pattern-writer. By stage 3 the
         input has :operation in the writer's ten-member domain.

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - The action handler is the only producer of writer calls for
       interactive add-to-scope. Tests assert that no other
       expansion-UI callsite calls scope-pattern-writer's entry
       function directly.

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-to-scope (and the other
         interactive add-* dispatchers; harden task will refactor
         to a single entry point)

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--write-pattern-to-drawer (writer)
       reads_output_of_stage: 3

   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/gptel-scope-in-org-properties/scaffolding/boundaries/scope-expansion-action-handler.el

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect (cycle-2 plan, in response to ask-10B and
     finding-9)
   #+end_src

** scope-pattern-writer
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-scope-pattern-writer
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/scope-pattern-writer
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): all five --add-*-to-scope action
     handlers route through --write-pattern-to-drawer; YAML serializer
     family deleted; (require 'jf-gptel-scope-yaml) gone. The boundary's
     "single writer" property holds. See .orchestrator/cycles/
     cycle-1777470320/reconciliations/boundary-scope-pattern-writer.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Single canonical drawer mutator (write-pattern-to-drawer)
   purpose: |
     The expansion UI's "add to scope" action used to write through a
     YAML mutator (parse → modify plist → re-emit YAML → write file).
     That pipeline is replaced by jf/gptel-scope--write-pattern-to-drawer:
     a single function that takes a buffer, an operation keyword, and a
     pattern; collapses the operation to a drawer key via register/
     vocabulary/operation-to-drawer-key; reads the existing values;
     dedups; writes via org-entry-put / multi-value helper; calls save-
     buffer. There is exactly one canonical mutator. Any other site that
     constructs ":GPTEL_SCOPE_<KEY>: <value>" lines, calls org-entry-put
     against scope keys directly, or otherwise mutates drawer scope state
     is a boundary-translation duplication bug.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: operation-to-drawer-key collapse
       input: violation-info plist (reads :operation)
       output: drawer-key string
       can_fail_with: [unmapped-operation]
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         Delegates to register/vocabulary/operation-to-drawer-key's
         canonical mapping function.
     - n: 2
       name: dedup against existing drawer values
       input: chat-buffer + drawer-key + new-pattern
       output: keep | skip
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: deliberate
       notes: |
         If the pattern is already in the drawer's values for the target
         key (case-sensitive equality after path normalization), the
         writer returns :success t :patterns_added [] and does not call
         org-entry-put. The skip is silent — no error, no warning — but
         visible in the callback's empty :patterns_added list.
     - n: 3
       name: org-entry-put / multi-value emit + save-buffer
       input: chat-buffer + drawer-key + new-pattern
       output: side-effect (drawer line(s) added; buffer saved)
       can_fail_with: [save-aborted, buffer-read-only, properties-drawer-corrupted]
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         First addition for a key uses the bare form (org-entry-put);
         subsequent additions use the +: variant via the multi-value
         helper. save-buffer triggers chat-mode's existing before-save-
         hook chain (metadata-yml updater) — this writer must not add new
         hooks.

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/scope-add-pattern-idempotent
     - register/invariant/scope-drawer-no-duplication
     - the writer does not modify any drawer key other than the one
       computed by stage 1 (verified by the regression test in
       add-drawer-corruption-regression.md)

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--write-pattern-to-drawer
       enters_at_stage: 1

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-to-scope
       reads_output_of_stage: 3
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-wildcard-to-scope
       reads_output_of_stage: 3
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-custom-to-scope
       reads_output_of_stage: 3
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-path-to-scope
       reads_output_of_stage: 3
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-bash-to-scope
       reads_output_of_stage: 3

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** expansion-transient-scope
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-shape-expansion-transient-scope
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/shape/expansion-transient-scope
   tier: shape
   status: speculated
   status_note: |
     speculated-with-deviation: the cycle-3 plan brief proposed three
     required keys (:violation :command-name :chat-buffer) plus two
     optional UI-disabled flags. The cycle-2 implementation
     (commit 18e290a, scope-expansion.org §State Management
     `--process-expansion-queue`) actually constructs a five-key plist
     of (:violation :callback :patterns :tool-name :chat-buffer); there
     is no :command-name key and no UI-disabled flags exist. Entry
     records the *as-built* shape; reconciliation will flip status to
     `confirmed` when scaffolding parity is verified by the harden task.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Five-key plist passed as transient-scope to the add-to-scope UI
   purpose: |
     The plist that lives on (transient-scope) for the expansion transient
     menu. Constructed in `jf/gptel-scope--prompt-expansion` (initial
     transient setup) and re-emitted in `jf/gptel-scope--process-expansion-
     queue` (subsequent queue entries; same five keys). Consumers are every
     transient action handler under `* Expansion Actions`
     (`--deny-expansion`, `--add-to-scope`, `--allow-once-action`,
     `--add-wildcard-to-scope`, `--add-custom-to-scope`, `--edit-scope`)
     and the `:description` lambda of `jf/gptel-scope-expansion-menu`.

     The :chat-buffer key was added in cycle-2 (rewire-expansion-writer,
     commit 18e290a) so action handlers can locate the chat buffer whose
     :PROPERTIES: drawer they must mutate when the transient closes.

   required_keys:
     - name: ":violation"
       type: plist (register/shape/violation-info)
       description: |
         The violation-info plist (cycle-1 reconciled shape). Action
         handlers read :validation-type, :resource, :tool, :reason,
         :metadata, :operation off it.
     - name: ":callback"
       type: function (string -> any)
       description: |
         The wrapper-callback closure provided by the gptel tool wrapper
         at violation-detection time. Action handlers funcall it with a
         JSON-serialized result plist (`:success`/`:user_denied`/
         `:patterns_added`/`:allowed_once`/`:message`).
     - name: ":patterns"
       type: list of strings
       description: |
         The patterns that motivated this expansion prompt (the queue
         entry's pre-computed pattern list). `--add-to-scope` echoes
         this list into `:patterns_added` on the success callback.
     - name: ":tool-name"
       type: string
       description: |
         Human-readable tool name for callback messages (e.g.
         "Added 1 pattern(s) to read_file").
     - name: ":chat-buffer"
       type: buffer
       description: |
         The chat buffer that hosted the violation, captured at
         expansion-trigger time. Retrieved via
         `jf/gptel-scope--current-chat-buffer` and used by the drawer
         writer (`--write-pattern-to-drawer`) and the `--edit-scope`
         handler.

   forbidden_keys: []

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--prompt-expansion
       constructs: initial transient-setup :scope plist
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--process-expansion-queue
       constructs: re-emitted plist when popping next queue entry

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope-expansion-menu (:description lambda)
       reads_keys: [":violation"]
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--deny-expansion
       reads_keys: [":callback"]
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-to-scope
       reads_keys: [":violation", ":callback", ":patterns", ":tool-name"]
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--allow-once-action
       reads_keys: [":violation", ":callback"]
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-wildcard-to-scope
       reads_keys: [":violation", ":callback"]
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--add-custom-to-scope
       reads_keys: [":violation", ":callback"]
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org
       function: jf/gptel-scope--current-chat-buffer
       reads_keys: [":chat-buffer"]

   validator: |
     (defun shape/validate-expansion-transient-scope (val)
       "Returns nil on success; an error symbol otherwise."
       (cond ((not (plistp val)) 'not-a-plist)
             ((not (plistp (plist-get val :violation))) 'violation-not-plist)
             ((not (functionp (plist-get val :callback))) 'callback-not-function)
             ((not (listp (plist-get val :patterns))) 'patterns-not-list)
             ((not (stringp (plist-get val :tool-name))) 'tool-name-not-string)
             ((not (and (bufferp (plist-get val :chat-buffer))
                        (buffer-live-p (plist-get val :chat-buffer))))
              'chat-buffer-not-live-buffer)
             (t nil)))

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/violation-info
     - register/boundary/scope-expansion-action-handler
     - register/invariant/expansion-queue-always-progresses

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect (cycle-2 audit, arch-cycle-1777470320-3)
   #+end_src

** chat-menu-scope-default
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-chat-menu-scope-default
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/chat-menu-scope-default
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-5 plan (2026-05-01) — speculation: introduced for the
     gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth change Decision 5.
     cycle-5 integrate (2026-05-01): reconciled (initial wiring). The
     chat-menu prefix body sets gptel--set-buffer-locally to t before
     transient-setup, after saving the prior value into gptel-chat--
     scope-prior. cycle-5 verification was nine specs that called the
     restore hook directly, bypassing the real transient call path.
     cycle-6 reviewer (2026-05-03) found the exit_invariant violated
     in production: the original mechanism — a transient-exit-hook
     guarded by (unless transient-current-prefix ...) — never fired
     on commit-style exit. transient-exit-hook runs *before*
     transient-current-prefix is cleared (transient.el:3072), and
     transient--export sets that variable for every committing suffix,
     so the guard always failed and gptel--set-buffer-locally was left
     stuck t globally. cycle-6 reconciliation (582b70e): switched to
     transient-post-exit-hook (which fires only after the transient is
     fully done and transient-current-prefix has been cleared);
     dropped the now-unnecessary discriminator. Verification rebuilt
     against the real call path: end-to-end specs that invoke
     transient--post-exit with command set to a known committing
     suffix and assert gptel--set-buffer-locally restores correctly,
     and parallel coverage for the abort path. The exit_invariant now
     holds "regardless of how the transient ends" without bespoke
     sub-transient discrimination. Cited by tasks default-chat-menu-
     scope-to-buffer-local (9485f3b) and fix-chat-menu-scope-restore-
     on-real-exit (582b70e).
     cycle-8 integrate (2026-05-23): architectural axiom appended
     per the cycle-6→cycle-8 resolution of ask-cycle-1777625426-2.
     Presets carry global defaults; the chat menu's per-infix scope
     is buffer-local by design because "this conversation" is the
     menu's semantic scope. Decision 5 is design-correct, not a
     defensible default. A future maintainer who reads this entry
     and wonders whether to "fix" the chat menu to inherit the
     preset's global scope should resist: the global/buffer-local
     split between presets and the chat menu is the WYSIWYG
     contract, not an accident. If a user wants chat-menu changes
     to land globally, that is a separate explicit gesture (e.g. an
     infix to commit-to-preset), not the menu's default.
   load_bearing: true
   title: gptel-chat-menu enters with gptel--set-buffer-locally bound to t

   purpose: |
     The configuration scope of gptel-chat-menu (chat-mode's transient
     wrapper around upstream gptel-menu) defaults to *buffer-local*, not
     *global*. Tool / model / temperature / etc. selections from the chat
     menu apply to the current chat-mode buffer, then serialise to the
     drawer on save. Upstream gptel-menu (invoked outside chat-mode) is
     unaffected.

   scope:
     in_scope:
       - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
         transient: gptel-chat-menu
         entry_point: setup hook (or :setup) on the transient
     out_of_scope:
       - upstream gptel-menu (defaults preserved at gptel--set-buffer-locally = nil)
       - any non-chat-mode buffer

   contract:
     entry_invariant: |
       From the moment the user invokes gptel-chat-menu until the
       transient finishes (commit, abort, or quit), gptel--set-buffer-
       locally is bound to t. Bindings are restored on transient teardown
       via unwind-protect or transient's :teardown hook.
     exit_invariant: |
       After the transient ends (regardless of how), gptel--set-buffer-
       locally returns to whatever value it had at entry — typically nil
       (upstream default) but possibly a user customisation.

   why_it_matters: |
     Without this default, "Select tools" from the chat-mode menu mutates
     the global gptel-tools and kills the buffer-local binding installed
     by the preset. The drawer save then sees the global value (or no
     value, depending on order). Invisible failure mode — user thinks
     they're editing the buffer, they're editing globally. Direct cause
     of the WYSIWYG-defeating behaviour the parent change addresses.

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: gptel-chat-menu (transient definition)

   consumers:
     - upstream gptel infixes that read gptel--set-buffer-locally:
       gptel-tools, gptel-system-prompt, gptel--infix-provider,
       gptel--infix-temperature, gptel--infix-max-tokens, gptel--infix-
       num-messages-to-send

   related_entries:
     - register/invariant/drawer-overlay-wins-over-preset
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block

   discovered_from: gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** chat-mode-session-display
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-chat-mode-session-display
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/chat-mode-session-display
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-7 plan (2026-05-22) — speculated by forward-mode against
     design.md §Addendum (Finding A / Decision A, Finding C /
     Decision C). Captures the seam where gptel-chat-mode overrides
     org-mode's generic presentation for session buffers.
     cycle-7 integrate (2026-05-22): reconciled. Override A landed via
     a buffer-local font-lock OVERRIDE keyword in mode.org/el (task
     fix-scope-drawer-value-emphasis, 4694625 / 7f290c4) with regression
     spec at config/gptel/chat/test/display/drawer-fontification-spec.el.
     Override C landed via the gptel-chat-mode-hook fold step (task
     fold-config-drawer-on-open, e5f57b3 / 5c6b490) with regression spec
     at config/gptel/chat/test/display/drawer-fold-spec.el. The
     implementor of fix-scope-drawer-value-emphasis rewrote the
     scaffold's override-A 're-stamps' assertion to inspect installed
     font-lock-keyword structure rather than environment-fragile
     plain-org-vs-chat-mode face contrast — a documented deviation
     toward implementation-pinning rather than observable-face contract.
     Reviewer flagged advisory; accepted with the trade-off recorded.
     Scaffold archived in favour of the co-located regression specs.
     See cycle-1779477564/reconciliations/boundary-chat-mode-session-
     display.md.
     cycle-8 integrate (2026-05-23): narrative update — the
     cycle-7 reviewer-advisory about the implementation-pinning
     scaffold proved exactly as costly as flagged. A user-testing
     finding 2026-05-23 surfaced cross-line emphasis spans pairing
     `/` delimiters across drawer lines (READ value opens with `/**`,
     DENY value closes with `**/.git/**`), italicising the
     intermediate KEY text and hiding the boundary slashes under
     `org-hide-emphasis-markers`. Task suppress-cross-line-emphasis-
     inside-config-drawer (merge_commit 8d619db) replaced the
     per-line `gptel-chat--drawer-value-matcher` with a span-level
     `gptel-chat--drawer-span-matcher` covering the entire
     `:PROPERTIES: ... :END:` interior (group 0), with an OVERRIDE
     facespec setting `(face org-property-value invisible nil)`. The
     forced `invisible nil` defeats `org-hide-emphasis-markers`;
     the span scope captures cross-line italic spans before org's
     emphasis keyword fires. Override A's rule and mechanism prose
     below were tightened (cycle-8 integrate) to name the broader
     contract — the override now stamps the drawer *interior* (keys
     + values + interior whitespace) rather than only *values*, and
     the invisible-property extension is explicit. The new
     regression spec is observable-contract end-to-end (face,
     italic, invisible text-property at specific positions) — the
     opposite of the cycle-7 implementation-pinning pattern.
     Co-located scaffold (boundaries/chat-mode-session-display.el)
     deleted inline by commit 73a1b73; scaffolding_path field
     nulled below. A latent overlay-vs-text-property coexistence
     with override-C is now documented in the mode.org prose:
     override-A relies on `org-fold-core-style` defaulting to
     `overlays` so the fold and the invisible-property override
     occupy non-conflicting channels — flipping to text-properties
     style would silently break override-C on every font-lock pass.
     See cycle-1779522837/reconciliations/boundary-chat-mode-
     session-display.md.
     cycle-8 hotfix (2026-05-23, post-integrate): override C
     extended after user-testing finding that the `* System Prompt'
     heading's `:VISIBILITY: folded' property was not being honored
     on session open. Root cause: org-mode auto-processes
     `:VISIBILITY:' only when `org-startup-folded' is set to certain
     non-default values; the prior `gptel-chat--fold-config-drawer'
     only called `org-fold-hide-drawer-all'. Helper renamed to
     `gptel-chat--apply-startup-visibility' and now also calls
     `org-cycle-set-visibility-according-to-property' so heading-
     level `:VISIBILITY:' is honored regardless of the user's
     `org-startup-folded' setting. Override C's rule, mechanism,
     and counter-assertion below were rewritten to name both folds;
     producers stanza below names the renamed helper. drawer-fold-
     spec.el fixture updated to mirror the canonical post-cycle-7
     layout (which the prior fixture didn't — that's why the
     existing tests passed against the dropped-fold defect). Three
     new regression specs pin the `:VISIBILITY:'-honored behaviour
     with `org-startup-folded' forced to `'showall' (the failure
     mode). See addendum in
     cycle-1779522837/reconciliations/boundary-chat-mode-session-
     display.md.
   load_bearing: false
   title: gptel-chat-mode overrides org-mode presentation for session buffers

   purpose: |
     gptel-chat-mode is a (define-derived-mode ... org-mode), so it
     inherits org-mode's generic fontification and visibility
     behaviour. For session.org buffers two pieces of that inherited
     presentation are wrong, and gptel-chat-mode overrides them. This
     boundary is the seam between org-mode's generic presentation and
     chat-mode's session-specific presentation. It is display polish —
     no real-time-cost incident history — hence load_bearing: false.

   scope:
     in_scope:
       - file: config/gptel/chat/mode.org
         function: gptel-chat-mode (define-derived-mode body / mode hook)
     out_of_scope:
       - upstream org-mode fontification and visibility machinery
       - any non-chat-mode org buffer
       - chat-turn prose (its /emphasis/ is intentionally preserved)

   contract:
     override_A_drawer_interior_emphasis:
       finding: Finding A (cycle-7 spec); cross-line refinement (cycle-8)
       rule: |
         The entire interior of a property-drawer in a chat-mode
         buffer — keys, values, and interior whitespace alike —
         renders as plain data (face org-property-value) and never
         inherits org /emphasis/ font-lock, even when an emphasis
         span opened on one drawer line is paired with a `/` on a
         later line. The override also forces the `invisible`
         text-property to nil across the drawer interior so that
         `org-hide-emphasis-markers` cannot hide boundary `/` (or
         other emphasis-trigger) characters. A directory value such
         as `:GPTEL_SCOPE_READ: /Users/jeff/emacs/` is a syntactically
         valid org emphasis span (leading `/` opens, trailing `/`
         closes); org would otherwise render it italic and dim or
         hide the boundary slashes. Two drawer lines whose values
         contain an odd `/` (e.g. `:GPTEL_SCOPE_READ: /**` followed
         by `:GPTEL_SCOPE_DENY: **/.git/**`) would otherwise pair
         across the line break and italicise the intervening KEY
         text — addressed by the cycle-8 span-level matcher.
       mechanism: |
         gptel-chat-mode installs a buffer-local font-lock keyword
         (`gptel-chat--drawer-span-matcher`, cycle-8 successor to
         the per-line matcher) that searches for `^:PROPERTIES:` to
         `^:END:` spans and stamps group 0 (the drawer interior,
         exclusive of the delimiter lines) with the OVERRIDE
         facespec `(0 '(face org-property-value invisible nil) t)`.
         `font-lock-multiline` is set buffer-locally so that edits
         inside the drawer refontify the whole span (the cross-line
         case is by definition multiline); `invisible` is added to
         `font-lock-extra-managed-props` so font-lock cleans up the
         text-property when buffer text changes. The OVERRIDE flag
         plus `'append` ordering ensure the keyword wins over org's
         emphasis keyword in `org-font-lock-extra-keywords`.
       counter_assertion: |
         /emphasis/ in chat-turn prose is unaffected — the override
         is scoped to drawer interiors only, not the whole buffer.
         A test in drawer-fontification-spec.el's "override A —
         drawer interior emphasis suppression" describe block
         positively asserts that `/emphasized/` text below `* Chat`
         keeps `italic` face and does NOT carry `org-property-value`.
       latent_coupling: |
         The `invisible nil` text-property override coexists with
         override C's overlay-based fold ONLY because
         `org-fold-core-style` defaults to `'overlays` — the two
         channels (text-property vs overlay) are non-conflicting in
         that mode. Flipping `org-fold-core-style` to
         `'text-properties` would cause override A to clobber
         override C's fold-as-text-property on every font-lock pass.
         The codebase has not flipped the style anywhere; if it
         ever does, override A must detect a folded drawer and skip
         it (or fall back to face-only suppression). Documented at
         config/gptel/chat/mode.org override-A prose.
     override_C_startup_visibility_applied_on_open:
       finding: Finding C (cycle-7 spec); cycle-8 hotfix extension
       rule: |
         On gptel-chat-mode activation TWO startup-visibility steps
         apply, producing the WYSIWYG "two headings, drawer folded,
         System Prompt body folded" experience for a persisted
         session.org:
           (1) the file-level config :PROPERTIES: drawer (the
               point-min drawer of register/shape/session-document-
               layout) is folded;
           (2) the `* System Prompt' heading's `:VISIBILITY: folded'
               property is honored — the heading body collapses, so
               the user sees the heading line but not the (often
               long) preset-seeded prompt text.
       mechanism: |
         A single helper (`gptel-chat--apply-startup-visibility',
         cycle-8 hotfix rename of `gptel-chat--fold-config-drawer')
         calls (a) `org-fold-hide-drawer-all' for the drawer fold
         and (b) `org-cycle-set-visibility-according-to-property' for
         the heading-level :VISIBILITY: processing. The helper MUST
         be installed via `gptel-chat-mode-hook' (after
         `run-mode-hooks'), NOT in the `define-derived-mode' body,
         because `org-fold-core' silently drops fold calls that fire
         before mode-hook invocation. cycle-7's first implementor
         placed the call in the body and the spec failed against the
         live buffer; the re-task moved it to the hook and the fold
         worked. This hook-vs-body discipline is the cycle-7
         mechanism note. Step (b) is the cycle-8 hotfix: org-mode
         only auto-processes `:VISIBILITY:' when `org-startup-folded'
         is set to certain non-default values (t / content /
         show2levels / overview / fold) — calling
         `org-cycle-set-visibility-according-to-property' explicitly
         from the hook makes the heading fold regardless of the
         user's `org-startup-folded' setting.
       counter_assertion: |
         A scratch buffer with no drawer AND no `:VISIBILITY:'
         properties — e.g. a gptel-chat-new scratch buffer with a
         bare `#+begin_user' block — is a no-op: both helpers find
         nothing to act on and raise no error. Subtrees without
         `:VISIBILITY:' properties (e.g. the `* Chat' heading) stay
         visible — step (b) is heading-property-scoped, not
         buffer-wide.

   why_it_matters: |
     Without override A, scope path values render italicised and the
     boundary "/" characters are dimmed or hidden — the user cannot
     read the path they scoped, defeating the WYSIWYG goal for the one
     drawer that most needs to be legible. Without override C, opening
     a session.org shows a wall of configuration instead of two
     headings plus a folded drawer. Both are presentation defects that
     erode the "session.org is a clean, readable source of truth"
     contract, but neither has caused a runtime fault — display polish,
     not load-bearing correctness.

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/mode.org
       function: gptel-chat-mode (define-derived-mode body)
       usage: |
         installs the buffer-local font-lock OVERRIDE keyword
         (gptel-chat--drawer-span-matcher) that re-stamps drawer
         interiors as `org-property-value` and forces `invisible
         nil` (override A; cycle-8 span-level refinement)
     - file: config/gptel/chat/mode.org
       function: |
         gptel-chat--apply-startup-visibility (cycle-8 hotfix rename
         of gptel-chat--fold-config-drawer; attached to gptel-chat-
         mode-hook at load time, NOT called from the define-derived-
         mode body — see override_C.mechanism for why)
       usage: |
         applies session.org startup visibility on gptel-chat-mode
         activation: folds every drawer (covering the file-level
         config drawer's wall-of-properties) AND honors the
         `* System Prompt' heading's `:VISIBILITY: folded' property
         (override C).

   consumers:
     - the chat-mode session buffer's font-lock and visibility state
       (no downstream module re-reads this; the contract is observable
       only via buffer face properties and fold state)

   scaffolding_path: null  # cycle-8 cleanup: the boundaries/chat-mode-session-display.el scaffold was deleted by inline-fix commit 73a1b73; the regression spec at config/gptel/chat/test/display/drawer-fontification-spec.el is the live enforcement (status_note "Scaffold archived in favour of the co-located regression specs")

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/session-sibling-system-prompt-file
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
     - register/boundary/chat-menu-scope-default
     - register/shape/session-document-layout                  # superseded predecessor

   discovered_from: gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth
** chat-block-body-indentation
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-chat-block-body-indentation
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/chat-block-body-indentation
   tier: boundary
   status: speculated
   load_bearing: true
   title: chat-mode body indent/dedent pipeline
   purpose: |
     Symmetric pipeline that keeps chat-block body content off column 0,
     where org's structural scanners (heading regex `^\*+ `, special-block
     closers `^#\+end_…`, drawers, keywords) are anchored. Every write path
     (streaming insertion, paste/yank, file-read migration) indents body
     content by `gptel-chat-content-indentation` spaces; the send path
     strips each message segment's common leading indentation so the LLM
     only ever sees clean, column-0 content. The pipeline carves ALL
     column-0 org structure out of chat-block bodies — they render as
     indented plain text — leaving real structural affordances to
     `gptel-chat-edit-turn` (deferred). See proposal.md and design.md
     (Decisions 1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7).  Supersedes the retired per-`*`
     heading-collision escape and the `,#+end_*` delimiter escape — a
     uniformly indented body neutralises every column-0 collision class.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: write-indent
       input: |
         Body content arriving at any chat-block insertion site:
           - assistant chunk via `gptel-chat--sanitize-chunk` (stream)
           - inserted region via `after-change-functions` (paste/yank)
           - existing on-disk content via mode-activation migration
         User typing reaches the indented state via a buffer-local
         `indent-line-function` + electric-indent — an editing affordance,
         not a write producer (design.md Decision 6).
       output: |
         Same content with every body line indented by
         `gptel-chat-content-indentation` spaces (default 2; runtime floor
         clamped to 1 by `gptel-chat--body-indent`). Blank lines are left
         empty. Delimiter lines — outer `#+begin_*` / `#+end_*` and nested
         `#+begin_tool` / `#+end_tool` — are never indented. Streaming
         indents per line; paste shifts the inserted region as a unit
         (`shift = max(0, N - region-min-indent)`, structure-preserving);
         migration shifts per content-region.
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         Streaming is idempotent by construction (fresh content). Paste
         and migration are idempotent — already-indented content shifts
         by 0.

     - n: 2
       name: parse-dedent
       input: |
         Indented chat-block body content as it appears in the buffer or
         on disk, captured per message segment by the parser.
       output: |
         Clean message content: each segment dedented by its own common
         minimum leading indentation (`org-do-remove-indentation` model —
         measured, not assumed). Robust against
         `gptel-chat-content-indentation` changes and legacy content with
         different widths. Delivered to the LLM via
         `gptel-chat--segment-to-messages` / `gptel-chat--turn-to-messages`.
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         Symmetric to stage 1 by intent, NOT by exact-character count:
         stage 1 indents by exactly N; stage 2 strips whatever common
         indent it measures. Per-segment, so a column-0 `#+begin_tool` /
         `#+end_tool` line never drags a segment's measured minimum to 0.
         See design.md Decision 3.

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/chat-block-body-is-indented
     - register/invariant/chat-body-indent-roundtrip-transparent
     - register/invariant/chat-block-delimiter-lines-stay-at-column-0
     - register/invariant/all-write-paths-indent-body

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/stream.org
       function: gptel-chat--sanitize-chunk
       enters_at_stage: 1
     - file: config/gptel/chat/mode.org
       function: gptel-chat--indent-inserted-region
       enters_at_stage: 1
     - file: config/gptel/chat/mode.org
       function: gptel-chat--migrate-buffer
       enters_at_stage: 1

   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/parser.org
       function: gptel-chat--turn-to-messages
       reads_output_of_stage: 2
     - file: config/gptel/chat/parser.org
       function: gptel-chat--segment-to-messages
       reads_output_of_stage: 2

   discovered_from: gptel-chat-heading-scoping
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** buffer-reincarnation-pipeline
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-buffer-reincarnation-pipeline
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/buffer-reincarnation-pipeline
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260524-200631 integrate (2026-05-24): 4-stage pipeline ships
     across existing helpers (capture-frameset → window-state-serialize →
     window-state-deserialize → restore-frameset). Stage 4 race-guard
     cl-incf moved INTO workspace--restore-frameset (inline-fix commit
     bc64784) so workspace--apply-saved-layout AND workspace-switch-layout
     entry paths uniformly participate. Follow-up task
     test-switch-layout-race-guard adds the missing switch-layout race
     test. Scaffolding archived. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260524-200631/reconciliations/boundary-buffer-reincarnation-pipeline.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Per-leaf buffer reincarnation pipeline (capture → walk → fallback chain → defer)
   purpose: |
     The end-to-end path that converts a live frame's window-state into
     persisted workspace-buffer structs and back. The boundary is
     four-staged because each stage owns a distinct contract: capture
     (live → struct), serialization (struct → eld), restore-walk (eld
     → leaf-by-leaf reincarnation), deferred apply (sync
     window-state-put → run-at-time). Stage 3 short-circuits on
     non-nil return (the fallback chain) and stage 4's deferral is
     load-bearing because =bookmark--jump-via= calls a buffer-display
     function as part of its restore protocol; running
     =window-state-put= in the same tick races against it (catalog
     pattern 14).

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: capture
       input: live frame (window-state-get)
       output: window-state form with workspace-buffer in each leaf's
         parameters slot
       can_fail_with: [no-bookmark-handler]
       notes: |
         Recursive descent over the window-state form. Detects the
         one-window-frame edge case (=(leaf . attrs)= at the top
         level) by =cl-position 'leaf= on the form. For each leaf,
         calls =workspace--capture-leaf= to build a workspace-buffer.
         A nil bookmark on the captured struct is non-fatal; restore
         falls through to the filename step.
     - n: 2
       name: serialize
       input: enriched window-state form
       output: eld text on disk
       can_fail_with: [eld-write-error]
       notes: |
         The standard =workspace--write-state= pretty-prints the form
         via =pp=. workspace-buffer structs serialize via
         =cl-defstruct='s default reader/writer; the embedded bookmark
         record is itself printable elisp data.
     - n: 3
       name: restore-walk
       input: persisted window-state form
       output: window-state form with each leaf's buffer replaced by
         a live reincarnated buffer
       can_fail_with: [help-mode-subr-read-error, bookmark-handler-error]
       short_circuit_policy: deliberate
       notes: |
         Walks the form (same recursive descent as stage 1). For each
         leaf, calls =workspace--reincarnate-leaf= (see register/
         vocabulary/buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain) and replaces
         the leaf's buffer reference with the returned live buffer.
         Per-leaf =condition-case-unless-debug= traps so one
         unreadable bookmark (Emacs bug#56643) does not abort the
         tree. The fallback chain itself short-circuits on non-nil
         buffer.
     - n: 4
       name: defer-apply
       input: reincarnated window-state form
       output: applied frame layout (=window-state-put= side effect)
       can_fail_with: [window-state-put-error]
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         Calls =(run-at-time nil nil (lambda () (window-state-put ...)
         frame)))= so the apply happens after the current command
         loop tick. Necessary because =bookmark--jump-via= calls a
         buffer-display function synchronously; running
         window-state-put in the same tick races for the selected
         window. The form passed to the timer is =copy-tree='d so the
         in-memory registry is not mutated by
         =--bufferize-window-state='s side effects. A generation
         counter (=workspace--restore-generation=, module-level
         defvar) guards against concurrent restores: the deferred
         lambda checks =(= gen workspace--restore-generation)= and
         no-ops on stale runs.

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/restore-deferred-via-run-at-time

   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/layouts.org
       function: workspace--capture-frameset
       enters_at_stage: 1
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--write-state
       enters_at_stage: 2

   consumers:
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--apply-saved-layout
       reads_output_of_stage: 4

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/boundaries/buffer-reincarnation-pipeline.el
   #+end_src

** autosave-guard-pipeline
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-autosave-guard-pipeline
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/autosave-guard-pipeline
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260524-200631 integrate (2026-05-24): stages 2+3 (capture +
     slot-route) collapsed into workspace--autosave-current-layout. Stage 1
     (anti-save-check) deferred to sibling task anti-save-predicates;
     attachment-point pinned (wrap the four autosave entry points, NOT
     inside workspace--autosave-current-layout — preserves
     explicit-save-bypasses-anti-save structurally). Stage 4 reuses
     pre-cycle debounce machinery. Scaffolding archived. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260524-200631/reconciliations/boundary-autosave-guard-pipeline.md.

     cycle-20260525-082618 integrate (2026-05-25): stage-1 attachment
     surface filled. The pre-cycle producer list named four stage-1
     entries; cycle-2 anti-save-predicates implementor surfaced that
     workspace--persistence-after-tab-switch (originally listed) is
     actually the lazy-restore path, not an autosave. The actual stage-1
     wrap surface is THREE persistence entries collapsed to two distinct
     functions (workspace--persistence-before-tab-switch — :before
     advice on both tab-bar-select-tab AND tab-bar-switch-to-tab —
     plus workspace--kill-emacs-flush) and one workspaces-mode entry
     (workspaces-mode--idle-tick). All four wrap call sites carry
     (unless (run-hook-with-args-until-success 'workspace-anti-save-
     predicates) ...). workspace-save still enters at stage 2 by
     design. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-082618/reconciliations/boundary-autosave-guard-pipeline.md.

     cycle-20260613-150904 integrate (2026-06-13, task registry-hydrate-
     only-startup): the lazy-restore =:after= advice this note spent a
     paragraph distinguishing the autosave boundary FROM —
     =workspace--persistence-after-tab-switch= (+ =workspace--activate-
     pending-workspace=) — was REMOVED in full (design.md §D2). The
     autosave stage-1 wrap surface is UNCHANGED and held verbatim: the
     =:before= advice (=workspace--persistence-before-tab-switch= on
     tab-bar-select-tab AND tab-bar-switch-to-tab), =workspace--kill-
     emacs-flush=, and =workspaces-mode--idle-tick=. The boundary is now
     cleaner — there is no longer a confusable =:after= sibling on the
     same tab-switch entry points. why_tests_missed: N/A — no test gap;
     the autosave specs (idle-tick, kill-emacs, before-switch capture)
     stayed green through the removal, proving the =:before= path intact.
     Independently confirmed by on-touch architect (0 findings) +
     author-blind review. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260613-150904/reconciliations/boundary-autosave-guard-pipeline.md.

     cycle-20260613-161149 integrate (2026-06-13, task corruption-safe-
     persistence-io): stage-4 (flush) gains an ADDITIVE session-scoped
     suppression precondition — =workspace--persistence-blocked=. When a
     startup load found a present-but-unreadable file, the flag is set and
     EVERY writer no-ops: =workspace--write-state= (the choke point, also
     covering =workspace--flush-state= and =workspace-save-state=), with
     explicit early-returns added in =workspace--kill-emacs-flush= and
     =workspace-save-state= (so no useless idle timer is armed). This is
     ADDITIVE: the stage-1 anti-save-predicate gate, the four wrap call
     sites, and the explicit-save stage-2 entry are unchanged. Purpose:
     prevent an autosave/idle/kill-emacs flush from clobbering a
     backed-up corrupt file with an empty registry (the data-loss
     cascade). New invariant register/boundary/persistence-robustness-io
     captures the full corruption-safety contract. why_tests_missed: the
     autosave-after-failed-load path was never exercised; serialization-
     robustness-spec.el now has the e2e clobber-cascade regression. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260613-161149/reconciliations/boundary-autosave-guard-pipeline.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Autosave guard pipeline (predicates → capture → slot route → flush)
   purpose: |
     The pipeline that every autosave trigger funnels through. The
     boundary makes anti-save short-circuit a structural feature
     (stage 1 returns non-nil → halt) rather than a per-trigger check
     repeated at three call sites. All three autosave triggers
     (tab-switch advice, idle timer, kill-emacs-hook) call into stage
     1; explicit =workspace-save= bypasses stage 1 entirely and
     enters at stage 2 with slot=:saved-state. The slot-routing stage
     reads from register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot's canonical
     mapping function.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: anti-save-check
       input: trigger symbol (one of: tab-switch, idle, kill-emacs)
       output: continue | halt
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: deliberate
       notes: |
         =(run-hook-with-args-until-success 'workspace-anti-save-
         predicates)=. If any predicate returns non-nil, return halt
         and the autosave is silent. Explicit =workspace-save= does
         NOT enter this stage; that path enters at stage 2 directly.
         The stage is the safety guard re-introducing tab-switch
         advice required (gap D8 in v1).
     - n: 2
       name: capture
       input: live frame (window-state-get on the outgoing workspace
         or current frame)
       output: enriched window-state form (per the capture stage of
         register/boundary/buffer-reincarnation-pipeline)
       can_fail_with: [capture-error]
       notes: |
         Identical capture path to explicit =workspace-save=; the
         output is structurally indistinguishable between the two
         entry points. This is what makes "trigger → slot" the only
         per-trigger decision; the captured form is one shape.
     - n: 3
       name: slot-route
       input: (captured-state, trigger | :explicit)
       output: layout plist with the captured state in the correct
         slot (per register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot)
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: |
         Calls =workspace--slot-for-trigger= (vocabulary entry's
         canonical mapping function). =:explicit= → :saved-state and
         additionally clears :working-state. Any other trigger →
         :working-state and leaves :saved-state untouched. The
         "leaves :saved-state untouched" half is the structural
         enforcement of register/invariant/autosave-never-writes-
         saved-state.
     - n: 4
       name: flush
       input: layout plist
       output: bytes on disk
       can_fail_with: [disk-write-error]
       notes: |
         Synchronous for explicit save and kill-emacs flush;
         debounced for tab-switch and idle-timer triggers (existing
         debounce in =workspace-save-state=).

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/explicit-save-bypasses-anti-save
     - register/invariant/autosave-never-writes-saved-state
     - register/invariant/explicit-save-clears-working-state

   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--persistence-before-tab-switch
       advised_primitive: tab-bar-select-tab, tab-bar-switch-to-tab (:before)
       enters_at_stage: 1
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--kill-emacs-flush
       enters_at_stage: 1
     - file: config/workspaces/workspaces-mode.org
       function: workspaces-mode--idle-tick
       enters_at_stage: 1
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace-save (explicit)
       enters_at_stage: 2
       note: |
         Bypasses stage 1 structurally — calls
         workspace--autosave-current-layout :saved-state directly. The
         absence of a stage-1 wrap on this call site is itself the
         enforcement of register/invariant/explicit-save-bypasses-anti-
         save. A future refactor that introduced a shared "guarded
         helper" feeding both autosave and explicit-save would break
         that invariant.

   consumers:
     - file: config/workspaces/persistence.org
       function: workspace--write-state
       reads_output_of_stage: 4

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/boundaries/autosave-guard-pipeline.el
   #+end_src

** home-org-read-pipeline
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-home-org-read-pipeline
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/home-org-read-pipeline
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260525-200459 integrate (2026-05-25): 5-stage pipeline
     implemented cleanly in config/workspaces/home-org.el. Two metadata
     refinements absorbed from the implementor's push-back:
     (1) producers list now notes loaded_via=workspaces.org submodule
     loader (not init.org/jf/enabled-modules — workspaces subsystem
     convention); (2) stage-5 trim-and-emptiness-filter gains explicit
     examples distinguishing #+TITLE:    \n (stage-5 reject) from bare
     #+TITLE: (stage-4 short-circuit — regex requires [ \t]+ after colon).
     Core contracts (5 stages; NO caching; NO org-mode load) hold as
     speculated. Reviewer (R1-F1) flagged the cross-module writer-allow-list
     lint from the related invariant scaffold was not ported in cycle 1;
     follow-up task port-cross-module-home-org-writer-lint filed
     (blocked-on scaffold-module). Scaffold archived; canonical mapping
     implemented in config/workspaces/home-org.el and tested in
     config/workspaces/test/home-org-spec.el (14 specs).
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-200459/reconciliations/boundary-home-org-read-pipeline.md.
   load_bearing: false
   title: home.org read-on-demand pipeline (no caching, no org-mode)
   purpose: |
     The on-demand read pipeline that resolves =<HOME>/home.org='s
     =#+TITLE:= keyword for use as a workspace's display name. The
     pipeline is structured deliberately to keep the per-call cost
     low enough that no caching layer is required — the spec
     promises "live read on every call" and that promise must remain
     cheap. Design D4 explicitly rejected =org-collect-keywords=
     because it would load =org-mode= for each read; the regex chain
     here is ~10× faster and adds no module load. The =no caching=
     and =no org-mode load= properties are themselves contractual:
     adding a cache or switching to a real parser is a divergence
     this entry catches.

     Single function: =workspace-home-org-title HOME= (in the new
     =home-org.org= module). The boundary entry exists to pin the
     stage contract; the implementation is one =with-temp-buffer=
     form. Cited from =home-org-reader-module= task.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: path-resolve
       input: HOME (absolute directory path)
       output: absolute path to "<HOME>/home.org"
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         =(expand-file-name "home.org" HOME)=. Pure; cannot fail.
     - n: 2
       name: readability-check
       input: absolute path to home.org
       output: t (continue) | nil (short-circuit to overall nil)
       can_fail_with: [file-missing, permission-denied]
       short_circuit_policy: deliberate
       notes: |
         =(file-readable-p path)=. If nil, the overall function
         returns nil (no title). This is NOT an error condition —
         a workspace whose home.org has been deleted should still
         be usable (display name falls back to registry-name per
         =registry-name-equals-basename=). Bypassing this stage
         and reading anyway would propagate an error from
         =insert-file-contents=.
     - n: 3
       name: load-to-temp-buffer
       input: readable home.org path
       output: temp buffer with file contents
       can_fail_with: [io-error]
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         =(with-temp-buffer (insert-file-contents path) ...)=. NO
         =org-mode= activation; NO file-handlers that would invoke
         major-mode. Just =insert-file-contents= and a temp buffer.
     - n: 4
       name: regex-scan-title
       input: temp buffer with file contents
       output: match-string (raw, possibly with trailing whitespace) | nil
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: deliberate
       notes: |
         =(re-search-forward "^#\\+TITLE:[ \\t]+\\(.*\\)$" nil t)=.
         Returns nil if no match (no title present); the overall
         function returns nil. Regex is case-insensitive via
         =case-fold-search= context inherited from =with-temp-buffer=
         (defaults to t in Emacs); spec scenario explicitly tests
         lowercase =#+title:= acceptance. If a future implementor
         tightens to case-sensitive, the spec scenario catches the
         regression.
     - n: 5
       name: trim-and-emptiness-filter
       input: raw match-string
       output: trimmed non-empty string | nil
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         =(let ((title (string-trim (match-string 1)))) (and (not
         (string-empty-p title)) title))=. A =#+TITLE:= keyword
         with only whitespace returns nil — equivalent to no title
         at all per spec intent (the user wrote the keyword but
         supplied no value).

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/home-org-user-authored-after-creation

   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/home-org.org
       function: workspace-home-org-title
       enters_at_stage: 1
     - file: config/workspaces/home-org.org
       function: workspace-home-org-exists-p
       enters_at_stage: 2
       note: |
         Sibling helper that runs only stage 2 (readability check)
         and returns the readability boolean directly. Shares stage
         1/2 contract; does not run stages 3-5.

   consumers:
     - file: config/workspaces/tabs.org
       function: workspace-display-name (per cycle-2+ tasks)
       reads_output_of_stage: 5
       note: |
         Falls back to =(workspace--name ws)= when stage 5 returns
         nil. The fallback is the =registry-name-equals-basename=
         path.
     - file: config/workspaces/scaffold.org (cycle-2)
       function: scaffold-module checks via =workspace-home-org-exists-p=
       reads_output_of_stage: 2
       note: |
         The anchor-existing flow's case-1 branch (repo + home.org)
         uses stage-2 output to distinguish from case-2 (repo + no
         home.org).

   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/add-workspace-home-directory/scaffolding/boundaries/home-org-read-pipeline.el
   #+end_src

** workspace-scaffold-pipeline
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-workspace-scaffold-pipeline
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/workspace-scaffold-pipeline
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260612-143558 (workspace-integration-menu) integrate:
     reconciled → reconciled (stage-list reconciliation). Task
     =wire-create-dispatch= deleted the broken
     =workspace--scaffold-initial-session= stub (and its pipeline
     call). The pipeline is now FIVE stages, not six: stage 5
     "create-initial-session" is GONE; =sessions/= is created empty at
     stage 4 and populated on demand / at birth by registered
     integrations (the gptel =:on-create= handler now writes the real
     session, which lands UNTRACKED after the Initial-workspace
     commit). The gated git-add-and-commit is now stage 5 (was 6);
     INIT-AND-COMMIT? gates stages 2 and 5. LEAVE-ON-FAILURE and
     "scaffold failure → no registry entry → no :on-create" semantics
     are UNCHANGED — only the stage enumeration drifted.
     why_tests_missed: the register entry is hand-maintained prose, not
     executed; the scaffold-spec.el behavioural cases were updated in
     the same task (creates-one-initial-session → sessions/-left-empty)
     and stayed green, so no test failed — the stage-list contract is
     documentation that only an Architect/register reconciliation
     catches. Reconciliation note at
     =.orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260612-143558/reconciliations/boundary-workspace-scaffold-pipeline.md=.
     Prior cycle's note preserved below.

     cycle-20260525-213500 integrate: speculated → reconciled.
     scaffold-module landed with the canonical signature
     =(cl-defun workspace-scaffold (home name &key init-and-commit?) ...)=
     — a realisation of the speculated keyword-arg shape (the
     scaffold's =&rest plist= form was an intentional placeholder).
     Six-stage pipeline holds verbatim; INIT-AND-COMMIT? gating
     unchanged. Behavioural assertions lifted into
     =config/workspaces/test/scaffold-spec.el= (14 spec cases). The
     anchor-existing-repo branch (cycle 3+) consumes the same
     function with INIT-AND-COMMIT? nil. Reconciliation note at
     =.orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-213500/reconciliations/boundary-workspace-scaffold-pipeline.md=.

     Prior status_note (preserved for lineage):
     "cycle-20260525-213500 plan: populated forward-mode. The
     six-stage scaffold pipeline produced by =scaffold-module=
     (cycle-2 task). Speculation: the pipeline is a single function
     =workspace-scaffold HOME NAME &key INIT-AND-COMMIT?= with six
     stages, the git-bracketed stages (2 and 6) controlled by
     =INIT-AND-COMMIT?=, and a fail-fast contract that does not
     auto-cleanup partial filesystem effects. Pressure-tested by
     =scaffold-module= and (next-cycle) =workspace-new-anchor-existing=.
     The =INIT-AND-COMMIT? nil= shape is the only knob the
     anchor-existing-repo branch needs; if the anchor flow turns out
     to require finer per-stage control during execute, this entry
     reconciles divergent."
   load_bearing: true
   title: workspace directory scaffolding pipeline (default-path and anchor-non-repo branches)
   purpose: |
     The fixed-order pipeline that scaffolds a workspace's filesystem
     anchor. The pipeline is the SINGLE producer of a freshly-set-up
     workspace directory; both =workspace-new= (default path) and
     =workspace-new= with prefix-arg (anchor existing non-repo) reach
     a working workspace by way of this function. The contract pins
     three concerns together so they cannot drift:

     1. =STAGE ORDER=: dirs and files are created in the order below.
        Re-ordering risks subtle issues (e.g. =git add .= before
        =home.org= exists would silently miss the file from the initial
        commit). NOTE (cycle-6): =sessions/= is created EMPTY and is no
        longer part of the initial commit's tracked content — any
        integration-written session lands untracked after the commit.
     2. =INIT-AND-COMMIT? GATE=: the only knob the anchor-existing-repo
        branch needs to suppress is the git bracketing. The same
        function handles both branches.
     3. =LEAVE-ON-FAILURE SEMANTICS=: partial-failure does not delete.
        Coupled with =register/invariant/scaffold-leave-partial-on-failure=.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: ensure-home-directory
       input: HOME (absolute target path)
       output: directory exists at HOME (=make-directory HOME t=)
       can_fail_with: [permission-denied, parent-missing, eexist-as-file]
       gated_by: always
       notes: |
         =(make-directory home t)=. The =t= recursive flag creates any
         missing parents. Fails if HOME exists as a non-directory file
         or the parent path is unwritable.
     - n: 2
       name: git-init
       input: HOME (existing directory)
       output: =HOME/.git/= initialized
       can_fail_with: [git-not-on-path, git-init-failure]
       gated_by: INIT-AND-COMMIT?
       notes: |
         =(workspace--scaffold-git HOME "init")=. Skipped entirely when
         INIT-AND-COMMIT? is nil (the "anchor existing git repo without
         home.org" branch — git state is the user's; we do not touch).
     - n: 3
       name: write-home-org-skeleton
       input: HOME, NAME
       output: =HOME/home.org= created if not pre-existing
       can_fail_with: [io-error]
       gated_by: always
       notes: |
         =(workspace--scaffold-write-home-org home name)=. The writer
         is =(unless (file-exists-p path) ...)=-guarded — if the user
         pre-created =home.org= (case-1 of the anchor flow), the
         skeleton write is a no-op and the user's content is preserved.
         This is the ONLY package code path that writes =home.org= —
         see =register/invariant/home-org-user-authored-after-creation=.
     - n: 4
       name: ensure-sessions-directory
       input: HOME
       output: =HOME/sessions/= exists
       can_fail_with: [permission-denied]
       gated_by: always
       notes: |
         =(make-directory (workspace--sessions-dir home) t)=. The
         spec promises =directory-files= over =HOME/sessions/= is the
         authoritative session inventory; this stage creates the
         directory EMPTY. As of cycle-6 (=wire-create-dispatch=) there
         is no scaffold stage that populates it — sessions are written
         on demand or at birth by registered integrations
         (=register/boundary/workspace-integration-registry=), which
         run AFTER the commit, so their output is untracked. The path
         is composed via the canonical helper =workspace--sessions-dir=
         (in data-model.el) per cycle-2's
         =arch-cycle-20260525-213500-05= finding.
     - n: 5
       name: git-add-and-commit
       input: HOME (with .git/ from stage 2 and files from stages 3-4)
       output: one initial git commit on HEAD
       can_fail_with: [git-add-failure, git-commit-failure, no-files-to-commit]
       gated_by: INIT-AND-COMMIT?
       notes: |
         =(workspace--scaffold-git HOME "add" ".")= then
         =(workspace--scaffold-git HOME "commit" "-m" "Initial workspace")=.
         Skipped entirely when INIT-AND-COMMIT? is nil. The commit
         message is exact — the cycle-2+ end-to-end test asserts on it
         via =git log --oneline=. This is the FINAL fail-fast scaffold
         step; integration dispatch (cycle-6) runs strictly after it
         and after registration.

   gated_by:
     parameter: INIT-AND-COMMIT?
     when_t: stages 1-5 (default-path; anchor-non-repo)
     when_nil: stages 1, 3, 4 only (anchor existing repo without home.org)
     rationale: |
       The same function handles three of the four workspace-new
       branches; only the existing-repo-with-home.org case bypasses
       =workspace-scaffold= entirely (because there's nothing to do).

   on_failure_contract:
     pipeline_aborts: true
     subsequent_stages_run: false
     filesystem_cleanup: |
       PARTIAL FILES LEFT IN PLACE. The function does NOT delete the
       partial scaffold on failure. See
       =register/invariant/scaffold-leave-partial-on-failure= for the
       full rationale.
     registry_mutation: |
       The function itself does NOT touch the registry; the caller
       (=workspace-new=) handles registration only after this function
       returns non-nil. A failed scaffold therefore never produces a
       registry entry, even partially. (The caller MUST honor this —
       any caller that registers BEFORE awaiting the scaffold's return
       is a defect.)
     signal_via: user-error
     signal_message_includes: [HOME-path, failing-stage-or-git-arg, suggested-remedy]

   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/scaffold.org
       function: workspace-scaffold
       enters_at_stage: 1
       note: |
         The single producer; all scaffold paths route through this
         function.

   consumers:
     - file: config/workspaces/tabs.org
       function: workspace-new (no prefix arg)
       reads_output_of_stage: 5 (return value: HOME)
       init_and_commit_value: t
       note: |
         Default-path branch. Passes =:init-and-commit? t=. Cycle-2+
         task =workspace-new-default-path=. Fires integration dispatch
         (=workspace--dispatch-create-integrations ... 'fresh=) after
         registration (cycle-6 =wire-create-dispatch=).
     - file: config/workspaces/tabs.org
       function: workspace-new (with prefix arg — anchor existing non-repo)
       reads_output_of_stage: 5 (return value: HOME)
       init_and_commit_value: t
       note: |
         "Not a git repo" sub-case of the anchor flow. Same pipeline as
         default-path. Cycle-3 task =workspace-new-anchor-existing=.
         Dispatch context =fresh= (cycle-6).
     - file: config/workspaces/tabs.org
       function: workspace-new (with prefix arg — anchor existing repo without home.org)
       reads_output_of_stage: 4 (return value: HOME)
       init_and_commit_value: nil
       note: |
         "Repo without home.org" sub-case. Skips git init/add/commit so
