         the user's git state is untouched (per design D2's "we never
         touch repos we did not initialize"). Cycle-3 task
         =workspace-new-anchor-existing=.

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/scaffold-leave-partial-on-failure
     - register/invariant/home-org-user-authored-after-creation

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/workspace-plist-v3
     - register/boundary/home-org-read-pipeline
     - register/invariant/home-required-no-floating-workspaces

   related_entries_added_cycle6:
     - register/boundary/workspace-integration-registry
   cited_by_tasks:
     - scaffold-module
     - wire-create-dispatch
   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/add-workspace-home-directory/scaffolding/boundaries/workspace-scaffold-pipeline.el
   #+end_src

** gptel-sessions-workspace-consult
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-gptel-sessions-workspace-consult
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/gptel-sessions-workspace-consult
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260612-143558 (workspace-integration-menu) integrate:
     CONFIRMED-HELD (no change). The directionality clause was the
     load-bearing on-touch trigger for all 5 cycle-6 tasks, which added
     three new workspaces-side modules (=integrations.el=,
     =workspaces-transient.el=) and the gptel-side consumer
     (=config/gptel/sessions/workspace-integration.el=). Post-merge
     grep =gptel-sessions-\|gptel-sessions/\|require.*gptel-sessions=
     over =config/workspaces/*.el= returns zero matches (the glob-based
     lint in =config/workspaces/test/gptel-integration-spec.el= now
     also covers the two new files automatically). The gptel consumer
     attaches via =with-eval-after-load 'workspaces= +
     =workspace-register-integration= (the PERMITTED direction); it
     adds NO require on workspaces. A SECOND published boundary now sits
     alongside this one — =register/boundary/workspace-integration-registry=
     — and is the structural successor: where this entry pins the
     gptel↔workspaces session-dir consult, the registry pins the
     generic extension point any subsystem (gptel first, git-worktree
     next) uses to attach. Contract intact.

     cycle-20260525-213500 integrate: speculated → reconciled.
     Producer side (=workspace-sessions-dir=) matched speculation
     verbatim. Consumer-side function drifted from speculated name
     =gptel-sessions--target-dir= to =jf/gptel--target-sessions-root=
     in =config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.el= (project =jf/gptel-=
     prefix convention). Directionality contract held: post-merge
     grep for =gptel-sessions-\|gptel-sessions/\|require.*gptel-sessions=
     in =config/workspaces/*.el= returns zero matches (verified after
     the orchestrator inline-fix at =63d60ec= removed a dead
     =featurep= branch from =scaffold.el= that triggered the
     cross-contract collision between two cycle-2 task scaffolds —
     see PM digest meta-discoveries).

     User-facing escape hatch was implemented as a separate command
     =jf/gptel-persistent-session-global= rather than rebinding the
     prefix arg (existing =jf/gptel-persistent-session= already
     consumes =current-prefix-arg= for preset selection). Function-
     arg contract (=force-global= routes correctly) preserved; UX
     surface diverges from =specs/workspaces/spec.md:264-270=. Routed
     to user as ask =ask-cycle-20260525-213500-2=.

     Reconciliation note at
     =.orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-213500/reconciliations/boundary-gptel-sessions-workspace-consult.md=.

     Prior status_note (preserved for lineage):
     "cycle-20260525-213500 plan: populated forward-mode. Cross-
     subsystem boundary between =workspaces= (producer of
     =workspace-sessions-dir=) and =gptel/sessions= (consumer via
     =gptel-sessions--target-dir=). Speculation: the consult is
     one-way, soft (featurep + fboundp guards), nil-returning when
     not applicable, and gated by an interactive prefix arg on the
     user-facing session-creation command. Pressure-tested by
     =gptel-sessions-workspace-consult= (cycle-2 task). The exact
     gptel function name being modified is forward-pinned to Q1 in
     design.md; the boundary contract holds regardless of what that
     function turns out to be called."
   load_bearing: true
   title: gptel sessions ↔ workspaces one-way soft-dependency consult
   purpose: |
     The cross-subsystem boundary that lets the =gptel/sessions=
     module file new session files under =<HOME>/sessions/= when a
     workspace is active, without making =gptel/sessions= depend on
     =workspaces= at load time. Three concerns pinned at the boundary:

     1. =DIRECTION=: one-way (gptel → workspaces). Workspaces never
        references gptel; if =workspaces= ever calls back into gptel,
        that is a divergence this entry catches.
     2. =SOFTNESS=: =featurep 'workspaces= + =fboundp
        'workspace-sessions-dir= guards. Either guard returning nil
        falls through to the global default cleanly.
     3. =NIL-DISCIPLINE=: the producer (=workspace-sessions-dir=)
        returns the directory OR nil; it does NOT signal. The consumer
        composes with =or= to fall through to the global default. Any
        future producer revision MUST preserve nil-on-not-applicable.

   producer_side:
     file: config/workspaces/workspaces.org
     function: workspace-sessions-dir
     signature: (workspace-sessions-dir) → string-or-nil
     return_values:
       - condition: no workspace is the current tab's owner
         returns: nil
         check: (workspace--current-name) returns nil
       - condition: current workspace is broken
         returns: nil
         check: (workspace--broken-p ws) returns t
       - condition: workspace exists but :home/sessions/ dir is missing
         returns: nil
         check: (file-directory-p dir) returns nil
       - condition: workspace exists, healthy, sessions/ dir exists
         returns: absolute path to sessions/
         shape: =(workspace--sessions-dir home)= (canonical helper;
                resolves to =(expand-file-name "sessions" home)=)
     contract: |
       MUST NOT signal. MUST NOT have side effects (no directory
       creation, no notice). Pure inspection over the current-tab and
       registry state.

   consumer_side:
     file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
     function: jf/gptel--target-sessions-root
     signature: (jf/gptel--target-sessions-root &optional force-global) → directory-string
     fallback_chain:
       - condition: not force-global AND featurep 'workspaces AND fboundp 'workspace-sessions-dir AND (workspace-sessions-dir) non-nil
         resolves_to: (workspace-sessions-dir)
       - condition: any of the above fails (including (workspace-sessions-dir) returning nil)
         resolves_to: jf/gptel-sessions-directory
     guard_pattern: |
       (or (and (not force-global)
                (featurep 'workspaces)
                (fboundp 'workspace-sessions-dir)
                (workspace-sessions-dir))
           jf/gptel-sessions-directory)

   user_facing_command:
     function: jf/gptel-persistent-session
     interactive_form: "P"
     escape_hatch_command: jf/gptel-persistent-session-global
     escape_hatch_rationale: |
       Implemented as a SEPARATE command rather than a prefix-arg
       rebind because =jf/gptel-persistent-session= already consumes
       =current-prefix-arg= for preset selection (pre-existing UX
       that predates this change). The =-global= sibling command
       binds FORCE-GLOBAL non-nil → skips workspace consult → falls
       through to global default. Function-arg contract preserved;
       UX surface diverges from =specs/workspaces/spec.md:264-270=
       — routed to user as ask =ask-cycle-20260525-213500-2= for
       spec disposition.

   directionality:
     forbidden: |
       =config/workspaces/*.el= MUST NOT reference any =gptel-sessions-*=
       symbol. If a future change needs workspaces to react to
       session-creation events, the producer publishes a hook;
       workspaces does not call gptel directly.
     permitted: |
       =config/gptel/sessions/*.el= MAY call =workspace-sessions-dir=
       guarded by =fboundp=. No other workspaces-side symbol is
       part of this contract.

   producers:
     - file: config/workspaces/workspaces.org
       function: workspace-sessions-dir
       enters_at_stage: producer-side (sole producer of the dir-or-nil value)
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/filesystem.org
       function: jf/gptel--target-sessions-root
       reads_output_of_stage: consumer-side fallback head
       note: |
         Resolved at cycle-2 integrate from design.md Q1.
         =jf/gptel--ensure-sessions-root= remains global-only (used
         by registry/listing); only =jf/gptel--target-sessions-root=
         consults workspaces (creation path).

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/workspace-plist-v3
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-broken-disposition
     - register/invariant/registry-name-equals-basename

   cited_by_tasks:
     - gptel-sessions-workspace-consult
   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/add-workspace-home-directory/scaffolding/boundaries/gptel-sessions-workspace-consult.el
   #+end_src

** workspace-integration-registry
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-workspace-integration-registry
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/workspace-integration-registry
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260612-164043 (add-workspace-git-worktrees) integrate:
     RECONCILED — the registration contract grew from TWO surfaces to
     THREE. Tasks =registry-on-purge-surface= and =purge-teardown-dispatch=
     added the =:on-purge= teardown surface, generalised
     =workspace--run-one-integration= with an optional surface key, and
     added a sibling =workspace--dispatch-purge-integrations= fired from
     =workspace-purge= before =delete-directory=. The git-worktree module
     (the named-but-deferred second client) is now a REAL consumer,
     attaching =:menu= + =:on-purge=. why_tests_missed: the registration
     shape below was hand-maintained prose ("at least one of
     :on-create/:menu"); the surface growth is intended and fully covered
     by new specs (on-purge-only registration, none-of-three user-error,
     purge dispatch order/skip/non-fatal), so only the doc contract lagged.
     Reconciliation note:
     =.orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260612-164043/reconciliations/boundary-workspace-integration-registry.md=.

     cycle-20260612-143558 (workspace-integration-menu) integrate:
     created as CONFIRMED (not speculated). This change's plan artifacts
     pre-dated orchestrator forward-mode, so the entry is recorded
     post-implementation from the merged, clean-reviewed shapes in
     =config/workspaces/integrations.el= (task
     =integration-registry-core=) and its consumers (=wire-create-dispatch=,
     =workspaces-transient-menu=, =gptel-session-integration=). All four
     implementor diffs realised this contract verbatim; all five
     reviewers returned clean. This is the new published extension point
     the proposal set out to create — the structural successor to the
     deleted =workspace--scaffold-initial-session= stub and the sibling
     of =register/boundary/gptel-sessions-workspace-consult=.
   load_bearing: true
   title: workspace integration registry — the published extension point
   purpose: |
     The single, generic boundary by which an EXTERNAL subsystem hooks
     into workspace creation and operation WITHOUT workspaces naming the
     consumer. Workspaces owns the registry and iterates it; consumers
     attach from their own side (the PERMITTED direction of
     =register/boundary/gptel-sessions-workspace-consult=). Four concerns
     are pinned so they cannot drift:

     1. =REGISTRATION SHAPE=: an integration is registered by
        =(workspace-register-integration ID &key LABEL ON-CREATE MENU ON-PURGE)=.
        At least one of =:on-create= / =:menu= / =:on-purge= is required
        (else =user-error=: "%s has none of :on-create, :menu, :on-purge").
        Re-registering an ID replaces in place, preserving order. The
        registry =workspace--integrations= is an ORDERED ALIST
        =((ID . PLIST) ...)= — dispatch order and menu order follow
        registration order (NOT a hash table). THREE surfaces:
        =:on-create= (non-interactive, at birth), =:menu= ((KEY . COMMAND),
        interactive, on-demand), =:on-purge= (non-interactive, at teardown).
     2. =PUSH, NOT CONSULT=: workspaces hands each handler an anchor
        payload (=register/shape/workspace-integration-anchor-payload=);
        handlers never reach for "the current workspace". This keeps the
        boundary one-way and lets the menu and the creation-dispatch site
        build identical payloads via one constructor
        =workspace--integration-payload=.
     3. =ADDITIVE, NEVER LOAD-BEARING=: each handler runs inside an error
        guard (=workspace--run-one-integration=, =condition-case=); a
        signalled error or a =(failed . reason)= return is surfaced via
        =message= but NEVER aborts workspace creation. A workspace is
        valid iff its OWN scaffold succeeded; integration results
        decorate, they never define. (Result vocabulary:
        =register/vocabulary/workspace-integration-outcome=.)
     4. =DISPATCH IS A SIBLING OF SCAFFOLD=: =workspace--dispatch-create-integrations
        NAME HOME CONTEXT= fires from the =workspace--new-*= entry points
        AFTER registration (and after the Initial-workspace commit), in
        all three birth contexts. It is NOT a scaffold stage — see the
        cycle-6 reconciliation of =register/boundary/workspace-scaffold-pipeline=
        (the deleted stage 5).
     5. =TEARDOWN DISPATCH (cycle-7)=: =workspace--dispatch-purge-integrations
        NAME HOME CONTEXT= is the purge mirror of create-dispatch — same
        payload constructor, same generalised runner
        (=workspace--run-one-integration ENTRY PAYLOAD &optional SURFACE-KEY=,
        SURFACE-KEY defaulting to =:on-create=, passed =:on-purge= here),
        same additive/never-signals contract. It fires from =workspace-purge=
        ONLY (never =workspace-delete=), AFTER confirmation, inside the
        home-exists guard, immediately BEFORE the recursive =delete-directory=
        of =:home=, so consumers can clean resources anchored under =:home=
        (git worktrees) while the dir still exists. Its result alist never
        gates whether the purge proceeds.

   producer_side:
     file: config/workspaces/integrations.org
     functions:
       - workspace-register-integration   # (id &key label on-create menu on-purge) -> id
       - workspace--integration-payload    # (name home context) -> payload plist
       - workspace--run-one-integration    # (entry payload &optional surface-key) -> (id . outcome); surface-key defaults :on-create
       - workspace--dispatch-create-integrations  # (name home context) -> result alist, never signals
       - workspace--dispatch-purge-integrations   # (name home context) -> result alist, never signals; runs :on-purge in registration order
     storage: "workspace--integrations  (ordered alist (ID . PLIST))"
     contract: |
       Registration is pure data manipulation. Both dispatchers NEVER signal.
       Handlers without :on-create are skipped by creation-dispatch; handlers
       without :on-purge are skipped by purge-dispatch. ONE normaliser
       (workspace--run-one-integration) serves every surface via SURFACE-KEY.
       :menu is a (KEY . COMMAND) cons consumed by the transient.

   consumer_side:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/workspace-integration.org
       function: jf/gptel--workspace-on-create / jf/gptel--workspace-add-session
       attaches_via: "(with-eval-after-load 'workspaces (workspace-register-integration 'gptel-session ...))"
       note: |
         First real client (cycle-2/6). Load-order-independent registration.
     - file: config/workspaces/git-worktrees.org
       function: jf/workspace--add-worktree (:menu) / jf/workspace--worktree-on-purge (:on-purge)
       attaches_via: "(with-eval-after-load 'workspaces (with-eval-after-load 'magit (workspace-register-integration 'git-worktree :menu (cons \"w\" ...) :on-purge ...)))"
       note: |
         SECOND real client (cycle-7). DOUBLE-gated (workspaces + magit), so
         absent magit ⇒ no registration (not an error). The first consumer to
         exercise =:on-purge=; pressure-tested the registry's genericity and
         it held. See =register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer=.
     - file: config/workspaces/workspaces-transient.org
       function: workspace-menu (Integrations group via :setup-children)
       reads: "workspace--integrations entries that carry a :menu"
       note: |
         The transient walks the registry to populate its Integrations
         group dynamically; it names no consumer.

   directionality:
     forbidden: |
       =config/workspaces/*.el= MUST NOT name any consumer's symbols
       (e.g. no =gptel-*= in the registry or the transient). Consumers
       attach; workspaces iterates a generic alist.
     permitted: |
       Any subsystem MAY call =workspace-register-integration= from its
       own side, guarded by =with-eval-after-load 'workspaces= so it is
       load-order-independent and degrades cleanly when workspaces is absent.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/workspace-integration-anchor-payload
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-integration-outcome
     - register/boundary/gptel-sessions-workspace-consult
     - register/boundary/workspace-scaffold-pipeline
     - register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
     - register/invariant/workspaces-transient-deferred-require
     - register/invariant/git-worktree-default-directory-binding

   cited_by_tasks:
     - integration-registry-core
     - wire-create-dispatch
     - workspaces-transient-menu
     - gptel-session-integration
     - registry-on-purge-surface     # cycle-7: added :on-purge surface
     - purge-teardown-dispatch        # cycle-7: wired purge dispatch
     - git-worktrees-module-scaffold  # cycle-7: second consumer
     - birth-transient-offer          # cycle-7
     - verify-change                  # cycle-7
   discovered_from: workspace-integration-menu
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): STAYS
     CONFIRMED; pins 2 and 3 AMENDED by worktree-candidates-git-first,
     canonical-predicate-consolidation and ownership-and-picker-polish
     (spec delta "Soft repo-source selection", BREAKING: projectile demoted
     then REMOVED as a source, DD-12; magit linked worktrees replaced by
     their main worktree, DD-13; one admission predicate). DD-15
     (b32523b8): =magit-repository-directories= = (("~/src" . 1)) when
     ~/src exists (config/major-modes/magit.el:16-22), so magit's list is
     now populated on this machine and the picker's cost is live
     (end-of-cycle finding 6). Pins 1, 4, 5 unchanged. Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1789935943/reconciliations/boundary-workspace-git-worktrees-consumer.md

     cycle-20260612-164043 (add-workspace-git-worktrees) integrate:
     created CONFIRMED from the merged, clean-reviewed shapes in
     =config/workspaces/git-worktrees.el= (tasks
     =git-worktrees-module-scaffold=, =add-worktree-command=,
     =on-purge-teardown-handler=). The SECOND consumer of the integration
     registry (after gptel-session), and the first to use =:on-purge=.

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): RECONCILED (was confirmed). Pins 2, 4 and 5
     hold; pins 1 and 3 and the directionality text had become false and
     are re-stated. (1) Pin 1's "registration is the SOLE coupling" is
     false twice over: the module now calls CORE's
     =workspace--same-file-p= unguarded (=jf/workspace--worktree-children=,
     git-worktrees.el:262; workspaces-file-equal-p-timestamp-race,
     arch-on-touch-1790453714-56), and portable-bookmarks now calls this
     module's =:menu= command =jf/workspace--add-worktree= directly (the
     missing-repo chooser; arch-on-touch-1790453714-32). (2) Pin 3: the
     command honours an optional payload =:source-repo= — present and a
     local directory: the worktree is made from it with NO source prompt
     and NO candidate discovery; present and unusable: =(failed . REASON)=
     with no prompt and no magit call; absent: the prompt as before. This
     closes the DIVERGENCE the missing-repo on-touch found against the real
     command (arch-on-touch-1790453714-31: it read only =:name= / =:home=
     and re-prompted with a free-form path; fixed by
     discovery-memo-dead-helpers-and-picker-scope, merge 4dec801c). (3) Both
     candidate sources are built inside ONE portable-bookmarks discovery
     call through a second public symbol, the scope
     =workspace-bookmark-call-with-discovery-memo=, reached through
     =jf/workspace--worktree-in-one-discovery-call=, which barriers the scope
     (arch-on-touch-1790453714-47). Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/boundary-workspace-git-worktrees-consumer.md
   load_bearing: true
   title: git-worktrees module — droppable registry consumer (worktrees inside a workspace home)
   purpose: |
     The first-party but DROPPABLE module that makes git worktrees a
     first-class part of the workspace lifecycle, attaching to the
     workspace integration registry from its own side. Pins:

     1. =DOUBLE-GATED ATTACHMENT=: registration
        (=workspace-register-integration=) is this module's only ATTACHMENT
        to core and is nested inside
        =(with-eval-after-load 'workspaces (with-eval-after-load 'magit ...))=
        (git-worktrees.el:318-323). Absent magit ⇒ no =git-worktree=
        integration registered (NOT an error). Removing the single
        =jf/load-module= line for =config/workspaces/git-worktrees.el= in
        =workspaces.org= leaves workspaces fully functional. It is NOT the
        sole coupling (cycle-1790453714): the module also CALLS core's
        identity predicate =workspace--same-file-p= (git-worktrees.el:262,
        unguarded, no =declare-function=; data-model loads first and is not
        droppable), and a sibling consumer calls INTO it — portable-bookmarks'
        missing-repo chooser funcalls the =:menu= command
        =jf/workspace--add-worktree= with an anchor payload (quoted,
        fboundp-guarded, and offered only while core reports the command
        registered as an integration's =:menu= command; see
        =register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer= sibling_consumer).
        This module must therefore keep registering the command BY SYMBOL
        (=:menu (cons "w" #'jf/workspace--add-worktree)=): a wrapper would
        silently withdraw the bookmark prompt's "add worktree here" option
        (arch-on-touch-1790453714-57).
     2. =MAGIT IS THE GIT BACKEND= for every git operation THIS module
        performs — =magit-worktree-branch= (create on new branch),
        =magit-worktree-delete= (remove), =magit-list-worktrees= (enumerate
        its own children + resolve source repo), =magit-main-branch= (main
        detection), =magit-branch-merged-p= + =magit-branch-delete=
        (GUARDED, merged-only, never force). git-worktrees.el makes NO
        non-magit git call (grep process-file/call-process/shell-command:
        zero). Read-only candidate DISCOVERY is delegated to
        portable-bookmarks' runner through the public function (amended
        cycle-1789935943); that is the discovery module's subprocess, not a
        second backend here. Every magit call is bound to the right repo via
        =register/invariant/git-worktree-default-directory-binding=.
     3. =SOFT REPO-SOURCE SELECTION, ONE ADMISSION TEST= (amended
        cycle-1789935943; DD-12, DD-13; cycle-1790453714):
        =jf/workspace--worktree-repo-candidates=
        (git-worktrees.el:129; pure, never prompts) merges, in order, and
        builds BOTH inside ONE portable-bookmarks discovery call
        (=jf/workspace--worktree-in-one-discovery-call=, :93-127 -> the
        public scope =workspace-bookmark-call-with-discovery-memo=,
        fboundp-guarded; unbound -> the build runs unscoped), so a
        repository reached from both sources asks each git question once:
        (a) the git-derived source,
            =(workspace-bookmark-canonical-checkouts-in-view)=
            (=jf/workspace--worktree-git-derived-candidates=, :56-70);
        (b) =(magit-list-repos)= when magit is loaded, first stripped of
            remote (TRAMP) and non-string entries by
            =jf/workspace--worktree-local-only= (:26; =file-remote-p= on the
            NAME only — no connection, so building the list never touches
            the network and the floor is always reachable), then passed
            through =(workspace-bookmark-canonical-checkouts-in-view DIRS)=
            (=jf/workspace--worktree-canonical-only=, :72-91).
        Projectile and project.el are NOT consulted (DD-12;
        git-worktrees-spec.el:128 pins it). portable-bookmarks' single
        predicate (=workspace-bookmark--offerable-canonical-p=) is the
        admission test for EVERY candidate the picker OFFERS: a magit-listed LINKED worktree
        contributes its repository's MAIN worktree, which magit need not
        list (the new worktree is created off main either way; only the
        default directory name follows the main worktree); a
        workspace-owned checkout or a husk contributes nothing. The module
        names exactly TWO portable-bookmarks symbols, both public (the entry
        point and the scope), each =fboundp=-guarded at CALL time AND
        error-barriered, never =require='d (DD-10; tripwire
        git-worktrees-spec.el:735). Unbound: the git-derived source is nil,
        magit's list passes through UNCLASSIFIED and the build runs
        unscoped; signalling: one warning per collection
        (=jf/workspace--worktree-warning-state=, :6) and that source
        contributes nothing; a SCOPE that signals before the build gives one
        warning and the build runs unscoped, after it gives one warning and
        the build's value stands; an error from the build itself propagates;
        =quit= is never caught; the build runs once. The merged list is de-duped by truename
        (=jf/workspace--worktree-dedupe-dirs=, :49; first spelling kept —
        needed across the two calls) and filtered by
        =jf/workspace--worktree-git-repo-p= (:111-113); both are the
        unbound-passthrough path's FLOOR, not the admission test
        (end-of-cycle finding 8: do not take =--git-repo-p= for the safety
        test). The =read-directory-name= floor and free-form entry
        (=jf/workspace--worktree-read-repo=, :164, require-match nil) are
        unchanged for a payload WITHOUT =:source-repo= (the transient's;
        known bug: .tasks/worktree-read-repo-empty-input.md). It is the SOLE
        prompting fn for the source and is SKIPPED when the payload carries
        =:source-repo= (cycle-1790453714, =jf/workspace--add-worktree=
        :191-237): present and an existing LOCAL directory -> the worktree is
        made from it, with no candidate build and no admission re-test (the
        caller decided it — today only portable-bookmarks' missing-repo
        chooser, handing the resolver's already-admitted =:checkout=);
        present but not a local directory (missing, nil, non-string,
        remote) -> =(failed . "source repository is not a directory: ...")=
        with no prompt and no magit call; absent -> the prompt as before.
        The branch prompt (=read-string=) is asked in every case.
     4. =NO PERSISTED GIT STATE=: repo/branch/worktree-set are DERIVED on
        demand from disk + git; the workspace plist gains no =:repo=/=:branch=
        slot (the closed serialize allow-list cannot leak them).
     5. =RESULT PROTOCOL=: the =:menu= command (=jf/workspace--add-worktree=)
        and =:on-purge= handler (=jf/workspace--worktree-on-purge=) return
        the registry vocabulary =ok= / =skipped= / =(failed . REASON)=
        (=register/vocabulary/workspace-integration-outcome=); teardown
        continues past a dirty worktree (records it, never aborts the purge).
        The direct caller (portable-bookmarks) consumes the same vocabulary
        and signals on anything else. Core never runs the =:on-purge=
        teardown for a REMOTE home (remote homes are unsupported; purge of
        one unregisters only, workspaces.el:182-270).

   producer_side:
     file: config/workspaces/git-worktrees.org
     functions:
       - jf/workspace--worktree-repo-candidates  # () -> list of expanded git-repo dirs; pure, no prompt (git-worktrees.el:129)
       - jf/workspace--worktree-read-repo         # () -> one repo dir; the SOLE source prompt (floor included); skipped for a :source-repo payload (:164)
       - jf/workspace--add-worktree               # (payload) -> ok|(failed . reason); :menu surface; honours optional :source-repo (:191); also called directly by portable-bookmarks
       - jf/workspace--worktree-on-purge          # (payload) -> ok|skipped|(failed . reason); :on-purge surface
       - jf/workspace--worktree-children          # (home) -> list of (:dir :branch :source) for child worktrees
       - jf/workspace--worktree-warn-discovery    # added cycle-1789935943: one warning per collection
       - jf/workspace--worktree-local-only        # added cycle-1789935943: drop remote/non-string entries unprobed (:26)
       - jf/workspace--worktree-dedupe-dirs       # added cycle-1789935943: truename de-dupe, first spelling kept (:49)
       - jf/workspace--worktree-git-derived-candidates  # added cycle-1789935943: source (a) (:56-70)
       - jf/workspace--worktree-canonical-only    # added cycle-1789935943: source (b) admission (:72-91)
       - jf/workspace--worktree-in-one-discovery-call  # added cycle-1790453714: the barriered discovery-scope call (:93-127)
       - jf/workspace--worktree-git-repo-p        # the post-filter floor (:156)
       - jf/workspace--worktree-sanitize-branch   # branch-prompt default (:177)
     attaches_via: "(with-eval-after-load 'workspaces (with-eval-after-load 'magit (workspace-register-integration 'git-worktree :label \"git worktree\" :menu (cons \"w\" #'jf/workspace--add-worktree) :on-purge #'jf/workspace--worktree-on-purge)))"

   environment_note: |
     =magit-repository-directories= is set in config/major-modes/magit.org
     (DD-15, b32523b8; magit.el:16-22), not here; the picker's cost is
     linear in its size (~4 spawns per magit entry at 20944160; 3 per
     canonical main clone and 1 per further checkout of a repository already
     seen at f860103c — the floor under the two-signal canonical test;
     =register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery= cost_contract).

   directionality:
     permitted: |
       This module (the CONSUMER side) MAY name magit and git symbols
       freely. It attaches via =workspace-register-integration=, exactly
       as gptel does — the PERMITTED direction of
       =register/boundary/gptel-sessions-workspace-consult=. It MAY name
       exactly TWO portable-bookmarks symbols, both public —
       =workspace-bookmark-canonical-checkouts-in-view= and the scope
       =workspace-bookmark-call-with-discovery-memo= — quoted,
       fboundp-guarded and barriered (git-worktrees-spec.el:735 pins that no
       =workspace-bookmark--= name appears in the tangled file's code). It
       MAY call core's identity predicate =workspace--same-file-p=
       (data-model; the first private core symbol it names,
       cycle-1790453714; unguarded like every consumer's call). It MUST keep
       registering =jf/workspace--add-worktree= by symbol and keep honouring
       =:source-repo= (the inbound portable-bookmarks edge, pin 1).
     forbidden: |
       Workspaces CORE (=data-model=/=integrations=/=workspaces=/=tabs=/
       =workspaces-transient= .el) MUST NOT name magit/git symbols. Verified
       cycle-7 by =verify-change=: the only core hit is the loader path
       STRING "git-worktrees.el", not a live symbol.

   test_corpus:

   related_entries:
