** work-root-activation-seam
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-work-root-activation-seam
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/work-root-activation-seam
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     CONFIRMED cycle-1781716077 integrate. binder-default-directory landed
     exactly ONE setq-local of default-directory in the gptel-chat-mode-hook
     binder, placed after the four identity setq-locals and BEFORE the
     condition-case registry block, normalised via file-name-as-directory +
     expand-file-name, with a genuine branch-dir no-op fallback for keyless
     sessions.  Author-blind review = CLEAN (0 findings): confirmed the seam
     is singular (no duplicate default-directory binding in any tool wrapper
     or second hook), correctly placed, and behaviour-verified by a co-located
     spec.  The implementor independently recommended the speculated->confirmed
     flip.  The READ-side proof that tool calls actually resolve against this
     default-directory is scope-relative-resolution-tests — LANDED
     cycle-1781718724 (merge 4a1af080, relative-resolution-spec.el, 6 specs
     green, author-blind review CLEAN): with default-directory bound to the work
     root a relative path resolves under it and lands in scope, and the SAME
     relative path under a DIFFERENT work root resolves differently (verdict +
     resolved :resource flip), so a hardcoded-root mutant fails.  Additionally
     agent-workroot-and-paths (cycle-1781718724, merge 614b95a7) EXTENDED the
     seam to agents: the agent's own :GPTEL_WORK_ROOT: drawer key is written at
     spawn and the SAME single binder seam sets the agent buffer's
     default-directory on activation (NO second binding point added —
     duplication invariant intact).  Seam confirmed end-to-end (chat + agent,
     validation + execution).
   load_bearing: true
   title: The binder's single default-directory setq-local is the one seam all tool path-resolution flows through
   purpose: |
     There is exactly ONE place a session buffer's default-directory is
     established for relative-path resolution: the gptel-chat-mode-hook
     binder (jf/gptel--bind-session-buffer) reads GPTEL_WORK_ROOT from the
     drawer alist it already scans and does one setq-local. Because
     gptel--handle-tool-use runs every tool funcall inside
     (with-current-buffer (plist-get info :buffer) ...) and info:buffer is
     the originating session buffer, this ONE buffer-local set reaches every
     tool (filesystem + bash, chat + agent) for BOTH validation and
     execution. No per-tool binding point, no second hook. A duplicate
     default-directory binding inside a tool wrapper or a second hook is a
     duplication bug (architect duplication severity = blocking here).

   seam_contract: |
     IN:  drawer-alist (already in hand), optional GPTEL_WORK_ROOT key, and
          jf/gptel--branch-dir (already setq-local'd just above).
     OUT: buffer-local default-directory =
          (file-name-as-directory
           (expand-file-name
            (or (cdr (assoc "GPTEL_WORK_ROOT" drawer-alist))
                jf/gptel--branch-dir)))
     Placed BEFORE the condition-case registry block so it survives a
     registry failure (matching the four identity setq-locals' discipline).
     Keyless sessions: branch-dir fallback == today's find-file value (a
     true no-op).

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/sessions/commands.org
       function: jf/gptel--bind-session-buffer (gptel-chat-mode-hook)
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-filesystem-tools.org
       note: bare (expand-file-name filepath) resolves against default-directory
     - file: config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
       note: bash validator passes directory=default-directory

   related_entries:
     - register/invariant/cwd-scope-agreement
     - register/boundary/drawer-first-identity-resolution
     - register/invariant/activation-and-identity-are-content-not-path
   cited_by_tasks:
     - binder-default-directory
     - scope-relative-resolution-tests
     - agent-workroot-and-paths
     - verify-change
   discovered_from: gptel-work-root-default-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** environment-block-input-neutrality
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-environment-block-input-neutrality
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/environment-block-input-neutrality
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781857401 integrate (2026-06-19): speculated → RECONCILED.
     env-block-builder (merge 8f05e41e) implemented the builder; the
     load-bearing CONTRACT held exactly (no workspaces symbol in executable
     code — the `grep workspace` gate matched only docstring/prose negations;
     reads only default-directory + the drawer; never the scope LOADER nor
     the prior composed value; on-touch + author-blind review CLEAN). What
     RECONCILED is the concrete read mechanism named in the speculation:
       prior_form: "reads the GPTEL_SCOPE_* drawer keys via the existing
         drawer scanner / +-collapse accessor".
       new_form: the verbatim per-key glob LISTS are read via
         org-entry-get-multivalued-property (org's native multi-value
         property support — which IS the +-convention read-back, so no
         custom +-collapse was re-implemented), behind a lazy (require 'org);
         the "is this a scoped session at all?" presence gate uses
         jf/gptel--scan-session-drawer-keys (a distinct, org-independent
         reader). Two readers, complementary: scanner = presence gate
         (drives D5 degradation), org-entry-get-multivalued-property = value
         read.
       why_tests_missed: the speculation named the scanner generically as
         "the accessor"; it is actually the presence gate, while the value
         read is org's multivalued-property accessor. The boundary contract
         (inputs permitted/forbidden) was never wrong — only the prose about
         WHICH primitive does the value read. Tests are green because both
         readers satisfy the contract.
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781857401/reconciliations/
     boundary-environment-block-input-neutrality.md.
     ---
     cycle-1781857401 plan (2026-06-19): forward-mode speculation for
     change gptel-dynamic-environment-preamble. The hard constraint from the
     exploration log: gptel's environment surfacing MUST work with the
     workspaces package absent. Pressure-tested by env-block-builder; the
     verification greps for `workspace` inside the builder must NOT match.
   load_bearing: true
   canonical_engine: gptel-chat--build-environment-block (config/gptel/chat/menu.org)
   title: |
     The environment block is built from intrinsic buffer inputs only —
     never from the workspaces package
   purpose: |
     The environment block (register/shape/environment-block) must be
     derivable from a single buffer with no live dependency on any external
     producer. The block's two inputs are intrinsic to the buffer: the work
     root (default-directory) and the file-access scope (the GPTEL_SCOPE_*
     drawer keys, read via jf/gptel--scan-session-drawer-keys). This entry
     is the boundary between "what the block may read" and "the rest of the
     world": the builder names no workspaces symbol (no featurep/fboundp/
     workspace-* reference), reads no home.org, calls no scope LOADER
     (jf/gptel-scope--load-config expands deny-all defaults for validation —
     the block wants the human-facing patterns AS WRITTEN, so it reads the
     raw drawer keys, design D3).
   boundary_contract: |
     INPUTS the builder may touch: (1) the current buffer's
     default-directory; (2) the buffer's point-min drawer GPTEL_SCOPE_*
     keys, via the existing drawer scanner / +-collapse accessor. INPUTS the
     builder may NOT touch: any workspaces-package symbol, home.org, the
     scope config loader, or any value derived from the prior composed
     gptel--system-message. With the workspaces package entirely unloaded,
     gptel-chat--build-environment-block still returns a valid block and
     signals no error referencing a workspaces function or variable.
   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: gptel-chat--build-environment-block
       usage: |
         SPECULATED. The single function on this boundary; reads only the
         two permitted inputs above. Added by task env-block-builder.
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: gptel-chat--refresh-system-prompt-from-file (widened to composer)
       usage: |
         SPECULATED. Calls the builder unconditionally per send; carries no
         workspaces dependency of its own.
   related_entries:
     - register/shape/environment-block
     - register/boundary/session-content-signature
   cited_by_tasks:
     - env-block-builder
   discovered_from: gptel-dynamic-environment-preamble
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** fragment-render
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-fragment-render
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/fragment-render
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: "cycle-1781883616 integrate: speculation matched. jf/gptel-fragment-render (fragment backend); stages dispatch->render->join; claude section (name . body) -> "<tag>\nbody\n</tag>", body verbatim, sections joined with a blank line. Unimplemented backend logs-then-signals error symbol jf/gptel-fragment-unimplemented-backend (define-error deriving from error). Author-blind review confirmed no Claude/Markdown fall-through."
   load_bearing: true
   title: fragment → backend-delimited text (jf/gptel-fragment-render)
   purpose: |
     The canonical section→block mapping: jf/gptel-fragment-render (fragment
     backend) → a backend-delimited text string. This is THE place section
     bodies become backend syntax; any other site emitting "<tag>...</tag>"
     from a section is a duplication bug. load_bearing because it is the one
     contract whose silent miswording (wrong tag, wrong casing, Markdown
     leak) corrupts every system message and is only caught by golden
     snapshots; golden-snapshot drift is the named risk (design.md Risks).

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: dispatch-on-backend
       input: backend (register/vocabulary/render-backend)
       output: per-backend renderer | reported-unavailable
       can_fail_with: [unimplemented-backend]
       short_circuit_policy: deliberate
       notes: |
         An unimplemented backend MUST be reported (signal / log+signal),
         never fall through to Claude or Markdown
         (register/invariant/renderer-never-markdown). This short-circuit is
         a correctness gate, not an optimisation.
     - n: 2
       name: render-sections
       input: fragment (register/shape/fragment), one section at a time
       output: per-section delimited block
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: |
         For claude: section (name . body) → "<tag>\nbody\n</tag>" where
         tag = register/vocabulary/section-name-to-tag of name. Body emitted
         verbatim (no Markdown re-formatting).
     - n: 3
       name: join
       input: ordered list of per-section blocks
       output: the fragment's rendered text
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: blocks joined in section order; order is significant.

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/renderer-never-markdown
     - register/invariant/static-prerender-dynamic-compose

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: jf/gptel-fragment-render
       enters_at_stage: 1
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: <composer> (composer.md task)
       reads_output_of_stage: 3
     - file: config/gptel/presets/<preset>/  (tangle-time pre-render)
       function: <static pre-render babel step>
       reads_output_of_stage: 3

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** fragment-parse
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-fragment-parse
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/fragment-parse
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: "cycle-1781883616 integrate: speculation matched. jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source accepts an Org string OR file path and is batch-loadable (heading regexp over a temp buffer; no org-mode/interactive deps), satisfying tangle-time pre-render."
   load_bearing: false
   title: org source → fragment (jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source)
   purpose: |
     The mapping from a fragment's Org source (string or file) to a parsed
     fragment value (register/shape/fragment). Top-level Org headings become
     sections (register/shape/section); the declared kind (or static default)
     becomes :kind. MUST stay batch-loadable (no interactive deps) because
     static fragments are pre-rendered at tangle time, when no interactive
     Emacs is available (tangle-time renderer availability is a named risk —
     design.md Risks).

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: read-source
       input: org source string or file path
       output: org buffer / element tree
       can_fail_with: [io-error, parse-error]
       notes: use org-element or a heading regexp; keep batch-loadable.
     - n: 2
       name: extract-kind
       input: source directives
       output: kind symbol (register/vocabulary/fragment-kind)
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: absent kind → static (default).
     - n: 3
       name: extract-sections
       input: top-level headings + bodies
       output: ordered list of (name . body) (register/shape/section)
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: bodies trimmed; order = source order.

   cross_stage_invariants: []

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: jf/gptel-fragment--parse-source
       enters_at_stage: 1
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: jf/gptel-fragment-render
       reads_output_of_stage: 3
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: <composer> (composer.md task)
       reads_output_of_stage: 3

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** composer-compose
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-composer-compose
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/composer-compose
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781885402 integrate: speculated → confirmed. jf/gptel-fragment-compose
     implements all three stages (resolve-composition → realize-fragments → join)
     as speculated; on-touch + end-of-cycle Architect both verified. One refinement
     (not a divergence): the `backend` arg is currently inert at compose time
     because static refs are pre-rendered (consistent with
     static-prerender-dynamic-compose) and dynamic fns own their own formatting —
     candidate to drop; probe in the migrate/source tasks.
     cycle-1781900938 (migrate-environment, a second consumer): the backend-arg-inert
     refinement is now CORROBORATED — the dynamic env fragment funcalls its :fn and
     owns its own output formatting, never reading `backend' at compose time. The
     drop candidate stands (would simplify jf/gptel-fragment-compose's signature);
     not dropped this cycle (no behaviour change), carried as a future cleanup.
     cycle-1781941375 (migrate-prelude-preamble) addendum — CONSUMER CLARIFICATION
     (not a divergence; arch-cycle-1781941375-eoc-2, design-sanctioned): the chat
     consumer (menu.org) DOES go through the composer (prelude lead via the seam, env
     dynamic tail). The persistent-agent consumer (persistent-agent.org) does NOT call
     jf/gptel-fragment-compose: at session-CREATION it materializes only the static
     prefix (agent-preamble seam text + the preset's rendered role) into the on-disk
     sibling system-prompt file; the dynamic environment is composed PER SEND on agent
     reload, deliberately kept OUT of the on-disk static prefix (preserves the cacheable
     prefix per static-prerender-dynamic-compose). So this boundary's "consumers read
     stage-3 output" is exact for chat; for the agent the composer's static-prefix
     contract is honored via direct seam read at creation time + per-send env. The
     consumers list below overstated the agent coupling; treat the agent as a
     static-prefix consumer of the seam, env-as-per-send.
   load_bearing: false
   title: ordered fragment list → effective system message (composer)
   purpose: |
     The composer maps an ordered fragment list (register/shape/composition)
     to the effective system message string: it renders each fragment in
     order and joins them. Static fragments consume pre-rendered text (no
     per-send rendering); dynamic fragments are evaluated at compose time and
     default to the tail (register/invariant/static-prerender-dynamic-compose).
     The default fragment list is context-derived
     (register/invariant/context-default-composition) with an override seam.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: resolve-composition
       input: send context + preset config/role fragment
       output: ordered fragment list (composition)
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: |
         Default list is context-derived (chat vs agent); a composition MAY
         override via the seam, but the common case supplies only a role
         fragment in the role position.
     - n: 2
       name: realize-fragments
       input: each fragment in order
       output: per-fragment text
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: |
         static fragment → its pre-rendered text (no rendering). dynamic
         fragment → evaluate :fn at compose time. Order preserved.
     - n: 3
       name: join
       input: ordered per-fragment texts
       output: effective system message string
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: |
         join in list order; dynamic-at-tail keeps the static prefix stable
         for prompt caching.

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/static-prerender-dynamic-compose
     - register/invariant/context-default-composition

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/fragments.org
       function: <composer> (composer.md task)
       enters_at_stage: 1
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: <chat composer wiring> (migrate-prelude-preamble.md task)
       reads_output_of_stage: 3
     - file: config/gptel/tools/persistent-agent.org
       function: <agent system-prompt writer> (migrate-prelude-preamble.md task)
       reads_output_of_stage: 3

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** preset-org-to-registration
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-preset-org-to-registration
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/preset-org-to-registration
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781885402 integrate: speculated → confirmed. registration loads tangled
     preset .el (loader convention config/gptel/presets/<name>/preset.el; preset name
     = source basename); scope keys (:paths :shell-commands :bash-tools :scope-profile)
     extracted into jf/gptel-preset--scope-defaults and stripped; :mode → --mode-defaults
     (default "org-mode"); :org-roam-patterns warned+dropped, not extracted; register
     idempotent (re-load updates, no duplicate). Render-locus clarified: the role text
     is pre-rendered at TANGLE time inside preset.el; registration forwards that
     pre-rendered :system and does NOT itself call jf/gptel-fragment-render (matches
     stage-1 of the speculation). YAML pipeline deleted (parse/normalize/coerce gone).
     cycle-1781900938 addendum (two presets authored against this boundary): (1)
     registration forwards the post-extraction keys to gptel-make-preset UNCHANGED —
     so each forwarded key's VALUE must satisfy gptel's own contract; in particular
     :backend must be the gptel backend NAME STRING, not a renderer symbol (see
     register/shape/preset-config-plist reconcile + arch-cycle-1781900938-2). (2)
     Render-locus realized concretely as LOAD-TIME render-once cached in a defconst
     inside preset.el (jf/gptel-preset-<name>--system), evaluated when the .el loads;
     functionally equivalent to tangle-time bake for the static-prerender invariant
     (no per-send re-render), via a constant rather than a baked string literal.
     cycle-1781941375 addendum (wire-fragment-sources-load): the <name>/preset.el
     discovery this entry describes gained a SIBLING — a flat-glob loader for
     presets/sources/*.el (register/boundary/sources-directory-load, confirmed this
     cycle) loading fragment SOURCE modules (the dynamic env, the static prelude/
     preamble). Both run from config/gptel/presets/registration.* and are sequenced in
     gptel.org after presets/fragments.el. arch-cycle-1781941375-eoc-1 flags the two
     loaders share ~all skeleton (differ only in subdir-descent vs flat-glob); a
     shared-helper extraction is the next-plan meta-discovery. workspace-flip relies on
     this boundary: it sets jf/gptel-workspace-initial-preset to the preset NAME symbol
     'workspace-assistant (= source basename registered via <name>/preset.el).
     cycle-1781944619 (FINAL) addendum: (1) the register-all <name>/preset.el discovery
     now routes through the shared jf/gptel--load-directory helper (with
     jf/gptel-preset--discover-preset-els as its thin discovery fn) alongside the sources
     loader — the eoc-1 duplication between them is closed; the descent contract itself is
     unchanged. (2) delete-old-presets removed the 11 legacy flat .md presets, which this
     boundary already ignored (it descends <name>/preset.el subdirs only) — confirming the
     flat .md files were inert at registration. NOTE (out-of-scope, externalised
     .tasks/repoint-session-creation-default-preset-off-executor): the session-creation
     default 'executor names a preset with no <name>/preset.el, so it resolves to nothing —
     pre-existing (executor.md was already inert), now without an on-disk trace.
   load_bearing: false
   title: preset .org → tangled .el → gptel-make-preset registration
   purpose: |
     The pipeline from a preset .org (config block + role fragment) to a
     registered preset. Tangling produces an .el that, when loaded, calls
     gptel-make-preset. Scope/mode keys are extracted into
     jf/gptel-preset--scope-defaults and stripped before registration;
     :system is set to the rendered role text. REPLACES the old YAML .md
     pipeline (parse / normalize / coerce deleted — design.md Decision 6).
     Load order is a named risk: the renderer/composer (fragments.org) must
     load before registration and before chat/agent/env consumers — sequence
     explicitly in gptel.org.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: author + pre-render role
       input: preset .org (config block + static role fragment)
       output: tangled .el with rendered role text inlined
       can_fail_with: [tangle-error, renderer-unavailable-at-tangle]
       notes: |
         role fragment pre-renders at tangle time via fragment-render;
         needs the renderer batch-loadable (tangle-time availability risk).
     - n: 2
       name: extract-scope-keys
       input: preset config plist (register/shape/preset-config-plist)
       output: jf/gptel-preset--scope-defaults entry + config sans scope keys
       can_fail_with: []
       short_circuit_policy: none
       notes: |
         extracts :paths :shell-commands :bash-tools :scope-profile into the
         alist keyed by preset-name symbol; REMOVES them from the plist
         passed downstream. :org-roam-patterns is NOT a scope key.
     - n: 3
       name: register
       input: config plist (scope-stripped) + :system = rendered role
       output: gptel-make-preset call (idempotent; updates existing entry)
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: |
         preset name = source basename; re-loading updates rather than
         duplicates (register idempotency).

   cross_stage_invariants: []

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/presets/registration.org
       function: <tangled-preset loader> (registration-rewrite.md task)
       enters_at_stage: 1
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/scope/...  (session creation)
       function: <scope init reading jf/gptel-preset--scope-defaults>
       reads_output_of_stage: 2
     - file: <gptel upstream>
       function: gptel-make-preset / gptel-get-preset
       reads_output_of_stage: 3

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** sources-directory-load
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-sources-directory-load
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/sources-directory-load
   tier: boundary
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781941375 plan forward-mode: NEW speculative boundary. Seeded from
     cycle-1781900938 handshake meta-discovery #3 (source-load-wiring): migrating a
     defun into presets/sources/*.el creates a production-dark seam until gptel.org
     loads it. migrate-environment shipped its env tail dark (the source is never
     loaded at runtime; jf/gptel-fragment-environment-fn stays #'ignore, menu.el's
     soft (require … nil t) silently no-ops). migrate-prelude-preamble will add two
     MORE such sources (emacs-prelude.el, agent-preamble.el) with the same need.
     This boundary speculates a SINGLE directory-load mechanism for presets/sources/
     mirroring the <name>/preset.el discovery in preset-org-to-registration, so one
     task (wire-fragment-sources-load) covers all three sources rather than an
     explicit per-source load list that must be extended each time. Pressure-tested
     by wire-fragment-sources-load this cycle.
     cycle-1781941375 integrate: speculated → CONFIRMED. wire-fragment-sources-load
     implemented all three stages exactly as speculated: a flat-glob directory loader
     (jf/gptel-fragment-sources-directory / load-sources-all) loads presets/sources/*.el
     once at gptel init, AFTER presets/fragments.el (seam defvars defined) and BEFORE
     the chat/agent/env consumers; post-condition holds (jf/gptel-fragment-environment-fn
     is the env producer, not #'ignore, in a loaded session). Implementor push-back
     confirmed the directory loader is the right mechanism (both open questions —
     inter-source ordering, must-not-autoload — resolved in its favor). On-touch
     architect (arch-cycle-1781941375-1) verified load order; end-of-cycle architect
     (arch-cycle-1781941375-eoc-3) confirmed implemented-as-speculated. The prior
     env-source production-wiring NB on static-prerender-dynamic-compose is now CLEARED
     (env source is loaded at init). KNOWN REFINEMENT (advisory, not divergent): the
     loader swallows a per-source load-time error with a warn-only log, leaving that
     source's seam at its default (dark) — same gap as the mirrored
     jf/gptel-preset-register-all. Externalised to
     .tasks/harden-sources-loader-against-partial-load-failure.md (reviewer +
     arch-cycle-1781941375-eoc-1/eoc-4); next plan meta-discovery proposes extracting
     ONE shared loader helper and setting the load-error policy symmetrically for
     presets and sources at once. Stage-2 can_fail_with:[load-error] remains the
     accurate contract; the failure POLICY is the open refinement, not the shape.
     cycle-1781944619 (FINAL) RECONCILED — the open load-error POLICY is now closed.
     extract-shared-loader-helper-failpolicy extracted ONE shared
     jf/gptel--load-directory(directory discover-fn seam label) helper that BOTH this
     loader and jf/gptel-preset-register-all route through (closing
     arch-cycle-1781941375-eoc-1 duplication→blocking), and gave it a symmetric
     NON-silent fail-policy: a per-entry condition-case logs the load error at ERROR
     level (naming the file + dark seam) and records it in jf/gptel--loader-failures,
     queryable via jf/gptel-loader-failures / jf/gptel-loader-seam-dark-p — a single bad
     source no longer hard-fails gptel init, but its dark seam is now DETECTABLE rather
     than silently warn-logged (closing eoc-4 invariant-gap). Stage-3's "seam NOT
     #'ignore after init" post-condition is now observably checkable. Author-blind
     review CLEAN (0 findings); duplication verified genuinely eliminated (the two
     discovery halves — subdir-descent vs flat-glob — are legitimately distinct, not
     copy-paste). The externalised .tasks/harden-sources-loader-against-partial-load-failure
     was PROMOTED in-change this cycle to realize this.
   load_bearing: false
   title: presets/sources/*.el → loaded at gptel init (directory loader)
   purpose: |
     The pipeline by which fragment SOURCE modules under
     config/gptel/presets/sources/ (the dynamic environment fragment today;
     the static emacs-prelude / agent-preamble fragments next) are loaded at
     gptel initialization so their seam-population side effects run in
     production, not only under absolute-path test loads. Mirrors
     register/boundary/preset-org-to-registration's <name>/preset.el
     discovery, but for flat source files in a single sources/ directory.
     Load order is the named risk: the sources must load AFTER the
     renderer/composer (config/gptel/presets/fragments.el) — they reference
     composer seam defvars — and BEFORE the chat/agent/env consumers that
     read those seams (menu.el, persistent-agent.el). Sequence explicitly in
     gptel.org, consistent with the load-order risk already named in
     preset-org-to-registration and static-prerender-dynamic-compose.

   stages:
     - n: 1
       name: discover-sources
       input: config/gptel/presets/sources/ directory
       output: ordered list of source .el files to load
       can_fail_with: [sources-dir-missing]
       notes: |
         SPECULATED: a directory loader (e.g. jf/gptel-fragment--load-sources-all)
         enumerates presets/sources/*.el, parallel to register-all's <name>/preset.el
         descent. Alternative under consideration: an explicit per-source load list
         in gptel.org. The directory loader is preferred (handshake meta-discovery #3)
         so emacs-prelude.el / agent-preamble.el are picked up without editing gptel.org
         again. Whichever wins, this stage's CONTRACT is "every *.el under sources/ is
         loaded exactly once at init".
     - n: 2
       name: load-in-order
       input: discovered source list
       output: sources loaded (seam defvars populated)
       can_fail_with: [load-error]
       notes: |
         loaded AFTER fragments.el (composer/renderer + seam defvars defined) and
         BEFORE the consumers (menu.el chat composer, persistent-agent.el writer).
         Respects the gptel load-order invariant (skills → gptel-agent → tool defs
         → sessions → … ). Inserted near the jf/gptel-preset-register-all call
         (gptel.org ~274–278).
     - n: 3
       name: seam-populated
       input: loaded sources
       output: composer seams are live producers, not defaults
       can_fail_with: []
       notes: |
         POST-CONDITION: after gptel init, jf/gptel-fragment-environment-fn is NOT
         #'ignore (env source loaded); migrate-prelude-preamble's prelude/preamble
         seam defvars are likewise non-empty once its sources land. This is the
         observable property the "dark seam" failure violated.

   cross_stage_invariants:
     - register/invariant/static-prerender-dynamic-compose
     - register/invariant/context-default-composition

   producers:
     - file: config/gptel/gptel.org
       function: <source-load step> (wire-fragment-sources-load.md task)
       enters_at_stage: 2
     - file: config/gptel/presets/registration.org
       function: <jf/gptel-fragment--load-sources-all OR register-all extension> (wire-fragment-sources-load.md task)
       enters_at_stage: 1
   consumers:
     - file: config/gptel/chat/menu.org
       function: env tail via jf/gptel-fragment-environment-fn (depends on stage 3)
       reads_output_of_stage: 3
     - file: config/gptel/presets/sources/emacs-prelude.org
       function: <prelude source loaded by this mechanism> (migrate-prelude-preamble.md task)
       reads_output_of_stage: 2
     - file: config/gptel/presets/sources/agent-preamble.org
       function: <preamble source loaded by this mechanism> (migrate-prelude-preamble.md task)
       reads_output_of_stage: 2

   validator: |
     wire-fragment-sources-load proves this boundary. Acceptance: (1) grep shows
     gptel.el loads the sources at init (sources/environment | load-sources |
     fragment-environment); (2) in a loaded session jf/gptel-fragment-environment-fn
     is NOT #'ignore; (3) the chat menu + presets suites stay green; (4) full-suite
     failing-set == baseline.

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** portable-bookmarks-consumer
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-portable-bookmarks-consumer
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): CONFIRMED.
     All four pins and both directionality clauses hold (module-scaffold
     on-touch CONFIRMS; re-verified end-of-cycle) — three field edits only
     (validator 2, the permitted list, a new sibling_consumer field;
     end-of-cycle finding 5). The speculated feature-symbol mismatch is the
     package convention, not a one-off (disc-module-scaffold-and-fixtures-1:
     data-model.el -> workspace-data-model, git-worktrees.el ->
     workspace-git-worktrees; 9 of the 12 modules in config/workspaces/
     rename, the other 3 have basenames that are already prefixed). Loader
     line: workspaces.el:62, after workspaces-mode (:56), before
     workspaces-transient (:66); git-worktrees loads at :49, BEFORE this
     module, which is why the sibling call is guarded at call time (DD-10).
     Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1789935943/reconciliations/boundary-portable-bookmarks-consumer.md

     cycle-1789935943 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     populated from design D10 (module placement and load order) + proposal
     "New module" + task =module-scaffold-and-fixtures=. The THIRD consumer
     of the workspaces one-way rule (after gptel-sessions and git-worktrees)
     and the first that is INSIDE config/workspaces/ while still being a
     consumer. SPECULATED on the feature symbol: the task says
     =(provide 'workspace-portable-bookmarks)= from a file named
     =portable-bookmarks.el=, which is a deliberate mismatch worth
     confirming rather than assuming.

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): RECONCILED (was confirmed). The four pins and
     forbidden (a)/(b) HOLD (re-verified: zero =magit= / =projectile= in
     the module; no core .el names =workspace-bookmark=; loader at
     workspaces.el:62 between :56 and :66; nothing in init.org). What moved
     is the PERMITTED core surface and the SIBLING edge, all derived from
     the code at f860103c, not from the drafts: (1) the permitted list
     gains =workspace--same-file-p= and =workspace--path-within-p= (core
     data-model; called UNGUARDED, deliberately — data-model loads first and
     is not droppable; arch-on-touch-1790453714-56),
     =workspace--integration-payload= and
     =workspace--integration-menu-command-p= (core integrations;
     fboundp-guarded; the latter is the published accessor that replaced a
     raw read of =workspace--integrations=, arch-on-touch-1790453714-57),
     and =workspace--restoring-layout-leaf= (core layouts; read only when
     =boundp=); it LOSES =workspace-anti-save-predicates= (the predicate was
     removed by user decision ask-cycle-1790453714-3). =workspace--registry=
     STAYS on the list: two guarded raw reads remain
     (=--workspace-home=, =--broken-workspace-p=). (2) sibling_consumer is
     now TWO-WAY and SOFT (arch-on-touch-1790453714-32): this module names
     exactly ONE git-worktrees symbol, the private =:menu= command
     =jf/workspace--add-worktree=, quoted and fboundp-guarded, gated on core's
     registration accessor; git-worktrees names TWO public symbols of this
     module. (3) A new core_protocol field records what core now knows:
     a layout leaf's jump runs with =workspace--restoring-layout-leaf=
     bound, and a handler's =user-error= is a DECLINE for EVERY handler
     (arch-on-touch-1790453714-41, redrafted after the fix pass removed the
     =inhibit-interaction= binding). The reverse tripwire the on-touch asked
     for does NOT exist as a spec; validator 5 is its grep. Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/boundary-portable-bookmarks-consumer.md

     cycle-1790604160 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 3 against c65efb86; status unchanged. Re-verified:
     validators 1-5 hold at c65efb86 (zero magit/projectile; loader
     workspaces.el:62 between :56 and :66; the two git-worktrees public
     symbols only; =jf/= surface = =jf/emacs-dir= + quoted
     =jf/workspace--add-worktree= at :613, :839, :842). CORRECTION recorded
     (eoc-1790453714-01): =workspace--integration-menu-command-p=
     (integrations.el:41-51) answers REGISTRATION — "is this command some
     integration's :menu command" — NOT menu VISIBILITY; the Integrations
     group's health gate is =workspace--menu-healthy-p=
     (workspaces-transient.el:11-14, applied at :98) and lives in a module
     this consumer may not name (workspaces-transient loads after it,
     deferred). So the consumer's "runnable" test
     (=--add-worktree-runnable-p=, :605-616) is registration only, and the
     health test is the consumer's own —
     =register/invariant/add-worktree-offer-shares-workspace-health-gate=.
     The permitted core surface does NOT grow: the gate uses
     =workspace--broken-p= and =workspace--home= (already listed) plus
     =file-remote-p= / =file-directory-p=. # owner:
     add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate (cycle-1790604160) for that clause;
     # owner: outcome-action-mapping (cycle-1790604160) for the inbound
     sibling edge's result handling (=skipped= arm kept, strict reading: see
     =register/vocabulary/workspace-integration-outcome=) and for removing
     the unreachable =(unless (fboundp 'jf/workspace--add-worktree) (error
     ...))= at :839-840 (eoc-1790453714-04) — the guard stays at :613 (the
     offer gate).
   load_bearing: true
   title: portable-bookmarks module — droppable bookmark consumer of the workspace registry
   purpose: |
     The module boundary for workspace-relative bookmark resolution. It is a
     CONSUMER: it may name =bookmark=, the workspace registry, and =git=;
     workspaces CORE must never name it. Four pins:

     1. =LOADED FROM THE PACKAGE LOADER, NOT init.org=: the loader line lives
        in =config/workspaces/workspaces.org=, because =init.org='s
        =jf/enabled-modules= holds the single entry
        =("workspaces/workspaces" ...)= for this whole package. Adding it to
        =init.org= instead would load it OUTSIDE the package's own ordering
        guarantees.
     2. =LOAD ORDER=: after =tabs.el= (owns =workspace--registry= and
        =workspace--current-name=) and after =persistence.el= (defines
        =workspace-restore=), therefore inserted between the existing
        =workspaces-mode.el= and =workspaces-transient.el= loader lines
        (confirmed: workspaces.el:56 / :62 / :66).
     3. =NO MAGIT, NO PROJECTILE=: git is reached by subprocess
        (=process-file=), never through magit. DD-6 recorded the durable
        reason: magit is a porcelain over the git CLI and would not remove a
        single subprocess, so it would be a dependency bought for
        convenience wrappers alone — not merely "avoids inheriting
        =git-worktrees='s hard magit dependency". See
        =register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer= pin 2, which is
        the OPPOSITE choice made deliberately for a different module.
     4. =DROPPABLE=: removing the single =jf/load-module= line removes the
        advice and returns behaviour to stock. Stamped records keep their
        absolute =filename= and keep working. Rollback is one line. (The
        git-worktrees picker then loses its git-derived source and passes
        magit's list through unclassified, and its discovery scope runs
        unscoped — see sibling_consumer. Core's layout chain keeps its
        decline gate, which then only stock or third-party handlers can
        trigger.) The module installs nothing at load but its two advices;
        =workspace-bookmark-remove-advice= undoes both. It defines no
        =workspace-portable-bookmarks-unload-function=, so =unload-feature=
        would leave the advice dangling (pre-existing; moot for the removed
        predicate — arch-on-touch-1790453714-44).

   directionality:
     permitted: |
       This module (the CONSUMER side) MAY name =bookmark=, =git=, and
       exactly these workspaces CORE symbols (re-derived from the code at
       f860103c, cycle-1790453714):
         tabs:         =workspace--registered-names=, =workspace--current-name=,
                       =workspace--registry= (read-only: =gethash= in
                       =--workspace-home= and =--broken-workspace-p=)
         data-model:   =workspace--home=, =workspace--broken-p=,
                       =workspace--same-file-p=, =workspace--path-within-p=
         persistence:  =workspace-restore= (the off-workspace flow)
         integrations: =workspace--integration-payload= (the add-worktree
                       payload), =workspace--integration-menu-command-p=
                       (is add-worktree registered?)
         layouts:      =workspace--restoring-layout-leaf= (a variable, read
                       only when =boundp=, in =--can-prompt-p=)
         config:       =jf/emacs-dir= (the configuration checkout in view)
       REMOVED this cycle: =workspace-anti-save-predicates= (no predicate is
       added any more). NOT on the list and not named: =workspace--integrations=
       (the raw registry shape; read through the accessor instead).
       Guarding: the registry, =workspace--home=, =workspace--broken-p=,
       =workspace-restore=, the two integrations functions and the layouts
       variable are guarded (=fboundp= / =boundp= / =declare-function=) at
       call time (portable-bookmarks.el:603-616, :806-817, :862-901,
       :1072-1095, :676-688); the two data-model PREDICATES are called
       UNGUARDED on purpose (=declare-function= only, :97-98): data-model is
       the package's first module and not droppable, and a guard would
       silently turn identity into "never equal"
       (=register/invariant/directory-identity-by-device-inode= corollary 3).

       Design accepts the registry-internals coupling explicitly for this
       slice ("a published accessor would be the clean follow-up if 1c needs
       the same data"). The fixtures want that accessor too
       (disc-module-scaffold-and-fixtures-3: portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el
       re-defvars =workspace--registry= defensively); when it lands, its
       first two consumers should be this module's resolution and the
       fixtures file.
     forbidden: |
       (a) Workspaces CORE (=data-model= / =integrations= / =workspaces= /
           =tabs= / =persistence= / =layouts= / =workspaces-mode= /
           =workspaces-transient= .el) MUST NOT name any
           =workspace-bookmark-*= symbol. The only permitted core mention is
           the loader PATH STRING "portable-bookmarks.el" in workspaces.org
           — a string, not a live symbol. (Same discipline verified for
           git-worktrees in cycle-7.)
       (b) This module MUST NOT name =magit-*= or =projectile-*= symbols, and
           MUST NOT =require= either. Grep-checkable; see validator.

   core_protocol: |
     (cycle-1790453714; arch-on-touch-1790453714-41, REDRAFTED after the
     off-workspace-restore fix pass, merge 30a8bba4.) What core's layout
     restore now knows about bookmark handlers — standard Emacs names only,
     nothing module-specific:
     - Each layout leaf's bookmark jump (=workspace--bookmark-buffer=,
       layouts.el:213-258) runs with the core variable
       =workspace--restoring-layout-leaf= bound non-nil (:244) and nil
       everywhere else. It SUPPRESSES NOTHING: interaction is not inhibited,
       and a stock handler that asks (the large-file prompt, unsafe local
       variables, =vc-follow-symlinks= =ask=) asks exactly as on any other
       visit.
     - A handler's =user-error= is a DECLINE, for EVERY handler, stock and
       third-party alike: steps 2 (stored filename) and 3 (same-name buffer)
       are skipped and the leaf lands on a reason buffer; the declines of
       one restore are reported in ONE message. Any other =error= (or no
       landing) is a FAILURE and falls through to steps 2-4 as before;
       =quit= propagates.
     - This module is the consumer that reads the variable: its
       =workspace-bookmark--can-prompt-p= (portable-bookmarks.el:678-688) is
       nil while it is bound (and in a non-interactive Emacs, and while
       =inhibit-interaction= is bound, because =read-multiple-choice= does not
       honour that variable itself and would loop), so the missing-repo
       chooser and the off-workspace picker DECLINE with =user-error=
       instead of putting one question per leaf.
     Consequence recorded: a third-party handler that =user-error=s on
     restore now loses the filename / name fallbacks that used to rescue the
     leaf. Narrowing the gate to this module's declines would need a
     core-defined condition (a new permitted symbol) — a user decision, not
     taken. The variable was added for one consumer and is neutral: core
     names no =workspace-bookmark-*= symbol (validator 2).

   producer_side:
     file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
     tangles_to: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el
     feature: workspace-portable-bookmarks   # the package convention, confirmed
     symbol_prefix: "workspace-bookmark- (public) / workspace-bookmark-- (internal)"
     symbol_prefix_note: |
       Matches the =workspace--= style of the core modules. NOT the older
       =jf/workspace--= pattern used by =git-worktrees.el=.
     attaches_via: |
       Advice installed at module load — NOT via
       =workspace-register-integration=. This module is a bookmark consumer,
       not a workspace-lifecycle integration; it has no :menu and no
       :on-purge surface, so the integration registry is the wrong seam.
       This is the boundary's notable asymmetry with
       =register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer=.

   sibling_consumer: |
     TWO-WAY and SOFT since cycle-1790453714 (DD-10 discipline both ways:
     quoted, =fboundp= at CALL time, never =require='d, unbound -> the
     feature degrades, never an error).
     OUTBOUND from git-worktrees: TWO public symbols of this module —
     =workspace-bookmark-canonical-checkouts-in-view= (git-worktrees.el:65,
     :67, :85, :88) and the discovery scope
     =workspace-bookmark-call-with-discovery-memo= (:108, :113) — every call
     fboundp-guarded and error-barriered, no =declare-function=. Every
     =workspace-bookmark--= symbol stays private (tripwire:
     git-worktrees-spec.el:735 "names no private portable-bookmarks function
     in its code" reads the tangled file and permits exactly these two). The
     full surface is =register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery=
     cross_module_surface.
     INBOUND to git-worktrees (NEW, missing-repo-choice): this module names
     exactly ONE git-worktrees symbol, the PRIVATE =:menu= command
     =jf/workspace--add-worktree=, always quoted — =(fboundp
     'jf/workspace--add-worktree)= and =(funcall 'jf/workspace--add-worktree
     PAYLOAD)= (portable-bookmarks.el:613, :839-842) — and offers the
     "add worktree here" answer only when core says the command is
     registered as an integration's =:menu= command
     (=--add-worktree-runnable-p=, :605-616, via
     =workspace--integration-menu-command-p=); unregistered or unbound -> the
     option is withheld with a stated reason. The call bypasses the
     integration registry's runner (there is no public "run integration ID's
     surface" accessor) but consumes the registry's own result vocabulary
     (=ok= / =skipped= / =(failed . REASON)=, signalling on anything else,
     :845-860). ASYMMETRY, recorded (arch-on-touch-1790453714-32): the
     outbound edge crosses public symbols behind a spec tripwire; the
     inbound edge crosses a private symbol and its tripwire is only a grep
     (validator 5). A public =jf/workspace-add-worktree= or a core
     =workspace-run-integration (id payload surface)= would make the two
     edges symmetric — a design choice, not owed.

   known_overlap:
     with: jf/workspace--worktree-children (config/workspaces/git-worktrees.org)
     nature: |
       Both enumerate a repository's worktrees. git-worktrees does it VIA
       =magit-list-worktrees=; this module does it via =git worktree list
       --porcelain= as a subprocess. Two enumerations of one fact.
     disposition: |
       ACCEPTED DELIBERATELY for this slice to avoid a magit dependency
       (DD-6 keeps this disposition: unification would require allowing
       magit, which is declined). Flagged for later unification once the
       cold path lands. The Architect should NOT report this as duplication
       (signal class 4) during this change — it is a recorded, reasoned
       decision, not drift. Re-open it if a THIRD enumeration appears
       (checked at cycle-1789935943 end-of-cycle: NEGATIVE — the
       =worktree list --porcelain= calls in discovery-spec.el are fixture
       assertions, not a third enumerator).

   validator: |
     Structural (no scaffolding in this project):
     1. grep -n 'magit\|projectile' config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el
        -> zero hits (verified at 20944160).
     2. grep -rn 'workspace-bookmark' config/workspaces/*.el
        -> hits ONLY in portable-bookmarks.el and, in git-worktrees.el, ONLY
        the two public symbols =workspace-bookmark-canonical-checkouts-in-view=
        and =workspace-bookmark-call-with-discovery-memo= (git-worktrees.el:58,
        65, 67, 74, 85, 88, 95, 97, 108, 113 at f860103c; never a =--= name;
        pinned by git-worktrees-spec.el:735). The loader path string in
        workspaces.el does not match this pattern; integrations.el's
        payload docstring says "portable-bookmarks" in PROSE (:59), which is
        not a symbol and is tolerated (arch-on-touch-1790453714-59).
     3. The loader line in workspaces.org sits strictly after
        workspaces-mode.el and strictly before workspaces-transient.el
        (verified: workspaces.el:56 / :62 / :66).
     4. grep -n 'portable-bookmarks' init.org -> zero hits (verified).
     5. (cycle-1790453714; the reverse tripwire as a grep — no spec exists)
        grep -o 'jf/[a-z/-]*' config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el | sort -u
        -> exactly =jf/emacs-dir= and =jf/workspace--add-worktree=, and every
        =jf/workspace--add-worktree= occurrence outside strings and
        docstrings is quoted (='jf/workspace--add-worktree=, :613, :839,
        :842); grep -oE 'workspace--[a-z-]+'
        (excluding =workspace-bookmark-=) -> only the core symbols on the
        permitted list.

   test_corpus:
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el
     - config/workspaces/test/git-worktrees-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-missing-repo-spec.el   # add-worktree offer follows git-worktrees' REAL registration; payload = core constructor + :source-repo
     - config/workspaces/test/buffer-reincarnation-spec.el             # core_protocol: the decline gate, the leaf flag bound only around the jump

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
     - register/boundary/gptel-sessions-workspace-consult
     - register/boundary/bookmark-advice-seams
     - register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery
     - register/invariant/workspaces-transient-deferred-require

   cited_by_tasks:
     - module-scaffold-and-fixtures
     - off-workspace-restore
     - canonical-predicate-consolidation
     - missing-repo-choice                        # cycle-1790453714: inbound sibling edge
     - workspaces-file-equal-p-timestamp-race     # cycle-1790453714: identity predicate moved to core
     - discovery-scope-hardening                  # cycle-1790453714: registration gate
     - cycle2-review-cleanups                     # cycle-1790453714: accessor + containment predicate
     - add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate         # cycle-1790604160: registration-vs-visibility correction; health gate in-module
     - outcome-action-mapping                     # cycle-1790604160: inbound edge result handling; dead fboundp error
   discovered_from: workspace-portable-bookmarks
   discovered_by: architect
   confirmed_in: cycle-1789935943
   #+end_src

** bookmark-advice-seams
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-bookmark-advice-seams
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/bookmark-advice-seams
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1790453714 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 2 against b56f385a; status unchanged. SEAM 1 is

     for this entry and the three jump-side invariants. OWNERS of the
     unexercised clauses: validator 3's seam-2 half, validator 4, the
     relocate-arm assertion in upstream_contract_risk and the jump-spec.el
     corpus entry -> jump-seam; the missing-repo hand-off arm ->

     execute to settle: (a) the exact arms of the :around body per outcome
     — =resolved= substitutes a COPY and calls through; =non-portable=
     calls through with the ORIGINAL argument untouched; =not-on-branch= /
     =no-clone= / =ambiguous= report and DECLINE to call through;
     =missing-repo= hands the whole plist to the prompt (report and open
     nothing until wave 3); a nil (workspace--current-name) on a STAMPED
     record hands off to the off-workspace flow (treated as non-portable

     normalised record, bookmark-prop-set on the copy, the copy keeps its
     own handler; (c) condition-case fall-through — the seam's own barrier
     calls ORIG-FN with the ORIGINAL argument on any internal error and is
     the SECOND barrier (the resolver's condition-case already maps
     discovery errors to no-clone, portable-bookmarks.el:992-1042), while
     =quit= propagates through both; (d) argument normalisation —
     bookmark-get-bookmark before --resolve for the STRING form, and that
     the value handed to ORIG-FN on substitution is a RECORD, which is
     exactly what suppresses the host's relocate arm (assert it, do not
     inherit it); (e) bookmark-jump-other-window (and -other-frame)
     coverage asserted through the REAL jump path, never the advice in
     isolation; (f) seam 2's cost is whatever
     =register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery= cost_contract says
     after =discovery-identity-memo-and-cost-contract= (wave 2) — the jump
     spec must not pin spawn counts. No clause is weakened.

     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): PARTIAL —
     SEAM 1 LIVE and CONFIRMED (stamp-seam 9f28f931, wave3-review-fixes;
     verified by the on-touch architect with real git and a stock-Emacs
     round trip). SEAM 2 is installed as =:around= (combinator SETTLED, see
     purpose) and PASS-THROUGH (portable-bookmarks.el:398-405); =jump-seam=
     is deferred to cycle 2, so the entry STAYS SPECULATED and seam 2's
     clauses are UNEXERCISED. The stamp-seam on-touch's cost_contract is

     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1789935943/reconciliations/boundary-bookmark-advice-seams.md

     cycle-1789935943 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     populated from design D1/D2 + tasks =stamp-seam= and =jump-seam=.
     Both seam functions VERIFIED against the running Emacs at plan time

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code

     =bookmark-error-no-filename=; closed for the seam by the pre-flight and

     Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/boundary-bookmark-advice-seams.md

     cycle-1790604160 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 3 against c65efb86; status unchanged. Validators
     1-5 hold at c65efb86 (advice-add :1818/:1820; one =bookmark-prop-set=
     :416; barrier :568-570 around =--jump-plan= only; =(apply orig-fn arg
     args)= :579). # owner: outcome-action-mapping (cycle-1790604160) —
     SPECULATED, execute settles (eoc-1790453714-05 §2-4): (a)
     degradation_contract's "stated residual" NARROWS — for a STAMPED record
     the barrier DECLINES naming the error (a =user-error= before ORIG-FN,

     record it falls through as today. The record's stampedness is decided

     below (its four variants; built today at =--plan-to-open= :438/:443,
     =--jump-plan= :523/:531/:537, the chooser :715-720,
     =--add-worktree-and-replan= :850-859, =--no-workspace-plan= :1020-1025,

     function becomes the ONE producer of the outcome-decided variants).
     CALLERS of the advised function, enumerated at plan per meta-discovery
     3 (in the task body; summary): inside the seam — bookmark.el:1267
     =bookmark--jump-via= (=bookmark-jump= :1319, =-other-window= :1328,
     =-other-frame=, bmenu :2283-2326), =bookmark-insert= :1534,
     layouts.el:246, bufferlo.el:2559, consult.el:4331 / :4370,
     embark.el:4568-4569, org.el:19092, org-refile.el:647; OUTSIDE the seam
     — bufferlo.el:3021-3022 and :4961 (direct handler funcall, residual
     (f)), consult.el:4322-4327 (preview opens the stored filename, new
     residual (g) on no-silent-wrong-file-open); bufferlo.el:1223 advises

     loaded by this config. No new caller, no new actor.
   load_bearing: true
   title: The two bookmark advice seams — stamp on make-record, substitute on handle-bookmark
   purpose: |
     This capability touches core Emacs at EXACTLY two points, and the choice
     of points is the whole architecture. Both are additive and both degrade
     to today's behaviour on any failure.

     SEAM 1 — STAMP. =:filter-return= advice on =bookmark-make-record=
     (=workspace-bookmark--stamp-record=, portable-bookmarks.el:382;
     installed at :1818). Receives the record Emacs just built; returns it
     either stamped or byte-identical. MUST NOT prompt, MUST NOT message,
     MUST NOT alter the bookmark name the user chose. Whole body wrapped in
     =condition-case= returning the ORIGINAL record (:395-402): a failure to
     stamp must never prevent a bookmark from being created.
     CALLERS (confirmed cycle-1789935943): every bookmark creation AND
     =layouts.el='s per-window-leaf capture (=workspace--serialize-buffer=)
     on the synchronous tab-switch autosave, the idle tick, kill-emacs and
     explicit saves. Layout records are stamped deliberately (ask 4); seam 1
     never detects or excludes its caller. RETURN SHAPE: an unstampable
     record comes back =eq=; a stamped record is a NEW record (original
     properties first, in order, then the three stamped properties); the
     input record is never mutated.

     SEAM 2 — SUBSTITUTE. Advice on =bookmark-handle-bookmark=
     (bookmark.el:1339). Chosen because =bookmark--jump-via= calls it, and
     =bookmark--jump-via= is the shared path behind =bookmark-jump=,
     =bookmark-jump-other-window=, =bookmark-jump-other-frame= and
     completion front-ends such as =consult-bookmark=. Advising
     =bookmark-jump= instead would silently miss the others — a coverage hole
     that would look like "it works" in the common case. It also covers
     every DIRECT caller of =bookmark-handle-bookmark= (e.g.
     =bookmark-insert=) and workspaces' own layout restore
     (=workspace--bookmark-buffer= calls =bookmark-jump=, layouts.el:246).
     NOT COVERED (arch-on-touch-1790453714-5): bufferlo's native tab/frame
     bookmark restore funcalls each nested record's handler directly
     (bufferlo.el:3015-3030), so a stamped record nested in a bufferlo tab
     or frame bookmark is restored by its stored filename, unresolved. No
     task follows unless bufferlo-native tab bookmarks become a supported
     workflow. Under =bufferlo-mode= every ordinary jump reaches the host
     with a RECORD (bufferlo's =:around= on =bookmark--jump-via= hands its
     OLDFN the record), so the host's string-gated tail and the seam's
     =bookmark-current-bookmark= bookkeeping are skipped for every jump —
     bufferlo's pre-existing behaviour, recorded, not a hazard.
     COMBINATOR: =:around= (=workspace-bookmark--handle-bookmark-advice=,
     portable-bookmarks.el:542-587; installed at :1820), forced by
     =register/invariant/no-silent-wrong-file-open= — =:filter-args= can
     substitute the record but cannot decline to call through, and the
     declining arms (=not-on-branch= "MUST NOT be visited", the other
     reports, a =missing-repo= cancel that ends the jump with =quit=) all
     require NOT calling ORIG-FN (disc-module-scaffold-and-fixtures-2). The
     body acts on the plan =workspace-bookmark--jump-plan= returns (:499):
     =(decline . MESSAGE)= -> =user-error= before ORIG-FN; =(cancel)= ->
     =(signal 'quit nil)=; =(call-through . ARG)= / =(view COPY . CHECKOUT)=
     -> ONE =(apply orig-fn arg args)= (:579); any other plan SIGNALS
     (:587), outside the barrier. After a substituted host call that LANDED
     (=workspace-bookmark--landed-p=, :447: the current buffer changed, or
     now visits the copy's filename by file identity) a view is marked
     read-only and a jump by name sets =bookmark-current-bookmark= there;
     every host call is first offered to
     =workspace-bookmark--note-jump-target= (:994), which records only the
     outer jump of a restore-then-jump. The originating buffer is never
     touched.

     ARGUMENT NORMALIZATION: the seam-2 parameter is
     =BOOKMARK-NAME-OR-RECORD= and may be a STRING NAME or a record.
     Normalize with =bookmark-get-bookmark ... 'noerror= before reading
     coordinates (:519); a value that normalises to no record calls through
     so the host signals its own error.

     HANDLER PRESERVATION: the substituted copy keeps its own =handler=
     property. This capability NEVER installs a handler of its own — that is
     why dired, org, pdf-view and third-party handlers keep doing their own
     restoration (point, narrowing, mode state).

   rejected_alternatives:
     - alternative: a custom bookmark handler
       why_rejected: |
         It would REPLACE the dired/org/pdf handler; we would have to
         reimplement their restoration behaviour.
     - alternative: advising find-file
       why_rejected: far too broad a surface for a bookmark-specific concern.
     - alternative: advising bookmark-jump
       why_rejected: |
         misses bookmark-jump-other-window, bookmark-jump-other-frame, and
         every completion front-end that goes through bookmark--jump-via.
     - alternative: ":filter-args on bookmark-handle-bookmark (seam 2)"
       why_rejected: |
         cannot decline to call through, so neither a cancel that opens
         nothing (now ended with =quit=) nor a reported-not-visited
         not-on-branch is expressible (settled cycle-1789935943; see
         COMBINATOR).

   cost_contract: |
     Seam 1, measured at 20944160 (probe, warm memo): 0 spawns for a gate
     rejection (no/relative/nonexistent filename, labels such as "3
     buffers", nested buffer/window values, TRAMP); 1 for a local path in no
     work tree; 2 for a tracked FILE (rev-parse + ls-files); 1 for a tracked
     DIRECTORY or the root (one rev-parse with HEAD:./; bounded by depth,
     not subtree); +1..2 once per checkout root per memo generation (git
     remote). ~17-50 ms per stamped record here; on the tab-switch path the
     cost is paid once per visible in-repo window (ask 6: accepted; revisit
     lazy export-time coordinates when slice 2 is planned). Memo
     (=workspace-bookmark--memoised-identity=, portable-bookmarks.el:243):
     per checkout root, keyed on the PAIR (shared git config mtime, the
     checkout's own =config.worktree= mtime or nil;
     =--config-stamp=, :220, cycle-1790453714) — two stats and a 4 KiB
     =.git=-file read, NO spawn, so the seam-1 counts above did not move;
     a nil identity is memoised; a nil stamp is never a hit; cleared on
     load and by M-x workspace-bookmark-clear-identity-cache. Any change
     adding a spawn to seam 1 must amend this field. Seam 2: the resolve
     path's cost is =register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery=
     cost_contract (resolved: 1 per checkout of the workspace's home); the
     pre-flight adds one =file-exists-p=; the jump spec pins no spawn count.

   upstream_contract_risk: |
     =bookmark-handle-bookmark= is NOT a documented extension point; its
     contract could change across Emacs versions. Mitigations, both required:
     (a) keep the advice minimal — normalize, resolve, substitute, call
     through; (b) test through the REAL jump path
     (=bookmark-jump= / =bookmark-jump-other-window=), never the advice in
     isolation, so an upstream change surfaces as a test failure rather than
     as silent pass-through.

     CONFIRMED at cycle-1790453714 (bookmark.el 30.2:1339-1386): seam 2's
     host function has its own =bookmark-error-no-filename= recovery arm
     that offers an interactive RELOCATE prompt, and that arm is gated on
     =(stringp bookmark-name-or-record)=. Passing a substituted RECORD
     therefore suppresses that prompt — the DESIRED direction
     (=not-on-branch= must not become a relocate offer, which on "yes" would
     rewrite and persist the stored record). ASSERTED, not inherited:
     jump-spec "declines with user-error, never bookmark-error-no-filename,
     and never offers to relocate" (=y-or-n-p= trapped by the hermetic
     macro).

     THE WHOLE CONSEQUENCE of a record argument (arch-on-touch-1790453714-1):
     for a record the host SWALLOWS =bookmark-error-no-filename= and
     returns nil, after which =bookmark--jump-via= runs its display
     function, fringe mark and =bookmark-after-jump-hook= on the ORIGINATING
     buffer. The seam closes this for its own substitutions two ways: (1) a
     =file-exists-p= pre-flight of the resolved =:path= immediately before
     the copy is built (=--plan-to-open=, :435-436) turns a path that
     vanished since the resolver looked into a DECLINE (a =user-error= naming
     it, so the host tail never runs); (2) the post-call bookkeeping (view
     marker, =bookmark-current-bookmark=) runs only when the host LANDED
     (=--landed-p=, :447). Residual: a path that vanishes in the
     microseconds between the pre-flight and the handler's own visit still
     meets the host's swallow. Pinned: jump-spec "declines when a resolved
     path vanished before the visit, touching neither the originating
     buffer nor the stored record" (asserts no new overlay and no
     =bookmark-current-bookmark= on the origin); missing-repo-spec "does not
     mark the originating buffer when the host does not land".

   plan_shape: |
     (cycle-1790604160, recorded from the code at c65efb86; owner outcome-action-mapping
     for the producer consolidation.) What =--jump-plan= returns and the seam
     acts on — FOUR variants, closed; the seam's own dispatch (:571-587) has
     an arm per variant and a SIGNALLING default:
       (call-through . ARG)    ARG is the ORIGINAL argument (non-portable, or
                               the barrier's fall-through for an UNSTAMPED
                               record) or a substituted COPY (resolved; after a
                               successful add-worktree). "Substituted vs
                               original" is decided by =eq= against the
                               original argument (:581, :1061-1062).
       (decline . MESSAGE)     open nothing; the seam raises =user-error=
                               MESSAGE before ORIG-FN.
       (view COPY . CHECKOUT)  hand COPY to the host, then mark the landed
                               buffer as a read-only view of CHECKOUT.
       (cancel)                open nothing, say nothing, =(signal 'quit nil)=.
     Every COPY comes from =--plan-to-open= -> =--substituted-record= (the one
     copy site). Not a shape-tier entry: the plan crosses no module boundary;
     it is the seam's internal protocol.

   degradation_contract: |
     Both seams wrap their bodies in =condition-case= and fall through to the
     ORIGINAL argument on any internal error. Consequence, and the point:
     a failure affecting ONE bookmark cannot affect other bookmarks or the
     bookmark file as a whole. (Seam 1: confirmed,
     portable-bookmarks-stamp-spec.el "returns the original record,
     unmutated, when building the stamp signals". Seam 2: the barrier wraps
     the DECISION only (portable-bookmarks.el:568-570); ORIG-FN runs once,
     outside it, so a handler's own error propagates unchanged and =quit=
     passes (jump-spec § "barrier and host contract").) Two deliberate
     narrowings on seam 2, both in the safe direction: the missing-repo
     chooser has its OWN barrier that turns any error inside the choice into
     a DECLINE (:704-726), and the off-workspace plan declines on any error
     and on a would-be call-through of the ORIGINAL argument (:1061-1070) —
     so neither can reach the stored path through the seam's fall-through.
     Stated residual: a programming error in the seam's own decision OUTSIDE
     those two degrades to stock, i.e. the stored path for a stamped record
     (jump-spec "still opens the stored location when the resolver
     signals" / "... when the seam's own copy step signals").

   producer_side:
     file: config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.org
     functions:
       - "workspace-bookmark--stamp-record — the :filter-return advice on bookmark-make-record (seam 1; portable-bookmarks.el:382)"
       - "workspace-bookmark--handle-bookmark-advice — the :around advice on bookmark-handle-bookmark (seam 2; portable-bookmarks.el:542)"
       - "workspace-bookmark--jump-plan — the seam's decision, a closed dispatch over the six outcomes with a signalling default (:499)"
       - "workspace-bookmark--substituted-record — the ONE copy site (:404)"
       - "workspace-bookmark--plan-to-open — pre-flight + copy, the only producer of an opening plan (:419)"
       - "workspace-bookmark--landed-p — did the host land (:447)"
     install_and_remove: |
       Advice installation AND matching removal are both defined
       (=workspace-bookmark-install-advice= portable-bookmarks.el:1815-1821,
       run at load :1831; =workspace-bookmark-remove-advice= :1823-1829), so
       the module can be reloaded during development without stacking advice
       (portable-bookmarks-spec.el pins idempotent installation). The module
       no longer adds anything else at load (the off-workspace anti-save
       predicate was removed by user decision ask-cycle-1790453714-3).

   validator: |
     Structural (no scaffolding in this project):
     1. grep -n 'advice-add' config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el ->
        exactly two seams, on bookmark-make-record (:filter-return) and
        bookmark-handle-bookmark (:around); NOT on bookmark-jump, NOT on
        find-file (verified portable-bookmarks.el:1818, :1820;
        portable-bookmarks-spec.el "installs advice on both bookmark seams",
        "advises neither bookmark-jump nor find-file").
     2. grep -n "bookmark-prop-set" config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el
        -> exactly ONE call, on ='filename=, on the fresh copy inside
        =--substituted-record= (:416); no =bookmark-prop-set= on ='handler=
        and no =:handler= write (jump-spec § "structure" reads the tangled
        file and pins both).
     3. Seam 1's body is one condition-case returning the ORIGINAL record
        (portable-bookmarks.el:395-402). Seam 2's barrier is a condition-case
        around =--jump-plan= only (:568-570), falling through with the
        ORIGINAL argument; the single =(apply orig-fn arg args)= (:579) is
        outside it.
     4. A behavioral spec drives bookmark-jump-other-window (not just
        bookmark-jump) and asserts resolution happened — proving the choke
        point (jump-spec "resolves through bookmark-jump-other-window too").
     5. portable-bookmarks-stamp-spec.el § "stamp seam subprocess budget"
        (:528) asserts the seam-1 cost_contract counts with spies on
        workspace-bookmark--git.

   test_corpus:
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-stamp-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-jump-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-missing-repo-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-off-workspace-spec.el

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/portable-bookmark-coordinates
     - register/vocabulary/bookmark-resolution-outcome
     - register/invariant/stored-record-never-mutated-by-jump
     - register/invariant/no-silent-wrong-file-open
     - register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer

   cited_by_tasks:
     - stamp-seam
     - jump-seam
     - module-scaffold-and-fixtures
     - wave3-review-fixes
     - missing-repo-choice        # cycle-1790453714: the missing-repo hand-off arm
     - off-workspace-restore      # cycle-1790453714: the nil-workspace hand-off arm
     - outcome-action-mapping       # cycle-1790604160: barrier declines for stamped records; plan_shape; one mapping producer
   discovered_from: workspace-portable-bookmarks
   discovered_by: architect
