** git-derived-checkout-discovery
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-boundary-git-derived-checkout-discovery
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery
   tier: boundary
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1790453714 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     entry stays =reconciled=; the corpus-survey clause below (ask 18) is
     untouched. Two cycle-2 owners, both in wave 2 and both editing
     portable-bookmarks.org (conflict rule: resolve the .org, re-tangle).
     (1) =discovery-identity-memo-and-cost-contract= owns the
     cost_contract's target line and validator 6: route
     --workspace-checkouts (:698) and --canonical-checkouts-for-identity
     (:784) through --memoised-identity (:239), memoise --canonical-checkout
     per common dir within one --canonical-checkouts-among call (:768), and
     add spawn specs (warm memo: resolved <= 2; missing-repo <= 1 + in-view
     count; picker <= 2 per magit entry) that FAIL with the memo bypassed;
     the seam-1 numbers in =register/boundary/bookmark-advice-seams=
     cost_contract must not move. (2)
     =discovery-cleanup-singulars-and-docstrings= owns removing
     --workspace-checkout (:718) and --canonical-for-identity (:800) from
     the =functions= list once deleted and re-homing their specs on the
     plurals; if missing-repo-choice wants a one-arm "the unique checkout"
     helper it is added THERE with its caller and this list gains it then.
     SPECULATED for execute to settle: whether the memo's invalidation
     rule — reuse while the COMMON dir's config mtime is unchanged
     (=workspace-bookmark--config-stamp=, :233-237; memo keyed per ROOT,
     :246-250) — is correct for a LINKED worktree once discovery shares
     the memo. The identity comes from =git remote get-url=, which reads
     the common config AND, under extensions.worktreeConfig, the
     worktree's own config.worktree; a per-worktree remote override would
     change the identity without touching the common config's mtime.
     Execute must either show get-url ignores config.worktree for remotes,
     or fold the worktree git-dir's config.worktree mtime into the stamp
     when that file exists — and pin whichever with a spec. Second thing
     to settle: discovery inherits "a nil identity is memoised"; a
     candidate that GAINS a remote after a memoised-nil lookup becomes
     visible only through the same mtime invalidation (remote add writes
     the common config, so it should) — pin it with a spec that adds a
     remote after a memoised-nil lookup and resolves again.

     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): RECONCILED.
     The architecture is confirmed (git-only, configuration-free, one
     runner, one normalizer, no magit/projectile/cache/scanning). The
     normalization edge cases named at plan (ports, non-forge hosts, path
     case) are pinned by portable-bookmarks-identity-spec.el; credential
     stripping too. STILL OWED: the corpus pass over this machine's real
     remote-URL spellings (the user chose to run it REDACTED; not confirmed
     as run) — until then =.GIT=, interior =//= (now collapsed) and IPv6
     literals are decided by the unit table only, so this note is NARROWED,
     not lifted. Reconciled with DD-10 (soft call), DD-11 (symlinks
     ignored), DD-12 (git-derived + magit only), DD-13 (same-repo worktree
     homes). The two singular discovery functions have no production caller
     (end-of-cycle finding 2; follow-up). Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1789935943/reconciliations/boundary-git-derived-checkout-discovery.md

     cycle-1789935943 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     populated from design D3/D5 + specs/portable-bookmarks/spec.md
     § "Discovery without configuration" + tasks =repo-identity=,
     =checkout-discovery=, =worktree-candidates-git-first=. SPECULATED on the
     normalization edge cases (port numbers, non-forge hosts, path case) —
     the task gives a rule set but no corpus has been run against it.

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): STAYS RECONCILED. Both plan-time speculations
     SETTLED by =discovery-identity-memo-and-cost-contract=: (1) =git remote
     get-url= DOES read the checkout's own =config.worktree= under
     =extensions.worktreeConfig=, so the identity memo's stamp is the PAIR
     (shared =config= mtime, the checkout's =config.worktree= mtime or nil;
     =workspace-bookmark--config-stamp=, portable-bookmarks.el:220-241; the
     checkout's git dir read from =<root>/.git= with no subprocess,
     =--checkout-git-dir= :199-218), pinned for a linked worktree and for the
     main worktree (portable-bookmarks-cost-spec.el § "Identity memo
     invalidation seen through discovery"); the remaining blind spot is a
     change in an =[include]= / =includeIf= file — documented, remedy
     =workspace-bookmark-clear-identity-cache= (arch-on-touch-1790453714-21);
     (2) a memoised NIL identity is replaced once =git remote add= writes the
     shared config (cost-spec "is replaced once the checkout gains a
     remote"). Discovery now shares the identity memo (=--workspace-checkouts=
     and =--canonical-checkouts-for-identity= call =--memoised-identity= on a
     position's ROOT and COMMON DIR, one key spelling with the stamp path)
     and a new PER-CALL discovery memo (=--with-discovery-memo=, a =let=-bound
     special that nests by sharing and never outlives the call). The whole
     resolve decision is ONE discovery call (=--resolve-coordinates=
     :1730-1761; arch-on-touch-1790453714-24). cost_contract RE-STATED to the
     measured floor (arch-on-touch-1790453714-22): 3 per canonical
     repository, not the plan's "<= 2 per candidate" — the three questions
     are three different git subcommands; 2 is reachable only by a design
     change (see the OPTION in cost_contract). The functions list is
     REPLACED: the singulars (discovery-cleanup-singulars-and-docstrings)
     and =--repo-root= / =--identity= (discovery-memo-dead-helpers-and-
     picker-scope) are DELETED; the two predicates =--same-file-p= /
     =--path-within-p= MOVED to core as =workspace--same-file-p= /
     =workspace--path-within-p= (data-model.el; workspaces-file-equal-p-
     timestamp-race, cycle2-review-cleanups). cross_module_surface is now
     TWO public functions: the entry point and the discovery SCOPE
     =workspace-bookmark-call-with-discovery-memo=, with a stated
     precondition (arch-on-touch-1790453714-48). Ask 18 (the ~/src
     remote-URL corpus survey) DEFAULT APPLIED 2026-09-27: survey skipped,
     so the cycle-1789935943 narrowing above STANDS (identity normalisation
     not field-tested against ~/src). Line anchors re-based
     (arch-on-touch-1790453714-12). Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/boundary-git-derived-checkout-discovery.md
   load_bearing: true
   title: Git-derived, configuration-free identity and checkout discovery (the warm path)
   purpose: |
     The single canonical answer to "which repository is this, and where else
     is it checked out on this machine?", derived entirely from git
     subprocesses against checkouts ALREADY IN VIEW. Its absence of
     configuration is the point: resolution behaviour must not differ between
     machines because of registration state.

     THE QUESTIONS AND THEIR COMMANDS (replaces the design D5 table;
     design.md is out of date — end-of-cycle finding 1):
       which repository?           git remote get-url origin (else the SOLE
                                   remote; `git config --get remote.origin.url`
                                   REJECTED: no insteadOf expansion, wrong
                                   multi-URL pick; magit itself uses get-url)
       root / common dir / prefix  git rev-parse --path-format=absolute
                                   --show-toplevel --git-common-dir --show-prefix
                                   [HEAD:./]   (git >= 2.31; older git -> nil
                                   -> no-clone, never a signal)
       what other checkouts?       git worktree list --porcelain (first entry
                                   = main; -z NOT used, it needs git >= 2.36)
       this file's relpath?        git --literal-pathspecs ls-files -z
                                   --full-name -- BASENAME (in the file's dir)
       does it hold the tree?      git ls-files -z (run in the candidate; one
                                   existing non-artifact path suffices)
       is it its own top level?    git rev-parse --show-toplevel (run in the
                                   candidate)

     CHECKOUTS IN VIEW (=workspace-bookmark--checkouts-in-view=,
     portable-bookmarks.el:1463): for the current workspace, then every other
     registered workspace: its =:home= when the home is itself a checkout
     (anchored / scaffolded homes, ask 7), then the home's IMMEDIATE children
     that are real directories containing =.git= (symlinks skipped, DD-11);
     then =jf/emacs-dir= (:1481). Enumerating a registered home's children is
     enumeration of a registered layout, not scanning; recursion or listing
     any other directory is scanning and out of scope. No cache, no
     machine-local configuration in this slice.

     IDENTITY NORMALIZATION (=workspace-bookmark--normalize-identity=,
     portable-bookmarks.el:139 — THE one normalizer) is part of this
     boundary because it is the translation between git's several URL
     spellings and the single string stored in
     =register/shape/portable-bookmark-coordinates=. Inlining it at a second
     site is exactly the boundary-translation drift this tier exists to
     catch. Rules are BRANCH-FIRST, not strip-first (disc-repo-identity-1/2):
       1. If the URL begins SCHEME://, parse scheme://[userinfo@]host[:PORT]/path
          and DISCARD userinfo and PORT (transport is not identity). A URL
          that has a scheme but does not parse is nil — NEVER retried as
          scp-like syntax (the fall-through cond mints `https/github.com`).
       2. Otherwise, if a colon precedes the first slash, parse the scp-like
          [userinfo@]host:path — there is NO port in this form.
       3. Otherwise nil.
     Then: downcase the HOST only (path case is significant on many forges),
     strip a trailing slash then a trailing =.git=, collapse interior =//=,
     reject an empty host or empty path. An identity REQUIRES an explicit
     host (a scheme, or an scp-like colon before the first slash): local
     absolute paths, relative paths and file:// URLs yield nil
     (disc-repo-identity-3). Userinfo is stripped in every form, so a
     credential never reaches the persisted record (disc-repo-identity-4).
     These MUST agree on =github.com/moogah/emacs=:
       git@github.com:moogah/emacs.git
       https://github.com/moogah/emacs
       ssh://git@github.com/moogah/emacs.git
     and the port pair on =example.com/team/repo=:
       ssh://git@example.com:2222/team/repo.git
       https://example.com/team/repo.git
     while the scp spelling =git@example.com:2222/team/repo.git= is
     =example.com/2222/team/repo= (git's own reading; no port in scp form).

     NO REMOTE MEANS NO IDENTITY. A path-based identity would be meaningless
     on another machine. On this machine 9 of 51 repositories under =~/src=
     have no remote — a normal case that must degrade cleanly, not an edge
     case. A non-portable remote (local path, file://) is, for this
     capability's purposes, no remote.

   subprocess_contract: |
     ONE runner, =workspace-bookmark--git= (portable-bookmarks.el:60; the
     single =process-file= call site is :92). =process-file=, NEVER a shell
     (=shell-command-to-string=): no shell means no quoting, which matters
     concretely here — directory names in this environment contain spaces
     (=~/src/Archived Repos/=). =default-directory= is bound to DIR (or to
     the directory of an EXISTING file; a missing DIR yields nil and is
     never resolved to its parent — the parent fallback turned a trimmed
     toplevel into another repository's identity, checkout-discovery
     on-touch finding 2). The 18 GIT_* variables in
     =workspace-bookmark--git-unset-environment= (:48) are removed for every
     call, so an inherited GIT_DIR / GIT_WORK_TREE cannot redirect the
     answer. OUTPUT: only git's final "\n" is removed (whitespace inside or
     at the end of a path is kept). Non-zero exit, no output, nil /
     non-string / REMOTE DIR -> nil. NEVER signals: the whole body is inside
     =condition-case=, and =file-remote-p= is tested (:79, :81) before any
     file-name-handler dispatch, so TRAMP can neither signal nor prompt
     (repo-identity on-touch finding 1). The memo layer keeps the same
     discipline: =--shared-position= / =--shared-tracked-file-p= ask a
     remote DIR only =file-remote-p= before keying it by truename (cost-spec
     § "A remote checkout name reaches no file-name handler but
     file-remote-p"). LOCAL PATHS ONLY: a remote
     checkout's identity re-anchored against a local one is a wrong-file
     answer. Path-returning callers use -z or multi-line output and verify
     re-anchoring by file identity. Same discipline as
     =register/invariant/git-worktree-default-directory-binding=, applied
     to raw git. Callers map nil to a degraded outcome (see
     =register/vocabulary/bookmark-resolution-outcome= error_mapping).

   porcelain_parsing: |
     =git worktree list --porcelain= is parsed
     (=workspace-bookmark--parse-worktree-porcelain=,
     portable-bookmarks.el:1109), NOT the human-readable form, which is
     column-aligned and lossy. Porcelain shape: one =worktree <path>= line
     per entry, blank-line separated, with bare / prunable / locked /
     detached flag lines; git documents the FIRST entry as the main
     worktree. A USABLE checkout is non-bare, non-prunable, exists, and
     holds a =.git= entry (=--usable-checkout=, :1145); locked and detached
     entries are kept. The first entry may be a git DIRECTORY for submodule
     / --separate-git-dir layouts, so =--checkouts-of= (:1157) is NOT
     reflexive (disc-checkout-discovery-5). =--checkouts-of= is the ONE
     reader of the listing (memo-aware: shared per common dir within a
     discovery call); =--main-worktree-of= (:1499) is its first element.

   functions: |
     (re-based cycle-1790453714 at f860103c; all portable-bookmarks.el
     unless noted)
     --git (:60; process-file :92; --git-unset-environment :48),
     --remote-url (:100), --normalize-identity (:139; THE one normalizer),
     --root-identity (:171), --identity-memo (:179),
     workspace-bookmark-clear-identity-cache (:185; public command),
     --checkout-git-dir (:199; no subprocess), --config-stamp (:220; the
     PAIR), --memoised-identity (:243; per checkout ROOT; used by the stamp
     path AND by discovery), --checkout-position (:284; ONE rev-parse:
     root, common dir, prefix [, HEAD tree]), --relpath (:314),
     --coordinates (:347; the stamp path's memo caller), --checkout-dir-p
     (:1097), --parse-worktree-porcelain (:1109), --worktree-entries
     (:1138), --usable-checkout (:1145), --checkouts-of (:1157; the ONE
     reader of the listing), the per-call memo: --discovery-memo (:1175),
     --with-discovery-memo (:1184), --memoised-in-call (:1194),
     --discovery-memo-key (:1206; truename), --shared-position (:1218),
     --shared-tracked-file-p (:1232), --shared-worktree-entries (:1242;
     keyed by common dir); --os-artifact-p (:1263), --holds-tracked-file-p
     (:1272), --canonical-checkout (:1300), --same-repository-worktree-p
     (:1352), --owning-workspace (:1374), --classify (:1392),
     --home-checkouts (:1415), --workspace-checkouts (:1437; PLURAL; drops
     a child that is not its own root), --checkouts-in-view (:1463),
     --offerable-canonical-p (:1485), --main-worktree-of (:1499),
     --canonical-checkouts-among (:1506), --canonical-checkouts-for-identity
     (:1525; PLURAL), and the TWO PUBLIC functions
     workspace-bookmark-canonical-checkouts-in-view (:1550) and
     workspace-bookmark-call-with-discovery-memo (:1574). The identity and
     containment predicates are CORE: =workspace--same-file-p= and
     =workspace--path-within-p= (data-model.el:264, :288;
     =register/invariant/directory-identity-by-device-inode=). Discovery
     returns LISTS; uniqueness is the consumer's decision (the resolver,
     =--resolve-coordinates= :1730). DELETED this cycle: the singulars
     --workspace-checkout / --canonical-for-identity, --repo-root,
     --identity, and the module copies of --same-file-p / --path-within-p.

   cross_module_surface: |
     TWO public functions (cycle-1790453714; was one).

     THE ENTRY POINT
     =(workspace-bookmark-canonical-checkouts-in-view &optional CHECKOUTS)=,
     portable-bookmarks.el:1550, cl-defun with supplied-p. No argument: the
     canonical checkouts of the repositories of =--checkouts-in-view=, in
     view order. CHECKOUTS given: the same for that list; an explicit nil
     yields nil. Each element passed =--offerable-canonical-p=; a linked
     worktree in CHECKOUTS contributes its repository's MAIN worktree, which
     may lie outside the view. Results are absolute, slash-ended,
     de-duplicated by file identity, first appearance kept. Never signals in
     normal operation (no barrier of its own; the consumer barriers).
     THE SCOPE =(workspace-bookmark-call-with-discovery-memo FUNCTION)=
     (:1574), a function, not a macro (so the soft edge needs no
     expansion): runs FUNCTION as ONE discovery call; EVERY discovery made
     while it runs joins the memo, not only the entry point; nothing
     survives it (let-bound, dropped on any exit). PRECONDITION, stated in
     its docstring (arch-on-touch-1790453714-48): FUNCTION must not create,
     move or remove a checkout, change a repository's configuration, or
     wait for input — after such a change an earlier answer is replayed
     from the memo. The one production sequence that mutates a repository
     and then asks again, the missing-repo add-worktree replan
     (=--add-worktree-and-replan=, :828-860), re-resolves under a FRESH memo
     (=workspace-bookmark--discovery-memo= bound to nil, :847) and runs
     outside every scope in production.

     STABILITY: names, calling conventions and meanings change only
     together with git-worktrees.org, which calls both through call-time
     =fboundp= guards and error barriers; tripwires git-worktrees-spec.el
     § "git-derived repo candidates" (:306) and § "git-worktrees.el's
     portable-bookmarks surface" — "names no private portable-bookmarks
     function in its code" (:735), which permits exactly these two. The
     reverse edge (portable-bookmarks naming git-worktrees'
     =jf/workspace--add-worktree=) is recorded on
     =register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer= sibling_consumer, not
     here. The three-function private surface that
     worktree-candidates-git-first first used (--checkouts-in-view /
     --checkouts-of / --classify) is GONE
     (disc-canonical-predicate-consolidation-2).

   second_consumer:
     file: config/workspaces/git-worktrees.org
     functions:
       - jf/workspace--worktree-git-derived-candidates   # git-worktrees.el:56-70
       - jf/workspace--worktree-canonical-only           # git-worktrees.el:72-91
       - jf/workspace--worktree-in-one-discovery-call    # git-worktrees.el:93-127; the barriered scope call (cycle-1790453714)
     nature: |
       This boundary is the PRIMARY candidate source for the git-worktrees
       repo picker, and its predicate admits magit's list too; projectile
       and project.el are NOT consulted (DD-12). This is the BREAKING
       (spec-level) part of the change and it AMENDS pins 2 and 3 of the
       =confirmed= =register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer=.
     consumer_filter: |
       None: every element already passed --offerable-canonical-p. The
       post-filters in git-worktrees.el:153-154 (truename dedupe,
       --git-repo-p) are the unbound-passthrough path's floor, not the
       admission test (end-of-cycle finding 8).
     constraint: |
       The =read-directory-name= floor (=jf/workspace--worktree-read-repo=)
       and free-form entry are UNCHANGED for the transient's payload. A
       payload carrying =:source-repo= (the missing-repo chooser's, holding
       the resolver's admitted =:checkout=) skips the prompt and discovery
       entirely (cycle-1790453714;
       =register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer= pin 3).
     dependency_direction: |
       RESOLVED (DD-10, worktree-candidates-git-first): git-worktrees does
       NOT require portable-bookmarks. Soft, call-time =fboundp= =funcall= of
       the public symbols (git-worktrees.el:65, :85, :108); no =require=, no
       =declare-function=, no third module, no duplicated enumerator. Load
       order is irrelevant (git-worktrees loads first, workspaces.el:49 vs
       :62): when the module is absent the git-derived source contributes
       nothing and magit's list passes through unclassified.

   cost_contract: |
     RE-STATED cycle-1790453714 (arch-on-touch-1790453714-22, -49). ONE
     runner is counted (=workspace-bookmark--git=); a call-through spy on it
     is the spawn count. magit's own =magit-toplevel= in the picker's
     post-filter (=jf/workspace--worktree-git-repo-p=) is outside it BY
     DESIGN (named in git-worktrees-spec § "repo picker subprocess budget").

     Unit costs on a warm identity memo: a checkout's POSITION 1
     (=rev-parse --show-toplevel --git-common-dir --show-prefix=, shared per
     truename within a discovery call); a repository's WORKTREE LISTING 1
     (shared per common dir within a call); the TRACKED-FILE check 1
     (=ls-files -z=, in the candidate); identity 0 warm, else 1 (=remote
     get-url origin=; 2 with no origin). =--classify= 0, plus 1-2 per
     registered home lying INSIDE the checkout
     (=--same-repository-worktree-p=, not memoised).
     THE CANONICAL TEST OF ONE MAIN CLONE IS THREE — position, listing,
     tracked file — and three is the FLOOR under this entry's two-signal
     design: three different git subcommands, one per process. A further
     checkout of a repository already seen in the call costs 1 (its
     position).
     Composed, and PINNED EXACTLY (portable-bookmarks-cost-spec.el
     § "Discovery subprocess budget on a warm identity memo", with
     memo-bypass companions that assert the higher count):
       resolved      = 1 per checkout of the workspace's home (2 on the
                       fixture; 4 with the identity memo bypassed).
       missing-repo  = 1 per checkout in view (the workspace's own among
                       them, positioned ONCE: the whole decision is one
                       discovery call) + 2 for the one matching repository
                       (listing + tracked-file check) = 6 on the fixture (11
                       with the identity memo bypassed, 12 with the per-call
                       memo bypassed).
       picker        = both sources built in ONE scope
                       (=jf/workspace--worktree-in-one-discovery-call=):
                       3 per canonical main clone + 1 per further checkout
                       of a repository already listed (9 for three clones,
                       11 with two linked worktrees added, 19 with the
                       per-call memo bypassed; computes no identity); a
                       repository reached from BOTH the view and magit's
                       list asks its position, listing and tracked-file
                       check ONCE whatever the spelling (10 vs 13 split,
                       git-worktrees-spec § "with a repository both in view
                       and in magit's list"). Paid PER SOURCE, not shared:
                       the contained-home ownership check and
                       =--canonical-checkout='s two fallbacks (=rev-parse
                       --git-common-dir= when there is no position,
                       =--show-toplevel= when the position's root is not the
                       candidate).
     Measured before, at 20944160: resolved 2, missing-repo 16 on the cost
     fixture, picker 17 on the picker fixture. All costs remain linear in
     candidates; under DD-15 (~51 repos) the picker's magit pass is ~150
     counted spawns cold. NOT pinned: the off-workspace candidate ordering
     (=--offerable-workspaces=, :903) asks =--workspace-checkouts= once per
     registered workspace, each its own discovery call (1 position per home
     checkout).
     OPTION (user disposition, NOT owed): 2 per canonical repository is
     reachable only by replacing the "first non-bare worktree-list entry"
     signal with "=--git-dir= equals =--git-common-dir=" from the same
     rev-parse; that re-states porcelain_parsing, the org § "The canonical
     checkout" and the discovery-spec's signals-disagree specs — a design
     change, not a cost fix. The seam-1 numbers in
     =register/boundary/bookmark-advice-seams= did not move.

   out_of_scope_cold_path: |
     Declared clone roots / directory scanning (replacing projectile for
     CREATING worktrees) belongs to the git-worktrees candidate seam and is
     DEFERRED. An implementation that adds scanning here is scope creep
     against this entry. (DD-15 restores picker suggestions through magit's
     own =magit-repository-directories=, which is magit's scan, not this
     boundary's.)

   validator: |
     Structural (no scaffolding in this project):
     1. grep -n 'shell-command-to-string' config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el
        -> zero hits (verified at f860103c).
     2. Exactly ONE function normalizes a remote URL to an identity; no
        second scheme/.git stripping site anywhere in config/ (verified).
     3. A behavioral spec asserts the SSH / HTTPS / ssh:// triple agree
        (portable-bookmarks-identity-spec.el:44, :225).
     4. A behavioral spec asserts a repo whose only remote is named
        =upstream= still yields an identity (origin-preferred, not
        origin-required; identity-spec.el:177, :247).
     5. Exactly one process-file call site (portable-bookmarks.el:92),
        default-directory bound, environment scrubbed
        (identity-spec.el:386, :408).
     6. DONE (cycle-1790453714): portable-bookmarks-cost-spec.el pins the
        resolved / missing-repo / canonical-test budgets with memo-bypass
        companions, and git-worktrees-spec.el § "repo picker subprocess
        budget" (:620) pins the picker per scope, overlap included; both spy

     7. (cycle-1790453714) Memo discipline: the identity memo's stamp

        invalidation seen through discovery"); the add-worktree replan

        enclosing scope's listing cannot hide the new worktree").

   test_corpus:
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-identity-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-discovery-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-stamp-spec.el

* Invariant

  Entries describe rules that must hold across the system but don't fit
  the structural tiers. Each entry names its =enforcement_mechanism=
  (=test= / =runtime-check= / =lint= / =structural-audit= /
  =review-discipline=) — an invariant whose only enforcement is review
  discipline is fragile and flagged for migration to a stronger mechanism.

  Candidate invariants for this codebase, to be entered during plan-phase
  forward-mode of the relevant change:

  - =lexical-binding-on-line-1= — every tangled =.el= begins with
    =;;; <name>.el --- ... -*- lexical-binding: t; -*-= on line 1.
    /Not in scope for gptel-scope-in-org-properties./
  - =module-load-order-transient-before-language= — module loader respects
    the documented load-order invariants (=transient= before
    =language-modes= and =major-modes/magit=; =major-modes/magit= before
    =major-modes/org=). /Not in scope for gptel-scope-in-org-properties./
  - =scope-allow-once-is-single-shot= — an =allow-once= permission grants
    the next invocation only and self-removes. /Not in scope for
    gptel-scope-in-org-properties (untouched by this change)./

** signature-anchored-to-point-min-drawer
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-signature-anchored-to-point-min-drawer
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/signature-anchored-to-point-min-drawer
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781448273 integrate (2026-06-14): speculated → confirmed.
     jf/gptel--session-signature-p (via jf/gptel--scan-session-drawer-keys)
     anchors to a real point-min :PROPERTIES: drawer; the :END: bound
     excludes a :GPTEL_ key after the drawer (tested) and src-block content
     past :END: (added bounded-scan spec, review F2). Strengthened this
     cycle: case-fold-search is bound nil (review F1), so the uppercase
     :GPTEL_[A-Z0-9_]+: contract is enforced — a lowercase :gptel_*: drawer
     does NOT match (new case-sensitivity spec). Reviewer + implementor both
     confirmed anchoring under direct probing.
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781448273/reconciliations/invariant-signature-anchored-to-point-min-drawer.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: The session signature matches only a real point-min drawer, never prose
   statement: |
     jf/gptel--session-signature-p (register/boundary/session-content-
     signature) MUST return non-nil ONLY for a buffer whose first
     non-blank content is a genuine :PROPERTIES: drawer carrying a
     :GPTEL_[A-Z0-9_]+: key line before :END:. It MUST return nil for:
     an org file that merely mentions :GPTEL_PRESET: inside a paragraph
     or a #+begin_src block; a file whose first content is anything other
     than :PROPERTIES:; a non-org / plain-text buffer (without signaling).
     The match is anchored to a real drawer at point-min — never a bare
     substring search.
   why_it_matters: |
     Because the predicate is wired into magic-mode-alist, a false match
     HIJACKS an ordinary user file into gptel-chat-mode at open time — a
     user-visible regression that bypasses org-mode on a non-session file.
     The whole content-addressed design rests on the claim "nothing but a
     real gptel session presents this signature". A bare substring check
     (the tempting shortcut) would match documentation of the feature,
     this very register, and any org notes quoting a drawer key. The
     anchoring is the load-bearing guard, not an optimization.
   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       Buttercup spec config/gptel/sessions/test/filesystem/
       session-signature-spec.el MUST cover the four scenarios: positive
       (point-min drawer with :GPTEL_PRESET: or :GPTEL_SESSION_ID:),
       false-match (prose / src-block mention), non-org buffer (nil, no
       error), and old-session (:GPTEL_PRESET: only) positive. The
       end-to-end false-match-on-open case is additionally asserted in
       config/gptel/chat/test/mode-activation-spec.el (opening such a
       file via find-file-noselect leaves it in org-mode).
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/session-content-signature
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
   discovered_from: gptel-content-addressed-session-activation
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** branch-drawer-shares-id-not-branch
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-branch-drawer-shares-id-not-branch
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/branch-drawer-shares-id-not-branch
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781448273 integrate (2026-06-14): speculated → reconciled.
     emit-identity-keys-in-writers (merge b286997) implemented the branch
     rewrite via jf/gptel--rewrite-branch-identity-keys /
     jf/gptel--set-drawer-property-in-buffer (branching.org). The Case-3
     spec proves no-leak (parent's :GPTEL_BRANCH: absent) AND no-duplicate
     (key count == 1) AND single PROPERTIES/END. why_tests_missed /
     RECONCILIATION: the speculation said "OVERWRITE values copied verbatim
     from the parent drawer" — but a LEGACY parent predating identity-key
     emission carries neither key, so there is nothing to overwrite. The
     correct semantics is SET = replace-or-insert (the buffer setter replaces
     an existing key-line, else inserts before :END:). Statement amended
     below to cover the legacy-parent case (implementor disc-2).
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781448273/reconciliations/invariant-branch-drawer-shares-id-not-branch.md.
   load_bearing: false
   title: A new branch shares the session id but carries its own branch name
   statement: |
     When a branch is created from a parent session (branching.org), the
     new branch's session.org drawer MUST carry the SAME
     :GPTEL_SESSION_ID: as the parent (the shared session id) and its OWN
     new :GPTEL_BRANCH: value. The parent's :GPTEL_BRANCH: value MUST NOT
     leak into the child. Because branch creation copies the parent drawer
     verbatim then overwrites, the overwrite of :GPTEL_BRANCH: must be a
     drawer-key REPLACE (not append) so no duplicate key survives. For a
     LEGACY parent that predates identity-key emission (no keys to
     overwrite), the writer SETs the keys — replace-or-insert: a missing
     key is inserted before :END: — not overwrite-only. (cycle-1781448273)
   why_it_matters: |
     The registry keys uniformly on "session-id/branch-name". If a branch
     inherits the parent's :GPTEL_BRANCH:, two branches collide on the
     same registry key and the second silently shadows the first — the
     exact identity-collision class this change moves identity into the
     drawer to make explicit. A leaked-then-appended key would also
     violate register/shape/drawer-text-block (duplicated key line).
   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       Buttercup spec config/gptel/sessions/test/commands/
       identity-keys-emission-spec.el: a branch created from a parent
       carries the same GPTEL_SESSION_ID as the parent and its own new
       GPTEL_BRANCH (parent's branch value absent). Splice via
       jf/gptel--append-drawer-property / a replace helper, preserving
       the :PROPERTIES:/:END: adjacency of register/shape/drawer-text-block.
   related_entries:
     - register/vocabulary/identity-drawer-keys
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
   discovered_from: gptel-content-addressed-session-activation
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

   title: Activation, identity, and binding derive from content, never path layout
   statement: |

     sessions/chat/persistent-agent subsystems may derive session
     ACTIVATION, IDENTITY, or buffer BINDING from directory-layout
     reverse-engineering. Specifically there MUST be: no find-file-hook
     registration that dispatches by path; no jf/gptel--auto-init-session-
     buffer; no branch/nested-agent/flat-agent layout regexes; no fixed
     ../.. / ../../.. walks used to recover identity; and no `current`
     symlink writer/reader/constant. The only path-derived value still
     permitted is the basename/segment FALLBACK in register/boundary/
     drawer-first-identity-resolution, used solely when a drawer key is
     absent. Locating a container directory by structural marker
     (register/boundary/session-dir-marker-walk) is permitted — that is
     navigation, not identity.
   why_it_matters: |
     This is the change's whole thesis. The pre-change pipeline paid a
     per-open cost on every unrelated file and coupled identity to where
     a session happened to sit on disk — fragile to any reorganization
     (the merged workspaces package being one). Leaving any write-only
     remnant (a dead symlink writer, a stale regex, a still-registered
     hook) would mean the coupling was not actually removed, only
     shadowed — the "complete removal" intent of D1/D4/D6 unmet.
   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test + grep-audit
     location: |
       Static remnant greps (regression-sweep task), run REPO-WIDE over
       all of config/gptel. The three MECHANISM greps MUST return nothing
       in production *.org sources:
         grep -rn "find-file-hook" ;
         grep -rn "auto-init-session-buffer" ;
         grep -rn "current-symlink\|get-current-branch-name\|current-link".
       Plus the IDENTITY-source grep (T7, operationalising the cycle-7
       resolver-as-single-identity-seam meta-discovery):
         grep -rn "session-id-from-directory" config/gptel
       which MUST return ONLY the sanctioned hits — the defun + docstrings
       in sessions/filesystem.{org,el}, the SOLE production use inside
       jf/gptel--resolve-session-id (the permitted basename fallback), one
       explanatory comment in tools/persistent-agent.{org,el}, and test
       fixtures (branching-integration-spec.el + identity-resolution-spec.el).
       ANY other production caller fails the audit (the entry would be
       divergent, not confirmed). Behavioral coverage: mode-activation-spec.el
       (content activation), mode-hook-binder-spec.el (binding without path
       test), and the reworked agent-buffer-activation-reload-spec.el /
       auto-init-resilience-spec.el (content-addressed equivalents of the
       former find-file-hook assertions).
     confirmed_by: |
       cycle-1781526321 (cycle-8). Audit PASSED, re-verified independently
       by orchestrator (plan) + implementor (Part A) + author-blind reviewer
       (Part 1, post-merge HEAD b15b66f). See the cycle-8 reconciliation note.
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/session-content-signature
     - register/boundary/drawer-first-identity-resolution
     - register/boundary/session-dir-marker-walk
     - register/boundary/chat-mode-session-display
   discovered_from: gptel-content-addressed-session-activation
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** persisted-form-readable-by-construction
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-persisted-form-readable-by-construction
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/persisted-form-readable-by-construction
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260613-161149 integrate (2026-06-13, task serialize-window-
     params-readable): created CONFIRMED from merged, clean-reviewed code
     (designed-change mid-stream adoption). Roots out the =#<killed
     buffer>= corruption class at its source.
   load_bearing: true
   title: No live Emacs object is ever written to the persistence file
   statement: |
     The form written to =workspaces.eld= MUST be reconstructible by
     =read= — it MUST NOT contain any live Emacs object that prints as
     =#<...>= (buffer, marker, overlay, frame, window, process, or
     non-symbol function object; see =workspace--unreadable-object-p=).
     This is enforced at THREE layers, defense-in-depth:

     1. Window-parameter translators — buffer-bearing window parameters
        (seeded: =window-preserved-size=, value =(BUFFER DIR SIZE)=) are
        converted to a readable form (buffer → name) by
        =workspace-window-parameter-translators= in
        =workspace--window-state-serialize=, and back in =-deserialize=.
        =window-state-get='s =writable= flag does NOT do this — it emits
        writable-param VALUES verbatim, which is the original bug.

     2. Bookmark-record scrub — =workspace--serialize-buffer= nulls any
        =bookmark-make-record= prop VALUE satisfying
        =workspace--unreadable-object-p= (keys preserved). Closes the
        bug#56643 class (a mode embedding an unreadable object in its
        record).

     3. Write-time assert (backstop) — the write boundary
        (register/boundary/persistence-robustness-io rule 2) refuses to
        write any form that does not round-trip through =read=, so an
        unknown future leak past layers 1–2 fails loudly instead of
        corrupting the file.
   enforcement_mechanism: test
   test_corpus: config/workspaces/test/serialization-robustness-spec.el (round-trip readability property tests incl. killed-buffer regression scenario (a), teeth-confirmed)
   rationale: |
     Modelled on activities.el / burly.el window-parameter translators
     (research/findings-*.md in the change). We copied activities'
     struct-based buffer reincarnation earlier but NOT its translators —
     that gap was the root cause of the persistence corruption.
   discovered_from: serialize-window-params-readable
   discovered_by: implementor
   relates_to:
     - register/shape/layout-v2-plist (window-state slots are now readable by construction)
     - register/shape/workspace-buffer-struct (the scrubbed bookmark slot)
     - register/boundary/persistence-robustness-io (layer 3, the write-side backstop)
   #+end_src

** scope-parse-complete-is-true
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-scope-parse-complete-is-true
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/scope-parse-complete-is-true
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): --validate-parse-completeness reads
     jf/gptel-scope--enforce-parse-complete defconst (value t) directly;
     the plist :security :enforce-parse-complete read path is gone.
     Invariant enforced by construction. See .orchestrator/cycles/
     cycle-1777470320/reconciliations/invariant-scope-parse-complete-
     is-true.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: enforce-parse-complete is the constant t at module level

   statement: |
     scope-validation.el treats jf/gptel-scope--enforce-parse-complete as
     a defconst whose value is exactly t. Stage 1 of the bash validation
     pipeline reads the constant directly; no caller passes it in, no
     plist key carries it, and no per-session override exists. Stage 1's
     "warn when not enforced" branch is removed entirely because the
     constant is unconditionally true.

   why_it_matters: |
     The :security plist key was the historical override mechanism;
     removing the key without enforcing the constant would silently
     downgrade Stage 1 to permissive mode (if a stale code path read
     a now-undefined plist value as nil). The user explicitly directed
     "no per-session escape hatch"; this invariant is the structural
     enforcement of that decision, not just a default.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: structural-audit
     location: |
       scaffolding test asserts (= jf/gptel-scope--enforce-parse-complete t)
       and the absence of any (plist-get config :security ...) read in
       scope-validation.el. Architect's invariant-gap audit re-checks at
       integrate.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/scope-config-plist
     - register/boundary/scope-config-loader

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** scope-no-security-key-in-plist
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-scope-no-security-key-in-plist
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/scope-no-security-key-in-plist
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): both layers hold. L1 — producer
     (--load-config / --load-from-buffer / --load-from-file) emits no
     :security key. L2 — no (plist-get _ :security ...) reads survive in
     scope-validation.el (verified by grep). Cycle-3
     delete-yaml-and-security-residue is the residual sweep across non-
     validation modules. See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1777470320/
     reconciliations/invariant-scope-no-security-key-in-plist.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Loaded scope-config plist has exactly :paths and :cloud — no :security

   statement: |
     The plist returned by jf/gptel-scope--load-config (and its two stage
     producers, jf/gptel-scope--load-from-buffer and --load-from-file)
     contains exactly two top-level keys: :paths and :cloud. The :security
     key is forbidden in the loaded plist; any reader that does
     (plist-member val :security) must observe nil; any (plist-get val
     :security ...) read in implementation code is a divergence signal
     from this invariant.

   why_it_matters: |
     Closing the :security removal cleanly. A key that exists "with default
     values" is dead structure that future readers will assume is real
     configuration. The cleanest "removed" state is "absent at the plist
     level"; tests that pin shape behaviour catch divergence early.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       Buttercup spec under config/gptel/scope/test/drawer/load-from-buffer-
       spec.el and load-from-file-spec.el: assert (null (plist-member
       result :security)) for every loader output.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/scope-config-plist
     - register/invariant/scope-parse-complete-is-true
     - register/invariant/scope-coverage-threshold-is-1

** scope-drawer-no-duplication
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-scope-drawer-no-duplication
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/scope-drawer-no-duplication
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): both creation paths (sessions,
     persistent-agent) write exactly one drawer via single with-temp-file
     + (concat drawer-text body); branching uses byte-copy from parent.
     Invariant enforced by structure. Runtime regression spec lands in
     cycle-3's add-drawer-corruption-regression. See .orchestrator/cycles/
     cycle-1777470320/reconciliations/invariant-scope-drawer-no-duplication.md.
     cycle-8 integrate (2026-05-23): narrative update — the
     enforcement test at config/gptel/scope/test/drawer/no-duplicate-
     drawer-spec.el was tightened (task tighten-scope-drawer-no-
     duplication-fixture-and-counter, merge_commit 8c497e0) to scope
     the :PROPERTIES:/:END: counters to the file-level drawer span,
     because the post-cycle-7 canonical session.org layout (register/
     shape/session-document-layout) introduces a second heading-level
     :PROPERTIES: drawer on `* System Prompt` that inflated the
     pre-cycle-8 buffer-wide counter to 2/2. The first cycle-8 cut
     of the tightened counter (`(point-min)..(first :END:)`) blinded
     the test to the stacked-drawer corruption class the invariant
     was created to catch (multiple consecutive file-level drawers
     stacked at point-min — see openspec/changes/gptel-org-mode-
     sessions/handoff-property-drawer-corruption.md). On-touch
     architect arch-cycle-1779522837-2 flagged the regression; inline
     fix (commit 7b771a5) widened the span to `(point-min)..(first
     ^* heading or point-max)`, restoring stacked-drawer detection
     while preserving the canonical-layout false-positive cure. A
     post-cycle-7-shape fixture (`jf/gptel-test--with-session-
     document`) lives in helpers-spec.el for further coverage. See
     cycle-1779522837/reconciliations/invariant-scope-drawer-no-
     duplication.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: After any add-to-scope sequence, the buffer has exactly one :PROPERTIES: drawer

   statement: |
     For every chat-buffer touched by jf/gptel-scope--write-pattern-to-
     drawer (or any caller of it), the buffer's text contains exactly one
     ":PROPERTIES:" line and exactly one ":END:" line for the file-level
     drawer at point-min. The writer must use org-entry-put against the
     existing drawer; it must not insert a second drawer; it must not
     allow a second drawer to be created by any of its save-buffer-
     triggered hooks.

   why_it_matters: |
     The gptel-org-mode-sessions change resolved a drawer-corruption bug
     traced to duplicate gptel--save-state hooks producing a second
     :PROPERTIES: block on save. The full root-cause analysis is
     preserved at ~/org/roam/20260419111957-gptel_preset_property_
     corruption.org. Adding more state to the same drawer expands the
     surface for any regression of that bug class. This invariant is
     the structural lockout: even if a hook regression is reintroduced,
     the architect's audit and the regression test will catch a doubled
     drawer immediately rather than after silent corruption.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       Regression spec at config/gptel/scope/test/drawer/no-duplicate-
       drawer-spec.el (created by add-drawer-corruption-regression
       task; tightened cycle-8 by tighten-scope-drawer-no-duplication-
       fixture-and-counter + inline arch-2 widening). The
       `--file-level-drawer-region` helper scopes the
       `:PROPERTIES:`/`:END:` counters to the pre-heading region
       `(point-min)..(first ^* heading or point-max)` — wide enough
       to catch stacked file-level drawers, narrow enough to exclude
       the canonical-layout heading-level drawer on `* System Prompt`.
       Runs an add-to-scope sequence (≥3 cycles, several distinct
       keys) and asserts exactly one `:PROPERTIES:` and one `:END:`
       line remain in that span after each save. The post-cycle-7-
       shape fixture macro `jf/gptel-test--with-session-document`
       (helpers-spec.el) exercises the writer against the canonical
       layout.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
     - register/shape/session-sibling-system-prompt-file
     - register/boundary/scope-pattern-writer
     - register/boundary/scope-profile-applicator
     - register/shape/session-document-layout                  # superseded predecessor

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect

** scope-add-pattern-idempotent
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-scope-add-pattern-idempotent
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/scope-add-pattern-idempotent
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-2 integrate (2026-04-29): --write-pattern-to-drawer reads
     existing values, returns nil and skips org-entry-put-multivalued-
     property when (member pattern existing). Invariant enforced by
     construction. Runtime spec lands in cycle-3's migrate-expansion-tests.
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1777470320/reconciliations/
     invariant-scope-add-pattern-idempotent.md.
     cycle-8 integrate (2026-05-23): narrative update —
     arch-cycle-1779522837-4 (informational, end-of-cycle architect's
     load-bearing-invariant fixture sweep) surfaced that
     `enforcement_mechanism.location` cited
     `config/gptel/scope/test/drawer/write-pattern-spec.el` which
     does not exist. The pre-existing drift was not introduced by
     cycle-8 (it long-predated the cycle's diffs). The buffer-text
     half of the contract IS exercised — `no-duplicate-drawer-spec.el`
     `it "is idempotent for duplicate patterns"` (line 169) covers
     the writer's no-op-on-duplicate behaviour. The callback-shape
     half (`:success t :patterns_added []` on dedup) is currently
     guaranteed only by construction at scope-expansion.org:686-690
     (the callback's `:patterns_added` is `(vconcat patterns)` where
     `patterns` is the writer's return value; on dedup the writer
     returns nil and the callback's vector arm at line 690 emits
     `:patterns_added (vector)`). Enforcement_mechanism.location
     prose below was updated to cite the live test plus the
     callback-shape gap. A separate cycle-8 candidate ask
     (`ask-cycle-1779522837-2`) tracks adding an explicit
     callback-shape spec.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Adding the same pattern twice is a no-op (idempotent writer)

   statement: |
     For any drawer key K and any pattern P, calling jf/gptel-scope--
     write-pattern-to-drawer with (BUFFER, OPERATION → K, P) when P is
     already in the drawer's values for K leaves the buffer text
     byte-for-byte unchanged. The callback still reports :success t with
     an empty :patterns_added list.

   why_it_matters: |
     The inline-flow trigger re-enters jf/gptel-scope-authorize-tool-call
     after each add-to-scope, and a multi-violation tool call can pass
     through the same pattern twice on retry. Without idempotency, the
     drawer would accumulate duplicate values, the buffer would gain
     spurious save events, and (under some org versions) duplicate values
     could trigger reader edge cases.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test + structural
     location: |
       Buffer-text half: buttercup spec at config/gptel/scope/test/
       drawer/no-duplicate-drawer-spec.el `it "is idempotent for
       duplicate patterns"` (line 169) — asserts the writer leaves the
       buffer byte-for-byte unchanged across a duplicate-pattern
       write.
       Callback-shape half: currently guaranteed by construction at
       config/gptel/scope/scope-expansion.org:686-690 — the
       `:patterns_added` field is `(vconcat patterns)` where
       `patterns` is the writer's return value; on dedup the writer
       returns nil and the callback's vector-arm path emits
       `:patterns_added (vector)`. NOT pinned by a dedicated
       buttercup spec; the structural guarantee is the contract
       surface. Adding an explicit callback-shape `it` block is
       tracked as cycle-8 candidate ask
       `ask-cycle-1779522837-2` (defer-if-archived since this
       invariant is unrelated to the gptel-drawer-as-source-of-
       truth change's outcome).

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/scope-pattern-writer
     - register/vocabulary/operation-to-drawer-key

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** expansion-queue-always-progresses
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-expansion-queue-always-progresses
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/expansion-queue-always-progresses
   tier: invariant
   status: speculated
   status_note: |
     speculated-with-deviation: cycle-3 plan brief framed this as "every
     `--add-*-to-scope` action handler pumps the queue", but the cycle-2
     code (commit 18e290a, scope-expansion.org `* Expansion Actions`)
     has the queue-pump call in *all five* terminal action handlers,
     including `--deny-expansion` and `--allow-once-action`, not only
     the three `--add-*` variants. Entry records the broader as-built
     contract (every terminal action handler progresses the queue).
     Reconciliation flips to `confirmed` after the harden task lands
     and demonstrates that its new refusal/dedup branches also pump.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Every terminal expansion-menu action handler pumps the queue

   statement: |
     After `transient-quit-one`, every terminal action handler of
     `jf/gptel-scope-expansion-menu` calls
     `jf/gptel-scope--process-expansion-queue`. The five terminal
     handlers are `--deny-expansion`, `--add-to-scope`,
     `--allow-once-action`, `--add-wildcard-to-scope`, and
     `--add-custom-to-scope`. Without the queue-pump call, queued
     expansion prompts would silently strand: a multi-violation tool
     call would prompt the user once, the user would resolve it (deny,
     add, allow-once), the transient would close, and the remaining
     queued violations would never surface — the wrapper's callbacks
     would never fire and the LLM's tool call would hang indefinitely.

     The harden task's Stage 1 refusal branches (nil-operation,
     `:match-pattern` no-sibling, `:parse-incomplete`) and Stage 4
     no-op success/refusal callbacks (bare-command, dedup) must
     preserve this invariant — every branch that calls the wrapper
     callback must also pump the queue, even when the writer was a
     no-op or the action was refused.

   why_it_matters: |
     The cycle-2 rewire (commit 18e290a) discovered that
     `--add-wildcard-to-scope` and `--add-custom-to-scope` previously
     did NOT pump the queue: queued expansions resolved via wildcard
     or custom-pattern silently dropped. That was a latent bug that
     pre-dated the cycle-2 work; the writer rewire fixed it
     incidentally, but no test pinned the new uniform contract. This
     invariant is the structural lockout: any future code path that
     adds a new terminal action handler, or any branch in an existing
     handler that returns without pumping, regresses the queue's
     liveness guarantee.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: structural-audit + test
     location: |
       L1 (structural): scaffolding test grep-asserts that each of the
       five terminal action handlers in scope-expansion.org contains a
       call to `jf/gptel-scope--process-expansion-queue`. Architect's
       on-touch audit re-runs whenever scope-expansion.org changes.
       L2 (runtime): scaffolding spec fires a synthetic three-element
       queue (three violations queued via `--prompt-expansion`),
       drives one action-handler invocation, and asserts the queue
       length monotonically decreases until empty.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/expansion-transient-scope
     - register/boundary/scope-expansion-action-handler
     - register/boundary/scope-pattern-writer

   discovered_from: gptel-scope-in-org-properties
   discovered_by: architect (cycle-2 audit, arch-cycle-1777470320-3)
   #+end_src

** drawer-system-key-write-exclusion
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-drawer-system-key-write-exclusion
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/drawer-system-key-write-exclusion
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-5 plan (2026-05-01) — speculation: introduced for the
     gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth change Decision 2.
     cycle-5 integrate (2026-05-01): reconciled. Three producers of
     register/shape/drawer-text-block — the renderer
     (jf/gptel-scope-profile--render-drawer-text), the buffer-mode
     applicator (jf/gptel-scope-profile--apply-to-drawer), and the
     chat-mode save path (gptel-chat--save-state via
     gptel-chat--write-config-drawer) — all skip the :GPTEL_SYSTEM:
     key unconditionally. Verified by spec absence assertions in:
     - config/gptel/test/snapshot-rendering-spec.el (renderer +
       applicator: 4 scenarios that supply non-nil :system in
       PRESET-SPEC and assert :GPTEL_SYSTEM: absent in output)
     - config/gptel/chat/test/menu/save-state-spec.el (writer:
       NEVER writes GPTEL_SYSTEM with gptel--system-message set;
       preserves manually authored entries on save)
     - config/gptel/test/session-creation-spec.el and
       config/gptel/sessions/test/commands/session-org-creation-
       spec.el and activity-session-chat-spec.el (creation
       integration: assert (string-match-p ":GPTEL_SYSTEM:") nil
       in the saved file)
     - config/gptel/sessions/test/commands/preset-application-
       spec.el (end-to-end save round-trip preserves
       :GPTEL_SYSTEM: absence)
     The asymmetric read-side contract (chat-mode overlay still
     respects manually authored :GPTEL_SYSTEM: entries) is locked
     in by the "drawer-authored :GPTEL_SYSTEM: still wins on read
     (back-compat)" scenario in preset-wiring-spec.el. Cited by
     tasks: extend-render-drawer-text (43d045f), replace-chat-save
     (5f9d762), verify-and-spec-drawer-overlay-wins (b1f06d1),
     update-preset-application-spec (3a8abaf).
   load_bearing: true
   title: Neither the chat-mode save path nor the renderer ever emits :GPTEL_SYSTEM:

   statement: |
     Two producers of register/shape/drawer-text-block — the renderer
     (jf/gptel-scope-profile--render-drawer-text, stage 2a of register/
     boundary/scope-profile-applicator) and the chat-mode save path
     (gptel-chat--save-state and any helper it delegates to in
     config/gptel/chat/menu.org) — never emit a :GPTEL_SYSTEM: line. The
     drawer overlay (register/invariant/drawer-overlay-wins-over-preset)
     DOES read :GPTEL_SYSTEM: when present (back-compat for hand-authored
     entries) but no code path under our control writes it.

   why_it_matters: |
     The user's stated constraint is that long, multi-line, special-
     character system-prompt strings are unwieldy as a single org
     property value (backticks, asterisks, embedded newlines mangle the
     drawer). Decision 2 of design.md picks "writer never emits, reader
     still respects manual entries". Without this invariant the drawer
     could grow a multi-line :GPTEL_SYSTEM: across saves and silently
     corrupt the drawer's structural anchors (register/shape/drawer-
     text-block's :PROPERTIES:/:END: discipline). The asymmetric
     write/read contract is exactly the bug surface this entry exists
     to keep visible.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: structural-audit + spec assertion
     location: |
       Spec scenarios in
       config/gptel/chat/test/menu/save-state-spec.el (writer side: assert
       (search-forward ":GPTEL_SYSTEM:") returns nil after save) and
       config/gptel/sessions/test/commands/preset-application-spec.el
       (renderer side: assert :GPTEL_SYSTEM: absent in fresh session.org).
       Architect's invariant-gap audit re-checks at integrate.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
     - register/boundary/scope-profile-applicator
     - register/invariant/drawer-overlay-wins-over-preset

   discovered_from: gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** drawer-overlay-wins-over-preset
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-drawer-overlay-wins-over-preset
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/drawer-overlay-wins-over-preset
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-5 plan (2026-05-01) — speculation: introduced for the
     gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth change Decision 3.
     cycle-5 integrate (2026-05-01): reconciled. Verification
     confirmed that gptel-chat--apply-drawer-overrides already
     implements the contract — for each non-nil upstream-tuple
     field, it does (set (make-local-variable sym) val) with no
     "if drawer matches preset, skip" branch. No code change was
     required; the task delivered docstring / commentary refresh
     plus three new spec scenarios that lock the contract in:
     - "drawer GPTEL_MODEL wins over preset model" (preset-
       wiring-spec.el)
     - "preset :system survives when drawer omits :GPTEL_SYSTEM:"
       (preset-wiring-spec.el — asymmetric exception verified)
     - "drawer-authored :GPTEL_SYSTEM: still wins on read (back-
       compat)" (preset-wiring-spec.el)
     Plus end-to-end coverage in
     config/gptel/sessions/test/commands/preset-application-spec.el:
     - "drawer GPTEL_MODEL wins over preset model on chat-mode
       activation" (real preset registration + real find-file +
       real chat-mode hook)
     - "save after drawer-wins reopen re-emits the drawer model,
       not the preset model" (round-trip integrity)
     Cited by task verify-and-spec-drawer-overlay-wins (b1f06d1)
     and update-preset-application-spec-for-snapshot (3a8abaf).
     cycle-7 integrate (2026-05-22): re-reconciled to three-tier
     :system precedence. design.md §Addendum Finding B / Decision B
     inserts the `* System Prompt` heading body ahead of the prior
     two-tier :system exception; the title and :system clause now
     describe the three-tier order. The "drawer always wins, no
     delta check" contract for non-:system keys is unchanged.
     Triggered by on-touch finding arch-cycle-1779477564-3 against
     task make-system-prompt-heading-authoritative; composes with
     (does not contradict) register/invariant/system-prompt-heading-
     authoritative — that invariant owns the heading-body tier, this
     one owns the drawer-vs-preset tier. See cycle-1779477564/
     reconciliations/invariant-drawer-overlay-wins-over-preset.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: On mode activation, every drawer-present key wins over the active preset (except :system, which falls through a three-tier precedence)

   statement: |
     The chat-mode activation overlay (gptel-chat--apply-drawer-overrides
     in config/gptel/chat/menu.org) installs every drawer-present key
     buffer-locally, regardless of whether the value matches the active
     preset's value for that key. There is no "if drawer matches preset,
     skip" check anywhere in the overlay.

     :GPTEL_SYSTEM: is the asymmetric exception and resolves through a
     three-tier precedence on chat-mode activation, highest first:
       1. sibling system-prompt.<ext> file referenced by
          :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: when present and non-blank
          (replace-system-prompt-heading-with-sibling-file; owned by
          register/invariant/system-prompt-file-authoritative)
       2. legacy :GPTEL_SYSTEM: drawer entry when present (back-compat
          for hand-authored entries; never written by code under our
          control — see register/invariant/drawer-system-key-write-
          exclusion)
       3. preset :system as the fallthrough seed
     A missing/unreadable sibling file and an absent drawer entry
     together fall through to the preset; an authored drawer entry
     still beats the preset on read even when the sibling file is
     missing. The historical heading-body tier (owned by
     register/invariant/system-prompt-heading-authoritative, now
     superseded) is no longer in the precedence chain — no
     producer emits the heading.

   why_it_matters: |
     This invariant is the *read-side* counterpart to register/invariant/
     drawer-system-key-write-exclusion and the structural reason the
     drawer is a credible WYSIWYG source of truth. If the overlay had
     "delta" semantics (skip when drawer == preset), then editing a
     preset value to match what the drawer carries would silently flip
     authority back to the preset on next reopen — a hidden state
     bifurcation. Decision 3 of design.md picks "drawer always wins" for
     simplicity and testability.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: spec assertion + Architect dead-branch audit
     location: |
       Spec scenarios in
       config/gptel/chat/test/menu/preset-wiring-spec.el (drawer-model-
       wins-over-preset; preset-system-prompt-survives-when-drawer-omits-
       it). Architect's dead-branch audit checks no "match preset, skip"
       branch is added to gptel-chat--apply-drawer-overrides.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/drawer-text-block
     - register/invariant/drawer-system-key-write-exclusion
     - register/boundary/chat-menu-scope-default

   discovered_from: gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** system-prompt-heading-authoritative
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-system-prompt-heading-authoritative
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/system-prompt-heading-authoritative
   tier: invariant
   status: superseded
   superseded_by: register/invariant/system-prompt-file-authoritative
   superseded_in_cycle: cycle-1779568860
   supersession_note: |
     replace-system-prompt-heading-with-sibling-file (cycles
     1779565028 + 1779568860 + 1779572373) deleted the `* System
     Prompt` heading this invariant defended, replacing it with a
     sibling system-prompt.<ext> file referenced by a new
     :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: drawer key. The three-tier restore
     precedence collapses to: sibling file > legacy :GPTEL_SYSTEM:
     drawer entry (back-compat) > preset :system — the heading tier
     no longer exists. The pre-send refresh
     (gptel-chat--refresh-system-prompt-from-file, :before advice on
     gptel-request) added in cycle-1779568860 completes the
     "file is canonical, buffer is per-request cache" mental model.
     cycle-1779572373 (2026-05-24) — task strip-visibility-folded-
     startup-hook removed the last runtime enforcement (the
     org-cycle-set-visibility-according-to-property call inside
     gptel-chat--apply-startup-visibility) and the three buttercup
     `it` blocks in drawer-fold-spec.el that asserted its behavior.
     The supersession is now structurally complete: no production
     code or tests reference the old heading-folding contract; the
     verification grep on the removed API symbol is clean across
     config/gptel/chat/. Replacement contract at
     register/invariant/system-prompt-file-authoritative.
   status_note: |
     cycle-7 plan (2026-05-22) — speculated by forward-mode against
     design.md §Addendum (Finding B, Decision B). Reverses Decision 2's
     "system prompt rides the preset only" model: the system prompt is
     relocated into a visible `* System Prompt` heading body and that
     body becomes authoritative.
     cycle-7 integrate (2026-05-22): reconciled. The three-tier restore
     precedence (heading body > legacy :GPTEL_SYSTEM: drawer > preset
     :system), blank-body fallback, and create -> restore -> save
     idempotence all held — implementation in config/gptel/chat/menu.org
     (gptel-chat--system-prompt-heading-body /
     gptel-chat--apply-system-prompt-heading /
     gptel-chat--write-system-prompt-heading) lands the contract.
     17 new specs across save-state-spec.el, preset-wiring-spec.el, and
     the integration suites pin the round trip; on-touch Architect
     finding arch-cycle-1779477564-4 re-verified composition with
     register/invariant/drawer-system-key-write-exclusion. Scaffold at
     scaffolding_path matured from failing stubs to a full contract test
     during implementation; archived in favour of the co-located specs.
     See cycle-1779477564/reconciliations/invariant-system-prompt-
     heading-authoritative.md.
     cycle-8 integrate (2026-05-23): narrative update — task
     extract-system-prompt-heading-region-helper (merge_commit
     ba0bc50) refactored the reader/writer pair to share a single
     `gptel-chat--system-prompt-heading-body-region` helper. This
     made the trim-vs-pad asymmetry an explicit, single decision
     point in the helper docstring and removed the four-place edit
     cost of the duplicate scan logic. Both the three-tier
     precedence and the round-trip idempotence are preserved. Task
     harden-system-prompt-save-against-missing-chat-heading
     (merge_commit d774f43) hardened the save path against the
     off-nominal `* System Prompt`-without-`* Chat` document layout:
     the write branch now bounds the in-place rewrite at the first
     `^#+begin_(user|assistant)` turn-block marker (rather than
     blindly at point-max) and re-materialises `* Chat` so the
     canonical layout is restored. Eight new specs cover the
     off-nominal layout. On-touch architect produced no finding
     against this entry — the helper extraction and harden are
     strict refactors against the invariant. See cycle-1779522837/
     reconciliations/invariant-system-prompt-heading-authoritative.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: The `* System Prompt` heading body is the authoritative source of gptel--system-message

   statement: |
     For a session.org document (register/shape/session-document-
     layout), the body of the `* System Prompt` heading is the
     authoritative source of the buffer-local gptel--system-message.

     Restore precedence on chat-mode activation is, highest first:
       1. `* System Prompt` heading body, when non-blank
       2. legacy :GPTEL_SYSTEM: drawer entry, when present
       3. preset :system
     A blank (whitespace-only or absent) heading body is treated as
     "not authored" and falls through to the next source — an empty
     heading never silently wipes the prompt.

     On before-save-hook the current buffer-local gptel--system-message
     is serialised back into the `* System Prompt` heading body. The
     writer still never emits a :GPTEL_SYSTEM: drawer line — this
     composes with, and does not contradict, register/invariant/drawer-
     system-key-write-exclusion: that invariant governs the *drawer*,
     this one governs the *heading body*. The preset's :system only
     *seeds* a fresh session's heading body at creation.

     The round trip create -> restore -> save -> re-restore is stable:
     a save that changes nothing produces no diff to the heading body
     (idempotent save), and re-restoring yields the same
     gptel--system-message.

   why_it_matters: |
     design.md Decision 2 excluded the system prompt from session.org
     because long, multi-line, special-character strings are unwieldy
     as a single org property *value*. Finding B observes that the
     objection is specific to property values; an org heading *body*
     carries multi-line special-character text with no escaping. If the
     precedence order were wrong — preset winning over an authored
     heading body, or a blank body wiping the prompt — the document
     would stop being a credible WYSIWYG source of truth: a user would
     edit the visible `* System Prompt` body and see no effect, or open
     a fresh session and find the prompt gone. The fallback-on-blank
     rule is the specific guard against an empty heading silently
     erasing the prompt.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: failing scaffolded test (buttercup) + Architect invariant audit
     location: |
       Scaffolded failing buttercup test at scaffolding_path asserts
       (a) the three-level precedence order, (b) blank-body fallback to
       preset, and (c) the create -> restore -> save -> re-restore
       round-trip stability. Implementor wires the restore path
       (config/gptel/chat/menu.org's `gptel-chat--system-prompt-heading-
       body` reader and `gptel-chat--apply-system-prompt-heading`
       applicator, invoked as the final step of `gptel-chat--apply-
       declared-preset`) and the before-save serialisation
       (config/gptel/chat/menu.org's `gptel-chat--write-system-prompt-
       heading`). Architect's invariant audit re-checks that no drawer
       :GPTEL_SYSTEM: write was added and that the precedence branch
       order matches this statement. cycle-7 integrate (arch-cycle-
       1779477564-7): corrected from the pre-cycle prose that named
       `mode.org` for the restore path — both halves live in `menu.org`.

   related_entries:
     - register/invariant/drawer-system-key-write-exclusion
     - register/shape/session-document-layout
     - register/invariant/drawer-overlay-wins-over-preset
     - register/invariant/system-prompt-file-authoritative     # replacement

   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth/scaffolding/invariants/system-prompt-heading-authoritative.test.el
