** system-prompt-file-authoritative
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-system-prompt-file-authoritative
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/system-prompt-file-authoritative
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1779568860 (2026-05-23) — created by task
     mark-superseded-interfaces-register-entries as part of
     `replace-system-prompt-heading-with-sibling-file`. Pinned by
     consumers (gptel-chat--apply-system-prompt-file at activation,
     gptel-chat--refresh-system-prompt-from-file before every
     gptel-request from a chat-mode buffer) and by buttercup specs
     covering activation precedence
     (config/gptel/chat/test/menu/system-prompt-file-spec.el and the
     "system prompt restore precedence" describe in
     preset-wiring-spec.el) and per-request refresh
     (config/gptel/chat/test/menu/pre-send-refresh-spec.el).
     Supersedes register/invariant/system-prompt-heading-authoritative.
     cycle-1779572373 (2026-05-24) — task replace-system-prompt-
     infix-with-file-opener routed the chat-mode menu's "Edit
     system prompt" entry to gptel-chat--edit-system-prompt-file,
     which opens the canonical sibling file in another window
     rather than offering an in-menu buffer-local mutation. This
     is the user-facing affordance that preserves the invariant:
     the alternative (keeping upstream gptel-system-prompt in the
     chat-mode prefix) would have mutated buffer-local
     gptel--system-message via the minibuffer, which would have
     been silently overwritten by gptel-chat--refresh-system-
     prompt-from-file on the next gptel-request. The negative
     assertion in menu-send-rebind-spec.el (gptel-system-prompt
     absent from the chat-mode prefix) is the test-side guard
     against a future commit re-introducing the competing
     mutation surface.
   load_bearing: true
   title: |
     The sibling system-prompt.<ext> file is the authoritative
     source of gptel--system-message

   statement: |
     For a session.org document (register/shape/session-sibling-
     system-prompt-file), the body of the sibling file referenced by
     the :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: drawer key is the authoritative
     source of the buffer-local gptel--system-message.

     Restore precedence on chat-mode activation is, highest first:
       1. sibling system-prompt.<ext> file (resolved via
          :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE:), when present and non-blank
       2. legacy :GPTEL_SYSTEM: drawer entry, when present (back-
          compat for hand-authored sessions; never written by code
          under our control — register/invariant/drawer-system-key-
          write-exclusion)
       3. preset :system
     A nil property, a missing/unreadable file, or a blank body all
     fall through to the next source — an empty file never silently
     wipes the prompt.

     Before every gptel-request submitted from a gptel-chat-mode
     buffer, the sibling file is re-read into buffer-local
     gptel--system-message
     (gptel-chat--refresh-system-prompt-from-file, installed as
     :before advice on gptel-request). The buffer-local value is a
     per-request cache, not durable state; the file is the source
     of truth. A mid-session edit to the sibling file is therefore
     reflected on the next send without reverting session.org.

     The save path never writes the sibling file; the file is
     mutated only by direct user edits. The drawer write also never
     emits :GPTEL_SYSTEM: (composes with — does not contradict —
     register/invariant/drawer-system-key-write-exclusion).

   why_it_matters: |
     The prior cycle (gptel-drawer-as-source-of-truth §Addendum)
     relocated the system prompt into a `* System Prompt` heading
     body inside session.org because long markdown system prompts
     were unwieldy as drawer property values. The heading body
     carried multi-line text fine but interacted badly with org-mode
     fontification, folding, and the chat parser (especially when
     the prompt contained literal #+begin_user lines or markdown
     constructs). The sibling-file shape lets the system prompt
     render, edit, and reason about as the format the preset
     authored it in (markdown today, .org tomorrow) with no org-mode
     interaction. The "file is canonical, buffer is cache" mental
     model is sustained by the pre-send refresh: a user-edit on disk
     becomes the active prompt on the next send without explicit
     reverts.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: behavioural buttercup specs + reviewer's three-direction
       audit on chat-mode menu changes
     location: |
       Activation precedence:
       - config/gptel/chat/test/menu/system-prompt-file-spec.el
         (9 focused specs covering the resolver + activation-time
         installer + all defensive paths; plus the cycle-3 "Edit
         affordance" describe — 4 specs covering open-existing /
         create-from-unset / create-when-property-but-missing /
         no-prompt-when-set; the unset-property spec additionally
         pins save-buffer + read-string DEFAULT-VALUE per the
         cycle-3 reviewer follow-up)
       - config/gptel/chat/test/menu/preset-wiring-spec.el
         (the "system prompt restore precedence" describe — 6 specs
         covering sibling-file-wins-over-preset, sibling-wins-over-
         legacy-drawer, legacy-applies-without-sibling, and the
         three fallback-to-preset cases)
       Per-request refresh:
       - config/gptel/chat/test/menu/pre-send-refresh-spec.el
         (6 specs covering the refresh body + :before advice on
         gptel-request + the unreadable / blank-body / no-op edge
         cases + the non-chat-mode buffer no-op)
       User-facing edit affordance (the path that preserves "file
       is canonical, buffer is cache"):
       - gptel-chat--edit-system-prompt-file in
         config/gptel/chat/menu.org (replaces upstream
         gptel-system-prompt in the chat-mode prefix; opens the
         sibling file in another window rather than offering an
         in-menu buffer-local mutation surface that the pre-send
         refresh would overwrite)
       - menu-send-rebind-spec.el's "shared configuration infixes"
         describe asserts gptel-system-prompt is absent from the
         chat-mode prefix (and gptel-chat--edit-system-prompt-file
         is present) — the negative-assertion guard against a
         future re-introduction of the competing mutation surface

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/session-sibling-system-prompt-file
     - register/invariant/drawer-system-key-write-exclusion
     - register/invariant/drawer-overlay-wins-over-preset
     - register/invariant/system-prompt-heading-authoritative  # superseded predecessor

   supersedes:
     - register/invariant/system-prompt-heading-authoritative

   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** chat-block-body-is-indented
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-chat-block-body-is-indented
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/chat-block-body-is-indented
   tier: invariant
   status: speculated
   load_bearing: true
   title: every chat-block body line is indented; none begins with column-0 org structure

   statement: |
     For any buffer in `gptel-chat-mode`, every non-blank line whose
     position is strictly between an outer `#+begin_user` /
     `#+begin_assistant` / `#+begin_tool` opener and its matching
     `#+end_*` closer SHALL be indented by at least
     `gptel-chat-content-indentation` spaces (runtime floor 1) — so no
     body line begins with an org structural token (`*`, `#+end_*`,
     `#+begin_src`, a drawer, a keyword) at column 0. The invariant is
     maintained at every write path (streaming insert, paste / yank,
     file-read migration) by the chat-block-body-indentation boundary's
     stage 1, and by a buffer-local `indent-line-function` for typing.

   why_it_matters: |
     Empirically (research.md Finding 2), an `^\*+ ` line at column 0
     inside a chat block causes `org-element-parse-buffer` to lose the
     special-block entirely — the AST collapses to
     `paragraph | headline | paragraph`, the block's delimiter lines
     demote to paragraph content, and the heading absorbs every line
     below it into its outline subtree. The same failure is latent for
     any column-0 structural token. Indenting the whole body moves all
     content off column 0 at once, so org's column-0-anchored scanners
     never mistake body text for document structure. The chat-mode
     regex parser is structurally indifferent — message construction
     still works — but the host buffer would otherwise become unusable
     as an editing surface. Past incident:
     `~/.gptel/sessions/heading-test-20260430145834/branches/main/session.org`.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: structural-audit
     location: |
       openspec/changes/gptel-chat-heading-scoping/scaffolding/invariants/
       chat-block-body-is-indented.test.el — failing-stub assertion over
       the write-path entry points listed in the boundary entry.
       Architect's invariant-gap audit re-checks at integrate.
     indent_floor: |
       The body indent width is read through `gptel-chat--body-indent`,
       which clamps `gptel-chat-content-indentation` (or 2, when unbound)
       to a runtime floor of 1 — a 0-width indent would leave content at
       column 0 and violate this invariant. The defcustom's `:type
       'natnum` is customize-time defense-in-depth only; the accessor
       owns the floor. A future "tighten :type" task MUST NOT remove the
       accessor clamp on the assumption that customize already guards it.

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/chat-block-body-indentation
     - register/invariant/chat-body-indent-roundtrip-transparent
     - register/invariant/all-write-paths-indent-body

   discovered_from: gptel-chat-heading-scoping
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** chat-body-indent-roundtrip-transparent
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-chat-body-indent-roundtrip-transparent
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/chat-body-indent-roundtrip-transparent
   tier: invariant
   status: speculated
   load_bearing: true
   title: body indent/dedent round-trip is content-preserving

   statement: |
     For any message segment destined for a chat-block body: applying
     the write-path indent (boundary stage 1) and then the parser dedent
     (boundary stage 2) yields content equal to the original modulo a
     uniform leading-indentation shift. The dedent measures and strips
     each segment's common minimum indentation, so it is robust against
     changes to `gptel-chat-content-indentation` and legacy content with
     different indent widths, and it preserves the relative indentation
     of nested content (lists, code) within the segment.

   why_it_matters: |
     The whole point of the indentation is that the LLM never sees it;
     what the user composes and what the LLM receives must be identical
     except for the on-disk body indent. A dedent that stripped a fixed
     assumed width would corrupt content under any width change; a dedent
     that stripped all leading whitespace would flatten intentional
     nested indentation. Measure-and-strip of the common minimum is the
     `org-do-remove-indentation` model and is the only form that
     round-trips both robustly and structure-preservingly. Known
     accepted edge case: a segment whose every line is indented loses
     its outermost indent level — identical to org src-block behaviour.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       openspec/changes/gptel-chat-heading-scoping/scaffolding/invariants/
       chat-body-indent-roundtrip-transparent.test.el — failing-stub
       round-trip property test exercised against the streaming indenter,
       parser dedent, and on-disk migration paths.

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/chat-block-body-indentation
     - register/invariant/chat-block-body-is-indented

   discovered_from: gptel-chat-heading-scoping
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** chat-block-delimiter-lines-stay-at-column-0
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-chat-block-delimiter-lines-stay-at-column-0
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/chat-block-delimiter-lines-stay-at-column-0
   tier: invariant
   status: speculated
   load_bearing: true
   title: all chat-block delimiter lines remain at column 0

   statement: |
     The body indentation applied by the chat-block-body-indentation
     boundary SHALL modify only content lines inside a chat-block body.
     Delimiter lines themselves — the outer `#+begin_user` /
     `#+begin_assistant` / `#+end_user` / `#+end_assistant` AND the
     nested `#+begin_tool` / `#+end_tool` — SHALL remain at column 0 in
     the buffer and on disk. The streaming indenter, the paste
     region-shift, and the migration pass each skip delimiter lines; the
     buffer-local `indent-line-function` indents a delimiter line to
     column 0. The display layer MAY render `#+begin_tool` / `#+end_tool`
     with a cosmetic `line-prefix` so they appear aligned — that affects
     display only, never buffer text.

   why_it_matters: |
     A delimiter line at column N breaks chat-mode's regex parser:
     `gptel-chat-parse-buffer` matches `^#\+begin_(user|assistant|tool)`
     and `^#\+end_…` strictly at column 0. An indented delimiter
     disappears from message construction. This is also what makes
     indentation an escape (design.md Decision 4): because outer
     delimiters are recognised only at column 0, a user can type the
     literal text `#+end_user` in a message — it is indented body
     content and the parser ignores it. The nested tool delimiters stay
     at column 0 for the same parser reason (Decision 5 — Path C);
     keeping them there is what lets the parser and the
     block-body predicate stay unchanged.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: structural-audit
     location: |
       openspec/changes/gptel-chat-heading-scoping/scaffolding/invariants/
       chat-block-delimiter-lines-stay-at-column-0.test.el — failing-stub
       assertion that walks every chat-block opener/closer (outer and
       nested tool) in a fixture buffer after a write-path operation and
       confirms column 0.

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/chat-block-body-indentation
     - register/invariant/chat-block-body-is-indented

   discovered_from: gptel-chat-heading-scoping
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** chat-migration-on-read-clean-buffer-stays-clean
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-chat-migration-on-read-clean-buffer-stays-clean
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/chat-migration-on-read-clean-buffer-stays-clean
   tier: invariant
   status: speculated
   load_bearing: false
   title: read-time migration MUST NOT mark an already-indented buffer as modified

   statement: |
     When `gptel-chat-mode` activates on a buffer whose chat-block
     bodies are already in the indented form (no legacy escape
     artifacts, every content region already at the body indent), the
     migration pass SHALL NOT mark the buffer as modified. The boolean
     "did-any-rewrite-happen" must be the sole determiner of whether
     `set-buffer-modified-p t` runs. Buffers that DID need migration
     ARE marked modified, so the next `save-buffer` persists the
     normalised form (this is intentional, see design.md Decision 7).

   why_it_matters: |
     Read-time migration is the design's reversibility promise: if the
     user opens a session and the migration was unnecessary, they
     SHOULD see no buffer-modified indicator and `M-x revert-buffer`
     SHOULD have nothing to undo. Spurious modification flags train
     users to ignore the modified marker, defeating the explicit
     reversibility guarantee. Sister property to "migration is scoped
     to chat-block bodies only" — both are about NOT touching content
     the user didn't ask us to touch. The migration must be idempotent:
     a buffer it produced, re-opened, is clean.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       openspec/changes/gptel-chat-heading-scoping/scaffolding/invariants/
       chat-migration-on-read-clean-buffer-stays-clean.test.el —
       failing-stub assertion: open a fixture session whose chat blocks
       are already indented, activate gptel-chat-mode, assert
       `(buffer-modified-p)` returns nil.

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/chat-block-body-indentation

   discovered_from: gptel-chat-heading-scoping
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** all-write-paths-indent-body
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-all-write-paths-indent-body
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/all-write-paths-indent-body
   tier: invariant
   status: speculated
   load_bearing: true
   title: every content-introduction path into a chat-block body produces indented content

   statement: |
     Every distinct path by which content enters a chat-block body
     SHALL leave that content indented by the body width:
       - streaming assistant insertion — `gptel-chat--sanitize-chunk`
         indents each line (boundary stage 1)
       - paste / yank / programmatic insert — `after-change-functions`
         via `gptel-chat--indent-inserted-region` shifts the region
       - file-read migration — `gptel-chat-mode` activation via
         `gptel-chat--migrate-buffer`
       - user typing — a buffer-local `indent-line-function` plus
         electric-indent start new body lines at the body indent
     Adding a fifth content-introduction path (e.g. drag-and-drop, a
     new yank-handler, a programmatic inserter that bypasses
     `after-change-functions`) WITHOUT ensuring it produces indented
     content SHALL be treated as a violation, even if no current test
     fails.

   why_it_matters: |
     The boundary entry's correctness is conjunctive: missing one path
     silently corrupts the host buffer's outline whenever that path is
     exercised, while the other paths remain demonstrably correct in
     tests. The invariant exists so a future change adding a new
     content-introduction route trips the structural audit even if the
     change's tests pass on existing paths. Sister invariant to
     `chat-block-body-is-indented` — that one names the property; this
     one names the enforcement enumeration. Note typing is maintained
     by an editing affordance, not a reactive producer (design.md
     Decision 6) — it is "best effort, org-babel-like," not a hard
     guarantee, and the audit treats it accordingly.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: structural-audit
     location: |
       openspec/changes/gptel-chat-heading-scoping/scaffolding/invariants/
       all-write-paths-indent-body.test.el — failing-stub assertion that
       greps the named functions/wirings and confirms each indents body
       content. Architect's module-purpose audit at end-of-cycle
       re-checks for new content-introduction sites.

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/chat-block-body-indentation
     - register/invariant/chat-block-body-is-indented

   discovered_from: gptel-chat-heading-scoping
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** restore-precedence-working-over-saved
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-restore-precedence-working-over-saved
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/restore-precedence-working-over-saved
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260524-200631 integrate (2026-05-24): speculation matched.
     workspace--layout-effective-state is the single dispatch point;
     data-model-spec.el pins all three precedence cases (working-wins,
     saved-fallback, both-nil). Scaffolding archived. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260524-200631/reconciliations/invariant-restore-precedence-working-over-saved.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Restore prefers :working-state when non-nil; falls back to :saved-state

   statement: |
     For every layout =L= in the registry, the buffer applied on restore
     by =workspace--apply-saved-layout= MUST be derived from
     =workspace--layout-effective-state(L)=, defined as: =L:working-state=
     when non-nil, else =L:saved-state=. There is no code path that
     restores from =:saved-state= when =:working-state= is non-nil; that
     would re-introduce the v1 MVP's "explicit save never wins on
     restart" behavior in inverse.

   why_it_matters: |
     The two-state layout's user-facing promise is "restart lands you
     back where you left off, not at your last explicit save". The
     promise is the precedence rule; violating it (e.g. an =or=
     reversed in the accessor, or =workspace--apply-saved-layout=
     bypassing the helper) silently breaks the autosave-on-tab-switch
     feature even when the slots themselves are correct. No
     per-action test catches a reversed precedence because both
     slots contain valid window-state forms; only a test that
     populates both slots and asserts the chosen one catches this.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/
       invariants/restore-precedence-working-over-saved.test.el —
       failing-stub spec that constructs a layout with distinct
       :saved-state and :working-state forms (each carrying a
       distinguishable marker) and asserts the restored frame matches
       :working-state. A second case sets :working-state to nil and
       asserts the restored frame matches :saved-state.
     notes: |
       The same precedence is asserted indirectly by
       =workspace-revert='s scenarios (revert clears :working-state
       then re-applies effective-state); the direct test is the
       definitive one because it isolates the precedence from the
       revert flow.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/layout-v2-plist
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/invariants/restore-precedence-working-over-saved.test.el
   #+end_src

** explicit-save-clears-working-state
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-explicit-save-clears-working-state
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/explicit-save-clears-working-state
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260524-200631 integrate (2026-05-24): scope narrowed to three
     variants (workspace-save, workspace-save-layout NAME, workspace-new
     home stamp); workspace-switch-layout dropped (navigation, not
     explicit-save, per design.md §D4). Three scenarios shipped as tests in
     layouts-spec.el; scaffolding revised in-place to mark the
     switch-layout scenario out-of-scope. Inline-fix commit b70a5b4.
     Scaffolding archived. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260524-200631/reconciliations/invariant-explicit-save-clears-working-state.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Every explicit workspace-save clears the matching :working-state

   statement: |
     After =workspace-save= (and its variants =workspace-save-layout=,
     =workspace-switch-layout='s save-on-switch, =workspace-new='s home
     stamp) completes, the affected layout's =:working-state= slot MUST
     be nil. The clear is part of the explicit-save semantics, not an
     optimisation; it makes the user's deliberate gesture the new
     clean baseline so restore precedence falls through to
     =:saved-state= rather than resurrecting stale =:working-state=.

   why_it_matters: |
     Without the clear, a subsequent restart prefers the stale
     :working-state (which is older than the just-written
     :saved-state). The explicit save would feel "ignored". A
     reviewer can read the code and think "set :saved-state, done"
     without seeing the missing clear; only a test that performs
     [autosave → workspace-save → restart-simulation] catches it.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/
       invariants/explicit-save-clears-working-state.test.el —
       failing-stub spec that (1) populates :working-state, (2) calls
       workspace-save, (3) asserts :working-state is nil. A second
       scenario exercises the same flow via workspace-save-layout on a
       named non-recent layout-group.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/layout-v2-plist
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot
     - register/boundary/autosave-guard-pipeline

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/invariants/explicit-save-clears-working-state.test.el
   #+end_src

** autosave-never-writes-saved-state
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-autosave-never-writes-saved-state
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/autosave-never-writes-saved-state
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260524-200631 integrate (2026-05-24): enforcement strengthened
     from review-discipline (`&optional slot` defaulted to :saved-state) to
     structural (required SLOT arg + workspace--state-slot-p closed-set
     predicate at function entry). Inline-fix commit b70a5b4. Meta-pattern:
     same shape as bookmark-reincarnation's gen-counter fix
     (boundary/buffer-reincarnation-pipeline) —
     invariant-at-wrong-layer-fix. Scaffolding archived. See
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260524-200631/reconciliations/invariant-autosave-never-writes-saved-state.md.
   load_bearing: true
   title: No autosave path writes :saved-state; clobber-impossible

   statement: |
     No code path entered from any of the autosave triggers
     (=tab-bar-select-tab= advice, =tab-bar-switch-to-tab= advice,
     =workspaces-mode= idle timer, =workspace--kill-emacs-flush=)
     writes to any layout's =:saved-state= slot. The slot-routing
     stage of =workspace--autosave-current-layout= (stage 3 of
     register/boundary/autosave-guard-pipeline) MUST direct every
     non-explicit-save trigger to =:working-state=.

   why_it_matters: |
     This is the structural fix for v1 MVP gap D8. The v1 code had
     a single state slot; the tab-switch advice wrote to it; an
     explicit save and a tab switch both modified the same slot,
     producing race-y user-visible clobbering of the explicit save.
     v2 makes the clobber impossible: distinct slots + the autosave
     code path has no syntactic ability to address :saved-state. A
     bug here would be subtle: the slot name is one symbol; a typo
     in a future contributor's let-binding could silently send
     autosaves to :saved-state on one trigger but not others.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/
       invariants/autosave-never-writes-saved-state.test.el —
       property test: for each autosave trigger (mocked at its
       entry-point boundary), pre-populate :saved-state with a
       distinguishable marker, fire the trigger, assert :saved-state
       equals the marker post-trigger. Repeat per trigger.
     notes: |
       A complementary lint check (=grep -n ':saved-state'
       config/workspaces/=) is cheap and catches the "wrong slot
       referenced from an autosave site" form of the bug at review
       time; the test catches the runtime-routing form.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/layout-v2-plist
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot
     - register/boundary/autosave-guard-pipeline

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/invariants/autosave-never-writes-saved-state.test.el
   #+end_src

** explicit-save-bypasses-anti-save
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-explicit-save-bypasses-anti-save
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/explicit-save-bypasses-anti-save
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   reconciled_in_cycle: cycle-20260525-082618
   reconciled_at: 2026-05-25T09:30:00Z
   load_bearing: true
   title: Explicit workspace-save never consults workspace-anti-save-predicates

   statement: |
     The =workspace-save= command (and its variants =workspace-save-
     layout=, =workspace-switch-layout='s save-on-switch, =workspace-
     new='s home stamp) MUST NOT call =run-hook-with-args-until-
     success= on =workspace-anti-save-predicates=. The user's
     deliberate gesture is unconditional; only the autosave triggers
     are guarded. Structurally, explicit-save paths enter the
     autosave-guard pipeline at stage 2 (capture), not stage 1
     (anti-save-check).

   why_it_matters: |
     Anti-save predicates exist so a debug session or open minibuffer
     does not have its transient state captured into the workspace.
     But a user invoking =M-x workspace-save= explicitly has decided
     "save now"; respecting a predicate at that point traps the user
     (they cannot escape a misconfigured predicate by saving
     manually). The bug is also subtle if the same helper function
     is reused for both code paths without a flag distinguishing
     them — a future refactor consolidating the two paths is exactly
     the change most likely to introduce the regression.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/
       invariants/explicit-save-bypasses-anti-save.test.el —
       failing-stub spec that adds a predicate that always returns
       non-nil to workspace-anti-save-predicates, then calls
       workspace-save, then asserts the save completed (state file
       updated, :saved-state populated). A second scenario asserts
       that with the same always-non-nil predicate, a tab-switch
       autosave is suppressed.

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/autosave-guard-pipeline
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/invariants/explicit-save-bypasses-anti-save.test.el
   #+end_src

** idle-tick-no-op-off-workspace
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-idle-tick-no-op-off-workspace
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/idle-tick-no-op-off-workspace
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   reconciled_in_cycle: cycle-20260525-082618
   reconciled_at: 2026-05-25T09:30:00Z
   load_bearing: true
   title: workspaces-mode idle-tick must short-circuit when no workspace is current

   statement: |
     The =workspaces-mode= idle-timer callback (the function passed to
     =run-with-idle-timer=) MUST consult =(workspace--current-name)=
     and return without side effect when the result is nil. The
     callback MAY NOT call =workspace--autosave-current-layout= unless
     a current-workspace name exists. This holds for every entry path
     into the idle-tick (timer fire, manual =funcall= via M-:, future
     re-entry from an enhanced =workspaces-mode= toggle).

   why_it_matters: |
     The idle timer fires globally — it does not know whether the
     user is in any workspace at all (e.g. transient state after
     =workspace-close= but before =workspace-switch=, or in a frame
     that never had any workspace activity). =workspace--autosave-
     current-layout= consults the workspace registry and the recent
     layout-group; if it runs against a missing workspace it either
     errors (best case: noisy *Messages* spew on every idle tick) or
     writes a degenerate layout under a sentinel key (worst case:
     corrupts the registry shape and breaks the next restore).

     This is the canonical "silent-fail-on-default-path" shape from
     cycle 1's highest-confidence meta-discovery (invariant-at-wrong-
     layer-fix): the enforcement must live inside the callback that
     fires, not in the timer-registration discipline.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test + structural
     location: |
       openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/
       invariants/idle-tick-no-op-off-workspace.test.el — failing-stub
       buttercup spec.

     enforcement_locus_check: |
       Where in the call graph is this enforced? Inside
       =workspaces-mode--idle-tick= as the FIRST form of its body,
       structurally a =(when (workspace--current-name) ...)= guard.

       Does that location cover ALL entry paths? Yes if and only if
       =workspaces-mode--idle-tick= is the single function passed to
       =run-with-idle-timer= and no other code path calls
       =workspace--autosave-current-layout= without first checking
       current-workspace existence. The tab-switch advice and kill-
       emacs hook are scoped to events that imply workspace context
       (tab-switch fires on tabs; kill-emacs flushes the registry
       that was already populated) so the guard is locally redundant
       on those paths.

       Does the enforcement prevent silent-fail (default values,
       optional arguments, fallthrough)? Risk vector: if
       =workspace--autosave-current-layout='s slot argument acquired
       a default in a future refactor (already explicitly rejected
       in cycle 1 inline-fix b70a5b4), an off-workspace tick could
       still call it with a degenerate slot. Mitigated by the
       required-slot signature being itself an invariant (=register/
       invariant/autosave-never-writes-saved-state= pins the four
       autosave entry points; this entry pins the prior guard).

   design_anchor:
     section: design.md §D6
     quote: |
       "Idle save is specifically the 'crash safety' net, not the
       primary persistence trigger."

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/autosave-guard-pipeline
     - register/invariant/autosave-never-writes-saved-state
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot

   discovered_from: refine-workspaces-two-state-layout
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/refine-workspaces-two-state-layout/scaffolding/invariants/idle-tick-no-op-off-workspace.test.el
   #+end_src

** home-required-no-floating-workspaces
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-home-required-no-floating-workspaces
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/home-required-no-floating-workspaces
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260526-171719 integrate (2026-05-26): reconciled. Cycle-3
     broken-home-tolerance absorbed cycle-2 architect finding
     arch-cycle-20260525-213500-04 by extending the persistence
     deserializer with a (not (file-name-absolute-p home)) filter arm
     that emits "Workspaces: skipping persisted entry %S — :home %S
     is not absolute". The cycle-3 broken-home-runtime-spec.el adds
     the corresponding it-clause. Enforcement surface now structurally
     includes absolute-path filtering at the deserialize boundary.
     OPEN: programmatic construction via workspace--make still admits
     relative paths (no cl-check-type or error in the constructor) —
     this is the spec-signal carried as ask-cycle-20260526-171719-2
     (workspace-default-home-builder's *scratch* fallback reachability).
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260526-171719/reconciliations/invariant-home-required-no-floating-workspaces.md.

     Prior status_note (cycle-20260525-200459 integrate, 2026-05-25):
     structural enforcement landed at workspace--make's required
     (name home) signature — single-arg calls signal wrong-number-of-arguments
     at every caller. Cycle-1 data-model task added the constructor
     signature; mid-cycle wire-home-into-callsites closed the
     76-failure cascade. Test-tier enforcement landed in
     config/workspaces/test/data-model-home-spec.el (:home accessor
     describe block, 5 specs). Persistence-side enforcement
     (deserializer's skip-with-notice for malformed entries)
     forward-pinned to cycle-2 persistence-schema-v3 (which landed
     the missing-directory arm; cycle-3 above adds the
     non-absolute-path arm).
   load_bearing: true
   title: Every workspace in the registry has :home set to an absolute path

   statement: |
     For every workspace =ws= present in =workspace--registry= at
     any time after package initialization completes:
     =(stringp (workspace--home ws))= AND =(file-name-absolute-p
     (workspace--home ws))= MUST hold. The package does not
     represent floating workspaces. No code path SHALL produce or
     accept a registry entry whose =:home= is nil, missing,
     non-string, or non-absolute. Constructors that would produce
     such a workspace MUST signal =user-error=; deserializers that
     encounter such a serialized entry MUST skip it with a
     =*Messages*= notice and continue.

   why_it_matters: |
     The pre-change v2 model permitted floating workspaces — a
     workspace was a pure in-Emacs construct. Anchoring is the
     whole point of this change. Without an enforced invariant, a
     future refactor (or a hand-written persistence file) could
     reintroduce a floating workspace, and the downstream code that
     now assumes =:home= is always callable (the scaffold pipeline,
     the gptel-sessions consult, =workspace-purge='s default-parent
     check, the broken-state machinery) would either crash on the
     missing accessor or silently fall through to default behaviour
     in a way that masks the data corruption. Pinning this
     invariant at the data-model boundary (=workspace--make='s
     signature) and at the persistence boundary (=workspace--
     persistence-deserialize-workspace='s skip-and-notice for
     malformed entries) gives the system two structural-audit
     enforcement points; the cycle-1 scaffold also adds a
     test-tier enforcement that runs against the live registry.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: structural-audit
     location: |
       Primary: =workspace--make='s required =name= and =home=
       parameters (cannot be invoked with =:home= nil). Secondary:
       =workspace--persistence-deserialize-workspace='s skip-with-
       notice path for entries lacking =:home= (cycle-2 task
       =persistence-schema-v3=) AND for entries whose =:home= is
       relative (cycle-3 task =broken-home-tolerance=, absorbing
       cycle-2 finding =arch-cycle-20260525-213500-04=). Architect-audit
       signal-class: shape-fragmentation — flags any future code
       path that inserts into =workspace--registry= without going
       through =workspace--make=.
     notes: |
       The =test= enforcement (scaffolded for cycle 1; promoted to
       =data-model-home-spec.el= and =broken-home-runtime-spec.el=) is a
       property check that maps over =workspace--registry= and asserts
       the invariant; the structural-audit catches new code paths the
       property check would only catch retroactively (after a bug
       has landed in the registry). Both layers together.

       OPEN (carried as spec-signal ask-cycle-20260526-171719-2):
       programmatic construction via =workspace--make= still admits
       relative-path =:home= (no =cl-check-type= in the constructor).
       If the invariant is treated as load-bearing-and-complete, a
       constructor-side guard is required; if "deserializer is the
       single point of entry", the existing deserializer arms are
       sufficient and the *scratch* fallback in
       =workspace-default-home-builder= becomes dead code.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/workspace-plist-v3
     - register/invariant/registry-name-equals-basename
     - register/invariant/broken-home-no-auto-recreate  # cycle-2+

   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/add-workspace-home-directory/scaffolding/invariants/home-required-no-floating-workspaces.el
   #+end_src

** registry-name-equals-basename
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-registry-name-equals-basename
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/registry-name-equals-basename
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260526-171719 integrate (2026-05-26): reconciled. Cycle-3
     reviewer F1 on broken-home-tolerance (BLOCKING, design-drift)
     caught that workspace-re-anchor's initial rename branch mutated
     only :home via workspace--set-home, leaving :name desynchronised
     — and the rename did not survive save/restore because the
     serializer writes (workspace--name ws) and the deserializer
     puthashes under the same. Inline-fix c6c1b22 introduced
     workspace--set-name (data-model.el:139-147) as the sibling
     setter to workspace--set-home and re-wrote workspace-re-anchor's
     rename branch to chain
     workspace--clear-broken ∘ workspace--set-name ∘ workspace--set-home
     in a single lockstep update. The divergent spec assertion was
     flipped from (expect ... :to-equal "foo") to "renamed". The
     invariant is now structurally enforced at the only mutation site
     the entry's statement cites: workspace-re-anchor.
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260526-171719/reconciliations/invariant-registry-name-equals-basename.md.

     Prior status_note (cycle-20260525-200459 integrate, 2026-05-25):
     cycle-1 enforcement is the canonical-pattern test in
     data-model-home-spec.el (constructed workspace's :name equals
     basename(directory-file-name(:home))). wire-home-into-callsites
     synthesized HOME values where basename(:home) == :name by
     construction in both the production callsite (tabs.org
     workspace-new placeholder) and the 14 fixture calls in
     data-model-spec.el. The data layer correctly does NOT enforce
     equality (spec delegates to the canonical caller).
     Property-test-over-registry and rename-on-re-anchor enforcement
     forward-pinned to cycle-3 broken-home-tolerance (where
     workspace-re-anchor does the remhash/puthash/:home/:name
     choreography). disc-add-home-slot-1 captures the cycle-3 sharp
     edge (workspace--set-home alone is insufficient); absorbed via
     the inline-fix above. Scaffold archived; 4 of 5 it clauses
     landed in data-model-home-spec.el; 5th clause forward-pinned.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Workspace registry-name equals basename of :home

   statement: |
     For every workspace =ws= present in =workspace--registry= at
     any time after package initialization completes:
     =(equal (workspace--name ws) (file-name-nondirectory
     (directory-file-name (workspace--home ws))))= MUST hold. The
     registry-name is mechanically derived from the filesystem
     anchor; the two cannot diverge. Display name (via
     =workspace-home-org-title=) MAY differ and overrides the
     registry-name only where the workspace is shown as prose to
     the user. Out-of-band directory renames (=mv= and restart)
     SHALL produce a new registry entry under the new basename;
     the package does not provide in-process rename.

   why_it_matters: |
     The registry-name is the persistence key, the =tab-bar= tab
     name, and the =completing-read= prompt key. Allowing it to
     diverge from =basename(:home)= creates ambiguity at restart:
     after =mv ~/emacs-workspaces/foo ~/emacs-workspaces/bar=, the
     workspace's =:home= ends in =bar= but its serialized
     =:name= would still be =foo=. The persistence-read path would
     either honour =:name= (registry has =foo=, filesystem says
     =bar= — confusing) or honour =basename(:home)= (registry
     gets =bar= silently — what the spec promises). Pinning the
     invariant forces every code path that mutates either =:name=
     or =:home= to update the other in lockstep, and the property
     check catches drift retroactively. The =workspace-re-anchor=
     command in particular MUST rename the registry key when the
     new path's basename differs from the old NAME (per task
     =broken-home-tolerance='s step 3).

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test + lockstep-helpers
     location: |
       Property test (Buttercup) over =workspace--registry= run
       after each of: =workspace--make=, =workspace-restore=
       (deserialization), =workspace-re-anchor=. Scaffolded at
       cycle 1; cycle-2+ tasks that touch any of these paths must
       keep the property test green or revise the scaffold (and
       explain in =## Discoveries=).

       Lockstep-helper enforcement (cycle-3 inline-fix c6c1b22):
       the data-model module exposes =workspace--set-name= and
       =workspace--set-home= as accessor-pair siblings. Every
       mutation site that touches either =:name= or =:home=
       SHALL call BOTH setters in a single non-destructive
       update — verifiable by grep:
       =grep -nE "workspace--set-(name|home)" config/workspaces/=
       at every match site shows both setters used together.
       =workspace-re-anchor='s rename branch is the canonical
       enforcement site.
     notes: |
       The =structural-audit= enforcement (architect on-touch
       signal-class: vocabulary-mismatch) is a secondary layer.
       The primary layer is the test because the invariant is
       cheap to assert as a property; structural-audit catches
       new code paths that the test would miss between cycles.

       The cycle-3 reviewer-caught BLOCKING violation
       (workspace-re-anchor mutating :home without :name) was a
       case where the data-model exposed an asymmetric helper set
       (only =workspace--set-home=) and the implementor followed
       the helpers rather than the invariant. The inline-fix
       restored symmetry. Future register entries with multi-slot
       lockstep clauses SHOULD declare the required setter set in
       this section so the architect can audit helper-completeness
       at on-touch time.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/workspace-plist-v3
     - register/invariant/home-required-no-floating-workspaces

   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/add-workspace-home-directory/scaffolding/invariants/registry-name-equals-basename.el
   #+end_src

** home-org-user-authored-after-creation
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-home-org-user-authored-after-creation
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/home-org-user-authored-after-creation
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260525-200459 integrate (2026-05-25): cycle-1 narrow enforcement
     landed — config/workspaces/home-org.el has zero file-write primitives,
     verified by both inspection and the in-spec lint in home-org-spec.el's
     "Invariant: reader module is read-only" case. The BROADER cross-module
     lint (walking every .el under config/workspaces/ for write primitives
     against a permitted-writers allow-list) is deferred to follow-up task
     port-cross-module-home-org-writer-lint (blocked on cycle-2 scaffold-module
     so the permitted writer workspace--scaffold-write-home-org exists when
     the lint lands). Cycle-1's narrower coverage is functionally equivalent
     today (no other workspaces module currently writes home.org); cycle-2+
     work makes the invariant DURABLE. Reviewer R1-F1 flagged the omission
     as advisory (not blocking); follow-up task filed. Scaffold archived;
     it #2 landed in home-org-spec.el, it #1 in the follow-up task, it #3
     (byte-for-byte snapshot) naturally lands with scaffold-module.
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-200459/reconciliations/invariant-home-org-user-authored-after-creation.md.
     POST-CYCLE-5 (ask-cycle-20260526-171719-1): the broader cross-module
     lint (home-org-writer-lint-spec.el) was REMOVED — it passed trivially
     (forward-only proximity window + cycle-4 helper routing left zero
     literals to match), giving false coverage. Enforcement reverts to
     per-module review plus home-org-spec.el's narrow read-only assertion;
     the invariant remains true (no other module writes home.org).
   load_bearing: true
   title: The package writes home.org exactly once (at scaffold time)

   statement: |
     The workspaces package SHALL write =<HOME>/home.org= exactly
     one time per workspace: during the scaffolding pipeline (the
     skeleton write in =workspace--scaffold-write-home-org=). After
     that single write, NO code path in =config/workspaces/*= SHALL
     write to, modify, or delete =home.org=. The package MAY read
     =home.org= at any time (live re-read, no caching). The user
     owns the file's content after creation; package operations
     (session create/delete, workspace activation, idle autosave,
     shutdown, any "refresh" command) MUST leave =home.org=
     byte-identical.

   why_it_matters: |
     =home.org= is the user's dashboard / metadata file. Auto-
     rendering session inventories, syncing display names back from
     the registry, or "refreshing" the skeleton would destroy
     user-authored content silently — a class of data-loss bug
     that is impossible to recover from once the autosave runs.
     The spec is explicit that the filesystem (=sessions/=)
     authoritative for session inventory and =home.org= is
     decorative for any user-curated session listing; the
     invariant pins that asymmetry at the code boundary. The
     home-org reader module (cycle 1) is the ONLY module that
     touches =home.org= post-creation, and it only reads. The
     scaffold module (cycle 2) is the ONLY producer, and it
     writes once with an =unless (file-exists-p path)= guard.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: review
     location: |
       Primary: per-module code review. =home.org= has exactly one
       producer, =workspace--scaffold-write-home-org= in
       =scaffold.el= (writing once under an =unless (file-exists-p
       path)= guard); every other module under =config/workspaces/*=
       only reads. A new writer is a reviewable, single-site change.
       Narrow automated backstop: =home-org-spec.el='s "reader module
       is read-only" case asserts =home-org.el= contains zero file-
       write primitives.
     notes: |
       The cross-module grep/proximity lint
       (=home-org-writer-lint-spec.el=) was REMOVED post-cycle-5
       (ask-cycle-20260526-171719-1). It passed for two wrong
       reasons — a forward-only proximity window missed the
       let-binding-before-write idiom (cycle-3), and after cycle-4
       routed the writer through =workspace-home-org-path= the
       writer body contained zero =home.org= literals to match at
       all — so it gave false "covered" confidence. A lint that
       passes for the wrong reasons is worse than none; per-module
       review is the real defense and the invariant remains true
       (no other module writes =home.org=).

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/home-org-read-pipeline
     - register/shape/workspace-plist-v3

   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/add-workspace-home-directory/scaffolding/invariants/home-org-user-authored-after-creation.el
   #+end_src

** scaffold-leave-partial-on-failure
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-scaffold-leave-partial-on-failure
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/scaffold-leave-partial-on-failure
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260525-213500 integrate: speculated → confirmed.
     scaffold-module's mid-pipeline-failure path leaves partial
     filesystem state in place (no =delete-directory= / =delete-file=
     in =config/workspaces/scaffold.el='s body — enforced by
     structural lint in =scaffold-spec.el=), signals =user-error=
     naming the HOME path, and does not mutate the registry. All
     four scaffolded assertions lifted into =scaffold-spec.el=:
     mid-pipeline git-commit failure leaves home.org + sessions/;
     no-registry-mutation (delegated to caller-side); user-error
     message names HOME path; structural lint on no-delete primitives.
     The caller-side complement (workspace-new MUST NOT register on
     failed scaffold) is owned by =workspace-new-default-path=
     (cycle 3+). Reconciliation note at
     =.orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-213500/reconciliations/invariant-scaffold-leave-partial-on-failure.md=.

     Prior status_note (preserved for lineage):
     "cycle-20260525-213500 plan: populated forward-mode. Pairs with
     the =register/boundary/workspace-scaffold-pipeline= entry: the
     pipeline's contract says it can fail; this invariant pins WHAT
     THE PACKAGE DOES NOT DO when failure happens. Speculation: any
     pipeline stage's failure leaves the partial filesystem state in
     place (no =rm -rf=, no =delete-directory= on the partial
     scaffold), signals =user-error= with the path named, and does
     not mutate the registry. Pressure-tested by =scaffold-module=
     (mid-pipeline-failure spec case) and downstream by
     =workspace-new-default-path= (caller-side: the failed scaffold
     MUST NOT yield a registry entry)."
   load_bearing: true
   title: Scaffold pipeline never deletes partial filesystem state on failure

   statement: |
     On any failure of any stage of =workspace-scaffold= (see
     =register/boundary/workspace-scaffold-pipeline=), the package
     SHALL:
       1. Abort the pipeline (subsequent stages do not run).
       2. Signal =user-error= naming the HOME path, the failing stage
          or git invocation, and a remedy hint ("inspect and remove
          manually if appropriate").
       3. LEAVE PARTIAL FILESYSTEM STATE IN PLACE. No call to
          =delete-directory=, =delete-file=, =rename-file=, or any
          other deletion primitive against the HOME path or its
          contents.
       4. Not mutate =workspace--registry= (the function itself does
          not touch the registry — but this clause is the contract:
          callers MAY NOT register a workspace whose scaffold call
          signalled).
       5. Not create a tab (the caller's =tab-bar= invocation MUST be
          gated on a non-signalling scaffold return).

   why_it_matters: |
     The default path (=~/emacs-workspaces/NAME/=) is safe to retry
     after manual cleanup. But the anchor-existing branches let the
     user point =workspace-new= at an arbitrary path via
     =read-directory-name=. A typo'd anchor target (=/Users/jeff/code/=
     instead of =/Users/jeff/code/myproj/=) followed by a silent
     =rm -rf= would catastrophically delete the user's data — not
     because we touched anything we created, but because we couldn't
     distinguish "the dir we just made" from "the dir that was
     already there". The conservative choice is: NEVER delete. The
     cost is an awkward manual =rm -rf= after a rare mid-pipeline
     failure; the alternative is unacceptable. Design D2 names this
     explicitly: "silent rm -rf on partial failure is too risky".

     The "no registry mutation" clause is the consumer-side
     enforcement: even if a future refactor adds early registration
     (e.g. to support resume-on-failure), it MUST roll back on any
     scaffold signal. Otherwise a half-scaffolded workspace appears
     in completion and the user activates it, with downstream code
     find-file'ing into a missing =home.org= or running =git= in a
     repo with no commits.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       Primary: =config/workspaces/test/scaffold-spec.el=, mid-pipeline-
       failure case. Stub =workspace--scaffold-git= via =cl-letf= to
       signal on the =commit= arg; invoke =workspace-scaffold=; assert
       =user-error= is raised, and assert =(file-exists-p HOME)=,
       =(file-exists-p (expand-file-name "home.org" HOME))=, and
       =(file-directory-p (expand-file-name "sessions" HOME))= ALL
       still hold after the signal. Then assert there is NO registry
       entry for the scaffold target (i.e. =(workspace--get name)=
       returns nil).
     notes: |
       The test layer is the primary enforcement — there is no
       cheap structural-audit for "did this function call
       delete-directory in its error path?" without behavioural
       inspection. The architect's signal-class 3 (responsibility-
       leakage) catches the converse defect (a new delete-call added
       to scaffold.el), and signal-class 8 (invariant-gap) catches
       the absence of this test if the spec file is removed.

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/workspace-scaffold-pipeline
     - register/invariant/home-required-no-floating-workspaces

   cited_by_tasks:
     - scaffold-module
   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/add-workspace-home-directory/scaffolding/invariants/scaffold-leave-partial-on-failure.el
   #+end_src

** broken-tag-runtime-only
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-broken-tag-runtime-only
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/broken-tag-runtime-only
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260525-213500 integrate: speculated → reconciled.
     persistence-schema-v3 implements the contract via explicit-
     enumeration in =workspace--persistence-serialize-workspace=:
     the helper returns a fresh plist with exactly
     =(:name :home :recent-layout-group :buffer-files :layout-groups)=,
     omitting =:broken= by construction (not by defensive filter).
     The deserializer re-derives the broken tag via
     =(file-directory-p :home)= on load.

     Enforcement mechanism revised: the scaffold's fourth assertion
     (structural lint on writer body — defun named =serialize=,
     =save=, =write= must not reference =:broken=) was substituted
     by a byte-equivalence property test (serialize-with-broken vs
     serialize-clean produce identical on-disk bytes). The
     byte-equivalence assertion is rename-stable and tests the
     INVARIANT outcome directly; the structural lint would have
     asserted a property of the defensive-filter factoring style,
     which the implementer did not use.

     Three of four scaffolded assertions live in
     =persistence-v3-spec.el= (serialize-omits-broken, byte-
     equivalence) and =broken-home-load-spec.el= (deserialize-
     derives-broken). Reconciliation note at
     =.orchestrator/cycles/cycle-20260525-213500/reconciliations/invariant-broken-tag-runtime-only.md=.

     Prior status_note (preserved for lineage):
     "cycle-20260525-213500 plan: populated forward-mode. The
     =:broken= plist tag introduced by cycle-1 data-model work is
     documented in =register/shape/workspace-plist-v3= as runtime-
     only; this invariant pins the PERSISTENCE-SIDE contract that
     enforces it. Speculation: the cycle-2 persistence serializer
     filters =:broken= from each workspace plist before writing to
     disk, and the deserializer re-derives the tag freshly on each
     load by =(file-directory-p :home)=. Pressure-tested by
     =persistence-schema-v3= (the round-trip :broken case). If the
     implementor finds a cheaper way to enforce (e.g. a single helper
     =workspace--persistence-serialize-workspace= that omits =:broken=
     by construction), the entry reconciles confirmed with a note on
     the implementation locus."
   load_bearing: true
   title: The :broken plist tag is runtime-only — never serialized to disk

   statement: |
     For every workspace =ws= written to the persistence file:
     =(plist-member (workspace--serialize ws) :broken)= MUST return
     nil. The =:broken= tag SHALL be set only at load time (by the
     deserializer's =(file-directory-p :home)= check) or by the
     explicit =workspace--mark-broken= helper, and SHALL be cleared
     by =workspace--clear-broken= or by =workspace-re-anchor=. The
     tag is an OBSERVATION about the current filesystem state, not a
     persistent attribute of the workspace.

     Equivalently: the on-disk representation of a workspace is the
     same whether the workspace was marked broken at load time or
     not. Reading a workspace as broken, serializing without change,
     reading back: produces a non-broken workspace IF the directory
     has reappeared, broken IF still missing.

   why_it_matters: |
     If =:broken= were serialized, the system would need a state
     machine for clearing it — when does a workspace become "no
     longer broken"? At the next successful activation? At the next
     restart? On =workspace-re-anchor=? Each choice introduces a
     drift point. Keeping the tag ephemeral means the answer is
     trivial: "the next load checks =file-directory-p= afresh".

     The persistence-write path is also the only path that turns
     in-memory state durable; if it omits =:broken= by construction,
     no future code path can accidentally persist the tag (e.g. by
     marking a workspace broken in memory and then immediately
     calling =workspace-save=). The invariant is therefore a
     load-bearing property of the serializer's output, not just of
     the runtime model.

     Pairs with =register/vocabulary/workspace-broken-disposition=:
     the vocabulary defines what commands DO when invoked on a
     broken workspace; this invariant defines how the broken state
     LIVES — at runtime only. The combination makes broken-state
     handling auditable: every code path that reacts to broken is
     in the vocabulary; every code path that produces broken state
     is the deserializer's =file-directory-p= check or an explicit
     =mark-broken= call.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       Primary: =config/workspaces/test/persistence-v3-spec.el=, the
       =:broken round-trip= case. Construct a workspace in memory,
       mark broken via =workspace--mark-broken=, serialize to a tmp
       file, read the file back as plain elisp (=read=), assert that
       the workspace's plist does NOT contain a =:broken= key.
       Secondary: =config/workspaces/test/broken-home-load-spec.el=,
       the load-side: a v3 file whose serialized workspace has
       =:home= pointing at a missing directory loads as broken
       (=workspace--broken-p= returns t), demonstrating the
       deserializer re-derives the tag from filesystem state, not
       from any persisted bit.
     notes: |
       Architect signal-class 8 (invariant-gap) catches the absence
       of either test if the spec files are removed. Architect
       signal-class 7 (interface-drift) catches a serializer revision
       that includes =:broken= in its output plist by inspecting the
       function body against this entry's statement.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/workspace-plist-v3
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-broken-disposition
     - register/invariant/home-required-no-floating-workspaces

   cited_by_tasks:
     - persistence-schema-v3
   discovered_from: add-workspace-home-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   scaffolding_path: openspec/changes/add-workspace-home-directory/scaffolding/invariants/broken-tag-runtime-only.el
   #+end_src

** workspaces-transient-deferred-require
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-workspaces-transient-deferred-require
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/workspaces-transient-deferred-require
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-20260612-143558 (workspace-integration-menu) integrate:
     created CONFIRMED from discovery disc-workspaces-transient-menu-1.
     Surfaced when an eager =(require 'transient)= in
     =workspaces-transient.el= pulled in the stale BUILT-IN transient and
     defeated the straight override, causing =transient--set-layout=
     void-function at init and aborting the whole test run (exit 255).
     Resolved by deferring all transient symbols behind
     =with-eval-after-load 'transient=.
   load_bearing: true
   title: workspaces-subtree modules must defer transient (load-order constraint)
   rule: |
     Any module under =config/workspaces/= that uses =transient= MUST
     defer its transient forms (=transient-define-prefix=,
     =transient-parse-suffixes=, etc.) behind
     =(with-eval-after-load 'transient ...)= and MUST NOT
     =(require 'transient)= at load time — for as long as
     =config/workspaces/workspaces= loads BEFORE =config/transient= in
     =init.el='s =jf/enabled-modules=. An eager require loads the stale
     built-in transient and defeats =config/transient.el='s straight
     override (=use-package transient :demand t=, which relies on
     transient not already being loaded).
   why_it_matters: |
     A real, init-breaking incident this cycle: eager require → built-in
     transient → =transient-showcase= fails with =(void-function
     transient--set-layout)= → exit 255 across the suite. The
     skills-transient module gets away with =(require 'transient)= ONLY
     because gptel loads AFTER =config/transient=; the constraint is
     load-order-relative, not absolute.
   enforcement_mechanism: review-discipline
   enforcement_note: |
     Currently caught only by a full-init test run going red (the symptom
     is global, not local). A stronger mechanism (a lint asserting no
     bare =(require 'transient)= under config/workspaces/, or moving
     =config/transient= before =workspaces= in the load order) is a
     candidate follow-up — see .tasks/ externalisation at cycle-6 integrate.
   applies_to:
     - config/workspaces/workspaces-transient.org   # honors the rule (deferred)
   keybinding_note: |
     =C-x w= binds the =workspace-menu= SYMBOL eagerly; the symbol is a
     valid keymap target before the prefix is defined, and transient
     loads later in the same init pass, so the binding works without an
     eager require.
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/workspace-integration-registry
   cited_by_tasks:
     - workspaces-transient-menu
   discovered_from: workspace-integration-menu
   discovered_by: implementor
   discovered_class: load-order-hazard
   #+end_src

** git-worktree-default-directory-binding
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-git-worktree-default-directory-binding
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/git-worktree-default-directory-binding
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   load_bearing: true
   title: every magit worktree/branch call is bound to its target repo via default-directory
   statement: |
     In =config/workspaces/git-worktrees.org=, EVERY =magit-*= call that
     operates on a specific repository or worktree MUST be wrapped in an
     explicit =(let ((default-directory REPO-OR-WORKTREE)) ...)=. magit
     functions act on =default-directory=; an unbound or wrong binding
     silently operates on the WRONG repo (the orchestrator's repo, or
     whatever the current buffer points at).
   why_it_matters: |
     The module manages worktrees across N independently-rooted source
     repos in one workspace. =magit-main-branch=, =magit-worktree-branch=,
     =magit-worktree-delete=, =magit-branch-merged-p=, =magit-branch-delete=
     and the =magit-list-worktrees= enumeration each need a precise root:
     creation/branch ops bind to the SOURCE repo; enumeration binds INTO
     the child worktree. A wrong binding could create a branch in, or
     delete a worktree from, an unintended repository — a destructive,
     hard-to-debug class of bug.
   enforcement_mechanism: test
   enforcement_detail: |
     Behavioral buttercup specs in =config/workspaces/test/git-worktrees-spec.el=
     spy each magit fn and capture =default-directory= at call time,
     asserting it equals the chosen repo (add-worktree) / each worktree's
     source repo (on-purge teardown). cycle-7: confirmed by tasks
     =add-worktree-command= and =on-purge-teardown-handler= (both with
     call-time default-directory-capture assertions).
   applies_to:
     - config/workspaces/git-worktrees.org
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
     - register/boundary/workspace-integration-registry
   cited_by_tasks:
     - add-worktree-command
     - on-purge-teardown-handler
   discovered_from: add-workspace-git-worktrees
   discovered_by: implementor
   discovered_class: load-order-hazard
   #+end_src

** cwd-scope-agreement
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-cwd-scope-agreement
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/cwd-scope-agreement
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     CONFIRMED cycle-1781716077 integrate — but RECONCILED through a
     blocking review finding (the speculation's "verbatim" wording was
     load-bearing and the first implementation broke it).  session-write-
     work-root initially stored GPTEL_WORK_ROOT as (expand-file-name
     project-root) while jf/gptel-scope-profile--expand-string substitutes
     ${project_root} VERBATIM (raw replace-regexp-in-string, NOT
     expand-file-name; scope-profiles.org §expand-string).  For any
     non-canonical input (relative / trailing-slash / ~) the two DIVERGED,
     so agreement was only incidental (true when expand-file-name is a
     no-op), not structural — and the agreement spec mocked the renderer to
     also expand-file-name, fabricating the agreement (tautology).  The
     author-blind reviewer caught both.  Inline fix (commit e0479746):
     store project-root VERBATIM (the byte-identical string the scope
     expansion consumes) so the equality is genuine for ANY input; the
     binder normalises to an absolute directory at READ time.  Case 2 of
     work-root-emission-spec rewritten to model the real verbatim
     substitution + a relative input, so a regression to expand-file-name
     fails it.  why_tests_missed: the original agreement test was tautological
     (mocked the renderer into agreement).  SPEC-SIGNAL RESOLVED by the user
     (cycle-1781716077 integrate): design.md D3's claim that ${project_root}
     "expands absolute" is FALSE (it is verbatim substitution).  User chose to
     CANONICALISE AT SOURCE — expand-file-name project-root ONCE at
     create-session-core entry, feeding BOTH the scope expansion and the
     GPTEL_WORK_ROOT write, so both outputs are absolute AND byte-identical
     (agreement preserved, scope patterns absolute, relative-path resolution
     lands in scope for all inputs).  RESOLVED cycle-1781718724:
     canonicalize-project-root-at-source (merge f50ebb80) landed Option A — one
     source-side (when project-root (setq project-root (expand-file-name
     project-root))) at create-session-core entry feeds BOTH the scope
     expansion AND the GPTEL_WORK_ROOT write, so the two outputs are now
     byte-identical AND ABSOLUTE for any input form.  The agreement spec
     (work-root-emission-spec.el Case 2) was strengthened to be genuinely
     NON-TAUTOLOGICAL (verbatim-substituting mock, non-canonical inputs
     "proj"/"/Users/x/proj/", asserts byte-identical absolute) — author-blind
     review CLEAN (0 findings), confirmed the mock cannot fabricate agreement.
     design.md D1/D3 corrected (${project_root} is verbatim substitution;
     canonical-absolute established at source, not by the renderer).  The READ
     side is now proven end-to-end by scope-relative-resolution-tests (merge
     4a1af080, 6 specs green, author-blind review CLEAN — verdict flips between
     work roots under identical scope-config, so the specs genuinely bite).
     This entry stays CONFIRMED, strengthened from verbatim-agreement to
     ABSOLUTE-agreement.  Reconciliation notes:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781716077/reconciliations/invariant-cwd-scope-agreement.md
     and .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781718724/reconciliations/invariant-cwd-scope-agreement.md
   load_bearing: true
   title: A session's work root and its scope boundary derive from one input and cannot disagree
   statement: |
     For any session created by jf/gptel--create-session-core, the
     GPTEL_WORK_ROOT drawer value (the directory against which the model's
     relative paths resolve at tool time) MUST equal the project-root used
     to expand that session's GPTEL_SCOPE_* patterns. The two are not
     computed independently: a single project-root input is persisted
     verbatim as GPTEL_WORK_ROOT and simultaneously expanded into
     GPTEL_SCOPE_*. A relative path located inside the work root therefore
     resolves inside the read/write scope BY CONSTRUCTION -- the allow/deny
     decision is meaningful, not incidental.
   enforcement_mechanism: |
     test (the confirm gate -- no runtime guard; the equality is structural,
     a single input fanned to two outputs).
     (1) session-write-work-root: create-session-core emits GPTEL_WORK_ROOT
     from the project-root argument it already threads into
     jf/gptel-scope-profile--create-for-session; a Buttercup spec asserts the
     persisted GPTEL_WORK_ROOT equals the scope-expansion root (one input ->
     two outputs). (2) scope-relative-resolution-tests: with default-directory
     bound to the work root, a relative path resolves under it and lands in
     scope; the same relative path under a different work root resolves
     differently.
   why_it_matters: |
     The cwd bug this change fixes is precisely a DISagreement: default-
     directory fell through to the branches/<branch>/ bookkeeping dir while
     scope was expanded from the real project root, so every relative-path
     decision judged "the wrong path universe" -- sometimes spuriously
     permissive, sometimes spuriously restrictive. Deriving both from one
     input removes the disagreement as a manual obligation.
   applies_to:
     - config/gptel/sessions/commands.org
     - config/gptel/scope/scope-validation.org
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/work-root-activation-seam
     - register/vocabulary/agent-path-params
     - register/shape/scope-config-plist
     - register/boundary/scope-config-loader
   cited_by_tasks:
     - session-write-work-root
     - scope-relative-resolution-tests
     - verify-change
   discovered_from: gptel-work-root-default-directory
   discovered_by: architect
   discovered_class: invariant-gap
   #+end_src

** composed-system-message-write-only
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-composed-system-message-write-only
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/composed-system-message-write-only
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781857401 integrate (2026-06-19): speculated → CONFIRMED.
     stable-role-base (merge 6807e0c2) added the buffer-local
     gptel-chat--system-prompt-base (role-only at every install site);
     pre-send-compose-and-wire (merge 4d8cad0e) widened the composer to
     recompose gptel--system-message WHOLESALE from stable sources and
     NEVER read back the composed value. Now ENFORCED by tests: the
     two-send idempotency spec asserts exactly ONE "# Environment" block
     after two composes (+ full-value equality), and the scope-grown spec
     asserts a mid-session GPTEL_SCOPE_* edit appears on the second compose.
     on-touch Architect + author-blind reviews CONFIRMED no read-back, single
     advice, defensive fallback preserved. ENUMERATION REFINEMENT (from
     stable-role-base ## Discoveries): the install sites are FOUR, not the
     three obvious ones — the preset :system is installed via the generic
     gptel--apply-preset SETTER LAMBDA in gptel-chat--apply-declared-preset
     (no literal gptel--system-message assignment to grep), which is the
     PRIMARY no-sibling path; the base is seeded there too. This refines the
     statement's parenthetical site list but not the invariant itself.
     OPEN (advisory, non-blocking, accepted-with-note): the "role base"
     describe block lacks a DIRECT unit assertion for the preset-only :system
     path; it is covered end-to-end by the composition specs. Low-priority
     test-coverage nicety, not externalised.
     See .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1781857401/reconciliations/
     invariant-composed-system-message-write-only.md.
     ---
     cycle-1781857401 plan (2026-06-19): forward-mode speculation for
     change gptel-dynamic-environment-preamble (design D2). Pressure-tested
     by stable-role-base (introduces the stable role source) and
     pre-send-compose-and-wire (the composer + its two-send idempotency
     spec).
   load_bearing: true
   title: |
     The composed gptel--system-message is write-only output of the
     pre-send composer — never an input to the next composition
   statement: |
     On every send from a gptel-chat-mode buffer, gptel--system-message is
     recomposed WHOLESALE as role + "\n\n" + environment-block from STABLE
     sources only — the sibling system-prompt file (re-read each send) when
     :GPTEL_SYSTEM_PROMPT_FILE: is set and readable, else the buffer-local
     gptel-chat--system-prompt-base (set wherever role content is installed:
     preset application at activation, gptel-chat--apply-system-prompt-file,
     and each sibling refresh). The composer MUST NOT read the prior
     gptel--system-message as a composition input. Consequences: repeated
     sends with no intervening change yield exactly ONE environment block
     (no accumulation), and a scope change between sends is reflected in the
     next send's block.
   why_it_matters: |
     The trap (design D2): the no-sibling-file case today sets
     gptel--system-message once at activation. If the composer appended the
     env block to that same variable and then read it back next send, every
     send would graft another "# Environment" block onto the model-visible
     system message — unbounded growth of model-visible text, and a system
     message that drifts further from the role on each turn. The fix is a
     dedicated role base that the composer rebuilds from, making
     gptel--system-message purely the per-send composed result. The sibling
     case is already safe (role re-read from the file); the base closes the
     no-sibling gap. Rejected alternatives: string-surgery to strip the
     known block before re-appending (fragile if the block format changes),
     and re-resolving the preset :system from the drawer each send
     (duplicates activation logic).
   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: |
       SPECULATED. Buttercup specs at config/gptel/chat/test/menu/
       environment-preamble-spec.el MUST cover: (1) "role base" — after
       activation/preset application gptel-chat--system-prompt-base equals
       the installed role body and contains NO environment block
       (stable-role-base); (2) two consecutive composer invocations →
       exactly one environment block (pre-send-compose-and-wire); (3)
       extending GPTEL_SCOPE_* between invocations is reflected in the
       second block. Until those land the enforcement is review-discipline
       only (flagged fragile per the tier preamble).
   related_entries:
     - register/shape/environment-block
     - register/shape/session-sibling-system-prompt-file
   cited_by_tasks:
     - stable-role-base
     - pre-send-compose-and-wire
   discovered_from: gptel-dynamic-environment-preamble
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src
