** static-prerender-dynamic-compose
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-static-prerender-dynamic-compose
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/static-prerender-dynamic-compose
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781885402 integrate: speculated → confirmed (load-bearing). composer
     consumes static refs verbatim (jf/gptel-fragment--realize-ref reads :text, no
     path through jf/gptel-fragment-render at compose time); dynamic refs funcall at
     compose time and default to the tail. On-touch Architect confirmed the stable-
     prefix property holds; one advisory (arch-cycle-1781885402-1): the
     "two-composes-differ-only-in-tail" property is demonstrated by construction but
     not asserted as a literal byte-identical-prefix spec — optional strengthening,
     non-blocking; golden/idempotent enforcement belongs to the source/migrate tasks.
     cycle-1781900938 (migrate-environment): RE-AFFIRMED + the advisory CLOSED. On-touch
     architect arch-cycle-1781900938-1 verified all five checks; environment-preamble-spec.el
     now adds a spec that literally asserts the static prefix is byte-identical across two
     composes with differing live input (substring equality), while the tail differs —
     satisfying arch-cycle-1781885402-1's optional strengthening. Env wired as the dynamic
     (:kind dynamic :fn FN) tail ref via jf/gptel-fragment-environment-fn; no static
     re-render at compose time. (NB production-load wiring of the env source is tracked by
     wire-fragment-sources-load; the invariant property itself holds.)
     cycle-1781941375 RE-AFFIRMED + NB CLEARED. (1) wire-fragment-sources-load loads the
     env source at gptel init, so the dynamic env tail is now live in production (the prior
     NB is resolved). (2) migrate-prelude-preamble migrated the chat prelude and agent
     preamble from defconsts into STATIC fragment sources (presets/sources/{emacs-prelude,
     agent-preamble}) consumed verbatim via the composer seam defvars — no per-send render.
     SPEC-SIGNAL noted (migrate reviewer + arch eoc): "render at tangle time" is realized
     as a LOAD-TIME render-once cached in the source .el + an idempotent committed .txt
     mirror, because org-babel-tangle-file cannot run jf/gptel-fragment-render during
     tangling. This preserves the invariant's INTENT (no per-send re-render; static prefix
     byte-stable & cacheable; committed text diffable) and is functionally equivalent to a
     tangle-time bake (a constant rather than a baked literal). The statement's "at tangle
     time" wording is therefore read as "ahead of send, once, into committed text", NOT as
     "literally during org-babel-tangle". On-touch (arch-cycle-1781941375-1) CLEARED all
     five checks incl. static-prefix byte-stability and env-out-of-prefix. KNOWN test gap
     (advisory, not a property failure): the .txt golden specs are tautological in a
     writable checkout (the load-time mirror rewrites the artifact before the spec reads it,
     cycle-1781944619 in-change followup harden-fragment-txt-golden).
     cycle-1781944619 (plan) WORDING RECONCILED per user disposition of
     ask-cycle-1781941375-invariant-wording: the user accepted that load-time render-once +
     committed .txt mirror satisfies this invariant and chose to reconcile the statement
     phrasing (no code change). The normative mechanism is now "rendered ahead of send, once,
     into committed text" rather than "literally during org-babel-tangle"; INTENT unchanged
     (no per-send re-render; static prefix byte-stable & cacheable; committed text diffable).
     title/statement/why_it_matters edited to match; the literal "tangle-time availability
     risk" retired (load-time render-once carries no tangle-step dependency). ORIGINAL spec-signal note follows:
     → in-change followup harden-fragment-txt-golden.
   load_bearing: true
   title: static fragments pre-render ahead of send (once, into committed text); dynamic evaluate at compose time
   statement: |
     A static fragment SHALL render to committed text ahead of send — once, at
     load time, into a committed mirror artifact — and the
     composer SHALL consume that pre-rendered text with no compose-time
     rendering work. A dynamic fragment SHALL produce its text by evaluating
     a function at compose time, and its default position SHALL be the tail
     of the composition. Consequently, the static prefix of every composed
     system message is stable across sends (a cacheable prompt prefix) and
     the only per-send work is the tail dynamic fragment(s).

   why_it_matters: |
     This preserves today's cost/cache profile, which the change is explicitly
     required to keep (design.md Decision 4, Goals): the chat prelude / agent
     preamble and the role content are constant (prelude/role pre-rendered,
     committed, diffable), while the environment block is recomputed each send.
     If a static fragment were re-rendered per send (or a dynamic fragment were
     placed ahead of the static prefix), the cacheable prefix would change on
     every send — silently inflating cost and defeating prompt caching with no
     visible error. The split is realized by rendering each static fragment
     once at load time into a committed text mirror (org-babel-tangle-file
     cannot run the renderer mid-tangle); the renderer remains batch-loadable
     so this load-time render-once has no interactive-Emacs dependency.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: config/gptel/presets/test/  (golden snapshots + composition-order/tail specs)
     notes: |
       Golden snapshots pin static text (no per-send rendering); composer
       specs assert dynamic fragments land at the tail and that two composes
       with differing live input differ only in the tail. An idempotent-rebuild
       spec asserts re-tangling a static fragment yields identical committed text.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/fragment
     - register/boundary/composer-compose
     - register/boundary/fragment-render
     - register/invariant/context-default-composition

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** renderer-never-markdown
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-renderer-never-markdown
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/renderer-never-markdown
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: "cycle-1781883616 integrate: speculation matched. fragments.el carries no markdown/yaml assumptions (grep guard clean); renderer emits only the backend format; unimplemented backend reported."
   load_bearing: false
   title: renderer never emits Markdown by default; unimplemented backend reported
   statement: |
     The renderer (jf/gptel-fragment-render) SHALL NOT emit Markdown by
     default. The output format SHALL be whatever the target backend's
     rendering specifies (for claude: XML blocks). When invoked for a backend
     with no implemented rendering, the renderer SHALL report the unavailable
     backend (signal, or jf/gptel--log 'warn + signal) rather than silently
     emitting Claude or Markdown output as if it were correct.

   why_it_matters: |
     The old preset model sent the Markdown body verbatim as :system; this
     change deliberately leaves Markdown behind for backend-appropriate
     delimiters (design.md Decision 2). A renderer that fell back to Markdown
     (or to Claude) for an unrecognized backend would produce a plausible-
     looking but wrong system message with no error — exactly the silent
     mis-render the backend seam exists to prevent. Reporting the gap keeps
     "add a backend" a visible, deliberate act rather than an accidental
     Claude-shaped default.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: config/gptel/presets/test/fragments-spec.el
     notes: |
       Spec asserts an unimplemented backend :to-throw (or logs+signals), and
       golden snapshots assert claude output is XML, never Markdown. A grep
       guard ("markdown"/"yaml" absent from fragments.el) is in the
       fragment-core task verification.

   related_entries:
     - register/vocabulary/render-backend
     - register/boundary/fragment-render

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** context-default-composition
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-context-default-composition
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/context-default-composition
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1781885402 integrate: speculated → confirmed. jf/gptel-fragment--default-composition
     yields chat → [emacs-prelude(static), role, environment(dynamic)] and
     agent → [agent-preamble(static), role, environment(dynamic)]; role injected via
     an optional role-ref arg; the override seam exists. Decided + documented:
     empty/nil contributions are SKIPPED (not rejected). The actual prelude/preamble/
     environment CONTENT is wired via seam defvars (jf/gptel-fragment-chat-prelude-text,
     -environment-fn, ...) whose population is deferred to the migrate/source tasks.
   load_bearing: false
   title: context-derived default composition (chat vs agent) with override seam
   statement: |
     The default composition SHALL be derived from send context. An
     interactive chat-mode context SHALL default to
     [emacs-prelude (static), role, environment (dynamic)]. A persistent-agent
     context SHALL default to [agent-preamble (static), role, environment
     (dynamic)]. A preset author SHALL normally supply only config plus a
     single role fragment that occupies the role position; the system SHALL
     provide a seam by which a composition MAY override the default list
     (add/remove/reorder) without requiring that of the common case.

   why_it_matters: |
     This reproduces today's hard-coded framing (prelude leads, env tails;
     agent preamble leads for agents) as data instead of concat, keeping the
     same mental model (design.md Decision 3). If the default list were not
     context-derived, every preset would have to re-declare the prelude/
     preamble and env wiring, re-introducing the duplication the fragment
     model removes — and a preset that forgot the env fragment would silently
     lose the environment block. The override seam exists so non-default
     compositions are possible without forcing complexity on the common case.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test
     location: config/gptel/presets/test/  (composition-default specs)
     notes: |
       Specs assert chat context composes [emacs-prelude, role, environment]
       and agent context composes [agent-preamble, role, environment]; a spec
       asserts a preset declaring only config + a role fragment yields the
       context default with that role in the role position.

   related_entries:
     - register/shape/composition
     - register/boundary/composer-compose
     - register/invariant/static-prerender-dynamic-compose

   discovered_from: gptel-fragment-presets
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** stored-record-never-mutated-by-jump

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/stored-record-never-mutated-by-jump
   tier: invariant
   status: confirmed
   status_note: |
     cycle-1790453714 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 2 against b56f385a; status unchanged; still
     UNEXERCISED (seam 2 pass-through, portable-bookmarks.el:398-405; zero
     copy-tree hits). OWNERS: validators 0, 1, 2 and the resolved-jump
     clause of validator 3 -> =jump-seam= (wave 1, FIRST task; on-touch
     fires on its first merge); validator 3's "after a missing-repo
     read-only view" clause -> =missing-repo-choice= (wave 3); validator
     3's "after an off-workspace restore-then-jump" clause ->
     =off-workspace-restore= (wave 3). The enforcement file
     portable-bookmarks-jump-spec.el is created by jump-seam; the other two
     clauses of validator 3 land in THEIR owners' spec files
     (missing-repo-spec.el, off-workspace-spec.el), so validator 3 is
     three-file at cycle-2 end and enforcement_mechanism.location is
     amended then. SPECULATED for execute to settle: (a) that the
     substituted record is constructed at exactly ONE site (validator 2)
     even after wave 3 adds two more actors — the read-only view and the
     restore-then-jump path must re-enter the seam's construction site, or
     open :path directly WITHOUT building a record; never a second copy;
     (b) copy-tree on the (NAME . ALIST) cons returned by
     bookmark-get-bookmark, so the alist AND its property conses are
     fresh, and bookmark-prop-set on the copy cannot reach bookmark-alist;
     (c) bookmark-save-flag is still 1 at b56f385a
     (config/core/defaults.el:114), so the why_it_matters premise holds;
     (d) whether the "stored filename unchanged" assertion is made against
     bookmark-alist AFTER a bookmark-save round trip to the temp bookmark
     file — the only form that catches a write-through, since a shared
     cons would still read back the same in memory. No clause is weakened.

     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): UNEXERCISED
     — status unchanged. Seam 2 is pass-through (portable-bookmarks.el:398-405);
     no substituted record is constructed anywhere; grep
     copy-tree/copy-alist/copy-sequence over portable-bookmarks.el -> zero
     hits. The enforcing task is =jump-seam= (cycle 2), which must be the

     (portable-bookmarks-stamp-spec.el:463) and the resolver never touches a

     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1789935943/reconciliations/invariant-stored-record-never-mutated-by-jump.md

     cycle-1789935943 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     populated from design D2 + specs § "Jump-time resolution on a copy" +
     task =jump-seam= step 3. The enabling behaviour was VERIFIED in
     =emacs -Q --batch= before the design: a modified COPY handed to

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): CONFIRMED. All three actors are live and the
     speculation held: (a) exactly ONE construction site survives wave 3 —
     =workspace-bookmark--substituted-record= (portable-bookmarks.el:404-417),
     reached only through =--plan-to-open= (:419-445) by the jump seam's
     =resolved= arm, the missing-repo chooser's view arm and its post-add
     re-resolve; the off-workspace flow re-enters =--jump-plan= and so the
     same site; the only =copy-tree= in the module is :415 and
     =copy-alist= / =copy-sequence= are absent (jump-spec § "structure"
     counts =(copy-tree = lines = 1; missing-repo-spec § "structure" pins
     that the chooser section builds no copy); (b) =copy-tree= on the
     normalised =(NAME . ALIST)= cons, ONE =bookmark-prop-set= on the
     copy's ='filename=, string VALUES shared and never mutated in place;
     (c) =bookmark-save-flag= is still 1 (config/core/defaults.el:114); (d)
     the resolved-jump assertion is made after a =bookmark-save= / reload
     round trip (jump-spec "opens the current workspace's copy at the
     recorded position, and the stored record survives a save round trip
     unchanged") plus a cons-disjointness check. Validator 3's other two
     clauses landed in their owners' files, in memory against a =copy-tree=
     snapshot: missing-repo-spec "opens the canonical copy in view-mode
     ... the stored record is untouched" (and the cancel / add-worktree /
     barrier specs), off-workspace-spec "prompts over the registry before
     resolving, restores the chosen workspace, and opens its copy in the
     selected window — not the stored path" (and six more). Mutation scan:
     the seam's only other write is =bookmark-current-bookmark= (a
     =defvar-local= holding the NAME string) in the LANDED buffer. The
     host's relocate arm — the only stock path that writes a stored record
     — is reachable only with a STRING argument on a missing file, i.e. the
     =non-portable= call-through, which is stock by definition. No field
     but enforcement_mechanism.location and validator anchors changes.
     Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/invariant-stored-record-never-mutated-by-jump.md
   load_bearing: true
   title: A jump never mutates the stored bookmark record
   statement: |
     Resolution MUST substitute the resolved location into a COPY of the
     bookmark record and hand the COPY to the native handler. The record held
     in =bookmark-alist= MUST be structurally unreachable from the copy —
     =copy-tree=, not =copy-sequence= and not =copy-alist=, so no cons cell
     is shared with the stored entry.

     After ANY jump, for ANY outcome, the stored record's =filename= property
     MUST equal what it was before the jump.

     This holds for every outcome, not just =resolved=: =missing-repo='s
     read-only view and the off-workspace restore path also open a location
     that is not the stored one, and neither may write it back.

   why_it_matters: |
     =bookmark-save-flag= is =1= in this configuration
     (=config/core/defaults.el:114=), which means the ENTIRE bookmark file is
     rewritten whenever the bookmark list changes. An in-place edit of the
     stored record would therefore be PERSISTED on the first jump.

     What that destroys is not cosmetic. The absolute =filename= is the
     fallback that makes every degraded outcome safe: it is what
     =non-portable= opens, what a stock Emacs without this module reads, and
     what the user gets back if the module is removed. Persisting a resolved
     path would silently re-pin every bookmark to whichever workspace the
     user happened to jump from — recreating the ORIGINAL BUG this whole
     capability exists to remove, but now baked into the file on disk and
     with no un-resolved value left to recover from.

     The failure is silent, cumulative, and one-way. That combination is what
     earns an invariant rather than a code comment.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test + structural
     location: |
       Three files, one per actor (cycle-1790453714):
       - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-jump-spec.el — the
         behavioral "stamped in worktree A, jumped from worktree B" spec
         asserts the opened buffer is B's copy AND that the stored record's
         filename is STILL A's path, after a bookmark-save / reload round
         trip; plus cons-disjointness of copy and stored alist, and the
         structural copy-tree count. That second assertion is the regression
         guard; a spec that only checks the opened file would pass against a
         mutating implementation.
       - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-missing-repo-spec.el —
         the read-only view, cancel, add-worktree and barrier arms compare
         the stored record to a copy-tree snapshot.
       - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-off-workspace-spec.el —
         the restore-then-jump, decline, restore-failure and empty-registry
         arms do the same.

     enforcement_locus_check: |
       Where in the call graph is this enforced? Inside the
       =bookmark-handle-bookmark= advice, at the single point where a
       substituted record is constructed — =copy-tree= then
       =bookmark-prop-set= on the copy.

       Does that location cover ALL entry paths? Yes if and only if the
       substituted record is constructed in exactly ONE place. If
       =missing-repo= (read-only view) or the off-workspace restore path
       builds its own substituted record separately, each is an independent
       opportunity to mutate the original, and the invariant's locus
       fragments. The structural check below therefore counts construction
       sites, not just asserts the outcome.

       Does the enforcement prevent silent-fail? Risk vector: a future
       refactor replacing =copy-tree= with =copy-alist= or =copy-sequence=
       would produce a copy whose VALUE cells are still shared. For a flat
       string-valued =filename= property =bookmark-prop-set= on a shallow
       copy happens not to write through — so the spec would still pass while
       the guarantee was gone, and the next nested property added would break
       it silently. The structural grep for =copy-tree= is what closes that
       gap; the behavioral spec alone does not.

   validator: |
     0. The three enforcement spec files named above exist (verified at
        f860103c).
     1. grep -n 'copy-tree' config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el -> one
        code hit (:415; :407 is the docstring);
        grep -n 'copy-alist\|copy-sequence' over the module -> absent.
     2. Exactly one construction site for a substituted record
        (=--substituted-record=; jump-spec § "structure" counts it).
     3. The stored record is unchanged after a resolved jump (jump-spec,
        after a save round trip), after a missing-repo read-only view
        (missing-repo-spec) and after an off-workspace restore-then-jump
        (off-workspace-spec).

   design_anchor:
     section: design.md §D2
     quote: |
       "bookmark-save-flag is 1, so an in-place rewrite would persist the
       resolved path and destroy the absolute fallback that makes every
       degraded outcome safe."

   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/bookmark-advice-seams
     - register/shape/portable-bookmark-coordinates
     - register/invariant/no-silent-wrong-file-open
     - register/invariant/persisted-form-readable-by-construction

   cited_by_tasks:
     - jump-seam
     - missing-repo-choice        # cycle-1790453714: validator 3, read-only view
     - off-workspace-restore      # cycle-1790453714: validator 3, restore-then-jump
   discovered_from: workspace-portable-bookmarks
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** no-silent-wrong-file-open

   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-no-silent-wrong-file-open

   #+begin_src yaml

   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |

     RE-STATED for cycle 2 against b56f385a; status unchanged. DECISION
     half LIVE (unchanged since ac50b041); ACTOR half still UNEXERCISED
     (seam 2 pass-through, portable-bookmarks.el:398-405). OWNERS by
     clause: prohibition 1 and validator 4 (not-on-branch creates NO
     buffer) plus the ambiguous / no-clone report-and-decline arms ->
     =jump-seam= (wave 1, FIRST task; on-touch fires on its first merge);
     prohibition 2, validator 3 (view-mode AND a visible marker), the
     cancel = open-nothing-signal-nothing rule and the "read-only withheld
     when :path is nil" rule -> =missing-repo-choice= (wave 3); prohibition
     4's empty-registry / user-quit fallback ("read-only canonical, or
     cancel" — never the stored path) -> =off-workspace-restore= (wave 3);
     prohibition 3 is LIVE (=register/vocabulary/checkout-classification=
     validators). Validator 0's three files are created by those three
     owners respectively. SPECULATED for execute to settle: (a) HOW the
     seam declines — return without calling ORIG-FN after a =message=,
     versus =user-error=; the entry requires only "report, open nothing",

     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): the DECISION
     half is live and confirmed (resolver-outcomes, ac50b041): pure decision

     (portable-bookmarks.el:959 signals). The ACTOR half (jump seam,
     missing-repo prompt, off-workspace flow) is deferred to cycle 2

     STAYS SPECULATED and the actor clauses are UNEXERCISED; seam 2 is
     pass-through at 20944160 (:398-405). The resolver-outcomes on-touch's
     blocking finding (an on-disk symlink escape sent a VALID stamp to

     was closed by DD-16 option (b): confinement is LEXICAL, links resolve
     by their in-checkout spelling. Statement, prohibition 4, residuals and
     validators reconciled below. Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1789935943/reconciliations/invariant-no-silent-wrong-file-open.md

     cycle-1789935943 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     populated from the proposal's "Why" + specs § "Closed set of resolution

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code

     (cycle-1790604160) — prohibition 2's neighbour: =add-worktree= must not

   load_bearing: true
   title: No resolution path opens a location the user did not ask for
   statement: |
     Exactly one outcome — =resolved= — may open a location without asking.
     =not-on-branch=, =ambiguous= and =no-clone= MUST report and open
     nothing; =missing-repo= offers; =non-portable= is stock behaviour.
     Every other outcome MUST report, prompt, or do nothing. Concretely,
     four prohibitions, each closing a distinct silent-wrong-file route:

     1. =not-on-branch= MUST NOT be visited. No =find-file= (and no
        =find-file-noselect=) on a path that does not exist. The seam
        DECLINES before the host runs. (Corrected cycle-1790453714: Emacs
        30.2's =bookmark-default-handler= SIGNALS =bookmark-error-no-filename=
        for an unreadable path rather than creating a buffer; the stock trap
        this closes is the host's RELOCATE prompt for a STRING argument,
        bookmark.el:1350-1380, which on "yes" rewrites the stored record and,
        with =bookmark-save-flag= 1, persists it — a violation of
        =register/invariant/stored-record-never-mutated-by-jump= as well.)
     2. The CANONICAL checkout MUST NOT be opened unless the user explicitly
        chose it at the =missing-repo= prompt. It is never a fallback.
     3. A =workspace-owned= worktree and an =other= linked worktree MUST NEVER
        be offered AS the canonical checkout
        (=register/vocabulary/checkout-classification=).
     4. The stored ABSOLUTE path MUST NOT be opened as though it were the
        current workspace's copy. Only the =non-portable= outcome may use it,
        and only because for that outcome it IS today's correct answer. In
        particular the empty-registry and user-quit fallbacks go to
        "read-only canonical, or cancel" — never through to the absolute path.
        =non-portable= is reached ONLY by a record whose stamp is absent,
        partial, of an unknown version, or LEXICALLY escaping (absolute,
        a =..= component, NUL). A VALID stamp never degrades to
        non-portable: a relpath that leaves the chosen checkout only
        through a symbolic link resolves to its in-checkout SPELLING
        (DD-16), which is the checkout's own file, never another
        workspace's copy — the =:path= of =resolved= / =missing-repo= is
        spelled inside =:checkout=; where a link on that branch points is
        the branch's business.
        OFF-WORKSPACE (cycle-1790453714): with no current workspace, a
        stamped record first establishes one (the workspace prompt, then
        =workspace-restore=) and is resolved against it; an empty or
        all-broken registry, or no workspace chosen, goes to the bounded
        =missing-repo= chooser or declines; a restore error declines; a plan
        that would call through with the ORIGINAL argument after the restore
        DECLINES (stricter than in-workspace =non-portable=: after the
        hand-off that plan can only come from the resolver's error barrier,
        whose :path is the stored filename). When no one can answer the
        workspace prompt, the jump declines without prompting; C-g at it
        ends the jump (=quit=), with no second prompt.
        LAYOUT RESTORE (core, the fourth actor): a leaf whose bookmark
        handler DECLINES (=user-error=) skips the filename and name steps
        and lands on a reason buffer, with ONE aggregated report per restore
        (=register/vocabulary/buffer-reincarnation-fallback-chain=); a leaf
        in a repository the restored workspace lacks therefore shows why,
        never the stored path. The seam declines rather than prompts while
        =workspace--restoring-layout-leaf= is bound.

     Cancelling MUST open nothing and REPORT nothing, and ends the jump with
     =quit= — deliberately not a silent nil return (inside
     =bookmark--jump-via= a nil return still runs the display function,
     fringe mark and after-jump hook on the ORIGINATING buffer) and not a
     =user-error= (caught by every =(error ...)= barrier, so a cancel would
     read as a decline or a failure). =quit= is what C-g at the prompt
     already does, reaches no debugger, and passes every =(error ...)=
     barrier (settled cycle-1790453714, missing-repo-choice).

     When the canonical checkout IS opened by explicit choice, it MUST be
     read-only (=view-mode=) and VISIBLY marked as the canonical checkout
     rather than a workspace checkout. =C-x C-q= is the deliberate way out
     (the marker stays); a revert that re-runs the major mode brings back
     the marker and — if it was on — =view-mode=.

   why_it_matters: |
     The defect being fixed is not "bookmarks break" — it is "bookmarks
     silently succeed at the wrong thing". Jumping from workspace B to a
     bookmark made in workspace A opens A's copy of the file, on A's branch,
     with NO indication you are editing the wrong checkout. That is the whole
     motivation in the proposal's first paragraphs.

     Every prohibition above is a route by which a well-meaning
     implementation re-creates exactly that failure while looking helpful:
       - visiting a missing path "so you can create it" (route 1);
       - falling back to the main checkout "so something opens" (route 2);
       - taking the nearest worktree in view as canonical (route 3) — which
         lands in ANOTHER workspace, the original bug verbatim;
       - falling back to the stored absolute path "because we have one"
         (route 4) — which is the original bug with extra steps, and which
         a file-identity confinement check would have re-opened for VALID
         stamps reached through a link (closed by DD-16).

     Each is the =silent-fail-on-default-path= shape this project has already
     paid for once (see
     =register/invariant/idle-tick-no-op-off-workspace=). The read-only
     requirement on the canonical view closes the last variant: a quick
     lookup that becomes an unnoticed edit of main.

   known_residuals: |
     (a) Stale stamps after bookmark-relocate / a hand edit follow the OLD
         relpath (user decision, ask 5;
         .tasks/portable-bookmarks-stale-stamp-detection.md — that task is
         also the place to route a stamp inconsistent with its record to a
         reported outcome).
     (b) A TAMPERED stamp (lexical escape) opens the stored filename, as
         stock Emacs would for that record. Accepted: whoever writes the
         stamp writes filename too.
     (c) (cycle-1790453714) A programming error in the seam's own decision,
         outside the missing-repo chooser's and the off-workspace plan's own
         barriers, degrades to stock: the stored path for a stamped record
         (portable-bookmarks.el:568-570; pinned by jump-spec "still opens the
         stored location when the resolver signals" / "... when the seam's
         own copy step signals"). The resolver itself never signals (errors
         map to no-clone / non-portable).
     (d) (cycle-1790453714) A resolved path that vanishes in the
         microseconds between the seam's =file-exists-p= pre-flight and the
         handler's own visit meets the host's swallow for a record argument:
         nothing opens and nothing is reported (see
         =register/boundary/bookmark-advice-seams= upstream_contract_risk).
         Opens nothing wrong.
     (e) (cycle-1790453714, arch-on-touch-1790453714-34) The canonical-view
         marker survives =C-x C-q= and a mode-resetting =revert-buffer=, but
         an explicit MAJOR-MODE change (e.g. M-x text-mode) drops the marker
         and =view-mode= while =buffer-read-only= stays t: read-only, not
         visibly explained. Opens nothing wrong.
     (f) (cycle-1790453714, arch-on-touch-1790453714-5) bufferlo's native
         tab/frame bookmark restore funcalls nested handlers directly and
         never reaches seam 2, so a stamped record nested in a bufferlo tab
         or frame bookmark opens its STORED filename (route 4) — outside the
         choke point, not a supported workflow here; no task unless it
         becomes one.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test + structural
     location: |
       Split across specs by outcome:
       - portable-bookmarks-resolver-spec.el — the decision function
         returns the right outcome and never performs I/O; the anchoring
         dispatch's default arm signals ("answers for an anchoring answer
         nobody wrote code for, never a location", :599); a VALID stamp
         through an escaping link resolves by spelling (:472-548).
       - portable-bookmarks-jump-spec.el — =not-on-branch= creates NO
         buffer (no buffer visits the missing path, nor the stored one);
         ambiguous / no-clone decline; a vanished resolved path declines
         without touching the originating buffer.
       - portable-bookmarks-missing-repo-spec.el — cancel opens nothing,
         signals =quit= and says nothing; the read-only view is =view-mode=
         and marked, and stays marked through =C-x C-q= and a mode-resetting
         revert; errors inside the choice decline.
       - portable-bookmarks-off-workspace-spec.el — empty registry offers
         the bounded fallback and never opens the stored absolute path;
         restore failure, C-g at the workspace prompt and "no one can
         answer" all open nothing; § "layout reincarnation chain: a leaf
         whose bookmark declines" restores a layout without visiting the
         stored path.
       - buffer-reincarnation-spec.el § "the gate: a bookmark handler that
         declines" — core skips steps 2-3 on a decline, and the step-1
         dispatch's default arm signals rather than opening the filename.

     enforcement_locus_check: |
       Where is this enforced? NOT in one place — and that is the entry's
       main risk. The resolver decides the outcome; the jump seam, the
       missing-repo prompt, and the off-workspace flow each act on it. The
       invariant is only as strong as the weakest of those three actors —
       plus, since cycle-1790453714, core's layout reincarnation chain,
       which acts on the seam's decline. All four exist at f860103c and
       each dispatches on a closed set with a signalling default.

       Mitigation, and the structural half of the enforcement (LIVE): the
       resolver is a PURE DECISION FUNCTION (no =find-file=,
       =switch-to-buffer=, =completing-read=, or =message= in its body — see
       =register/vocabulary/bookmark-resolution-outcome= producer discipline)
       and every actor dispatches on the closed outcome set rather than on
       ad-hoc conditions. A =pcase= over
       =workspace-bookmark--valid-outcomes= with no permissive default arm is
       the shape that makes a missed case a loud error instead of a
       fallthrough-open; =workspace-bookmark--resolve-anchored=
       (portable-bookmarks.el:1702-1723) has that shape: its =_= arm signals
       and the barrier maps the signal to =no-clone=.

       Does the enforcement prevent silent-fail? The specific vector to watch
       is a =t= / =_= catch-all arm in any outcome dispatch whose body opens
       something. That arm is the invariant's failure mode; the validator
       greps for it.

   validator: |
     0. The three module enforcement spec files above exist (verified at
        f860103c).
     1. No outcome dispatch has a permissive default arm that opens a
        location. A catch-all arm must error or no-op, never open. Concrete
        sites, each SIGNALLING (verified at f860103c):
        =workspace-bookmark--resolve-anchored= (portable-bookmarks.el:1723;
        resolver-spec "answers for an anchoring answer nobody wrote code
        for, never a location"), =--jump-plan= (:539-540), the seam's plan
        dispatch (:587), =--decline-message= (:485), =--plan-to-open= (:445),
        =--offer-missing-repo='s action dispatch (:721, inside the chooser
        barrier, so it declines), =--add-worktree-and-replan= (:860,
        likewise); and core's =workspace--deserialize-buffer= step-1
        dispatch (layouts.el:329; buffer-reincarnation-spec "signals, never
        opening the saved filename, when step 1 answers anything but a
        buffer, nil or a decline").
     2. grep -n 'find-file\|find-file-noselect' in the resolver section
        (portable-bookmarks.el:1597-1813) -> zero hits (verified); the
        missing-repo section names no file-visiting function
        (missing-repo-spec § "structure").
     3. The canonical read-only view sets view-mode AND writes a visible
        marker (missing-repo-spec § "view read-only").
     4. A spec asserts NO BUFFER visits the missing path after a
        not-on-branch jump — not merely that no error was signalled
        (jump-spec "declines not-on-branch: no buffer visits the missing
        path, nor the stored one").
     5. A VALID stamp resolved in a second workspace through an escaping
        link yields that workspace's spelling, never the stored filename
        (portable-bookmarks-resolver-spec.el § "Resolution from a second
        workspace through links (DD-16)").
     1'. (cycle-1790604160, SPECULATED; owner outcome-action-mapping; supersedes the
        first sentence of validator 1 once reconciled) No catch-all arm
        opens, AND no barrier turns a signal raised while deciding a STAMPED
        record's plan into a call-through: the seam's barrier
        (portable-bookmarks.el:568-570) declines for a record whose
        =workspace-bookmark--stamped-coordinates= is non-nil and calls through
        with the ORIGINAL argument only for an unstamped one. Spec: jump-spec
        "an error inside the seam's decision for a stamped record opens
        nothing" (+ the unstamped twin), and a layout-leaf spec.

   design_anchor:
     section: design.md §D6, §D7
     quote: |
       "A workspace-owned worktree is never offered as the canonical
       checkout: doing so would open one workspace's files while the user is
       in another, which is the defect being fixed."

** bookmark-jump-not-captured-as-working-state
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-bookmark-jump-not-captured-as-working-state
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/bookmark-jump-not-captured-as-working-state
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1790453714 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 2 against b56f385a; status unchanged; still
     UNEXERCISED (zero workspace-anti-save-predicates hits in
     portable-bookmarks.el; consult sites unchanged at persistence.el:301,
     :317 and workspaces-mode.el:35; layouts.el still defers
     window-state-put via run-at-time). OWNER of every clause and of
     validators 0-4 -> =off-workspace-restore= (wave 3, after jump-seam;
     on-touch fires on its first merge); it creates
     portable-bookmarks-off-workspace-spec.el. SPECULATED for execute to
     settle: (a) the suppression MECHANISM — an explicit-reset flag (a
     module defvar set before workspace-restore and cleared by an event
     that runs AFTER the deferred window-state-put: the predicate's own
     first hit, or a one-shot timer queued after the restore's timer)
     versus a plain let-binding, which the statement already rejects
     because the run-at-time restore fires after the let unwinds; execute
     must name WHICH event clears the flag and show it is ordered after the
     deferred restore; (b) that the reset also runs on the error and quit
     paths — unwind-protect covers the synchronous part, but the deferred
     part needs its own clearing, so validator 4 (a later autosave still
     writes :working-state) is the deciding spec; (c) exactly ONE predicate
     added to workspace-anti-save-predicates and removed on unload next to
     the advice removal (portable-bookmarks.el:1055-1058 today); (d) the
     predicate keys on the module's flag ONLY, never on a jump inferred
     from the call stack or from bookmark internals. No clause is weakened.

     cycle-1789935943 integrate (2026-09-26, against 20944160): UNEXERCISED
     — status unchanged. The module names no
     =workspace-anti-save-predicates= and adds no predicate (grep: zero hits
     in portable-bookmarks.el); the three consult sites are unchanged
     (persistence.el:301, :317; workspaces-mode.el:35). The enforcing task
     is =off-workspace-restore= (cycle 2). The timing hazard (deferred
     =window-state-put=) is unchanged. Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1789935943/reconciliations/invariant-bookmark-jump-not-captured-as-working-state.md

     cycle-1789935943 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     populated from design D9 + task =off-workspace-restore= step 5. The
     suppression mechanism is SPECULATED: design says "hook into
     =workspace-anti-save-predicates=", the task adds that a plain =let= may
     unwind before the DEFERRED =window-state-put= runs. Verified at plan
     time: =config/workspaces/layouts.el:339= does defer via
     =(run-at-time nil nil ...)=, and =workspace-anti-save-predicates= is a
     defcustom at =config/workspaces/workspaces.el:35= consulted by both
     autosave paths (=persistence.el:301,317=) and the idle tick
     (=workspaces-mode.el:35=). The design's premise holds; the timing
     hazard is real.

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): RECONCILED — RESTATED by user decision
     ask-cycle-1790453714-3 (b), "REMEMBER IT: a jump-triggered restore
     behaves like any find-file in a workspace". The plan-time statement
     ("the perturbation MUST NOT be written into :working-state") is
     RETIRED: no mechanism could hold it past the next ordinary autosave
     (arch-on-touch-1790453714-39: the spec's own leak-guard autosave wrote
     the perturbed layout), and the anti-save predicate, its add/remove and
     the =workspace-bookmark--restore-in-progress= flag were REMOVED
     (off-workspace-restore fix pass, merge 30a8bba4; grep
     =workspace-anti-save-predicates= / =suppress-autosave= /
     =restore-in-progress= over portable-bookmarks.el -> zero at f860103c).
     What remains TRUE, and is what this entry now states: (1) PAINT THEN
     SHOW, strictly ordered — the finish step is queued at
     =workspace-bookmark--finish-delay= (0.001 s) from an =unwind-protect=
     cleanup, strictly later than the restore's =run-at-time nil= paint,
     because timer.el fires equal-time timers newest first
     (arch-on-touch-1790453714-40); (2) OUTER-JUMP TARGET SCOPING — only the
     outer jump's own host call can be recorded, because
     =workspace-bookmark--awaiting-jump-target= is raised only after
     =workspace-restore= has RETURNED (the restore's own leaf jumps pass
     the same seam first; arch-on-touch-1790453714-38); (3) no autosave
     suppression at all, so the arrangement is remembered like any visit.
     The entry_id and CUSTOM_ID are kept for lineage; the title and
     statement are new. design.md D9 still says "is not remembered" and its
     implementation note still names the anti-save predicates at f860103c —
     the ask-3 resolution records D9 as amended, but the text is not; a

     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/invariant-bookmark-jump-not-captured-as-working-state.md

     cycle-1790604160 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 3 against c65efb86; status unchanged. Validators
     0-5 hold at c65efb86 (zero anti-save hits; =--finish-delay= :882 =
     0.001; =--restore-for-jump= :973-992 raises the flag once after
     =workspace-restore= returns at :988-989; =--note-jump-target=
     :994-1007; =--finish-restore= :956-971). CONFIRMED STALE at HEAD:
     design.md D9 (:200-218) still reads "is not remembered" / "deliberately
     **not** captured as the workspace's :working-state" and still names
     =workspace-anti-save-predicates= (:213). # owner: doc-sync-cycle2
     (cycle-1790604160) — correction D-h of eoc-1790453714 § Doc
     corrections; the entry's design_anchor is re-stated at integrate from
     "(text NOT yet amended)" to the amended D9 once the grep in that task's

   load_bearing: true
   title: A restore-then-jump shows its target in the painted layout (paint, then show; outer jump only) and is then remembered like any visit
   statement: |
     When a bookmark jump triggers =workspace-restore= (the off-workspace
     flow, =workspace-bookmark--restore-for-jump=), three things hold:

     1. PAINT, THEN SHOW. The restored layout is painted first; the
        buffer the jump landed in is then shown in the SELECTED window of
        the painted layout — same window, no split — by
        =workspace-bookmark--finish-restore=. The finish step is queued at
        a strictly LATER timer time than the restore's own deferred
        =window-state-put= (=layouts.el= queues it with =run-at-time nil=;
        the finish step with =workspace-bookmark--finish-delay=, 0.001 s),
        because timer.el orders by time and fires EQUAL-time timers newest
        first: "same delay" is not an ordering.

     2. THE OUTER JUMP ONLY. The only host call that may be taken for the
        jump's target is the outer jump's own. The restore's leaf walk runs
        every bookmarked leaf through the same advised
        =bookmark-handle-bookmark=, synchronously inside =workspace-restore=;
        the target is therefore armed
        (=workspace-bookmark--awaiting-jump-target=) only once
        =workspace-restore= has RETURNED, and the first host call after
        that disarms it. A jump that declines or is cancelled after the
        restore leaves the painted layout exactly as saved; a jump started
        from the buffer the new tab inherited still shows that buffer
        (=workspace-bookmark--landed-p= also counts "now visits the
        substituted location").

     3. REMEMBERED LIKE ANY VISIT (user decision 2026-09-27). Nothing
        suppresses autosave: the module adds nothing to
        =workspace-anti-save-predicates=. After the jump, the next ordinary
        autosave (tab switch, idle tick, kill-emacs flush) records the
        arrangement on screen — the jump's buffer included — as the
        workspace's =:working-state=, exactly as for any =find-file= in the
        workspace. =:saved-state= is never written by an autosave
        (=register/invariant/autosave-never-writes-saved-state=).

     The pending state is released on EVERY path: the finish step is queued
     from an =unwind-protect= cleanup whether =workspace-restore= returned,
     signalled or was quit, and it clears the target and the armed flag in
     its own =unwind-protect=; a quit at the workspace prompt queues
     nothing.

   why_it_matters: |
     The user asked for a file in a workspace that was not open. If the
     target is shown BEFORE the deferred paint, the paint replaces it and
     the user sees the layout without the file they asked for; if a leaf
     of the restore is taken for the target, a jump that opened NOTHING
     visibly rewrites the restored layout (a leaf painted over the selected
     window), and the next autosave then records that rewrite — a layout
     change caused by a declined jump (both reproduced on the scratch
     probes behind arch-on-touch-1790453714-38 / -40, and pinned).

     The earlier clause "never captured as :working-state" was a design
     preference (D9: "navigation, not a statement about how the workspace
     should look"). The user reversed it: a jump-triggered restore is
     remembered like any other visit, so :working-state means "where I was",
     jumps included.

     The ordering hazard is timing-dependent and silent — a tie between
     two =run-at-time nil= timers happened in 27% of back-to-back calls on
     the probe — which is what earns an invariant rather than a comment.

   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test + structural
     location: |
       config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-off-workspace-spec.el:
       "paints the saved layout first, then shows the file in its selected
       window; nothing suppresses autosave, and a later tab switch records
       the jump's arrangement as :working-state" (events = (paint finish)
       with REAL timers; no anti-save predicate answers; a real
       =tab-bar-select-tab= then writes :working-state with the jump's file
       and leaves :saved-state equal); "keeps the paint-then-show order when
       the two timers read the same clock value" (every =run-at-time nil=
       pinned to one time); "leaves the painted layout alone when the jump
       declines after restoring a layout of bookmarked leaves: the restore's
       own leaf jumps are never the jump's target"; "shows the file when the
       jump starts from a buffer visiting it, which the new tab inherits";
       "declines, never falling through to the stored path, when the restore
       itself fails — and still clears the pending jump state";
       portable-bookmarks-jump-spec.el "leaves nothing of the off-workspace
       jump pending for the next spec" with the fixtures-owned
       =workspace-bookmark-fixtures-reset-jump-state=.

     enforcement_locus_check: |
       Where is this enforced? In =workspace-bookmark--restore-for-jump=
       (portable-bookmarks.el:973-992: arms the target only after
       =workspace-restore= returns; queues the finish step from its
       =unwind-protect= cleanup at a strictly later time),
       =--note-jump-target= (:994-1007: records only while armed, disarms
       on the first host call) and =--finish-restore= (:956-971: shows,
       then clears both in an =unwind-protect=).

       Does it cover all entry paths? Only the off-workspace flow triggers
       a restore from a jump; an in-workspace jump restores nothing and is
       ordinary navigation already.

       Does it prevent silent-fail? Residuals: (a) the order depends on
       layouts.el's paint being deferred by =run-at-time nil= — a paint
       deferred by more than 1 ms would fire after the finish step and hide
       the target (stated in the =--finish-delay= docstring; the "paints
       the saved layout first" spec would go red); a backwards wall-clock
       step of more than 1 ms between the two calls could reorder them;
       (b) a host call nested inside the outer one (a find-file hook that
       itself jumps a bookmark) would take the target first, and two jumps
       with no event loop between them (a keyboard macro) show the first
       target — speculative edges, no such hook in this config.

   validator: |
     0. portable-bookmarks-off-workspace-spec.el exists (verified at
        f860103c).
     1. grep -n 'workspace-anti-save-predicates\|suppress-autosave\|restore-in-progress'
        config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el -> zero hits: the module
        suppresses no autosave.
     2. The finish step is queued with
        =(run-at-time workspace-bookmark--finish-delay nil ...)= from an
        =unwind-protect= cleanup, and =workspace-bookmark--finish-delay= is
        strictly positive (portable-bookmarks.el:882, :990).
     3. =workspace-bookmark--awaiting-jump-target= is set to t in ONE
        place, after the =(workspace-restore name)= form (:988-989), and
        cleared by =--note-jump-target= and =--finish-restore=.
     4. Specs: the five off-workspace specs named above (order under real
        timers and under a tied clock; decline after a bookmarked-leaf
        layout; inherited-buffer target; restore failure clears state).
     5. Spec: the next ordinary autosave after the jump records the jump's
        arrangement as :working-state and leaves :saved-state unchanged
        (the old "leak guard", now the positive statement 3).

   design_anchor:
     section: design.md §D9 (text NOT yet amended at f860103c — see status_note)
     quote: |
       User decision ask-cycle-1790453714-3 (b), 2026-09-27: "REMEMBER IT.
       A jump-triggered restore behaves like any find-file in a workspace.
       Drop D9's 'must not be written into :working-state' claim; remove
       the inert anti-save predicate + flag." (D9's other half stands: "The
       restored layout is painted, then the bookmark's file replaces the
       selected window.")

   related_entries:
     - register/invariant/autosave-never-writes-saved-state
     - register/invariant/explicit-save-bypasses-anti-save
     - register/invariant/idle-tick-no-op-off-workspace
     - register/boundary/autosave-guard-pipeline
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-state-slot
     - register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer
     - register/boundary/bookmark-advice-seams

   cited_by_tasks:
     - off-workspace-restore
     - doc-sync-cycle2   # cycle-1790604160: design.md D9 amendment (ask-cycle-1790453714-3 b)
   discovered_from: workspace-portable-bookmarks
