   #+end_src

** directory-identity-by-device-inode
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-directory-identity-by-device-inode
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/directory-identity-by-device-inode
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1790453714 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     SEEDED from cycle-1 meta-discovery "path-identity-by-device-inode"
     (five tasks hit it: checkout-discovery, worktree-candidates-git-first,
     stamp-seam, wave3-review-fixes, ownership-and-picker-polish) and
     end-of-cycle finding 7. The predicate EXISTS and is already the rule
     inside portable-bookmarks (=workspace-bookmark--same-file-p=,
     portable-bookmarks.el:96-118; every same-file comparison in the module
     routes through it — =register/vocabulary/checkout-classification=
     validator 2). The entry is SPECULATED because its scope is all of
     config/workspaces/ and two CORE sites still violate it
     (known_violations). Promotion of
     .tasks/workspaces-file-equal-p-timestamp-race into this cycle is plan
     ask P2 (pending the user); until it is decided the violations are
     recorded here, owned by that .tasks item, not by a batch task. On-touch
     fires on any diff to the predicate, to tabs.org
     § workspace--registered-for-home-p, or to git-worktrees.org
     § jf/workspace--worktree-children.

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): RECONCILED. Ask P2 was answered "fix now" and
     the task ran in wave 4, then two follow-ups (cycle2-review-cleanups,
     registration-and-purge-guards). Settled at f860103c: (1) ONE identity
     predicate, =workspace--same-file-p=, in CORE (data-model.el:264;
     body byte-identical to the deleted module copy); portable-bookmarks
     and git-worktrees call it (=grep 'defun .*same-file-p'= -> one body);
     (2) ONE containment predicate, =workspace--path-within-p=
     (data-model.el:288), built on it, also core — the purge safeguard's
     =file-in-directory-p= (whose last step is =file-equal-p=) was a
     second, grep-invisible violation (arch-on-touch-1790453714-52) and is
     gone; (3) both CORE known_violations are CLEARED (tabs.el
     =workspace--registered-for-home-p=, git-worktrees.el
     =jf/workspace--worktree-children=), with race specs whose
     =file-equal-p= / =file-in-directory-p= controls fail under the same
     model; (4) test-side =file-equal-p= is gone from every spec but the
     controls (arch-on-touch-1790453714-55). REMOTE: the predicates answer
     nil for any remote name without a connection, which made the
     duplicate-home guard fail OPEN for remote homes
     (arch-on-touch-1790453714-51). User decision
     ask-cycle-1790453714-4: remote homes are UNSUPPORTED and REFUSED —
     every command that registers a home refuses one FIRST
     (=workspace--refuse-remote-home=, tabs.el:133); a remote home in the
     state file loads BROKEN with no filesystem call (persistence.el:136);
     =workspace-purge= on it unregisters only and contacts nothing
     (workspaces.el:182-270, merge 45df6dfd). Statement AMENDED (the
     predicates' names and home, the containment predicate, corollaries 4-6);
     known_violations re-stated (cleared; one externalised residual).
     Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/invariant-directory-identity-by-device-inode.md
   load_bearing: true
   title: Directory and file identity — and containment — are decided by ONE device+inode predicate each, in core; path strings are keys, never verdicts
   statement: |
     Across config/workspaces/ — core AND consumers — whether two paths name
     the SAME directory or file is decided by exactly one predicate that
     compares the device and inode numbers of the two paths' truenames:
     =workspace--same-file-p= (data-model.el:264). Whether a path lies
     INSIDE a directory is decided by exactly one predicate built on it:
     =workspace--path-within-p= (data-model.el:288) — the path is
     truenamed and it and each of its ancestors is compared to the
     directory with =workspace--same-file-p=; a path is within itself; a
     missing path is within nothing. Their contract: nil when either
     argument is not a string, is remote, or does not exist (for
     containment, DIR must be a directory); never signals; a remote name is
     asked only =file-remote-p=, so no connection is ever opened. Six
     corollaries:
     1. =file-equal-p= is REJECTED for identity and =file-in-directory-p=
        for containment. =file-equal-p= compares the two truenames' whole
        =file-attributes= lists, timestamps included, so a directory written
        to between its two stats compares unequal to ITSELF (4,817 of
        20,000 calls under a concurrent writer; wave3-review-fixes step 1);
        =file-in-directory-p= compares truename components case-sensitively
        and ENDS in =file-equal-p=. No production code under
        config/workspaces/ calls either.
     2. String comparison of PATH spellings (=equal=, =string==,
        =string-equal=, =string-prefix-p= on expanded or truenamed paths) is
        permitted ONLY as a hash key or a de-duplication PRE-FILTER whose
        false negatives are harmless, a loop guard, or a documented
        spelling check — never as the identity or containment verdict.
        Permitted at f860103c, by name so the grep does not misfire: the
        truename hash key in =jf/workspace--worktree-dedupe-dirs=
        (git-worktrees.el:34-54; a dedupe); the ROOT key of
        =workspace-bookmark--identity-memo= (portable-bookmarks.el:252-253)
        and the truename key of the per-call discovery memo
        (=--discovery-memo-key=, :1206-1216); the loop guards
        =(equal current previous)= in =workspace--path-within-p=
        (data-model.el:309; the verdict is =workspace--same-file-p= at :310)
        and in =workspace--purge-in-scope-p='s nearest-existing-ancestor
        walk (workspaces.el:145); the de-dupe of a workspace's
        =:buffer-files= (data-model.el:227); the LEXICAL confinement
        assertion in =workspace-bookmark--anchor= (portable-bookmarks.el:1692-1693),
        which is by design a spelling check (DD-16). Comparing two
        normalised IDENTITY strings (:1457, :1545) or registry KEYS (tabs.el
        :196 EXCEPT-NAME, workspaces.el:110) is not a path comparison and is
        outside this rule.
     3. Both predicates have ONE home: data-model, the package's FIRST
        module (workspaces.el:44), because git-worktrees loads before tabs
        and core must never name a =workspace-bookmark-*= symbol. data-model
        never writes, holds no state beyond the registry shapes it defines,
        and reads the filesystem only in these two stat-level predicates
        (data-model.org header; home-org.org agrees). Consumers call them
        UNGUARDED — an unguarded call to a module that always loads first is
        the honest dependency; a guard would silently turn identity into
        "never equal". No module keeps a copy.
     4. nil means NOT PROVABLY the same / inside — never "different". A
        caller that REFUSES on a match (a uniqueness guard) must decide the
        remote case itself. Remote (TRAMP) workspace homes are UNSUPPORTED
        (user decision 2026-09-27): every command that registers a home —
        =workspace-new= on both branches and =workspace-re-anchor= — refuses
        a remote home FIRST (=workspace--refuse-remote-home=, tabs.el:133;
        tabs.el:255, :319; workspaces.el:90), so the duplicate-home guard's
        nil is never read as "different"; a remote home found in the state
        file is loaded TAGGED BROKEN with no filesystem call and one warning
        (persistence.el:136-148), never restored; =workspace-purge= of it
        unregisters only and touches nothing on the remote host.
     5. A DESTRUCTIVE scope check needs STRICT containment:
        =workspace--purge-in-scope-p= (workspaces.el:129) refuses the default
        parent itself in any spelling, judges a vanished home by its nearest
        existing ancestor, and is RE-TAKEN after the confirmation prompt,
        immediately before teardown and the recursive delete.
     6. One home per workspace: no two registry entries' homes are
        =workspace--same-file-p=. Enforced by =workspace--registered-for-home-p=
        at anchor (tabs.el:256) and re-anchor (workspaces.el:93, EXCEPT-NAME =
        the re-pointed key); the default branch relies on its home not
        existing, which is sound because NAME must be a single directory
        component (=workspace--refuse-non-basename=, tabs.el:144) and not
        already a registry key (=workspace--refuse-taken-name=, tabs.el:159).
   why_it_matters: |
     The project paid for this three ways in one cycle. (a) Ownership:
     =file-equal-p= in the workspace-owned test made a workspace's OWN full
     clone classify =canonical= while its home was being written to — a
     wrong-file offer, the defect this change exists to remove
     (wave3-review-fixes; the reverted fix failed 153 of 1000 calls under
     a real writer). (b) Spelling: outcome plists carry the registry's
     spelling (/var/...) for a workspace checkout and git's truename
     (/private/var/...) for the canonical one (end-of-cycle finding 7), so
     a consumer comparing :path or :checkout by string disagrees with
     itself between the two. (c) Case and links: a case-flipped home
     spelling and a symlinked prefix are the same directory on this
     machine's filesystem and must own / dedupe the same
     (portable-bookmarks-discovery-spec.el "never offers it when the home is
     registered with its letter case flipped" and the spelling specs). Each
     is a silent wrong answer, not an error, which is what earns an
     invariant over a comment. Cycle-1790453714 added a fourth: a
     predicate whose nil means "cannot tell" was read as "different" by a
     uniqueness guard, and re-anchoring an already-registered REMOTE home
     REPLACED that workspace's registry entry (arch-on-touch-1790453714-51)
     — hence corollary 4.
   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: lint + test
     location: |
       Lint: the greps in validators 1-3 and 6 over config/workspaces/*.el,
       run by the architect on-touch and at end-of-cycle. Test:
       config/workspaces/test/file-identity-spec.el — both predicates'
       contracts (spellings, links, case, missing, non-string, remote with
       NO filesystem call, never signals) and the write-between-reads and
       real-concurrent-writer races for both core sites, each with a
       =file-equal-p= / =file-in-directory-p= control asserted to FAIL
       under the same model; workspace-delete-purge-spec.el § "workspace-purge
       scope safeguard decides containment by file identity", § "... is
       STRICT", § "workspace-purge on a remote :home", § "... re-checks scope
       after the confirmation prompt"; workspace-new-anchor-spec.el § "remote
       (TRAMP) workspace homes are refused" and § "registry-name guard";
       broken-home-load-spec.el § "A remote :home in the state file" (a
       handler that SIGNALS on any filesystem operation);
       broken-home-runtime-spec.el § "workspace-re-anchor refuses another
       workspace's home (duplicate-home guard)"; workspace-new-default-spec.el
       (name guard); portable-bookmarks-discovery-spec.el § "Canonical safety
       while the workspace home is being written to" for the module's
       callers.
     enforcement_locus_check: |
       Where is this enforced? At each comparison site — which is why the
       validator counts sites rather than asserting an outcome: one
       remaining =file-equal-p= or =file-in-directory-p= re-opens the race
       at that site only and nothing else fails. Does it prevent silent-fail?
       Only the grep does for NEW sites; the race is timing-dependent and a
       behavioural spec passes almost always against the wrong predicate
       (hence the deterministic write-between-reads model, with a control
       that must fail). The remote corollary is enforced at the registering
       commands and the load path, not in the predicates (validator 6).
   validator: |
     Structural (no scaffolding in this project):
     1. grep -n 'file-equal-p\|file-in-directory-p' config/workspaces/*.el
        -> hits ONLY in the rejecting docstrings (data-model.el:268,
        :297-298; verified at f860103c). Test side: grep -n
        '(file-equal-p\|(file-in-directory-p' config/workspaces/test/ -> only
        the CONTROLS (file-identity-spec.el, workspace-delete-purge-spec.el)
        and the spy that asserts it is never called
        (portable-bookmarks-discovery-spec.el).
     2. grep -n 'defun .*same-file-p\|defun .*path-within-p'
        config/workspaces/*.el -> exactly ONE body each, both in
        data-model.el; no =workspace-bookmark--same-file-p= /
        =--path-within-p= anywhere under config/.
     3. grep -n 'string=\|string-equal\|(equal \|string-prefix-p' config/workspaces/*.el,
        read at every site whose operands are PATHS -> each hit is one of
        the permitted uses listed in statement 2; no hit decides ownership,
        containment, "is this the home" or "is this the same worktree".
     4. Both predicates return nil, never signal, and make NO filesystem
        call for a remote path (file-identity-spec asserts no
        =file-attributes= / =file-truename= call), and return nil for a
        non-string and a missing path.
     5. Core's sites pass the write-between-reads race specs
        (file-identity-spec § "workspace--registered-for-home-p while the
        home is being written to", § "jf/workspace--worktree-children while
        the child is being written to"; purge-spec's containment race).
     6. Every =puthash= into =workspace--registry= that ADDS a key
        (tabs.el:280, :329; workspaces.el:122; persistence.el:148, :159) is
        preceded on its path by the remote refusal (commands) or the remote
        arm (load), and, for commands, by =workspace--refuse-taken-name= /
        =workspace--refuse-non-basename= and a duplicate-home decision.
   known_violations: |
     CLEARED cycle-1790453714 (verified at f860103c):
     - tabs.el =workspace--registered-for-home-p= (now a =maphash= of
       =workspace--same-file-p=, :196-197) and git-worktrees.el
       =jf/workspace--worktree-children= (:260-262) — the two core sites at
       b56f385a.
     - workspaces.el purge safeguard (=file-in-directory-p=) — now
       =workspace--purge-in-scope-p= (:129).
     - Test-side =file-equal-p= in portable-bookmarks-jump-spec.el and
       -off-workspace-spec.el; persistence-spec.el's =file-in-directory-p=.
     - =workspace-re-anchor= had no duplicate-home guard (now :93).
     OPEN, externalised (owner .tasks/workspaces-registration-hardening-leftovers.md,
     arch-on-touch-1790453714-70..72; remote homes are never used here):
     - the tab-bar display name (=workspace--display-name=, tabs.el:72-82)
       reads =home.org= of ANY registered workspace a tab is named for,
       including a broken REMOTE one — a TRAMP contact on redisplay, the
       one remaining filesystem operation on a remote home;
     - load-time duplicate names or homes in a hand-edited state file win
       silently; a case-variant NAME duplicate is still possible on a
       case-insensitive parent.
   design_anchor:
     section: tasks/open/wave3-review-fixes.md § step 1; tasks workspaces-file-equal-p-timestamp-race, cycle2-review-cleanups, registration-and-purge-guards (cycle-1790453714); user decision ask-cycle-1790453714-4
     quote: |
       "Add one private predicate that compares device and inode numbers
       of the two paths' truenames (nil when either does not exist), and
       route EVERY same-file comparison in the module through it. No
       module code may call file-equal-p afterwards." — and, 2026-09-27:
       remote homes are never used; REFUSE them at create/anchor with a
       clear message.
   test_corpus:
     - config/workspaces/test/file-identity-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/workspace-delete-purge-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/workspace-new-anchor-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/workspace-new-default-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/broken-home-load-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/broken-home-runtime-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-discovery-spec.el
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery
     - register/vocabulary/checkout-classification
     - register/vocabulary/bookmark-resolution-outcome
     - register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer
     - register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
     - register/invariant/home-required-no-floating-workspaces
     - register/invariant/registry-name-equals-basename
   cited_by_tasks:
     - wave3-review-fixes
     - discovery-cleanup-singulars-and-docstrings   # its docstring edits touch the anchoring section that carries the lexical exception
     - jump-seam                                    # consumer of :path / :checkout
     - missing-repo-choice                          # consumer of :path / :checkout
     - off-workspace-restore                        # candidate ordering compares checkouts
     - workspaces-file-equal-p-timestamp-race       # cycle-1790453714: the predicate moved to core; both core sites routed
     - cycle2-review-cleanups                       # cycle-1790453714: containment predicate; remote refusal; anchor name guard
     - registration-and-purge-guards                # cycle-1790453714: strict purge scope; remote load/purge; name guards; re-anchor dup-home guard
   discovered_from: cycle-1789935943 meta-discovery path-identity-by-device-inode
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** spec-registry-isolation-via-fixtures
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-spec-registry-isolation-via-fixtures
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/spec-registry-isolation-via-fixtures
   tier: invariant
   status: reconciled
   status_note: |
     cycle-1790453714 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     SEEDED from cycle-1 meta-discovery
     "shared-global-test-state-worked-around-per-file" and end-of-cycle
     finding 4 (three spec files each swap =workspace--registry= for a
     fresh table; three commit-a-file helpers; five git wrappers). OWNER:
     =consolidate-portable-bookmarks-test-fixtures= (wave 1, alongside
     jump-seam; on-touch fires on its first merge). load_bearing is TRUE:
     the class has already cost gate time in this project (see
     why_it_matters), which is the template's bar. SCOPE: the statement
     binds the specs the fixtures module serves
     (portable-bookmarks-*-spec.el, git-worktrees-spec.el) and every NEW
     spec under config/workspaces/test/; the 20 pre-existing core specs
     that =clrhash= the live table in place are recorded below as the
     repo-wide generalisation, owned by
     .tasks/test-suite-shared-global-state-isolation (promotion = ask 19,
     pending), NOT by this cycle's fixtures task. Test hygiene is in scope
     by the user's 2026-09-20 amendment. SPECULATED for execute to settle:
     the macro's exact shape — that it saves the live table, installs a
     fresh =equal= table, runs fixtures-setup, and restores inside
     =unwind-protect= so a signalling spec body cannot leak the empty
     table; and whether the four pre-existing call-process git wrappers
     can share the fixtures' process-file wrapper without changing their
     assertions (if not, the task says why in ## Observations and this
     entry records them as accepted).

     cycle-1790453714 integrate (2026-09-27, against f860103c; code
     identical to 45df6dfd): RECONCILED to the shipped shape
     (consolidate-portable-bookmarks-test-fixtures, then every new spec of
     the cycle). The three per-file registry swaps and the local commit
     helpers are GONE; nine spec files use the one macro. The speculated
     SHAPE did not hold: the registry scope is a DESCRIBE-LEVEL,
     HOOK-INSTALLING macro =workspace-bookmark-fixtures-with-empty-registry=
     (portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el:176) that expands to a =before-each=
     (isolate, start a fixture scope, run SETUP-BODY) and an =after-each=
     (tear down; the live table comes back in =--teardown='s
     =unwind-protect=), not a body-wrapping =unwind-protect= form; it rests
     on four PUBLIC primitives (=-isolate-registry=, =-restore-registry=,
     =-live-registry=, =-setup-with-empty-registry=). DECIDED here (review
     consolidate #5): the primitives stay public (renaming them is churn for
     no safety) and validator 1 now COUNTS a direct call of the swapping
     primitives outside the fixtures file and its own spec; the macro's
     expansion pin (portable-bookmarks-spec.el "expands to the before-each /
     after-each pair the specs rely on", review #3 accepted-with-note) is
     the codified contract the parallel tasks built against. Validators
     REWORDED per review #4: validator 1's scope is the statement's files
     (transient-menu-spec.el's six =cl-letf= bindings belong to the
     repo-wide item); validator 3 counts commit-HELPER definitions, not the
     literal "commit"; validator 2 permits the =puthash= of an updated plist
     under a FIXTURE-registered name into the isolated table
     (arch-on-touch-1790453714-46). Generalisation (arch-on-touch-1790453714-43,
     -54, -64): module GLOBALS other than the registry, and the CURRENT
     BUFFER / =default-directory=, leak across files too — the jump state
     now has a fixtures-owned reset (=--reset-jump-state=, run first in
     =--teardown=); the deleted-directory buffer class is recorded on the
     externalised item (user declined promotion, ask-cycle-1789935943-19).
     Note:
     .orchestrator/cycles/cycle-1790453714/reconciliations/invariant-spec-registry-isolation-via-fixtures.md

     cycle-1790604160 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode:
     RE-STATED for cycle 3 against c65efb86; status unchanged. Validators
     1-3 re-verified at c65efb86 (no registry rebinding; primitives called
     only in fixtures.el and portable-bookmarks-spec.el; zero local commit
     helpers). The "Observed, advisory" stand-in count is CONFIRMED at HEAD
     and WIDENED (eoc-1790453714-03): =workspace--registered-names= stand-in
     in SIX files (cost-spec.el:44, git-worktrees-spec.el:53,
     discovery-spec.el:49, jump-spec.el:47, missing-repo-spec.el:64,
     resolver-spec.el:44); fake-remote file-name handler in FIVE files, two
     dialects (name-ops-pass: broken-home-load-spec.el:151,
     workspace-delete-purge-spec.el:579, workspace-new-anchor-spec.el:363;
     only-file-remote-p: git-worktrees-spec.el:209,
     portable-bookmarks-cost-spec.el:366); bookmark-jump harness in THREE
     (jump-spec.el:69-170, missing-repo-spec.el:103-215,
     off-workspace-spec.el:103-280; off-workspace's =--visits-p= :273 lacks
     the Dired arm jump-spec's :161 has). # owner:
     test-stand-in-consolidation (cycle-1790604160, wave 3, LAST) —
     SPECULATED, execute settles: the statement's "one owner" discipline
     EXTENDS from the registry scope to every stand-in (validator 6 below, a
     COUNTING spec, is the durable half); the fake-remote handler's home is
     a CORE test-helper file, because the accepted rule in known_violations
     ("a core spec must not depend on a droppable consumer's fixtures")
     binds broken-home-load / workspace-delete-purge / workspace-new-anchor;
     the registered-names stand-in and the jump harness go into
     portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el. The other two code tasks of this cycle
     (add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate, outcome-action-mapping) are told
     in their bodies: any NEW stand-in goes into the fixtures module, none
     inline, so the wave-3 count lands on a set that did not regrow in waves
     1-2.
   load_bearing: true
   title: Specs never rebind the shared workspace registry themselves; the fixtures module owns the one registry scope
   statement: |
     SCOPE: the specs the fixtures module serves — portable-bookmarks-*-spec.el,
     git-worktrees-spec.el, file-identity-spec.el — and every NEW spec file
     under config/workspaces/test/. In scope, no =*-spec.el= =setq=s,
     =let=/=cl-letf=-binds, or keeps its own saved copy of
     =workspace--registry=, and none calls the swapping primitives
     directly. The ONE registry-scope construct is the fixtures module's
     describe-level macro =workspace-bookmark-fixtures-with-empty-registry=
     (portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el:176): it installs a =before-each= that
     sets the live table aside, installs a fresh =equal= hash table, starts
     a fixture scope (=workspace-bookmark-fixtures-setup=) and runs the
     describe's SETUP-BODY, and an =after-each= that tears the scope down
     and puts the live table back. Restoration holds on EVERY exit:
     buttercup runs the =after-each= even when the body or an earlier hook
     fails, =--teardown= restores the registry in an =unwind-protect= even
     when a temp directory cannot be deleted, and a SETUP-BODY that signals
     deliberately leaves the scope OPEN so the failing spec's body still
     runs isolated. The ONE commit-a-file helper is
     =workspace-bookmark-fixtures-commit-file (repo relpath &optional
     contents)= (:295); multi-file or merge-conflict commits go through the
     fixtures' runner =workspace-bookmark-fixtures-git= (:224). Every spec is
     green in ISOLATION (./bin/run-tests.sh -d config/workspaces exits 0)
     AND in the FULL run (failure set = the cycle baseline;
     compare-baseline.py prints NO-REGRESSION); a spec that passes in one
     mode and not the other is a violation of THIS invariant before it is
     anything else. The fixtures file's own =(defvar workspace--registry
     ...)= (fixtures.el:47) is a load-order fallback definition, not a
     rebinding, and stays.

     The same discipline covers the portable-bookmarks module's own jump
     globals: =workspace-bookmark-fixtures-reset-jump-state= (:88) cancels
     a pending restore-then-jump finish step and clears the jump target and
     its armed flag, and runs FIRST in =--teardown=, so an off-workspace
     jump that did not drain its timers cannot leak into the next spec
     file.
   why_it_matters: |
     The buttercup runner loads every spec file into ONE Emacs process and
     runs once, so any global a spec leaves changed is the next file's
     starting state (.tasks/test-suite-shared-global-state-isolation: 21 ->
     3 gptel failures from one contaminating fixture). In THIS change's
     blast radius the class is already on the books: the fifth baseline
     failure is a config/workspaces spec (broken-home-runtime-spec.el:365-380)
     that errors on an out-of-range =substring= of a =*Messages*= buffer
     polluted by every suite before it, and passes in isolation — which is
     why every merge in cycle 1 paid TWO gates (isolated run exit 0 AND a
     full-run failure-set comparison) instead of one, and why the
     14-failure baseline cannot be read from an exit code
     (.orchestrator/baseline-failures-cycle-1789935943.txt § Notes). Cycle
     1 then wrote the same save / swap / restore plumbing three times
     (discovery, resolver and git-worktrees specs) because the fixtures
     module, written in wave 1, did not yet own a registry scope; each copy
     is a place where a missed restore on a non-local exit leaks a fresh
     EMPTY registry into every later spec and turns one failure into a
     load-order puzzle. One owner, one macro, one helper is the cheapest
     structural fix; the repo-wide runner change (per-file processes) is
     the .tasks item. Cycle-1790453714 showed the class is wider than the
     registry: an off-workspace jump spec that did not drain its timers
     left =workspace-bookmark--restore-in-progress= t and a pending finish
     step for whichever file loaded next (arch-on-touch-1790453714-43), and
     specs that left a buffer current in a deleted directory made every
     later subprocess spawn fail in the full run only
     (arch-on-touch-1790453714-54, -64).
   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: lint + test
     location: |
       Lint: validators 1-3 over the statement's files. Test: the two gates
       in the statement, run after every merge, plus the fixtures' own specs
       (portable-bookmarks-spec.el § "portable-bookmarks fixtures: registry
       isolation": a fresh =equal= table holding only the spec's fixtures;
       an entry the spec writes stays out of the live table; the scope stays
       open when setup signals; the live table comes back even when a temp
       directory cannot be deleted; and the expansion pin), and
       portable-bookmarks-jump-spec.el "leaves nothing of the off-workspace
       jump pending for the next spec: the fixtures' teardown cancelled the
       finish step and cleared the jump state".
     enforcement_locus_check: |
       Where is this enforced? In ONE macro and ONE teardown, so
       restore-on-exit is written once and a bug in it fails every spec at
       once (loud) rather than one spec's private copy (quiet). Does it
       cover all entry paths? Only for specs that use the macro — which is
       why validator 1 counts direct references to the registry AND direct
       calls of the swapping primitives instead of trusting the macro.
   validator: |
     Structural (no scaffolding in this project):
     1. Over the statement's files (portable-bookmarks-*-spec.el,
        git-worktrees-spec.el, file-identity-spec.el, and any NEW spec file):
        grep -n 'setq[^)]*workspace--registry\|saved-registry\|(workspace--registry '
        -> zero hits; AND
        grep -n 'fixtures-isolate-registry\|fixtures-restore-registry\|fixtures-setup-with-empty-registry'
        -> hits only in portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el and its own spec,
        portable-bookmarks-spec.el (verified at f860103c).
     2. grep -n 'workspace--registry' over the same files -> reads
        (gethash / maphash) and =remhash= of a fixture name, plus =puthash=
        of an UPDATED plist under a fixture-registered name into the
        isolated table (off-workspace-spec's saved-layout and broken-flag
        helpers) — never a rebinding.
     3. grep -n 'defun [^ ]*commit' config/workspaces/test/*-spec.el -> zero
        local commit HELPER definitions; single-file commits go through
        =workspace-bookmark-fixtures-commit-file=, others through
        =workspace-bookmark-fixtures-git= (inline =commit= arguments to it,
        e.g. resolver-spec's tracked-link commits and stamp-spec's conflict
        and batch commits, are permitted).
     4. ./bin/run-tests.sh -d config/workspaces exits 0 AND the full run's
        failure set equals the cycle baseline (compare-baseline.py).
     5. A fixtures spec pins restore-on-non-local-exit for the macro, and
        jump-spec pins that no jump state survives a spec.
     6. (cycle-1790604160, SPECULATED; owner test-stand-in-consolidation) COUNTING
        validator, run as a spec that reads the test directory: over
        config/workspaces/test/*-spec.el,
        grep -c '(defun workspace--registered-names\|remote-handler (operation\|--visits-p (path)\|defmacro [a-z-]*--hermetic'
        -> 0 (the one home of each is portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el or the
        core test helper the task names). Any spec that needs a stand-in
        requires it from there; the validator fails the suite when a copy
        regrows.
   known_violations: |
     CLEARED cycle-1790453714 (consolidate-portable-bookmarks-test-fixtures,
     verified at f860103c): the three per-file swaps (discovery-spec,
     resolver-spec, git-worktrees-spec) and the three local commit helpers
     (discovery-spec, resolver-spec, stamp-spec) are gone.
     ACCEPTED, recorded: the four =call-process= git wrappers in core specs
     (birth-offer-spec.el, integration-dispatch-spec.el, scaffold-spec.el,
     workspace-new-anchor-spec.el) stay — a core spec must not depend on a
     droppable consumer's fixtures, and scaffold-spec's wrapper ignores exit
     status by design, which =workspace-bookmark-fixtures-git= refuses to.
     Observed, advisory: the =workspace--registered-names= stand-in
     (=(unless (fboundp ...) (defun ... (maphash ...)))=) is copied in SIX
     spec files (cost, discovery, jump, missing-repo, resolver,
     git-worktrees); it reads only, and belongs in the fixtures module
     (arch-on-touch-1790453714-4 note). Naming debt: a CORE spec
     (file-identity-spec.el) now loads portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el for
     the one registry-scope macro; when the repo-wide item lands, the macro's
     home should be a core test helper that the portable-bookmarks fixtures
     build on.
     Repo-wide generalisation — owner
     .tasks/test-suite-shared-global-state-isolation (promotion DECLINED by
     the user 2026-09-27, ask-cycle-1789935943-19); NOT this change:
     - 20 core spec files =clrhash= the live table in before-each with no
       restore (31 sites at f860103c; one more than at b56f385a, in a new
       describe of workspace-delete-purge-spec.el) — grep 'clrhash
       workspace--registry'; transient-menu-spec.el:51, :60, :69, :80,
       :135, :150 =cl-letf=-bind it per =it= (dynamic scope is the safe
       shape, but a second scope construct). Both mutate production state
       in place; the full-run gate tolerates them today only because each
       file starts by clearing.
     - The current buffer / =default-directory= naming a deleted directory
       (buffer-reincarnation-spec.el fixed at its source; six more carriers,
       arch-on-touch-1790453714-64) — appended to the same item.
   design_anchor:
     section: design.md §D11; end-of-cycle-1789935943 finding 4
     quote: |
       "Fixtures needed: a temp-repo builder (init a repo, set a remote,
       add a worktree), a fake workspace registry entry pointing at a temp
       home, and a temp bookmark-default-file so no test touches the real
       bookmark list."
   test_corpus:
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-fixtures.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-spec.el
     - config/workspaces/test/portable-bookmarks-jump-spec.el
   related_entries:
     - register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer
     - register/boundary/git-derived-checkout-discovery
     - register/boundary/workspace-git-worktrees-consumer
   cited_by_tasks:
     - consolidate-portable-bookmarks-test-fixtures
     - jump-seam                                   # new jump-spec.el must use the macro, not a fourth swap
     - missing-repo-choice                         # new missing-repo-spec.el likewise
     - off-workspace-restore                       # new off-workspace-spec.el likewise
     - discovery-identity-memo-and-cost-contract   # new spawn specs likewise
     - workspaces-file-equal-p-timestamp-race      # cycle-1790453714: a core spec (file-identity-spec) uses the macro
     - test-stand-in-consolidation                 # cycle-1790604160: stand-ins -> fixtures / core helper; validator 6
     - add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate          # cycle-1790604160: new stand-ins go to the fixtures module only
     - outcome-action-mapping                      # cycle-1790604160: likewise
   discovered_from: arch-cycle-1789935943-eoc-04
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

** add-worktree-offer-shares-workspace-health-gate
   :PROPERTIES:
   :CUSTOM_ID: register-invariant-add-worktree-offer-shares-workspace-health-gate
   :END:

   #+begin_src yaml
   entry_id: register/invariant/add-worktree-offer-shares-workspace-health-gate
   tier: invariant
   status: speculated
   status_note: |
     cycle-1790604160 (workspace-portable-bookmarks) plan, forward-mode: SEEDED from
     eoc-1790453714-01 (BLOCKING) and meta-discovery 1 ("registration
     invariants were enforced command by command; a fourth gap in a
     consumer gate"). # owner: add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate
     (cycle-1790604160, wave 1, FIRST merge). load_bearing TRUE: the violation has
     already cost a blocking finding and, on the probes, a recreated home
     with no home.org (the template's bar). SPECULATED for execute to
     settle: (a) the predicate's NAME and home — one module-private
     function next to =--broken-workspace-p= (portable-bookmarks.el:892), e.g.
     =workspace-bookmark--workspace-usable-p (name)= = registered AND not
     =workspace--broken-p= AND :home a string that is not =file-remote-p=
     AND =file-directory-p= (in that order: remote first, so a remote home
     is never stat'ed); (b) that =--offerable-workspaces= (:903-915) is
     routed through the SAME predicate, so the off-workspace picker also
     drops a workspace whose home vanished mid-session (today it drops
     tagged-broken only) — one spec row; (c) the prompt wording of the two
     new withheld reasons. Core-side gap (menu gate is broken-p only) is
     NOT this entry's enforcement locus; see why_it_matters.
   load_bearing: true
   title: The add-worktree offer is gated by ONE workspace-health predicate — the menu's notion, never a second one
   statement: |
     portable-bookmarks offers "add worktree here" (the =add-worktree=
     member of the missing-repo action set) ONLY for a current workspace
     that is USABLE: registered, not tagged broken (=workspace--broken-p=),
     and whose =:home= is an existing LOCAL directory (=file-remote-p= nil,
     then =file-directory-p= t). Exactly ONE predicate in the module
     answers "usable"; =workspace-bookmark--missing-repo-withheld=
     (portable-bookmarks.el:618-643) and the off-workspace candidate
     filter =workspace-bookmark--offerable-workspaces= (:903-915) both read
     it; no site re-derives health from the registry plist. The predicate
     is the consumer's mirror of core's Integrations-menu gate
     (=workspace--menu-healthy-p=, workspaces-transient.el:11-14, which the
     consumer may not name: workspaces-transient loads after it) PLUS the
     on-disk check the load-time broken tag cannot carry
     (=register/invariant/broken-tag-runtime-only=). "Runnable"
     (=--add-worktree-runnable-p=, :605-616, via
     =workspace--integration-menu-command-p=) answers REGISTRATION and is
     a separate, orthogonal condition — it says nothing about health.
     Withheld answers name their reason in the prompt.
   why_it_matters: |
     eoc-1790453714-01: in a BROKEN current workspace (a desktop-restored or
     hand-renamed tab carrying its name, or a home deleted mid-session)
     every stamped jump resolves =missing-repo= (=--home-checkouts= of a
     missing home is nil), the chooser offered "add worktree here", and
     =git worktree add= (git-worktrees.el, =jf/workspace--add-worktree=)
     recreated the missing home as a bare directory holding only the
     worktree: no home.org, no sessions/, not the workspace's repository.
     The broken tag is runtime-only, so the next load found the home present
     and loaded it HEALTHY with no home.org. Core never offers the command
     in a broken workspace (the Integrations group is =:if
     workspace--menu-healthy-p=); the consumer copied the menu's REGISTRY
     walk (=--add-worktree-runnable-p=) but not its GATE — "a new command
     added later that forgets the broken-state guard", the failure
     =register/vocabulary/workspace-broken-disposition= exists to prevent,
     arriving through a consumer. The same module applied the broken
     disposition to its OTHER workspace-facing prompt (the off-workspace
     picker), i.e. two health notions in one file — the second-notion
     drift this entry forbids.
   enforcement_mechanism:
     kind: test + lint
     location: |
       Test: portable-bookmarks-missing-repo-spec.el § "missing-repo choice:
       the offer" (:243) gains two rows — current workspace tagged broken
       -> add-worktree withheld with its reason; home deleted after
       registration -> withheld; healthy -> offered (existing) — each red
       with the gate reverted; portable-bookmarks-off-workspace-spec.el §
       "candidates" (:518) gains "home vanished mid-session is not
       offered". Lint: grep -n 'workspace--broken-p\|file-directory-p'
       config/workspaces/portable-bookmarks.el -> the health predicate only
       (plus =--checkout-dir-p= :1097, which is about checkouts, not
       workspaces); =--missing-repo-withheld= and =--offerable-workspaces=
       each call the predicate by name.
     enforcement_locus_check: |
       Where is this enforced? In the consumer, at the OFFER — before any
       directory-creating call. It does not enforce anything in core: the
       menu's gate stays =broken-p= only, so a home deleted mid-session
       still reaches =jf/workspace--add-worktree= through
       =workspace--menu-invoke-integration= (workspaces-transient.el:25-38)
       — the core half of the same cell (path "add worktree" x invariant
       "home exists on disk"), recorded as finding arch-cycle-1790604160-plan-01
       and routed to .tasks/workspaces-registration-hardening-leftovers.
       When core grows a published "usable" predicate (a permitted symbol
       on =register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer=), this module's
       predicate should route to it and the two notions become one in
       code, not only in statement.
   validator: |
     Structural (no scaffolding in this project):
     1. Exactly ONE defun in portable-bookmarks.el tests both
        =workspace--broken-p= and =file-directory-p= of a workspace home;
        =--missing-repo-withheld= and =--offerable-workspaces= both call it.
     2. The withheld alist has an arm for the unusable-workspace case whose
        REASON names why (broken / home missing / home remote); the prompt
        prints it (=--missing-repo-prompt= :657-674).
     3. Specs: the two offer-matrix rows and the candidates row above; the
        probe P1 scenario (broken-p = t, home absent) yields withheld
        non-nil and NO call of =jf/workspace--add-worktree=.
     4. =file-remote-p= is tested BEFORE =file-directory-p= (a remote home is
        never contacted — =register/invariant/directory-identity-by-device-inode=
        records remote homes unsupported).
   related_entries:
     - register/vocabulary/workspace-broken-disposition
     - register/invariant/broken-tag-runtime-only
     - register/boundary/portable-bookmarks-consumer
     - register/vocabulary/bookmark-resolution-outcome
     - register/invariant/no-silent-wrong-file-open
   cited_by_tasks:
     - add-worktree-broken-workspace-gate   # cycle-1790604160: owner
   discovered_from: eoc-1790453714-01
   discovered_by: architect
   #+end_src

| confirmed   | Implementation matched the speculation; entry is now load-bearing for next cycles.      |
| divergent   | Implementation revealed the speculation was wrong; awaits user disposition or rewrite.  |
